# thesis-fidelity

VERDICT: Partly fit, and unevenly so. The *mechanism* half of the thesis — policy as a signed, semver, pinned dependency, composed from parents and distributed by Flux — is genuinely built, machine-checked on the citable run (verify-feed-contract PASSes 24 pin assertions against real remotes), observed live in an ephemeral KinD cluster on the adopters' clock, and in one respect exceeds the thesis: semver is computed from measured verdict movement and a weaker declaration is refused, which the 2022 post never asked for. The *doctrinal* half is where fitness breaks. (a) The one requirement the thesis calls non-negotiable — at least three coexisting versions with a retirement window — stands at ONE declared version; coexistence, retirement and shift-left all SKIP on run 21 from that single root cause; the gate's own bar is two, not three; the owned remedy (tickets 58 Q1 / 63) also reaches only two; and the retirement window has no mechanism at all — the DECIDED replacement (price a stale pin by the EOL ramp, open a retirement PR) is unimplemented in both halves, so the only retirement the estate ever performed was a flag day. (b) The mea culpa's locked-door half is demonstrated by verify/proportionality — the best single piece of thesis fidelity in the estate, deriving Audit-vs-Deny from each party's own signed £ band — but its control, encrypt-at-rest, exists ONLY in the hub's harness, in no unit repo, under no tag, pinned by nobody; and no policy anywhere in the estate governs access control, data protection or key management, the three the mea culpa names. Every Deny that ships is a meta-rule about the policy-version claim. (c) The human-governance layer's carrier is absent: three accepted, unsuperseded ADRs and CONTEXT.md require created/lastReviewed/rationale on every policy version, and there are zero such annotations, zero rationale.md, zero lastReviewed across all eight units — nothing is dated, reviewed on a cadence, or removed if undefended. (d) The last mile is behind the 2022 org: zero "handbook" hits in any unit. What would make it fit: declare a second and third policy line so the crux beat has a subject; implement the DECIDED supersede-pricing and the retirement proposal so a window exists; publish encrypt-at-rest (or another real data-protection control) as a versioned member of platform's line so the locked-door half is a dependency like everything else; land the ADR-0007 metadata (or amend ADR-0007 to say the £ replaced it); and add a customManager over policy/v* so a policy version can arrive as a Renovate PR at all.

STRENGTHS:
- The dependency graph is real and machine-checked against the real remotes on the citable run: talk/captures/verify_feed-contract_verify-feed-contract.out (run 21) ends PASS after 24 individual pin assertions across eight repos, e.g. 'PASS: driftwood pins feeds/feed/threat-register@v2: tag threat-register/v2.0.0 on feeds'. driftwood/composed/HEADER.yaml records each parent by {party, kind, version, sha}. This is a stronger consumption surface than the 2022 original, which pinned one policy repo rather than a five-parent inheritance graph.
- Semver is COMPUTED, not merely declared — an advance beyond the thesis. platform/computed-semver/ (7,524 lines of .py/.sh, the largest module in platform) derives the bump from measured verdict movement over a generated corpus; versions.yaml:79 carries a reviewed 'bump: "major"' field and cut-release-gate.py refuses a tag whose arithmetic disagrees. verify-gate PASSes on run 21, its capture naming 'a declared bump weaker than computed publishes DEGRADED ... the declared number is never rewritten'.
- The mea culpa's proportionality split is turned into a computation rather than an opinion. verify/proportionality/verify-proportionality.sh PASSes on run 21: 'risk_bought £21,107 | driftwood band £40,000 -> Audit | ludlow band £5,000 -> Deny ... ok committed policies match the £-derived render'. The script first asserts risk_bought is byte-identical across the two orgs and diffs the two rendered policies to prove they differ in <=6 lines. Nothing in the thesis proposed a mechanism for deciding which side of the lane-keeping/gate line a control falls on; this estate built one.
- 'Informed debate through pull requests rather than exemption requests' (research/03:79-88) is enforced structurally. grep -rn 'exemption' across all eight units returns 20 hits and every one is a negation or a reference to a deleted mechanism (compose/composition.py:36 'not an exemption: it is a DECLARED INABILITY, priced'; versions.yaml:173 'Silence is not an exemption'; render-governed-namespace-guard.py:18).
- 'Usable' and 'testable' are exercised on every adopter PR, not asserted. driftwood/.github/workflows/shift-left.yml reads the platform pin under review, checks platform out AT that tag, verifies the commit against the pin, runs the pinned kyverno CLI via platform/shift-left/ci-check.py against driftwood/deploy/pod.yaml, recomposes the party artefact, and signs a keyless cosign attestation posted into the PR. It went green on driftwood #16/#18/#19/#20 (ticket 61 Answer).
- The updatable loop completed once for real and is graded from the PR record rather than simulated. verify-renovate-merged-feed-pr PASSes on run 21: 'driftwood #20: Renovate raised threat-register v1 -> v2, Chris Nesbitt-Smith merged it, and party.yaml and composed/ moved together'. Independently confirmed: gh pr view 20 returns mergedAt 2026-09-01T08:57:50Z.
- Live, cross-org distribution fidelity IS observed on a clock and imported into the citable number. driftwood/.github/workflows/drift-sample.yml:127 runs 'kind create cluster --name ${name} --wait 120s' on a fresh ephemeral cluster, installs checksum-pinned kyverno/flux-operator/flux, reconciles from the real GitHub remotes and samples drift/five-facts.py's five facts; the three verify-reconcile.sh scripts grade that sample before looking for a local cluster. On driftwood's latest sample facts 1/3/4/5 are true — 16 of 16 rendered objects present, byte-equal and in the Flux inventory.
- The estate refuses to fake a subject it no longer has. With one declared version, verify-coexistence.sh:35-45 explicitly declines to loop a one-element array ('looping a one-element array to claim coexistence would be the false pass this project forbids. Do not invent a second version to keep the beat alive') and SKIPs with a stated reason; verify-retirement.sh and verify-shift-left.sh do the same. This is the 2026-08-25 Docker post-mortem's lesson holding under pressure.
- A real, original human-governance mechanism exists that the 2022 work had no equivalent of: platform/wargamer/rejection_ledger.py derives proposal suppression from closed, unmerged PRs with a half-life ('suppress while sum(0.5 ** (age_days / h)) >= reject_suppress'), keyed <org>/<kind>/<slug>, refuses to count a rejection whose curve hash or selection-policy version has moved, and when it cannot read the PR list returns empty AND says so, so a blind clock neither silently suppresses nor silently re-asks.

## [critical] TF-01 The estate cannot demonstrate multi-version coexistence at all today, and both the gate's threshold and the only planned remedy settle for a bar below the one the thesis and CONTEXT publish
(original title: The thesis's one non-negotiable — three coexisting versions — is at one, and the owned remedy tops out at two)
CLAIM: The thesis's one explicitly non-negotiable requirement — a runtime serving ≥3 semver policy versions concurrently (research/03-blogs-thesis.md:40, :172, :207; restated in CONTEXT.md:153-155 as "(≥3) ... *The crux of the original implementation*", never relaxed by any ADR or owner decision) — is met at one. platform/distribution/versions.yaml:77 holds the array's sole element, `{ version: "4.0.0", tag: "policy/v4.0.0", commit: "64635dfd...", bump: "major" }`, and it is the estate's only versions.yaml; driftwood's composed artefact carries only policies/v4.0.0.

At one declared version, four beats go SKIP on run 21 rather than proving anything live: coexistence (verify-coexistence.sh:36, threshold `-lt 2`), retirement (verify-retirement.sh:45, `-lt 2`), shift-left's Audit->Deny flip (verify-shift-left.sh:50, `-lt 2`), and verify-publisher-gate.sh:372 part C, which is the one check that does name three ("needs at least three declared versions and distribution/versions.yaml declares ['4.0.0']"). So the *coexistence* threshold is 2, not 3 — but the estate is not uniformly at 2: it has one three-version threshold, for a different property.

Two corrections to the fit impact. First, the estate is not unable to demonstrate coexistence "at all": verify-coexistence.sh step 1 passes offline, running `kyverno test` over the frozen retired trees policies/v2.0.0 and policies/v3.0.0 and asserting each self-scopes away from the other's pods. What is undemonstrated is coexistence of *declared* versions in one live runtime and one ValidatingWebhookConfiguration — the beat's own stated gap. The run-21 capture says exactly that: "SKIP: offline proof holds; live tail could not look". Second, the beat grades SKIP, not FAIL (run 21: pass=57 fail=7 skip=18), so it is a could-not-look, not a red — which is the instrument behaving correctly, since the script explicitly refuses to loop a one-element array to manufacture a pass (verify-coexistence.sh:41-43).

The owned remedy still tops out at two, as claimed: ticket 58's Q1 is PROVISIONAL on "(a) the ticket-63 isolated flip ... becomes the second line" (issues/58:18-19, 30-31), and ticket 63 records "the flip cut IS the second declared line (the engine computes a major, so 5.0.0)" (issues/63:18). Across all 74 tickets, grep for three-version phrasings returns nothing: the count target of three is owned by no ticket, and a green coexistence beat at two would assert something weaker than CONTEXT.md's own definition.

For completeness: the 2022-lineage reference org is also at two (fleet clusters/cluster1 and cluster2 policy-versions.yaml each declare 2.0.0 and 2.2.0) — but it reached three and was reduced by its own sunset automation in commit d2da2d0, "Sunset: retire policy 1.0.0 (scheduled 2026-08-15)"; its README still advertises three, now stale.

Two citation slips in the original finding, both off by a couple of lines and neither substantive: versions.yaml:77 not :79, and verify-coexistence.sh:36 not :35.
EVIDENCE: platform/distribution/versions.yaml:79 is the sole array element: `- { version: "4.0.0", tag: "policy/v4.0.0", commit: "64635df...", bump: "major" }`. platform/distribution/verify-coexistence.sh:35 reads `if [ "$n_versions" -lt 2 ]; then ... live_tail_skip`. Run 21 captures (git show origin/main:talk/captures/...): `.estate-clone_platform_distribution_verify-coexistence.out` ends `SKIP ... declares one version (4.0.0); coexistence needs two declared versions to show side by side, and retirement left one`. .scratch/ecosystem/issues/58-...md Answer records Q1 as PROVISIONAL on '(a) the ticket-63 isolated flip is the next computed major and becomes the second line'; issues/63-...md says 'this cut can be the coexistence subject'. grep for 'three versions|at least three' across .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ returns nothing. For completeness, the 2022 reference org is also at two: gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-flux/fleet/contents/clusters/cluster1/policy-versions.yaml decodes to versions 2.0.0 and 2.2.0 only.
AMBITION: research/03-blogs-thesis.md:40-41 and §7.2 ('Multi-version runtime is non-negotiable (≥3 semver versions concurrently, with a retirement window)'); CONTEXT.md:153-155; docs/PRD.md goal 2.
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket issues/58 (resolved, PROVISIONAL) -> issues/63 (open) — The "one version, need coexistence" gap is owned: REVIEW-2026-08-31 M11(1) -> ticket 58 (resolved provisional, decision: "declare a second policy line so coexistence, retirement and shift-left have a subject") -> ticket 63 (open, blocked on 58's second-version decision, notes "this cut can be the coexistence subject"). No ticket, REGRILL answer, or ADR targets the thesis's literal ≥3 bar (research/03-blogs-thesis.md:40-41, CONTEXT.md:153-155) — grep for "three version(s)|at least three" across .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ returns nothing, and ticket 58's own answer frames the remedy as reaching two ("second declared version"), matching verify-coexistence.sh's n_versions -lt 2 threshold. REVIEW-2026-08-31's "Refuted claims" list does not mention this gap, so it is not refuted. So: the ownership chain covers "demonstrate coexistence at all" but does NOT cover "reach three" — the finding's central claim (gate/tickets top out at 2, thesis demands ≥3) is correct and is a real, currently-unowned gap. Recommend: keep TF-01 open as a residual/new finding layered on top of the ticket-58/63 chain, specifically flagging the count mismatch (2 vs ≥3) that no open work item addresses.
FIT IMPACT: The single claim the thesis calls non-negotiable is the one the estate cannot currently demonstrate at all, and the planned fix would still leave the estate one version short of its own published bar. A demonstration that reaches two would let a green gate assert something weaker than CONTEXT.md's own definition.
REMEDY: Either declare three lines (ticket 63's isolated flip plus one more, e.g. re-issuing a Priority-triple-repaired 3.x line) and raise verify-coexistence.sh's threshold from 2 to 3 so the check matches CONTEXT.md; or the owner amends CONTEXT.md:153-155 and NORTH-STAR to say two is the estate's bar and records why the thesis's three was dropped.

## [critical] TF-02 Policy cannot be retired the way a dependency is: one version runs, falling behind costs nothing and nothing proposes moving — the thesis's transition window is a regression against the 2022 org that had it working
(original title: The retirement window has no mechanism, and the DECIDED replacement is unimplemented in both halves)
CLAIM: The thesis's stated reason for multi-version coexistence is a transition window (research/03-blogs-thesis.md:40-41; CONTEXT.md:153-155). ADR-0010's consumer-side `sunset:` was retired in favour of ticket 13 Answer item 5 (`.scratch/ecosystem/issues/13-lift-or-retire-the-original-mechanisms.md:83`, DECIDED, therefore binding): "a pin behind a newer published version is priced by the existing EOL ramp ... the adopter's scheduled proposer reads pins against the feed and opens a retirement PR". Neither half is built. The pricing half is not merely unbuilt but architecturally blocked where it would live: `eol_ramp`/`eol_scenario` (platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py:74,92) have no caller outside their own file, no policy version is a component of any eol feed (platform/feeds/eol/v2/eol-feed.json and feeds/eol/v2/feed.json both carry only python-3.9, ubuntu-20.04, istio-1.18, kyverno-1.10), no adopter pins the eol feed (driftwood/party.yaml:51-61 and peers), composition.py:190,3560 records that "an `eol` parent kind does not exist in the party artefact schema", and composition.py:3566-3570 asserts the module imports no clock at all — so the "from the newer version's publish date" term cannot be computed there. The proposal half is a reserved key namespace only: tier_pr.py builds cage-tier PRs alone (:3,288-291) while rejection_ledger.py:17,82-84 and proposer_bounds.py:70-73 merely note that a retirement proposal would be a different key.

The estate is not, however, without any retirement mechanism, and the finding should not say so. Deleting the array element prunes the version and denies stragglers (platform/distribution/verify-retirement.sh, render-orphan-guard.py --retire); a retirement forces a MAJOR bump in the publisher's own release number (platform/computed-semver/comparison_window.py:126-133,197-204); and the adopter's gate folds retirements as major and names them (driftwood/.github/scripts/adopter-gate.py:196-200,304-315,389-390) when Renovate's platform-pin customManager (driftwood/renovate.json #2, and peers) opens the bump PR. What the estate lacks is the WINDOW: no advance signal before a version is deleted, no price for sitting behind one, and no proposal asking an adopter to move before its version disappears — the estate has retirement and an after-the-fact grade, and nothing in between.

That gap was live on 2026-08-29: platform commit 3dab34d deleted 2.0.0, 2.0.1 and 3.0.0 from the array and driftwood commit cb2bd3c moved deploy/pod.yaml from label "2.0.0" to "4.0.0", same day, same commit title, by hand. Mitigating and recorded in versions.yaml itself: those lines could not admit a pod at all and read the tier from the pod's own forgeable label, so no adopter could have been running them — an emergency retirement, not a routine break. Run 21 still cannot exercise the beat at all: verify-retirement.sh SKIPs because the array declares one version (4.0.0).

Ownership is not merely absent; it was handed off and dropped. Ticket 13:87 gives the retirement PR to ticket 10, ticket 10:75,90 accepts only the dedupe key, ticket 13:90 carries items 1, 3 and 4 into build tickets and excludes item 5, and ticket 10 graduated the ledger without the proposal. No build ticket among the 74 carries either half.
EVIDENCE: grep -rn 'sunset' across all eight unit clones returns two prose comments only (platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py:17, platform/feeds/README.md:70) — no field, no code. The EOL ramp exists (platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py:74 `def eol_ramp`) but platform/feeds/eol/v2/eol-feed.json's `components` are python-3.9, ubuntu-20.04, istio-1.18, kyverno-1.10 — no policy version is an entry. platform/compose/composition.py:1952-1967 (`compute_prices`) iterates only edges whose kind is in FEED_KINDS; driftwood/composed/evidence.json prices[] has four entries (ico, feeds, insurer, twin) and no policy-staleness line. The only proposal kind the proposer builds is cage-tier (platform/wargamer/tier_pr.py:3,288-291); platform/wargamer/rejection_ledger.py:16-17,82-84 merely reserves a key namespace for a retirement proposal. .scratch/ecosystem/issues/13-...md Answer item 5 is one of only five DECIDED items in the tracker. Run 21: .estate-clone_platform_distribution_verify-retirement.out ends `SKIP: ... declares one version (4.0.0), so a retirement would leave an empty allow-list`.
AMBITION: research/03-blogs-thesis.md:40-41 ('transitionary periods for old policy versions to be retired'); CONTEXT.md:404-406 (the supersede definition); ticket 13 Answer item 5 (DECIDED, therefore binding).
OWNER: duplicate-of-0831-finding-still-open REVIEW-2026-08-31.md M11 (retirement clause) — M11's first clause ("coexistence, retirement and shift-left without a subject") graduated only to ticket 63 (open), which builds the second declared policy version (the coexistence subject, a 5.0.0 major cut) — .scratch/ecosystem/issues/63-the-unlabelled-default-flips-to-isolated.md's Question and its 2026-08-31 comment say only "the flip cut IS the second declared line ... re-carries the root-if-attested conditional branch"; nothing in ticket 63 mentions pricing or a retirement proposal. Ticket 13's Answer item 5 (DECIDED, .scratch/ecosystem/issues/13-lift-or-retire-the-original-mechanisms.md:83) and ticket 10's cross-ticket note C16 (issues/10-schedules-and-skills.md:75) design the EOL-ramp pricing of a stale policy pin and the adopter's scheduled proposer opening a retirement PR, but grepping every open ticket (17,27,30,31,33,34,35,37,38,39,44,45,46,48,51,53,56,59,62-70,71,72,73,74) for compute_prices/rejection_ledger/EOL ramp/retirement/proposer finds none that builds it — ticket 74 (open) only watches the existing cage-tier proposer's EOL-ramp/feed-bump crossings (component EOL, not policy-version EOL), not a retirement-kind proposal. So TF-02 restates the still-true, still-unowned half of M11: only the "subject" half (two coexisting versions) was picked up by ticket 63; the pricing-and-proposal mechanism itself has no owning ticket.
FIT IMPACT: The 2026-08-29 retirement of 2.0.0/2.0.1/3.0.0 was a flag day: three versions deleted in one edit with no window, no advance signal and no price for being behind — the exact failure mode CONTEXT.md:153-155 says coexistence exists to prevent. An adopter that falls behind today pays nothing and is warned by nothing, so 'policy behaves like a dependency' is untrue on the axis dependencies are hardest on.
REMEDY: Smallest honest fix: add the platform policy line to the eol feed (or an equivalent `supersedes: {version, published}` on the newer release element) and extend compute_prices to price a parent pin behind the newest published version; then teach the adopter's scheduled proposer to emit a retirement-kind proposal on the key rejection_ledger already reserves. Alternatively the owner records that the window is dropped and the £ alone is the signal, and ADR-0010 gets its superseding ADR.

## [major] TF-03 The £ never selects the enforcement action in any shipped artefact: proportionality is proven only in a hub bench rig, while every Deny an adopter actually pins was hand-authored upstream
(original title: The mea culpa's locked-door half is demonstrated only by a harness fixture that is not a versioned dependency)
CLAIM: encrypt-at-rest — the only control for which the £-to-enforcement-action render produces an actual Kyverno policy body — exists solely as hub-side fixtures under verify/proportionality/ and appears in zero unit repositories: it is in no party's publishes[] (platform publishes only distribution/policies and identity), in no composed/ tree, under no signed tag (the hub carries no tags at all, and no platform tag tree contains a file matching 'encrypt'), is not distributed by Flux and is pinned by no adopter. verify-proportionality.sh:59 proves it by re-checking hub-committed renders (`render.py --check`), and it passes in run 21.

Two corrections to the surrounding framing. (1) It does not carry the *whole* proportionality demonstration: platform/risk/verify-risk-tuned.sh, which lives in a real unit repo under signed tags, demonstrates the same £-picks-Audit-vs-Deny flip over driftwood-cart-pii.json and also passes in run 21; encrypt-at-rest uniquely carries only the rendered-policy-body half. (2) The dependency mechanism HAS carried gate-shaped controls: posture-trust-boundary ships with validationActions: [Deny] in platform's distribution/policies/v4.0.0, sits under the signed policy/v4.0.0 tag, and is composed into driftwood, ludlow and tuppence; cage-netpol generates empty-ingress/egress NetworkPolicies for the isolated rung.

The accurate shortfall is narrower and sharper: no *published* control's enforcement action varies by adopter. The three adopters' composed/policies/v4.0.0 trees are byte-identical but for the `composed-for` label — require-nonroot is Audit everywhere, posture-trust-boundary Deny everywhere — so the £→action seam that the mea culpa's gate-vs-lane split turns on has never crossed the publish boundary; it is exercised only on hub fixtures. A larger instance of the same gap sits alongside it: platform's publishes[] declares an identity-substrate package (SPIRE, OpenBao, Pomerium — the thesis's own access-control and crypto-key-management categories) whose flux-pin.yaml pins identity-substrate/v1.1.0, a tag that does not exist in the repo, and which no adopter's gitops/ references.
EVIDENCE: Files: verify/proportionality/control/encrypt-at-rest.tmpl.yaml, verify/proportionality/policies/encrypt-at-rest-{driftwood,ludlow}.yaml, verify/proportionality/scenarios/encrypt-at-rest.json, verify/proportionality/tests/encrypt-at-rest/. `grep -rn 'encrypt-at-rest'` across scratchpad/units/{platform,nist,ico,driftwood,tuppence,ludlow,feeds,insurer} returns zero hits. verify-proportionality.sh:59 checks the committed fixtures with `python3 "$HERE/render.py" --check` — i.e. the policies are hub-side committed renders, not published artefacts.
AMBITION: research/03-blogs-thesis.md:122-139 (the locked-door limit) and §7.1 ('Do not build a gate-only system'; equally, the gate half must exist); docs/PRD.md:37-38 'Build both.'
OWNER: new TF-03 — No ownership found. Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md, docs/adr/*.md, .scratch/ecosystem/REVIEW-2026-08-31.md, .scratch/drift-review-2026-08-27/REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, NORTH-STAR.md §6/§7/§8, and .scratch/ecosystem/map.md's Out-of-scope section for "proportionality", "encrypt-at-rest", and "locked-door"/"locked door" — zero hits in every file except docs/adr/0003 and 0014 (matched only on unrelated substrings, no proportionality content). No open ticket claims this gap, no resolved ticket claims to have fixed it, REVIEW-2026-08-31's C1/M1-M18 and Refuted-claims lists don't mention it, and NORTH-STAR's out-of-scope/owner-decision sections (power layer, covert sensing, surveillance data, video-as-clock) don't cover it either. This is a genuinely new finding.
FIT IMPACT: The lane-keeping half of the split (require-nonroot at Audit) is a genuine versioned dependency; the locked-door half is a bench rig. So the estate proves the *pricing rule* that decides which side a control falls on, but has never shipped a catastrophic-boundary control through the dependency mechanism the thesis is about — the one case where the thesis says the mechanism must yield to a gate is the one case the mechanism has never carried.
REMEDY: Publish encrypt-at-rest as a real member of platform's version tree (it already has a template, a scenario and kyverno tests), let composition render it per adopter from each party's own appetite band, and have verify-proportionality read the composed artefacts instead of hub-side fixtures. If the owner prefers it stay a harness demonstration, say so in ADR form so a reader is not misled by the capture's 'committed policies match the £-derived render' line.

## [critical] TF-05 The estate's replacement for the thesis's locked door — that the bottom rung runs and reaches nothing — has never been observed by the only surface the project permits itself to cite, so "a platform enacts priced cages" is an unciteable claim with no ticket that would make it citable
(original title: 'There is no gate, everything is caged' has never been observed on any citable run, and the hub gate cannot ever observe it)
CLAIM: The beat "the bottom rung runs and reaches nothing" — the estate's replacement for the mea culpa's locked door — has never been graded PASS on any of the 18 TRUTH runs recorded in talk/truth.log, and cannot be while the citable gate has no cluster. .estate-clone/platform/graded/verify-graded.sh has never exited 0: it FAILed on runs 9, 12 and 13 (offline core, the kyverno-1.19 instrument fault of ticket 54) and has exited 3 SKIP from run 15 through run 21, verified on run 21 from the workflow log itself (gh run view 33616685427 --log: "SKIP  SKIP: offline proof holds; live tail could not look: kind cluster 'driftwood' is not listed"). The cause is structural: no version of .github/workflows/truth.yml has ever installed kind or kubectl (0 matches across all 7 commits touching it), and platform/lib.sh substrate_ok() gates every live tail on `kind get clusters`, so 11 of run 21's 18 skips are cluster-absence skips. What the citable instrument DOES observe on every run is the cage's decision logic offline, with the estate's own pinned Kyverno 1.18.2 (`kyverno test tests/cage-tier` and `tests/cage-netpol`, verify-graded.sh:74,78): tighten-only at every rung, isolated mutated-not-refused, isolated's generated NetworkPolicy an ingress+egress deny-all. What it never observes is enforcement — no pod is created, admitted by a real API server, or blocked by a CNI on any citable run; the live tail that does that (verify-graded.sh:416-535, a real pod plus an exec and TCP connect) never runs. The compensating driftwood lane does create a cluster (drift-sample.yml:127) and its facts 4 and 5 do citably observe the cage OBJECTS live and Flux-owned, but drift/five-facts.py:73-79's five facts are all distribution fidelity; none is an admission verdict. Ownership is not absent, as the auditor claimed: ticket 52 asked for exactly this — "drives the seven NORTH-STAR §4 steps in order on an ephemeral KinD cluster inside the scheduled truth run" (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/52...md:9) — and is marked Status: resolved, with verify/e2e/lib.sh:21-25's `cluster_up()` (`kind create cluster --name pav-e2e`) defined and called by no step script. The gap is a ticket closed resolved without its cluster half, plus dead harness code, so the load-bearing claim rests on offline engine renders plus an undated code comment (platform/distribution/versions.yaml:43-49).
EVIDENCE: talk/captures/.estate-clone_platform_graded_verify-graded.out on origin/main ends `SKIP: offline proof holds; live tail could not look: kind cluster 'driftwood' is not listed by kind get clusters — the Namespace declares the tier and the pod wears it; the cage only tightens; the bottom rung runs and reaches nothing; TCoR booked`. `git log --format='%H %ci' origin/main -- talk/captures/.estate-clone_platform_graded_verify-graded.out` shows the file last changed at f3b87c7, 2026-08-31 20:41 — byte-identical through run 21. `grep -n 'kind create' .github/workflows/truth.yml` returns nothing; the workflow runs on ubuntu-latest and installs only gitsign, kyverno, cosign and flux CLIs (lines 70-91). The compensating live lane (driftwood drift-sample.yml:127) does create a cluster, but drift/five-facts.py:75-79's five facts are all distribution-fidelity facts (ready-at-pin, tag signature, last-applied-revision, byte-equality, Flux inventory) — none observes an admission verdict or a cage.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 2 ('Everything is always caged ... There is no gate', with its own caveat 'that a refusal is therefore the bottom rung ... is my reading, not your words'); NORTH-STAR §5 ('a green that could not look is a red'); research/03:128 (the locked door).
OWNER: new none — Not owned. Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/* (03, 09, 54, 58, 60 read in full/part), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's M1-M18 and Refuted-claims list, REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, and NORTH-STAR/map Out-of-scope. Ticket 03 (resolved) owns the design that a live tail SKIPs honestly when no cluster exists ("Every live-claiming script asserts its substrate first" / SKIP with reason) — this is the "substrate rule" the finding itself credits as honest and ticket-03-owned. But no ticket, ADR, or REGRILL answer owns actually giving the citable truth.yml surface (or a lane-committed sample, per ticket 60's pattern) a cluster on which cage/admission behavior (the bottom-rung 'runs and reaches nothing' claim) can be graded true. Ticket 54 fixed toolchain pins (kyverno/cosign/jsonschema) on the citable gate but only made existing offline checks pass/SKIP correctly — it does not add cluster-based admission grading. Ticket 60 rewired verify-reconcile/e2e-step4 to grade cluster-free from driftwood's ephemeral-cluster lane samples, but confirmed (and TF-05's evidence corroborates via five-facts.py:75-79) those five facts are distribution-fidelity only (tag/signature/revision/byte-equality/inventory) — none is an admission verdict. Ticket 58's Q4 (observation-lane server-side cage on public repos) is a different topic (enforcing the observation lane's own protection, not grading cage behavior on workloads). Refuted-claims list item "The observation lane is conventional, not enforced" is also a distinct claim about ADR-0024/rulesets, not this one. REVIEW-2026-08-31's M13-M18 (the only "Owning ticket: none" rows) cover map staleness, fall-detection, twin scoping, tags, enact_guard, and the demo-check step — none is this finding. Conclusion: no existing ticket, ADR, or owner decision makes this moot or owns closing it; it should be raised as a new ticket/grilling question.
FIT IMPACT: The estate's replacement for the mea culpa's locked door is the load-bearing doctrinal claim of the whole redesign, and it has never been graded true by the instrument the project says is the only citable source. Everything asserted about the cage rests on offline renders plus one undated local observation recorded in versions.yaml's comment.
REMEDY: Either extend the adopters' existing ephemeral-cluster lane (drift-sample.yml already builds one correctly) with two more facts — 'a pod at the isolated rung is admitted' and 'it reaches nothing' — so cage behaviour enters the citable number the same way distribution fidelity already does; or record in ADR form that cage behaviour is proven only offline and say so on the deck.

MINORS:
- TF-09 'Communicable' is half-built: 14 of 24 signed tags carry no release, and no notification spine exists: The PRD's named mechanisms for the communicable -able — release notes and a notification-controller Alert firing on a new tag — are respectively partial and entirely absent.
- TF-10 'Measurable' answers a truthfulness question, not the thesis's adoption question, and the better instrument may not be cited: The estate built a genuinely better adoption instrument than a PR search (verify-feed-contract's 24-assertion pin matrix against the real remotes) but grades only tag existence rather than distance behind, and NORTH-STAR §5 makes the TRUTH line — which measures whether the estate's claims are true, not who has adopted what — the only citable source.
- TF-11 The 'reviewed PR' premise has no instance of an independent reviewer: Across all nine repositories there is no branch protection and no ruleset on main, and every human PR merge — including the one merged Renovate bump the thesis's updatable -able rests on — was authored and merged by the same identity.
- TF-12 The estate's coexistence bar (2) is weaker than its own glossary definition (≥3): verify-coexistence.sh grades the beat as showable at two declared versions, so a fully green truth surface would still not evidence CONTEXT.md's own '(≥3)' definition of multi-version coexistence.
- TF-13 ADR-0004's 'one engine, both planes' has no code and no dated sequencing note: The PRD's goal 3 and the thesis's runtime-multi-version-distribution hook across a cloud plane have zero implementation in any unit repo, and ADR-0004 reads plain `status: accepted` despite ticket 13 item 3 saying it would gain a dated sequencing note.
- TF-14 research/20's falsified recommendation was never marked superseded in place: research/20-synthesis-faithful-flux-mapping.md:97 recommends keeping the 2022 objectSelector self-scoping mechanism 'verbatim'; building it revealed Kyverno flattens every ValidatingPolicy's objectSelector onto one shared webhook config, silently breaking coexistence, and the correction lives only in docs/HISTORY.md:27-34 and docs/PRD.md §6.4 — research/20 still reads as current advice.

Nobody has decided whether the target is two, three, or none. | CHANGES: If three: tickets 63 and 58 need a sibling ticket for the third line, verify-coexistence.sh's threshold moves from 2 to 3, and TF-01/TF-11's shortfalls become owned. If none: the whole retirement/window family (TF-02) becomes a documented retirement rather than a gap, and three verify scripts should be deleted rather than left SKIPping.
- Ticket 13 item 5 is one of only five DECIDED items in the tracker and says a pin behind a newer published version is priced by the EOL ramp and that the adopter's proposer opens a retirement PR. Neither exists in code. Do you still want it built, or did the decision lapse? | WHY: It is the estate's entire replacement for ADR-0010's sunset mechanism and for the thesis's transition window. Today the eol feed prices python, ubuntu, istio and kyverno but not a policy version, compute_prices touches only feed edges, and the only proposal kind the proposer builds is cage-tier. So an adopter that falls a major behind pays nothing and is told nothing. | CHANGES: If built: a new build ticket lands two changes — a policy-line entry (or a supersedes field) that compute_prices can read, and a retirement-kind proposal on the key rejection_ledger already reserves — and TF-02 closes. - The mea culpa names access control, data protection and cryptographic key management as the things that belong behind a locked door, and the estate ships no policy in any of the three; the one control that demonstrates the split (encrypt-at-rest) lives only in the hub's verification harness. Should encrypt-at-rest be published as a real versioned member of platform's line, or is the harness demonstration deliberately the end of it? | WHY: As it stands the estate proves the *pricing rule* that decides which side of the split a control falls on, but has never carried a catastrophic-boundary control through the dependency mechanism the thesis is about. verify-proportionality's capture reads 'committed policies match the £-derived render' — true, but those are hub-side fixtures, not artefacts any adopter pins. | CHANGES: If published: platform's version tree gains a member, composition renders it per adopter from each party's own appetite band, verify-proportionality reads composed artefacts instead of fixtures, and the split is demonstrated by the mechanism — which also gives TF-01's second line a candidate subject other than the isolated flip. If not: an ADR should record that the locked-door half is demonstrated by comparison only, so the deck and the talk do not imply otherwise.
- ADR-0007, ADR-0006 and ADR-0001 all require every policy version to carry created/lastReviewed/rationale as annotations plus a versioned rationale.md, and none of it exists anywhere. Did the £ replace the review metadata — a price re-derived on a clock instead of a review date — or is the metadata still owed? | WHY: 'Every accepted practice carries a date. Every practice must be regularly reviewed... it gets removed. Not archived. Not deprecated. Removed.' is the mea culpa's second and most emphasised addition. Today nothing in the estate is dated, has a review cadence, or is removed for being undefended, and 'Purposeless policy...' appears zero times in the units. If the £ is the substitute, it is arguably a stronger test than a date — but no document says so and three accepted ADRs still say the other thing. | CHANGES: If the £ replaced it: ADR-0007 gains a dated addendum, CONTEXT.md:127-128's definition is removed, and TF-06 becomes a documentation fix. If still owed: a build ticket adds the annotations to the generated policy bodies plus a release check that fails on a stale lastReviewed — and the human-governance layer stops being the plank with the least code behind it.
- The twin and its test suite are 53,301 lines — 54% of the estate's executable code — against ~14,600 (15%) implementing the versioned-dependency thesis, and 23% of tickets versus 35% for pricing/twin/insurance/feeds. Is the thesis still the subject of this estate, or is it now one participant in an eco-system whose subject is priced governance? | WHY: Both readings are defensible and the widening is your own ratified decision (NORTH-STAR §2 gives the twin and the insurer their own rows). But docs/PRD.md still frames the whole build as the thesis, NORTH-STAR §1 does not mention semver, coexistence or Renovate at all, and no single document reconciles the two. A reader arriving to learn 'policy as a versioned dependency' meets the forecasting code first. | CHANGES: If the thesis is still the subject: the dependency-thesis tickets (TF-01, TF-02, TF-08) outrank the twin's open tickets in the remediation order, and the PRD's framing stands. If the eco-system is the subject: the PRD needs a dated banner saying it is now one chapter, the seven -ables stop being acceptance criteria for the whole build, and the shortfalls above are re-graded as gaps in one participant rather than in the project's premise.
- No citable run has ever observed a policy denying or caging anything, because .github/workflows/truth.yml creates no cluster and verify-graded has SKIPped on every scheduled run since 2026-08-31. The adopters' drift-sample lane does build a real ephemeral KinD cluster, but its five facts are all distribution-fidelity facts. Should cage behaviour join that lane? | WHY: 'Everything is always caged... the bottom rung runs and reaches nothing' is the estate's substitute for the mea culpa's locked door and the load-bearing claim of the redesign. It currently sits on a SKIP line every run, and by construction the hub gate can never grade it. NORTH-STAR §5 says a green that could not look is a red — this is a red that is honestly reported as could-not-look, permanently. | CHANGES: If yes: two facts are added to drift/five-facts.py (a pod at the isolated rung is admitted; it reaches nothing), the doctrinal claim enters the citable number the same way distribution fidelity already does, and TF-05 closes. If no: the deck, the talk and the ADRs should say that cage behaviour is proven offline only, and verify-graded's PASS-line wording after the em-dash should stop asserting what the SKIP could not observe.

# participants

VERDICT: Half the §2 clause is genuinely built and the other half is not. "Each participant lives in its own GitHub organisation" and "signs its own artefacts" hold: eight of nine rows are single-repo orgs (the ninth, the twin, has no org at all — `gh api orgs/policy-as-versioned-twin` is a 404), and all 24 tags across the eight units verify against Rekor under gitsign, each identity-pinned to that repo's own cut-release.yml OIDC subject, with no exception. "Ships on its own cadence" half-holds: nine clocks exist and are registered, but feeds' and insurer's have failed every scheduled run they have ever had, tuppence's and ludlow's propose-tier fail at checkout every day, driftwood's twin-sweep fails every day, and nist's succeeds while writing null. "Consumed only through a pinned, signed dependency" does not hold: exactly two of the five parent edges (platform, nist) carry a real tag+commit Flux pin — and those two are exactly correct, which shows the pattern is understood — while ico, feeds and insurer are consumed with no pin at all, at a moving `main` in driftwood and at a branch that no longer exists in tuppence and ludlow; three publisher release gates load the platform's default branch as their instrument; and the truth surface itself clones every unit's default branch rather than its now-existing signed tag. "No participant reaches into another" fails in one structural place: driftwood's twin-sweep runs inside `hub/.estate-clone/driftwood` because the twin has no tag to pin. The sharpest single defect is not coupling but provenance: the insurer's gitsign-verified tag v1.0.0 publishes quotes asserting they were priced against `driftwood/tuppence/ludlow exposure v1.1.0`, and no adopter's v1.1.0 tag contains an `exposure` section at all — two of the three digests match the adopters' unsigned working trees and driftwood's matches nothing, while the insurer's own clock has REFUSED on all three legs on both of its runs. To be fit, this dimension needs, in order: a signed adopter tag that actually carries the exposure section (or a commit-level pin) so the insurer's claim becomes true; ticket 62's twelve stale refs closed; ico/feeds/insurer given real tag+commit pins including a Flux source; the publisher release gates and clone-estate.sh moved onto tags; and a twin tag so driftwood can stop being a tenant of the hub's directory layout.

STRENGTHS:
- Signing is real and I re-derived it: 24 of 24 tags across all eight unit repos return `Good signature from [.../cut-release.yml@refs/heads/main]` and `Validated Rekor entry: true` under `git -c gpg.format=x509 -c gpg.x509.program=gitsign tag -v`. No unsigned tag, no bad signature, anywhere. This is the most load-bearing §2 property and it holds without exception.
- Eight of the nine §2 rows are their own single-repo GitHub organisation, confirmed live: `gh api orgs/policy-as-versioned-<u>` returns repos=1 for platform, nist, ico, driftwood, tuppence, ludlow, feeds and insurer (only `policy-as-versioned-twin` 404s).
- Every pin that exists is exactly correct. I resolved each against the real tag: platform v2.0.1 -> 533dccb0a823001b396fd60ab08014bf75065a37 (matches all three adopters' gitops/platform/platform-pin.yaml); nist v1.1.0 -> 33a05df1f5241bca6ffbc1c69a70075cdb7a5819 (matches all three gotk-sync-nist.yaml); insurer's platform pin v1.1.1 -> 58ef9c57e53543997e5ddbe829a7b7c9a2282c60. Tag and commit, both right, in every case.
- One participant genuinely carries a real external datum: nist's catalogue is the verbatim NIST SP 800-53 rev 5.2.0 OSCAL file; `scripts/verify_catalog.py` re-derives 1196 controls across 20 groups and the on-disk sha256 matches CATALOG_VERSION.json exactly.
- Refusal-over-guessing is implemented in participants' own code and observable under live load: insurer/pricing/quote.py exits `REFUSED: missing instrument: ... carries no `exposure` section -- there is no signed exposure to attach a layer to` (gh run view 33615860064) rather than emitting a number, and nist's fetch clock writes `"it looked and found none, which is a fact with a date on it, not a silence"` rather than staying silent.
- The estate records its own defects in-tree with dates: insurer/party.yaml documents the fabricated-v1.2.0 laundering found 2026-08-29; tuppence's and ludlow's gotk-sync.yaml explain why `./apps` never becomes Ready; driftwood/gitops/apps/pod.yaml records the runAsNonRoot-on-nginx kubelet defect and its runAsUser:101 fix.
- The worst live coupling breach is already owned and accurately diagnosed: ticket 62's 2026-09-01 comment independently names the same twelve `ecosystem/thin-slice` checkouts my own grep counted, and correctly notes the feeds half is now unblocked because feeds cut threat-register/v1.0.0 and v2.0.0.

## [critical] P1 The signature stops being evidence: a gitsign-signed quote and a signed adopter balance sheet both attest a priced exposure that exists at no signed version — the second shipment of the same laundering class
(original title: The insurer has never priced a quote from a signed exposure; its signed tag asserts a provenance that cannot exist)
CLAIM: The insurer has never priced a quote from a signed exposure; its signed tag v1.0.0 asserts a provenance that has never existed.

Every published insurer quote names `<adopter> exposure v1.1.0` plus an `exposure_sha256` as its provenance (insurer party.yaml:51-53; quote/{driftwood,tuppence,ludlow}/v1/feed.json). No adopter's v1.1.0 tag tree contains an `exposure` section — the string does not occur anywhere in the tuppence or ludlow v1.1.0 trees, and in driftwood's only as prose in drift/verdict.yaml:8. Nor could it: v1.1.0 was cut on 2026-08-25 18:06 and the exposure section first landed on driftwood's main on 2026-08-29 02:19. The adopters' v1.1.0 party.yaml also carries no `publishes:` key at all, so the ADR-0019 point 5 discovery record the pin's own justifying comment cites does not exist at the pinned version either — the pin resolves to nothing by two independent routes, exactly as the fabricated v1.2.0 it replaced did.

The quotes were priced from the adopters' unsigned main working trees. Recomputing with insurer/pricing/quote.py:104-109 over current main: tuppence and ludlow reproduce their recorded digests exactly; driftwood's recorded 397abe81 was real on main between 2026-08-29 02:19 and 2026-08-31 17:19 and moved on 2026-09-01 to 5822260b, so it is now stale as well as unsigned. The insurer's scheduled requote clock, which does check out the adopter at the pinned tag (.github/workflows/fetch.yml:267-273), has refused on both of its only two firings (runs 33496526156 and 33615860064) with "carries no `exposure` section -- there is no signed exposure to attach a layer to". The offline gate does not catch it because verify-insurer-quote.sh reads sibling working trees (ADOPTERS_DIR=..) and calls that content "signed exposure", passing 20 of 21 checks.

The false provenance is inside a genuinely sigstore-verified artefact: gitsign reports "Good signature", "Validated Rekor entry: true" on insurer v1.0.0 (632db22), which contains all three quotes. Two corrections of degree to the original: driftwood's composed/evidence.json premium line of £113,403.30 carrying the same non-existent provenance is on main only, not in any signed driftwood tag (driftwood v1.1.0's evidence.json contains no insurer line), so that half is unsigned; and the earlier v1.2.0-to-v1.1.0 "fix" is not a separate commit — git log --all -S'v1.2.0' in insurer returns only db76705, the commit that first authored both the quote and the v1.1.0 pin, so the fix narrative exists only as retrospective prose written in the same commit as the defect.

Ownership: none. Ticket 69 covers driftwood's pin on an untagged quote, ticket 62 covers adopters' unpinned CI checkouts; neither covers the insurer's own pin naming a tag whose tree lacks the priced section. Ticket 36's resolved Answer ("the insurer pins the platform and the adopter signed exposure") remains false with no dated correction. Critical stands.
EVIDENCE: insurer/party.yaml:51-53 pins `{party: driftwood|tuppence|ludlow, kind: feed, name: exposure, version: "v1.1.0"}`. `git -C <adopter> show v1.1.0:composed/HEADER.yaml` yields top-level keys `policy-as-versioned.dev/composed, parents, baseline, governed-namespaces, holes, selected-controls, ungoverned-namespaces` and no `exposure`, for all three; each adopter has only tags v1.0.0 and v1.1.0. `git -C insurer show v1.0.0:quote/driftwood/v1/feed.json` carries `priced_against ... {"party":"driftwood","name":"exposure","version":"v1.1.0","exposure_sha256":"sha256:397abe81d6cbdbd7b27a5d2516dd9c35417e9922f995b8b6af7d1fca34829820"}`. Recomputing with the insurer's own canonicalisation (insurer/pricing/quote.py:104-109) over each adopter's current unsigned main HEAD: tuppence sha256:25418091ed892212... MATCH, ludlow sha256:b0808d7448a35bae... MATCH, driftwood recorded 397abe81... vs computed 5822260bab861329... MISMATCH. Live: `gh run view 33615860064 --repo policy-as-versioned-insurer/insurer` (2026-09-02 schedule) shows requote (driftwood|tuppence|ludlow) all `failure` with `REFUSED: missing instrument: .adopters/<adopter>/composed/HEADER.yaml carries no `exposure` section -- there is no signed exposure to attach a layer to`; identical on run 33496526156 (2026-09-01). Those are the insurer's only two scheduled runs.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2 insurer row ('a signed quote against an adopter's declared attachment... consumes the adopter's priced exposure'); §2 'consumed only through a pinned, signed dependency'; §3 principle 6 ('every artefact carries a signature that says what it does and does not assert'); §4 step 4 ('the residual is on the balance sheet').
OWNER: new none — No ticket owns this. Ticket 69 covers driftwood's pin on an *untagged* quote (pricing a hole for lack of signature) — a different defect. Ticket 62 covers unpinned/branch-ref checkouts of ico/feeds/insurer — also different (this finding's pin is already tag+commit, just false). Ticket 36 is resolved with Answer text "the adopter signed exposure" — the finding shows that's now false, and per the ownership rule a resolved ticket whose Answer claims fixed does not own a finding that says it's still true, and there is no dated correction. REVIEW-2026-08-31's M1 (unrunnable feeds/insurer repos) and M8 (Renovate/step-2 never real) are adjacent but do not mention the exposure-section-missing-from-v1.1.0-tree defect at all, and this finding isn't in that review's Refuted-claims list. insurer/party.yaml's own comment (lines ~36-45) documents a prior fabricated-v1.2.0 laundering incident "found 2026-08-29" and claims the fix (pinning to v1.1.0) is now correct ("the one whose tree carries the exposure section") — the finding directly contradicts that self-claimed fix with fresh command output (HEADER.yaml keys, live gh run 33615860064/33496526156 REFUSED errors), showing the comment's claim is itself false and uncorrected. No ADR or REGRILL-ANSWERS entry addresses this. This is a new, unowned P1.
FIT IMPACT: This is the second occurrence of the same laundering class in the same file — insurer/party.yaml's own comment records the first (a fabricated v1.2.0 that reached driftwood's signed evidence.json as the provenance of a six-figure cost line, found 2026-08-29). The fix substituted a real version number without checking the tag's tree, so driftwood's composed/evidence.json still carries a £113,403.30 premium line whose stated provenance does not exist. A signed artefact that asserts an untrue provenance is precisely what principle 6 exists to prevent, and it defeats the insurer row's whole purpose.
REMEDY: Cut a signed adopter tag whose tree carries the exposure section (or move the pin from a tag to a commit), then re-run the requote so a quote is produced from something signed. Independently: make quote.py refuse to emit a `priced_against` entry naming a tag whose tree lacks the named section, and add a gate check that resolves every `priced_against` pin against the tag it names. Add a dated correction to ticket 36.

## [major] P2 Two of the three named adopters cannot consume their publishers at all: a deleted branch ref kills compose, propose, shift-left and release on the clock, so §2's "ships on its own cadence" and §4's steps 2-3 hold for driftwood alone
(original title: Twelve cross-participant checkouts name a deleted branch, so two of three adopters cannot compose, propose, shift-left or release on any clock)
CLAIM: Twelve cross-participant checkouts in tuppence and ludlow name `ecosystem/thin-slice`, a branch that exists on neither feeds nor insurer since ticket 57 renamed their default branch on 2026-09-01, so two of the three adopters cannot compose, cannot run their scheduled tier proposal, and cannot cut a release. The twelve sites are {tuppence, ludlow} x {propose-tier.yml, shift-left.yml, cut-release.yml} x {feeds, insurer} (tuppence propose-tier.yml:182,189, shift-left.yml:360,367, cut-release.yml:97,104; ludlow 185,192 / 354,361 / 97,104), and they are present on both repos' live main (tuppence 4cc958a, ludlow 880868c, both 2026-09-02). Observed live: tuppence propose-tier run 33633036907 (schedule, 2026-09-02T12:59:26Z) and ludlow run 33518603510 (schedule, 2026-09-01T14:18:00Z) both die with "A branch or tag with the name 'ecosystem/thin-slice' could not be found", as does the compose-check job of the latest shift-left run in each repo (33558565944, 33558571123, 2026-09-01T21:00Z). Precisely: only propose-tier is on a clock (cron "49 8 * * *"); shift-left runs on pull_request and its policy-check job still passes (ludlow's did in the same run where compose-check failed), so what the stale ref kills there is composition, not the policy gate; and cut-release is workflow_dispatch-only and has not run since 2026-08-25, so its breakage is read from the code (the stale checkouts at :97/:104 precede tag creation at :124) rather than observed. driftwood, re-pinned to `ref: main` by ticket 61, is unaffected and its scheduled propose-tier succeeds. The comment beside the ref (tuppence shift-left.yml:353-354, "neither repo cuts a tag this adopter pins yet") is stale: feeds cut threat-register/v1.0.0 and v2.0.0 and insurer v1.0.0 on 2026-09-01. Owned by ticket 62 (open, blocked by 57 which is only "claimed"), whose 2026-09-01 comment records the same twelve checkouts and the same failure; no open PR on either adopter repo fixes it.
EVIDENCE: grep of `ref: ecosystem/thin-slice` gives 12 sites: {tuppence,ludlow} x {propose-tier.yml, cut-release.yml, shift-left.yml} x {feeds, insurer} (e.g. tuppence/.github/workflows/propose-tier.yml:182,189; shift-left.yml:360,367; cut-release.yml:97,104). `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-feeds/feeds/branches --jq '.[].name'` -> fetch/cve-2.0.1, fetch/eol-2.0.1, fetch/fx-1.1.0, fetch/threat-register-2.0.1, main, observations; insurer -> main, observations. Live: tuppence propose-tier run 33633036907 (2026-09-02T12:59:26Z) `##[error]A branch or tag with the name 'ecosystem/thin-slice' could not be found`; ludlow run 33518603510 (2026-09-01T14:18:00Z) identical. The comment beside the ref (tuppence/.github/workflows/shift-left.yml:353) says '`main` matches the ico line above: neither repo cuts a tag this adopter pins yet' — but the code says the stale branch, and feeds has since cut threat-register/v1.0.0 and v2.0.0 and insurer v1.0.0.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2 'ships on its own cadence' and 'consumed only through a pinned, signed dependency'; §4 steps 2-3 (the re-price and the proposal PR).
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 62 — Ticket 62 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/62-the-feed-parents-are-consumed-pinned-and-signed.md) is open and its 2026-09-01 comment already names this exact finding: "twelve checkouts carry the ref: tuppence and ludlow × {shift-left.yml, propose-tier.yml, cut-release.yml} × {feeds, insurer}", cites the same live failure ("A branch or tag with the name 'ecosystem/thin-slice' could not be found", tuppence propose-tier 13:42Z first firing), and already notes "feeds now carries real tags ... so the feeds half no longer waits on ticket 57; the insurer half still does" — matching the finding's own observation that feeds has since cut tags. Ticket status is open (not resolved), so this is a live open-ticket ownership, not a stale claim. Blocked-by field lists 57, but per the ticket's own comment that block is now partial (insurer only). No refutation of this appears in REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's Refuted claims list, and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md was not found to moot it.
FIT IMPACT: Two of the three adopter rows have no working path to compose, propose a tier, run shift-left or cut a release. NORTH-STAR §4's demonstration order cannot reach step 3 for tuppence or ludlow at all. The blocking ticket 57 is only 'claimed', but the feeds half no longer depends on it.
REMEDY: Re-point all twelve refs to the tag+commit party.yaml declares (feeds threat-register/v2.0.0 is available now; insurer v1.0.0 is available now), and add the branch-ref refusal check ticket 62 already specifies so a composing job cannot regress to a branch ref.

## [major] P3 The silent-change failure the thesis exists to prevent is live on three of five parent edges: driftwood's daily price is derived from unpinned, unsigned branch tips while its signed party.yaml declares pinned versions
(original title: Three of five parent edges are consumed with no pin of any kind — no Flux source, no tag, no commit)
CLAIM: Three of driftwood's five parent edges — ico, feeds and insurer — are consumed by ref, not by pin. There is no Flux GitRepository for any of the three in any adopter (PIN_FILES holds only nist/controls and platform/implementations), and party.yaml records only a bare floating-major version string ("v3", "v2", "v1") with no commit. That version string is not inert: verify-feed-contract resolves it to a real tag on the publisher's remote and PASSED for all three in run 21 (ico v3.0.0, feeds threat-register/v2.0.0, insurer v1.0.0), and step 6 verified those tags' gitsign certs and Rekor entries. What is missing is any binding of the consumed bytes to that verified tag: driftwood checks all three out at `ref: main` in nine places (propose-tier.yml:129,144,151; cut-release.yml:83,97,104; shift-left.yml:343,363,370), and the `sha` recorded in composed/HEADER.yaml is derived after the fact from whatever branch was read (`_resolve_unpinned_sha`, composition.py:405-435), not asserted before it. driftwood's scheduled propose-tier succeeded 2026-09-02T11:43:35Z, so the daily re-price genuinely read three parents' moving branches. tuppence and ludlow are worse on two of the three: they name `ref: ecosystem/thin-slice` for feeds and insurer in 12 places, and that branch no longer exists on feeds, so their propose/shift-left/cut-release path is dead rather than merely unpinned; neither declares an insurer edge at all. NORTH-STAR §2's "pinned, signed dependency" therefore holds end-to-end for exactly two of driftwood's five edges — and estate-wide only 7 of 18 declared parent edges carry a Flux pin, since ico's own nist edge and insurer's three exposure edges are unpinned too. Principle 4's "bump only by reviewed PR" is intact for the feeds and insurer declarations (Renovate customManagers #3/#4; driftwood PR #20 merged 2026-09-01) but not for the content inside a pinned major directory, which moves with no PR; ico has no bump surface at all. Ticket 62 (open, blocked by 57) owns the fix.
EVIDENCE: `grep -rn 'kind: GitRepository' {driftwood,tuppence,ludlow}/gitops` yields sources only for self, platform and nist — no ico, feeds or insurer object anywhere. party.yaml records only `version: "v3"` (ico) and `version: "v2"`/`"v1"` (feeds) — no tag, no commit. driftwood consumes all three at a moving default branch in nine places: propose-tier.yml:129,144,151; cut-release.yml:83,97,104; shift-left.yml:343,363,370, each `ref: main`. ico has a signed v3.0.0 (gitsign-verified) that driftwood names as 'v3' and never checks out. driftwood's propose-tier succeeded 2026-09-02T11:43Z, i.e. its daily re-price genuinely read three participants' unsigned moving branches.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2 'consumed only through a pinned, signed dependency'; §3 principle 4 ('Adopters pin by tag and commit').
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 62 — Ticket 62 ("The feed parents are consumed pinned and signed", open, blocked by 57) directly owns this finding: it names the same three edges (ico at ref:main, feeds/insurer at ref:ecosystem/thin-slice), the missing Flux pin for ico (GAPS 1.6), and the missing branch-ref verifier — closing REVIEW-2026-08-31's M10 (.scratch/ecosystem/REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:67, "§2's 'pinned, signed dependency' holds for platform and nist only. Owning ticket: none → new ticket 62"). Ticket file: .scratch/ecosystem/issues/62-*.md. Status is still open — a 2026-09-01 comment on the ticket even escalates it (feeds' ecosystem/thin-slice branch no longer exists, so tuppence/ludlow checkouts now fail hard, not just unpinned), so ownership is current, not stale. No REGRILL-ANSWERS.md entry or ADR supersedes it. Finding P3 adds independent evidence (driftwood's 9 ref:main line numbers, the 2026-09-02T11:43Z propose-tier run) but the defect and its fix are already fully scoped under ticket 62.
FIT IMPACT: §2's central coupling rule holds for exactly two of five parent edges. The £ that driftwood publishes in composed/evidence.json is derived, every day, from content that could change under it with no PR and no signature check — which undercuts principle 4's 'bump only by reviewed PR' for the three unpinned parents.
REMEDY: Move the three checkouts to the tag+commit party.yaml declares, add an ico Flux GitRepository alongside the nist one (same gitsign-identity annotation pattern), and land ticket 62's branch-ref refusal so this cannot silently return.

## [major] P4 A publisher's signature attests a release gate nobody can reconstruct: ico, insurer and feeds decide what they may assert using whatever platform main said that day
(original title: Three publisher release gates load the platform from its moving default branch, so what a publisher may assert depends on unpinned platform main)
CLAIM: Three publisher release gates load the platform from its moving default branch, so what a publisher may assert depends on unpinned platform main.

ico's, insurer's and feeds' release.yml each check out policy-as-versioned-platform/platform with no `ref:` (ico/.github/workflows/release.yml:59-62, insurer/.github/workflows/release.yml:60-63, feeds/.github/workflows/release.yml:50-53), which under actions/checkout means that repository's moving default branch. Those checkouts supply the code and schema the gates decide on: ico/verify-penalty-feed.sh:21 sets `fair="$here/../platform/fair/fair.py"`, hard-fails at :29 if it is missing, executes it at :55-56 and :71-72, and asserts on its numeric output at :64 and :78; feeds/verify-feeds.sh:23-34 resolves the ADR-0019 envelope schema `platform/feeds/schema.json` from `PLATFORM_DIR`, which feeds/release.yml:65-68 sets to that unpinned checkout; insurer/verify-insurer-party.sh:83-88 resolves `${PLATFORM_DIR:-../platform}/party/party_artefact.py`, which in release.yml's layout exists and runs, failing the release at :88 on a non-zero exit.

feeds is internally inconsistent: cut-release.yml:56-61 pins `ref: v2.0.1` for the same schema its release.yml takes from main. insurer additionally mis-documents itself — release.yml:9-16 claims the platform checkout is "Unused by the current gate", kept "ahead of ticket 36", which is false against verify-insurer-party.sh:83-88 today. nist's release.yml has no cross-repo checkout and is clean. An estate-wide sweep found only one other unpinned cross-repo checkout (tuppence/shift-left.yml:99-105), and it is an explicitly documented and separately compensated live bug, so these three are the only undocumented instances.

The exposure is latent rather than realised: platform main (46cd775) is two commits past v2.0.1 and neither touches fair/fair.py, feeds/schema.json or party/party_artefact.py, so the pinned and unpinned gates currently see identical files. But platform main demonstrably moves, and a change to any of those three files would change what a regulator's, an insurer's or a feed publisher's release gate accepts or rejects with no PR in the consuming repo and no version movement.

Ownership: none. Ticket 62's scope sentence (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/62-...md:9) and its parent finding M10 (REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:67) both cover adopters consuming feed parents, not publishers consuming platform; no verifier under verify/ checks cross-repo checkout pinning, and platform/verify-publisher-gate.sh despite its name covers gate.py bump computation only. Ticket 62's proposed compensating check ("a verifier that refuses branch refs in composing jobs") would not catch these three as scoped, since they carry no ref at all and a release gate is not a composing job.
EVIDENCE: ico/.github/workflows/release.yml:60-62, insurer/.github/workflows/release.yml:61-63 and feeds/.github/workflows/release.yml:50-53 each specify `repository: policy-as-versioned-platform/platform` with a `path:` and no `ref:`. The gates depend on that tree: ico/verify-penalty-feed.sh:21 `fair="$here/../platform/fair/fair.py"`; feeds/verify-feeds.sh:25-29 resolves `platform/feeds/schema.json` (the ADR-0019 envelope every feed is validated against); insurer/verify-insurer-party.sh:83 `checker="${PLATFORM_DIR:-../platform}/party/party_artefact.py"`. feeds is internally inconsistent: its cut-release.yml:59-61 does pin `ref: v2.0.1` while its release.yml does not. nist's release.yml has no cross-repo checkout at all and is therefore clean.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2 'consumed only through a pinned, signed dependency' and 'The only shared things are the artefact contracts and the £' — a contract consumed from a moving branch is not a pinned contract.
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (62, 18, 57, 21, 04), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (M10 + refuted-claims list), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, NORTH-STAR §2, map.md Out-of-scope. Ticket 62 only covers adopters (driftwood/ludlow/tuppence) checking out ico/feeds/insurer unpinned — it never names the publisher repos' own release.yml checking out platform. Ticket 18 covers the publisher release gate's bump/tier logic in depth but never inspects the platform-checkout ref in ico/insurer/feeds release.yml. Ticket 57's progress log (line 90-93) shows feeds' cut-release.yml was fixed to pin platform at ref v2.0.1, but release.yml was left alone — corroborating, not owning, the finding. No ticket, review finding, or regrill answer addresses release.yml's unpinned platform checkout.
FIT IMPACT: A change on platform main can silently change what a regulator's or the insurer's release gate accepts or rejects, with no PR in the consuming repo and no version movement. The publisher rows are supposed to be the least coupled participants in the model; today their release decision is the most coupled thing they do.
REMEDY: Give each publisher's release.yml the same `ref:` its own cut-release.yml or gitops platform pin already names (feeds already has v2.0.1 to copy), and extend ticket 62's branch-ref refusal check to publisher workflows, not only adopter ones.

## [major] P5 The only citable instrument grades each participant's unsigned moving tip, not the signed version its own Flux sources pin — so a green says the working branches work, not that the released eco-system does
(original title: The truth surface clones every participant's default branch, though the signed tags its own script waits for now exist)
CLAIM: The truth surface grades the estate from each unit's *default branch*, not from the signed tags the estate's own release discipline produces, and the decision to pin was deferred on a premise that has since expired.

`talk/verify-all.sh:32` unconditionally runs `clone-estate.sh`, whose line 57 clones each unit with no ref (`git clone --quiet "https://github.com/$org/$u" "$dir"`), and whose lines 37-39 still say "No signed tag exists yet (ticket 09/12: known, accepted partial state) ... Once a signed v1.0.0 lands, pin it here (--branch v1.0.0)". That premise is false: six units cut their first signed tag on 2026-08-21 — the same day the comment was authored (c9d0f20) — and feeds and insurer on 2026-09-01. All 24 tags across the eight units verify (gitsign, Good signature, Rekor entry validated). The file has been edited twice since without revisiting the pin, and no ticket in `.scratch/ecosystem/issues/` charts one.

Three corrections to how this should be stated. (1) The graded HEADs are *untagged*, not unsigned — all eight carry a signature (driftwood/tuppence/ludlow gitsign x509, feeds/insurer GPG by the owner, platform/nist/ico GitHub merge signatures). (2) It is five of eight, not all eight: ico, feeds and insurer currently sit exactly on a signed tag; platform is 2 commits past its latest, nist 6, ludlow 24, tuppence 29, driftwood 41. (3) The signed line is not ungraded — `verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:88-96` runs real `gitsign verify-tag` against Rekor on each unit's newest tag, and `release_integrity.py` reads released trees from their own tags, both relying on the full (non-shallow) clone. What floats unpinned is the *verify scripts and working-tree content* the gate reads, so the TRUTH number describes the estate's tip rather than its released line.

The consequence to record is narrower than "the surface cannot be cited": a green says the tip works, and says nothing about whether the specific tagged versions an adopter pins still compose. The remedy is also not the one the comment names — `--branch v1.0.0` cannot be applied uniformly (feeds has no `v1.0.0`, and six units are past it), and pinning would make the gate blind to in-flight repairs, a property ticket 55:19 relies on by name. This needs a charted decision on which ref the observer reads (and whether scripts and subjects should be pinned separately), plus deletion of the stale comment — not a one-line flag.
EVIDENCE: /Users/cns/httpdocs/controlplane/policy-as-versioned-flux/clone-estate.sh:37-39 reads '# No signed tag exists yet (ticket 09/12: known, accepted partial state) so # this clones the default branch. Once a signed v1.0.0 lands, pin it here # (--branch v1.0.0) so the offline harness matches what Flux actually runs.' and line 57 is `git clone --quiet "https://github.com/$org/$u" "$dir"` with no --branch. All 24 tags across the eight units verify (gitsign, Rekor). The TRUTH run=21 line's units=[...] are default-branch HEADs — driftwood=6cf0671 is a `drift sample: five facts on an ephemeral cluster [skip ci]` commit, not any tag. `grep -l clone-estate` across .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ hits tickets 10, 20, 40, 55, 57, 60; none charts pinning it.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5 ('One command ... is the only source any document may cite for "what works"'); §2 'consumed only through a pinned, signed dependency' applied to the observer itself.
OWNER: new none — No open ticket, no REVIEW-2026-08-31 finding/refutation, no REGRILL-ANSWERS entry, and no NORTH-STAR §6/map Out-of-scope line owns this. Checked tickets 10, 20, 40, 55, 57, 60 (the only ones grepping clone-estate): ticket 55 only notes clone-estate.sh clones default-branch so unmerged repairs stay invisible to the truth surface (a different claim about latency, not about pinning to signed tags); ticket 40's title (composed set from signed sources) doesn't mention clone-estate.sh in its body at all. REVIEW-2026-08-31's confirmed M1-M18 list and its Refuted-claims list contain no line about clone-estate.sh's own pin. Nothing in REGRILL-ANSWERS.md addresses it either. This is a genuinely new gap: the comment at clone-estate.sh:37-39 was written when no signed tag existed; the precondition it names ('once a signed v1.0.0 lands') is now true (all 24 tags across 8 units verify per the finding's evidence), but no ticket tracks applying the pin.
FIT IMPACT: The one instrument NORTH-STAR permits any document to cite grades content that nobody signed. A green on the truth surface today is evidence about working state, not about the signed line an adopter would actually consume — so the surface cannot, as written, be cited for whether the signed eco-system works.
REMEDY: Either pin clone-estate.sh to each unit's newest signed tag (the script's own stated plan) and record the tag in the TRUTH line beside the SHA, or state explicitly in NORTH-STAR §5 that the surface grades default-branch state and add a second, tag-pinned lane for the signed claim.

## [major] P6 The twin is unattestable: an unsigned, untagged artefact sets a £1.9m priced line and the cage tier in driftwood's composed evidence, so "signed by an agent identity" and "every actor is attestable" are not true of the one participant that produces forecasts
(original title: The twin is the only §2 row with no organisation, no tag and no signature, and is consumed by verbatim copy inside another participant's directory layout)
CLAIM: The twin is the only participant that exists and has no GitHub organisation, no release tag and no agent signature, and it is consumed by verbatim copy from inside another participant's checkout. Specifics, all re-verified: `gh api orgs/policy-as-versioned-twin` returns 404 while all eight unit orgs resolve with repos=1; the hub carries zero tags on three independent reads (`git tag -l`, `git ls-remote --tags origin`, `gh api .../tags` -> 0) while every unit repo carries 1 to 11; driftwood/twin/world holds exactly the 30 files of the hub's `twin.fixtures.LIBRARY_WORLD_FILES` with 0 byte differences, under PIN.yaml `tag_cut: false`; and no signed tag anywhere covers a twin artefact — driftwood's own v1.1.0 tree contains no `twin/` directory at all, and the hub has only truth.yml and twin.yml, no release workflow. Two corrections to the mechanism. First, the twin bytes are not unsigned: the commits carrying them are GPG-signed by the owner (`%G?` = G on 6a2df16, f75509a, 055e340); what is missing is a signed release tag and the agent identity NORTH-STAR:19 asks for. Second, the tenancy is not path-exact: emit-forward-intel.py:86-88 finds the hub by walking upwards for any ancestor containing `twin/repo.py` and `clone-estate.sh`, so driftwood's twin-sweep requires only that it run nested inside some hub checkout — `hub/.estate-clone/driftwood` mirrors clone-estate.sh by convention. It remains the only unit workflow in all eight repos that checks out another participant's repository to run its own code, so the nesting breach is unique even though unpinned cross-org consumption is not: driftwood, tuppence and ludlow together carry 29 checkouts of ico, feeds and insurer at `main` or at the branch `ecosystem/thin-slice`, owned by tickets 62 and 69. Mitigation stands as written — the vendoring is documented in VENDORED.md, justified by the loader's same-ModelRepo constraint, and double-pinned (twin_version against the hub's twin/VERSION, world_ref c2d0733… against the staged bytes) — and ticket 64 (open, HITL) owns cutting the tag and widening to tuppence and ludlow.
EVIDENCE: `gh api orgs/policy-as-versioned-twin` -> 404 Not Found (every other participant org resolves with repos=1). `git tag -l` in the hub returns nothing. driftwood/twin/PIN.yaml: `twin_version: 0.1.0`, `twin_tag: twin/v0.1.0`, `tag_cut: false`. driftwood/twin/VENDORED.md: 'Files | 30', 'Copied | byte-for-byte, verbatim, no edits', staged to `world_ref: c2d07330a778ed547b60cfbb87217bcf9813181f`. driftwood/.github/workflows/twin-sweep.yml:64-71: `working-directory: hub/.estate-clone/driftwood`, first checkout `repository: policy-as-versioned-flux/policy-as-versioned-flux, path: hub, ref: main`, second checkout of driftwood itself into `hub/.estate-clone/driftwood`, with the comment 'the package does not self-version yet, so there is no tag to pin'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2 twin row ('Priced forecasts and forward intelligence ... signed by an agent identity'), §2 'each participant lives in its own GitHub organisation ... consumed only through a pinned, signed dependency. No participant reaches into another.'
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 64 — Ticket 64 (open, HITL) explicitly owns the untagged/unsigned twin (M16: "hub has zero tags and no release workflow; PIN.yaml says tag_cut: false") and the driftwood-only-of-three-adopters gap (M15). Its Done criteria match P6's core claim: "twin/v0.1.0 and the forward-intel tag exist signed, and PIN.yaml's tag_cut flips true." However, ticket 64's text does not mention the cross-repo directory-layout tenancy issue (twin-sweep.yml running from hub/.estate-clone/driftwood, i.e. driftwood reaching into the hub's assembled clone path) -- that specific sub-claim of P6 is not owned by any ticket text found (REVIEW-2026-08-31.md and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md were also checked; no mention of the working-directory/tenancy detail). The tagging/org/signature core of P6 is fully owned by ticket 64; the tenancy/directory-layout detail is a new, unowned angle.
FIT IMPACT: This is the one place where a participant is structurally a tenant of another: driftwood's job only works because it sits at the exact path the hub's clone-estate.sh assembles. Until twin/v0.1.0 is cut there is no signed twin artefact of any kind, so the twin row's 'signed by an agent identity' is unmet. Mitigating: the vendoring is documented, justified by a real loader constraint, and content-pinned two ways (twin_version against the hub's twin/VERSION, world_ref against the staged bytes) — a good substitute for a tag, but not a tag.
REMEDY: Cut twin/v0.1.0 with a hub release workflow so driftwood can pin it the way it pins platform, then either give the twin its own org/repo or record in NORTH-STAR §2 that the twin ships from the hub by design. Re-point twin-sweep so it does not require the hub's clone layout.

## [major] P7 The twin's clock can only ever say "nothing moved": §4 step 5's forward-intelligence proposal lane has never run and cannot, and ticket 72 can close green without it ever running
(original title: driftwood's twin sweep can never propose: the 'moved' branch is unreachable dead code under GitHub's default `bash -e` shell)
CLAIM: driftwood's twin sweep can never propose: the `moved=true` branch is unreachable dead code under GitHub's default `bash -e` shell — a real, structural estate defect that the owner already found, diagnosed and ticketed a day before this review.
