twin-sweep.yml:88 opens the sweep step with `set -uo pipefail`, which does not clear `-e`; GitHub runs the step as `shell: /usr/bin/bash -e {0}` (observed in both run logs). `twin/emit-forward-intel.py --check` returns 1 as its *moved* signal (emit-forward-intel.py:316-318), so any genuine forecast movement aborts the step at line 90, before `rc=$?` at line 91 — making the `moved=true` arm (line 95) and the propose-PR step gated on it (line 103) structurally unreachable. Proof is the step's own conclusion, not the absent `::error::` line (the `elif` arm prints nothing either): the per-step API for run 33627910027 shows sweep `failure`, propose `skipped`, observe `skipped`, where `-e` being off would have yielded sweep `success` with moved=true. Reproduced locally: the exact shape under `bash -e` exits 1 without reaching `rc=$?`; with `set +e` prepended it reaches it. Both scheduled runs ever (33627910027, 33508119299) failed identically. NORTH-STAR §4 step 5 is therefore unreachable on a clock as written, not merely red today.

Two corrections to the finding as filed:

1. Ownership: ticket 72 (open) already names this precisely — issues/72-*.md:34-37 states the `bash -e` diagnosis, the unreachable `rc=$?` branch, and two fixes (`set +e` or `|| rc=$?`), committed 82754dd on 2026-09-01 13:38, with ticket 60:94-95 and map.md:52 cross-referencing. The finding's "no ticket names the unreachable branch" is false. This is a known, owned, correctly diagnosed defect, which lowers it from a discovery to a confirmation — and argues against major severity on novelty grounds, though the structural impact stands.

2. The fix ticket 72 prescribes is necessary but not sufficient, and this part is genuinely unrecorded: the `else`/`::error::` arm (lines 96-99) is unreachable independently of `-e`, because every failure path in emit-forward-intel.py exits 1 (`sys.exit("REFUSED: ...")` at lines 89/113/130/149/162/168/197/214/297, and uncaught exceptions). Once `set +e` lands, a render REFUSAL becomes indistinguishable from a move and will open a gitsign-signed PR carrying whatever the unchecked re-render (line 113) writes. The script needs a distinct exit code for "could not render" before `set +e` is safe.
EVIDENCE: driftwood/.github/workflows/twin-sweep.yml sweep step body: `set -uo pipefail` then `python3 twin/emit-forward-intel.py --check` / `rc=$?` / `if [ "${rc}" -eq 0 ] ... elif [ "${rc}" -eq 1 ]; then echo "moved=true" ... else echo "::error::the sweep could not render the overlay at all (exit ${rc})"`. The live log of run 33627910027 (2026-09-02T12:04:23Z) records `shell: /usr/bin/bash -e {0}`, and its last two lines are `FAIL: twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json is not what the overlay renders` then `##[error]Process completed with exit code 1.` — the workflow's own `::error::the sweep could not render the overlay at all` line is absent, proving the shell died at the python line rather than reaching the elif or the else. Both scheduled runs ever (33627910027, 33508119299) failed identically.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §4 step 5 ('The twin, on its schedule, plays a dated external signal forward ... emits a scored forecast, and publishes forward intelligence the platform consumes'); §3 principle 5 ('Intelligence re-prices on a clock; enactment happens only by reviewed PR').
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 72 — Ticket 72 (open) at .scratch/ecosystem/issues/72-a-feed-bump-re-renders-the-twin-s-derived-artefacts.md already names this exact defect in its 2026-09-01 12:31Z note: "the sweep exists precisely to re-render a moved feed, but it cannot: the job step executes under GitHub's default `bash -e`, so the `rc=$?` branch that maps exit 1 to `moved=true` is unreachable — any real move aborts the step before the branch runs. The step needs `set +e` around the check (the same pattern verify-reconcile.sh uses), or `|| rc=$?`." This is the identical mechanism and identical fix as P7. The finding's "Claimed ownership" line ("no ticket names the unreachable branch") is incorrect — ticket 72 does name it, alongside the render-mismatch and the signal-lookup gap, as one of three things the fix must cover. P7 is not new; it should be folded into ticket 72 rather than filed separately. Checked: no other ticket, REVIEW-2026-08-31.md, or REGRILL-ANSWERS.md mentions `bash -e`/unreachable-branch/pipefail for twin-sweep.
FIT IMPACT: Even after ticket 72 re-derives the twin's artefacts, the next genuine forecast movement will also exit 1 and also abort the job, so the only code path that opens the forward-intel PR can never run. §4 step 5 is unreachable on a clock as written, not merely red today.
REMEDY: Add `set +e` (or `|| rc=$?` / `shell: bash` without -e) around the emit-forward-intel.py call so rc is actually captured, and add a check that the moved path has fired at least once — a branch that has never executed on any run is not proven.

## [major] P9 Principle 5's clock is registered on nine rows and productive on none of the three that matter most — the intelligence publisher has never once refreshed a feed, and the regulator's green clock writes a dated falsehood about its own signed catalogue
(original title: Two participants' clocks have failed every run they have ever had, and the regulator clock that succeeds observes nothing)
CLAIM: Three of the nine registered clocks produce nothing usable, and the citable run cannot see any of it.

(a) feeds' clock has failed both scheduled runs it has ever had (33488014777 2026-09-01, 33605912295 2026-09-02). Eleven of its twelve matrix legs failed; one leg, `fetch (market-moves)` on 2026-09-01, succeeded and wrote the only observation feeds owns (commit 63c7904, payload_sha256 71d4c8ff…, published_version 1.0.0). Five of the six feeds declared in party.yaml have never recorded an observation. The cause is a branch-switch bug in the clock's own cage, not the lane rules: `.github/workflows/fetch.yml:120` cleans without `-x`, then line 123 checks out the orphan `observations` branch, which carries no `.gitignore`, so `fetch/lib.py`'s `__pycache__/*.pyc` become untracked-and-unignored and line 179's cage refuses the run. No ticket mentions this; ticket 70 covers detective lane enforcement, not this.

(b) insurer's clock is half dead, not dead. Its `fetch` job succeeded on both scheduled runs and recorded real, hash-carrying observations for all three quotes on both days (quote-driftwood/ludlow/tuppence, payload_sha256 present, published_version 1.0.0). What fails, on 100% of runs, is the three `requote` legs: `REFUSED: missing instrument: .adopters/<unit>/composed/HEADER.yaml carries no 'exposure' section`. That is the P1 cause, and it kills the re-price half of principle 5 while leaving the observe half working.

(c) Two publishers' clocks succeed daily and record no version and no hash. nist writes `payload_sha256: null, published_version: null` on both days, and platform writes the same on BOTH its entries (identity-substrate, policy). The cause is a shape assumption, not a regulator problem: `newest()` globs `<path>/v<N>/feed.json`, which only `kind: feed` publishers ship. All three non-feed entries in the estate (nist `controls`, platform `implementations` x2) record null; all four feed-kind entries that ran (ico penalty-schema 3.0.0, insurer quote-x3) record real hashes. So the other regulator, ico, is fine — this is not "the regulator clock". nist's catalogue does carry a real version and hash (catalog/CATALOG_VERSION.json: publishedVersion 1.1.0, sha256 d820835a…), just not in the shape the reader looks for, and nist's line does carry a real `declared_bump: "none"` read from catalog/bump.yaml, so "observes nothing" is too strong: it observes no version and no hash. This falsifies ADR-0024:131-135's recorded scope ("their clock records what they have published and the sha256 of its payload each day… identical hashes are evidence nothing moved") for the two non-feed publishers. Unticketed.

(d) None of this is "green on the surface". On run 21 every liveness check SKIPped — talk/captures/verify_schedules_verify-schedules.out records `GitHub unreachable … cannot look at whether this clock ran inside its period` for all nine clocks — so the truth surface shows could-not-look, honestly counted in skip=18. verify/schedules/schedules.py:650-655 would red a dead clock if the gate had a credential; that blindness is ticket 56 (open). The unowned residue is therefore narrower than the finding stated but still real: no check anywhere grades observation CONTENT, so a clock that succeeds while writing null in every value field is indistinguishable from one that works, even once ticket 56 restores liveness sight (schedules.py:646-650 names this as a ponytail).
EVIDENCE: `gh run list --repo policy-as-versioned-feeds/feeds`: fetch/schedule failure 2026-09-01T08:37Z (33488014777) and 2026-09-02T07:54Z (33605912295) — the only two. feeds' observations branch (`git ls-tree -r origin/observations`) carries only observations/README.md and observations/market-moves.jsonl, so five of six feeds have never recorded an observation. `gh run view 33615860064 --repo policy-as-versioned-insurer/insurer --json jobs`: fetch success, requote(driftwood|tuppence|ludlow) all failure; same on 33496526156. nist: `git -C nist show origin/observations:observations/sp-800-53.jsonl` is two lines, both `"payload_sha256": null, "published_version": null, "note": "no catalog/v<N>/feed.json in this repository yet"` (2026-09-01, 2026-09-02) — the catalogue lives at catalog/, not catalog/v<N>/feed.json. Hub truth.yml and twin.yml both failed their latest scheduled runs (2026-09-02T09:54Z, 09:36Z).
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2 'ships on its own cadence'; §3 principle 5 ('Feeds refresh, the twin re-forecasts, cages re-price, and proposals open, all on a schedule'); §5 ('A green that could not look is a red').
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 57 (feeds/insurer clock) + new for nist null-observation leg — Split finding. (1) feeds' and insurer's fetch-clock failures: squarely owned by open ticket 57 ("feeds and insurer become runnable... workflows registered, first signed tags cut", Status: claimed, not resolved) — its own 2026-09-01 progress note explicitly leaves open "the first scheduled fetch run on each repo (2026-09-02, 03:17 and 05:31 UTC)" as the remaining unresolved item before the ticket can close, which is exactly the 09-01/09-02 fetch failures the finding cites. REVIEW-2026-08-31 M1 (feeds/insurer mechanically unrunnable) is the same root finding, already routed to ticket 57. (2) nist's clock succeeding but recording payload_sha256:null/published_version:null because its reader checks catalog/v<N>/feed.json instead of nist's actual catalog/ path: searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md (incl. 04-the-feed-contract.md, 21-build-the-feed-contract.md, 60-..., 70-...), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md, and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md — no ticket, refuted-claim entry, or owner decision addresses this specific nist path mismatch. Ticket 21 records that "nist carries catalog/{rule,bump}.yaml beside its published path" (i.e. nist's layout diverges from the v<N>/feed.json convention) but never connects this to a broken observation reader. This half of the finding appears new and unowned; no ticket currently covers it. NORTH-STAR §5 ("a green that could not look is a red") is directly implicated since nist's clock is green while observing nothing, but no ticket cites this specific mechanism.
FIT IMPACT: Principle 5's 'intelligence re-prices on a clock' is nominally satisfied — nine registered clocks fire daily — but substantively three participant rows produce nothing from theirs. The regulator row in particular is green on the surface and blind in substance: two days of observations with null in every value field.
REMEDY: Point nist's fetch reader at catalog/ + CATALOG_VERSION.json so it observes the artefact it actually publishes (or record explicitly that a controls catalogue has no observable feed version). Fix the __pycache__ leak into the observation lane once, shared across hub/feeds/insurer. The insurer clock unblocks with P1.

MINORS:
- P10 tuppence and ludlow ship a Flux-reconciled ConfigMap naming a deleted platform file as 'the single source of truth': Both repos commit gitops/apps/risk-appetite-configmap.yaml declaring platform/risk/appetite.json authoritative for their appetite, but that file was retired by ADR-0021 and no longer exists in the platform repo.
- P11 The platform still ships its own copies of the intelligence publisher's feeds, and one live consumer reads the copy: platform/feeds/ still carries threat-register, cve and eol at v1 and v2 alongside the feeds org repo's own copies, and platform/honesty/verify-honesty.sh reads the platform's copy rather than the publisher's feed.
- P12 driftwood's published forward-intel derives from a feed version driftwood no longer pins: driftwood/twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json records derivation from feeds/threat-register version '1' while driftwood/party.yaml pins v2.
- P13 Each adopter's two Flux objects for itself disagree on which of its own tags is current, and driftwood's Kustomization path is unreconcilable against its own remote: In all three adopters gotk-sync.yaml self-pins v1.0.0 while composed-set.yaml pins that same repo at v1.1.0; and driftwood's Kustomization still uses `path: ./apps`, the exact value tuppence and ludlow document as never becoming Ready against the real GitHub remote.
- P14 NORTH-STAR §2's own table understates what was built: two rows still read 'Does not exist yet': The ratified §2 table records the intelligence-publisher and insurer rows as not existing, but both are now their own org with gitsign-verified tags.
- P15 Organisational separation is real for artefacts and nominal for authority: All nine repos have zero rulesets and no branch protection on main, and every merged non-Renovate PR I sampled was authored and merged by the same identity, so no participant can technically refuse another's change.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- Is the insurer's row meant to prove that a quote can be priced from a *signed, tagged* exposure, or only that the layer arithmetic and the refusal discipline are correct? Concretely: must an adopter cut a tag whose tree carries the exposure section before the row counts as built? | WHY: Today no adopter tag contains an `exposure` section, so the quotes were priced from unsigned working trees and the insurer's own clock has REFUSED on all three legs on both of its runs. If the ambition is only the arithmetic, this is done and P1 is a labelling fix; if the ambition is the signed chain, this row is not built and the estate's largest £ line (driftwood's £113,403.30 premium) has no signed provenance. | CHANGES: Either P1 becomes critical work (cut an adopter tag carrying exposure, re-quote, and add a gate check resolving every priced_against pin against the tag it names) plus a dated correction on ticket 36, or NORTH-STAR §2's insurer row and ticket 36's Answer get a recorded scope narrowing and P1 drops to a documentation defect.
- Should the truth surface grade the estate at each unit's signed tag, or at its default branch? clone-estate.sh's own comment promises the former 'once a signed v1.0.0 lands', and all eight have landed. | WHY: NORTH-STAR §5 makes the truth surface the only citable source for 'what works'. Grading unsigned default-branch HEADs means a green there is evidence about working state, not about the signed line an adopter would actually consume — which is a different claim from the one the deck and the tickets make when they cite a TRUTH line. | CHANGES: If tags: P5 becomes a build ticket, the TRUTH line gains a tag field beside each SHA, and several currently-green checks may move because they would grade different bytes. If default branch: §5 needs a sentence saying so, and every document citing a TRUTH line needs to stop implying the signed artefacts were the thing graded.
- Does the twin have to become its own organisation with its own signed tag, or is shipping from the hub and being vendored into adopters the intended shape? | WHY: The twin is the only §2 row with no org, no tag and no signature, and driftwood's twin-sweep only works because driftwood is checked out inside `hub/.estate-clone/driftwood` — the one structural violation of 'No participant reaches into another'. The vendoring itself is honestly documented and content-pinned, so the question is whether the copy is a stopgap or the design. | CHANGES: If its own org: ticket 64 grows a repo-split and driftwood's twin/ becomes a pinned dependency like platform's. If hub-shipped by design: §2's twin row and the 'own GitHub organisation' sentence need an explicit carve-out, twin/v0.1.0 still needs cutting so the vendoring has a signed source, and twin-sweep needs re-pointing so it does not depend on the hub's clone layout.
- For the intelligence-publisher row, is 'consumes public sources on a clock' a requirement, or is a committed-fixture clock with real cited grounding the accepted permanent shape? | WHY: No feed has ever ingested an external datum — every fetch reads fetch/source/<feed>.json — and the one feed whose rule is about external verifiability (news) has three of four admitted events citing GitHub Release pages that 404. The build discloses the fixture reads everywhere, so this is a scope question, not a honesty one; but the answer decides whether P8 is a defect or a recorded boundary. | CHANGES: If real ingestion is required: at least one feed needs a live urllib fetch with a could-not-look outcome, and news' resolvability check needs implementing to match its own prose. If fixtures are the accepted shape: §2's intelligence-publisher row should say so, and news/rule.yaml's 'resolvable' should be softened to 'well-formed' so the artefact stops claiming more than the code checks.
- Is nine separate organisations meant to demonstrate separation of *artefacts* (own signing identity, own clock, own tags) or separation of *authority* (a participant can refuse another's change)? | WHY: All nine repos have zero rulesets and no branch protection, and every merged non-Renovate PR sampled was authored and merged by the same identity. The artefact separation is genuinely real and verifiable; the authority separation exists only as convention plus enact_guard.py. Insurer's own rulesets README already records that its ruleset is inert because the repo is public. | CHANGES: If artefact-level is the ambition: §2 gains a sentence saying so and P15 closes as recorded scope. If authority-level: this becomes a build question about private repos or org-level required workflows, and the 'No participant reaches into another' claim gains a named check instead of resting on discipline.
- When a participant's pin becomes unusable — a deleted branch, a tag whose tree lacks the section, a moving `main` — should the consuming clock go red, or should it degrade to a priced hole and keep running? | WHY: Three separate rows currently express three different answers: tuppence/ludlow's propose-tier dies hard at checkout, the insurer's requote refuses with a named missing instrument, and driftwood's composition silently proceeds off `ref: main`. Ticket 69 proposes the priced-hole answer for one instance. There is no single rule. | CHANGES: A single answer would let one check cover all of P2, P3, P4 and P1 (refuse a branch ref in any composing job; resolve every declared pin against the tag it names; price what cannot be resolved). Without it, ticket 62, ticket 69 and the insurer fix will each invent their own convention and the next stale pin will fail in a fourth way.

# principles

VERDICT: Against §3 as written the estate is fit in the two principles that have engines (3, One £; 4, versioned dependency) and in the honesty machinery of 6; partly fit in 5 and 7; not yet fit in principle 1's conditional arm or in principle 2 beyond version-claiming pods. The pattern is consistent and is the finding: wherever the estate built an ENGINE it built it to an unusually high standard and instrumented its own drift (computed-semver cut a real gate-determined tag with a signed evidence file that names its own coverage gaps; pound_seam refuses a sum that crosses a perspective and cross-checks two selection engines at every band boundary; the five-fact sampler pre-registered its falsifiers before sample one and refuses hand-taken samples by run id and signature; twenty scheduled jobs are structurally proven unable to stage a declaration). Wherever the estate needed a SUBJECT for that engine, the subject is missing and the number still goes green because the check honestly SKIPs: one declared policy version so coexistence, retirement and shift-left have nothing to compare; no workload reconciled by Flux anywhere, so "the workload keeps running, caged tighter" has never been observed; no conditional-policy instance alive; no proposal PR ever opened; humans, devices and the twin not on the ladder at all. To become fit, in order: give principle 2 a Flux-reconciled workload and settle the two live Deny policies against ADR-0022's "one refusal"; cut the second policy line so principle 4's coexistence and patchability arms have a subject and principle 1's conditional branch comes back; wire gitsign into the proposer before step 3 fires so principle 5's enactment leaves an attestable trace; and close the two "green that could not look" holes in the five-fact falsifier and the step-6 tag glob so principle 6's own rule holds in its own instruments.

STRENGTHS:
- policy/v4.0.0 is a real, gate-determined release: platform/computed-semver/evidence/4.0.0.json records bump {declared: major, computed: major}, outcome passed, counts {old 153, new 152, union 164}, corpus_checksum sha256:ae12308f64efd, wall_clock 88.78s, 28 named movement entries and an explicit limits[] with three open, named limitations; run 21's verify-first-gate-determined-release capture confirms declared == evidence == computed and that the tag resolves one commit ahead of the array. I verified the tag against Rekor myself: `git -c gpg.format=x509 -c gpg.x509.program=gitsign tag -v policy/v4.0.0` -> tlog index 2664927495, Validated Rekor entry: true.
- The signature spine is real across four separate orgs and I re-derived it independently: platform policy/v4.0.0 (tlog 2664927495), driftwood v1.1.0 (tlog 2585920387), ico v3.0.0 (tlog 2664928228), feeds threat-register/v2.0.0 (tlog 2673492733) — every one 'Good signature from .../<org>/<repo>/.github/workflows/cut-release.yml@refs/heads/main' with 'Validated Rekor entry: true'.
- The cage is tighten-only in fact, not only in prose, in the served copy every adopter composes from (platform/distribution/policies/v4.0.0/cage-tier.yaml): readOnlyRootFilesystem and runAsNonRoot are ORed with what the container declared, cpu/memory ceilings are a MIN via quantity().isLessThan(), privileged/allowPrivilegeEscalation are written false, and hostNetwork/hostPID/hostIPC are clobbered false at every rung with the reason recorded — 'hostNetwork: true walked straight out of the isolated rung ... the bottom rung reached the API server and the internet (observed live, 2026-08-28)'.
- The bottom rung is a running cage, proven by a real TCP connection rather than a YAML read: platform/graded/verify-graded.sh:480-505 execs `nc -w 4 -z <apiserver ClusterIP> 443` and `nc ... 1.1.1.1 80` from the isolated pod and fails if either succeeds after 60s, asserts the baseline pod still reaches, and asserts `kubectl label` on a running isolated pod is not refused ('an UPDATE to a running isolated pod was REFUSED — the cage denied a workload').
- One £ is enforced and green on the citable run: verify_pound-seam PASS — 'every price names its perspective, currency, source and per-customer share; no sum crosses either'; verify_proportionality PASS — 'same control, same £ (risk_bought £21107) — Audit in driftwood, Deny in ludlow'. platform/risk/appetite.json is genuinely retired (enforce.py's selfcheck asserts the file does not exist) and pound_seam check 6 asserts no code still reads it.
- Two independent tier-selection implementations are cross-checked to the band boundary and pass: pound_seam check 9 runs driftwood's own published selection-policy package and platform/graded/cage.py over the same residuals at each boundary and with every rung as a floor and refuses any disagreement; check 8 asserts the estate's recorded curve_hash equals the one the adopter's own package computes over its own published curve (run 21: 'one curve, two implementations, no drift').
- The clock cage is structural and PASSes for all twenty scheduled jobs: run 21's verify_schedules capture prints, per job, 'caged — no shell step in this job stages a declaration or mints a signed artefact'; OBSERVATION_LANE is declared once as data in each workflow (truth.yml:38; driftwood drift-sample.yml declares only drift/samples.jsonl) and parsed by the hub's verify-schedules.sh, and propose-tier.yml's cage step does `git reset -q` first, a fix for a real 2026-08-28 defect where a staged declaration was committed while the step printed 'this clock declared nothing'.
- The Flux falsification test as re-scoped by REGRILL 1 exists, is pre-registered before sample one (driftwood/drift/window.yaml five_fact_sample, declared_on 2026-08-28, three named falsifiers, coverage floor 0.9), refuses rehearsals ('a sample taken by hand ... is a REHEARSAL and is never cited'), and produced its first real cross-org green: on run 21 both nist and platform are five-of-five true on an ephemeral cluster reconciling the real remotes, including fact 5, 'all 16 rendered objects appear in a Flux inventory' — the fact that separates 'Flux put it there' from 'somebody ran kubectl apply'.
- A forecast has been scored against reality and the red survived: tests/test_royal_mail_beat.py docstring records Brier 0.9025, 'worse than a coin flip', and test_the_score_lands_poor_and_says_so asserts worst brier > 0.25 AND adjusted_brier > 0.25 ('the discount rescued a forecast it should not'). Blind emission is structural, not promised: twin/capabilities/forecast-book.yaml criterion 2 records that forecast_book.py exposes exactly emit, score_resolution, is_blind, asserted as an allow-list by a harness guard.
- No exemption mechanism exists anywhere: grep -rni 'PolicyException|exemption' across all eight unit clones returns 19 non-markdown hits, every one a negation or a reference to the deleted render-exemption.py. platform/computed-semver/coverage-exclusions.yaml is empty (declared_holes: []) and its header states only four keys are ever read and that no key in the file can promote a declared hole to a proved exclusion.
- Honesty of reporting is graded separately from outcome and works: run 21's verify-e2e-step7-honesty is PASS with '(verdicts: PASS PASS PASS FAIL PASS PASS PASS)' — a red step 4 and a green step 7 in the same tally is correct, not a contradiction. The truth clock itself reddened honestly on run 21: gh run view 33616685427 shows step 9 'the observation cage' success and step 10 'fail if the gate failed' failure.

## [major] P6-1 The falsification test is not honest about itself: the adopter's cross-org signature falsifier can never run for platform or nist, and the sampler records that inability as "did not fire", so the next green sample will claim a falsifier passed that nobody ran
(original title: A falsifier that could not look is recorded as 'did not fire' and rides inside a PASS)
CLAIM: In driftwood's five-fact sampler, falsifier 2 (`cluster_side_verification_passes_a_tag_that_identity_pinned_ci_rejects`) records `fired: false` — "did not fire" — for every source whose CI-side check was never run: `gitsign_verifies()` is called only when `src["party"] == "driftwood"`, the else branch returns `ok = None`, and `(ok is False)` collapses that to `False` (drift/five-facts.py:524-528). The grader's only could-not-look branch tests `state.get("fired") is None` (:683), so those records are silently skipped — contradicting the comment three lines above (:674-676, "a falsifier nobody ran is exactly the state that must never ride along inside a PASS"), the tri-state contract in the function's own docstring (:498-499), and drift/window.yaml:253-256, which declares this falsifier "reads could-not-look -- which is not a pass, and is recorded as such in every sample". The pattern is present in all three real sampler runs (33556795181, 33558850420, 33624104359): platform and nist each carry `fired: false, ci_accepts: null` with the record's own `why` reading "<party>'s own release.yml is not in this checkout, so the identity it pins cannot be read here"; the code is byte-identical at the truth-line commit 6cf0671. The defect is latent rather than realised: the 2026-09-02 sample grades FAIL because driftwood's own fact 2 is false (ticket 73, "certificate is not yet valid"), so no PASS has yet carried an unrun falsifier. But it is reachable and load-bearing — flipping only driftwood's fact 2 green in a local counterfactual yields "PASS: all five facts observed true for every source; no falsifier fired" with no FALSIFIER NOT LOOKED AT line for platform or nist, and verify-reconcile.sh:37-39 converts a five-fact PASS into a whole-script PASS with no cluster at all, in a script the gate counts (talk/verify-all.sh:45, :73). Once ticket 73 lands, every PASS on this sample will be a PASS with exactly two unrun falsifiers inside it — on the two sources whose cross-org proof is the point of the test. No ticket or ADR owns this.
EVIDENCE: driftwood/drift/five-facts.py:522-528: `ok, why = gitsign_verifies(...) if src["party"] == "driftwood" else (None, f"{src['party']}'s own release.yml is not in this checkout...")` then `f2 = {..., "fired": (ok is False), "ci_accepts": ok, ...}` — `ok is False` is False when ok is None. five-facts.py:683 grades could-not-look only on `state.get("fired") is None`. The comment at :674-677 reads 'a falsifier nobody ran is exactly the state that must never ride along inside a PASS'. Confirmed live in drift/samples.jsonl run 33624104359: platform -> fired false, ci_accepts null; nist -> fired false, ci_accepts null; driftwood (the only party the check can run for) -> fired null.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 6 ('A green that could not look is a red'), §5 three-outcomes rule
OWNER: new none — Verified the code exactly matches the finding's evidence: driftwood/drift/five-facts.py:527 sets f2["fired"] = (ok is False), which is False (not None) when ok is None (the CI-side check could not run, e.g. for platform/nist whose release.yml isn't in this checkout, or for driftwood itself when cluster_verified is false at line 519). The grader at line 683 only treats fired is None as could-not-look, so this false-for-could-not-look case is invisible to the could-not-look branch and can ride inside a PASS at line 690, exactly as claimed. Searched for ownership: (1) REVIEW-2026-08-31.md line 87 lists a minor 'five-facts falsifier 1 can silently not fire' folded into tickets 54/55/59/64/66/67 — but that is falsifier 1 (unhealed-across-N-samples, line 670), a different mechanism from falsifier 2 (identity/gitsign, line 674-687) that this finding targets. (2) Ticket 55's Answer fixes verify_gitsign.py (the standalone verify-source-verification script) to emit rc=3 could-not-look instead of REJECTED on trust-material absence — that is a different script/check entirely from the five-fact sampler's own internal grading logic in five-facts.py; ticket 55 does not touch _falsifier_state or the grade() function. (3) No ticket, REVIEW-2026-08-31.md entry, GAPS.md entry, or REGRILL-ANSWERS.md entry mentions falsifier 2's fired=False-for-None-ok defect specifically. (4) NORTH-STAR §3 principle 6 and §5 three-outcomes rule are the ambition this violates, not an owner decision that moots it. No existing ticket or prior review finding owns or refutes this specific defect — it is new.
FIT IMPACT: The estate's flagship falsifiability instrument violates the estate's own headline honesty rule, and it does so on the two sources whose cross-org proof is the whole point of the test. Any future PASS on the five-fact sample is a PASS with two unrun falsifiers inside it.
REMEDY: One line: `"fired": (False if ok is False else (True if ok is True else None))` — i.e. carry None through when ok is None. Then grade()'s existing could-not-look branch converts the sample to SKIP with a reason, as intended.

## [major] P6-2 The truth surface's provenance line does not measure the artefacts the eco-system actually ships: it declares a signed publisher unsigned and verifies the platform's abandoned tag line instead of the policy line the demonstration runs on
(original title: The provenance check reports a real, Rekor-verified signature as absent, and still passes)
CLAIM: verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:88 selects each unit's tag with `git tag -l 'v*.*.*'`, a pattern anchored on a leading `v` that cannot match a namespaced tag. feeds' only tags are threat-register/v1.0.0 and threat-register/v2.0.0, so the selector finds nothing, :89 files feeds under `queued`, and TRUTH run 21's citable capture states "ok no signed tag yet, honestly queued for cut-release.yml: feeds" and passes with "7 of 8". That statement is false: threat-register/v2.0.0 is gitsign-signed at tlog index 2673492733 with "Good signature from [.../policy-as-versioned-feeds/feeds/.github/workflows/cut-release.yml@refs/heads/main]" and "Validated Rekor entry: true", on commit 69c89b0 — the exact feeds SHA the run-21 TRUTH line records. Part 1 of the same script, in the same capture, reports 0 queued and resolves feeds' threat-register tags on the real remote, so the capture contradicts itself. The consequence is not only a false line: feeds is the one unit of eight whose anchored identity regexp (release.yml:38) is never run against a real Fulcio cert by the gate, because feeds ships no verify-identity-regexp script of its own, and the deck (talk/deck.md:179) narrates the false claim as "the units with no tag yet are named". Separately, and more weakly, the selector checks only one tag per unit and so covers only one of platform's two release lines — but that is a coverage limit, not a misreport: the tag it picks (v2.0.1) is platform's genuinely newest tag and verified live, and policy/v4.0.0 is itself signed and Rekor-validated (tlog 2664927495), so no unsigned release is hidden. Widening the glob alone would not change platform's selection, since sort -V still ranks v2.0.1 last. No ticket owns either half.
EVIDENCE: verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:87: `tag="$(git -C "$ESTATE/$u" tag -l 'v*.*.*' 2>/dev/null | sort -V | tail -1)"`; :89 `[ -n "$tag" ] || { queued+=("$u"); continue; }`. Run 21 capture: `ok no signed tag yet, honestly queued for cut-release.yml: feeds` and `PASS: ... 7 of 8 anchored identity regexps matched`. But in the feeds clone `git tag -l` returns threat-register/v1.0.0 and threat-register/v2.0.0, and I verified the latter: `git -c gpg.format=x509 -c gpg.x509.program=gitsign tag -v threat-register/v2.0.0` -> tlog index 2673492733, 'Good signature from .../policy-as-versioned-feeds/feeds/.github/workflows/cut-release.yml@refs/heads/main', 'Validated Rekor entry: true'. In the platform clone `git tag -l` returns policy/v2.0.0 policy/v2.0.1 policy/v3.0.0 policy/v4.0.0 v0.1.0 v0.1.1 v1.0.0 v1.1.0 v1.1.1 v2.0.0 v2.0.1 — the glob picks v2.0.1.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 6 ('Every artefact carries a signature'), §4 step 6
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (grep for step6/glob/policy-v4/tag), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (M1-M18 and Refuted claims list), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, NORTH-STAR/map out-of-scope. M1 (REVIEW-2026-08-31) said feeds had zero tags — true then, false now (feeds has two gitsign-signed namespaced tags), so M1 does not own this. M5 and its owning ticket 54 addressed a different mechanism entirely: step6 hard-SKIPping because jsonschema was never installed on the clock; ticket 54's Answer only confirms step6 now PASSes once jsonschema is present — it never inspects which tag the script selects. No ticket, ADR, or REGRILL answer discusses the `git tag -l 'v*.*.*'` glob failing to match namespaced tags (threat-register/vX.Y.Z, policy/vX.Y.Z) or platform's implementations-tag/policy-tag ambiguity. This is a genuinely new finding: the check silently mis-selects/mis-reports tags for two of the estate's most important units (feeds, platform) despite now passing.
FIT IMPACT: The check that exists to prove provenance under-reports the estate it is proving, and the release the whole §4 story turns on (policy/v4.0.0) is not the one it verifies. A reader of the citable run concludes feeds is unsigned when it is not.
REMEDY: Enumerate each unit's tags through the same parser cut-release uses (or glob '*v[0-9]*.[0-9]*.[0-9]*' and sort by the semver suffix) so namespaced and policy-line tags are both reachable, and verify the newest tag per published line rather than one tag per repo.

## [major] P5-3 Step 3 — the beat that exists to prove an agent proposer is attestable — will land an unsigned commit and be self-certified as signed, so the first real proposal proves nothing about who proposed it
(original title: The proposal commit will be unsigned when step 3 fires, while the estate asserts it is signed)
CLAIM: Reversal 16 ("sign the proposal commit with the workflow Actions identity; add it to the expected-identity regexp", REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:79, confirmed binding :87) is unbuilt in both halves. propose-tier.yml on all three adopters installs no gitsign and sets no signing config — its proposer step (driftwood propose-tier.yml:184-185) sets only user.email/user.name — while twin-sweep.yml:77-83,116,211-213 and drift-sample.yml:107-109,251-253 in the same repo do pin and configure gitsign for their observation commits. tier_pr.py:350 commits with no -S and no gpgsign config, then pushes over git (:351), so GitHub's own web-flow signing cannot apply. No expected-identity regexp in the estate names propose-tier: every one of the 29 EXPECTED_*IDENTITY* hits across the eight units is anchored to cut-release.yml (adopter-gate.py:75-77; platform release.yml:54; driftwood release.yml:47). The `"signed": True` literals GAPS 1.9 says to delete are still at wargamer.py:200 and :232. Consequence, stated exactly: the first real proposal PR — the event open ticket 74 waits for, and no wargamer/retune-* branch yet exists — will carry an unsigned, unattributable commit, so §4 step 3's "signed by the proposer's identity" and principle 6's attestability fail at the one commit a machine authors. Correction to the finding's second half: the false assertion is narrower than stated. wargamer.py:324's `assert p["signed"] is True` runs over `proposals(intel)`, which derives from `wargame()` and never yields a cage-tier row, so the selfcheck vouches for the demo fixture's proposals, not for the commit tier_pr.py lands; and the cage-tier literal at :232 reaches neither the PR body (_pr_body, tier_pr.py:283-308, makes no signature claim) nor the proposer's printed output (_land's result is branch/proposal_kind/manifest/landed/error only). So the estate will not print "signed" over the unsigned commit — it carries a dead literal that has never been made true and a fixture selfcheck that asserts signedness generically. GAPS.md:29 states the split correctly ("The only real PR opener does not sign; the one that claims `signed: True` never commits") and names both remedies. Ownership stands as found: tickets 02, 10, 12, 13 and 32 all read Status: resolved, ticket 74 contains no occurrence of "sign", and of the 31 open tickets only 62 (deleted thin-slice refs) and 74 mention the proposer at all. No open PR on platform or driftwood adds signing.
EVIDENCE: driftwood/.github/workflows/propose-tier.yml: grep for gitsign|gpg|sign returns exactly one hit, a prose comment at line 29 about a stale signed size. Its proposer step sets only `git config user.email/user.name`. platform/wargamer/tier_pr.py:350 is `_git("commit", "-q", "-m", p["title"], cwd=adopter_dir)` with no -S and no gpgsign config anywhere in the file. driftwood/.github/scripts/adopter-gate.py:75-78 pins only `.github/workflows/cut-release\.yml@refs/heads/(main|release/...)`. platform/wargamer/wargamer.py:200 and :232 both contain `"signed": True,` and wargamer.py:324 asserts `p["signed"] is True and "Rekor" in p["identity"]`. Contrast driftwood/.github/workflows/twin-sweep.yml:77-83,116,212 which does install pinned gitsign and set `git config gpg.x509.program gitsign` for its observation commit. GAPS.md:29 row 1.9 states the remedy verbatim: 'gitsign in tier_pr.py; delete the literal "signed": True'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principles 5 and 6, §4 step 3 ('A proposal PR opens, signed by the proposer's identity'), REGRILL reversal 16 (binding)
OWNER: new GAPS.md:29 (row 1.9); REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:79 (reversal 16) — Reversal 16 (REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:79) and GAPS.md:29 row 1.9 record the decision and name the exact remedy ("gitsign in tier_pr.py; delete the literal \"signed\": True"), so the finding is not inventing a requirement. But no ticket has built it and none currently owns it: ticket 28 (resolved) built signing/caging only for the observation lane (drift-sample/truth-log commits on six repos), not the tier_pr.py proposal-PR commit — its own Answer text confirms scope. Ticket 10 (resolved) only settled scheduling/dedupe/rejection-ledger, no signing. Ticket 74 (open, "step 3 happens once for real") has zero occurrences of "sign" anywhere in its file — confirmed by direct grep — so it does not carry this as scope despite being the ticket that will make step 3 fire for real. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's M9 (its only step-3-adjacent open finding) is about propose-tier's "no parent tree provided" failure, a different defect, and is not in the Refuted-claims list. No ADR or map "Out of scope" entry addresses proposal-commit signing. This is a live, unowned gap: a ratified, specifically-remedied decision that has not been built and has no open ticket carrying it.
FIT IMPACT: The first real proposal PR — the event ticket 74 is waiting for — will land an unsigned, unattributable commit, and the estate's own war-gamer selfcheck will still report it as signed. §4 step 3 would then be reported complete on a fabricated attribute.
REMEDY: Add the pinned-gitsign install and `git -c gpg.format=x509 -c gpg.x509.program=gitsign` config to propose-tier.yml (copy twin-sweep.yml's block verbatim), add propose-tier.yml to each adopter's expected-identity regexp with a verify check, and delete the literal "signed": True from wargamer.py:200,232 so the selfcheck stops asserting an unobserved attribute. Do this before ticket 74 fires.

## [major] P4-2 The cage release was never distributed: what Flux actually puts on an adopter cluster is a retired policy line, and the step-4 instrument cannot tell which line it is
(original title: What Flux actually installs on an adopter is the three retired, unsafe policy lines)
CLAIM: All three adopters' composed ResourceSets reconcile policy versions 2.0.0, 2.0.1 and 3.0.0 — the three lines platform retired on 2026-08-29 — while the cage release 4.0.0 exists only at each adopter's HEAD, outside every tag any cluster reads (driftwood, tuppence and ludlow each have only v1.0.0 and v1.1.0; v1.1.0 carries composed/policies up to 3.0.0). Evidence: composed-set.yaml:85-87 (driftwood; :87-89 in tuppence and ludlow), and the run-21 sample at driftwood 6cf0671 recording last_applied_revisions = {composed-v2-0-0, composed-v2-0-1, composed-v3-0-0}. Sharper still: `python3 scripts/render_composed.py --list` in driftwood's HEAD worktree returns ONE object (the orphan guard alone), against 16 at --ref v1.1.0 — the HEAD array names three trees HEAD no longer carries, and the v4.0.0 tree it does carry is named by no element.

Two qualifications the finding should carry. (a) The reconciled lines are inert rather than exploitable: their cage-tier writes priorityClassName without the priority/preemptionPolicy the API server re-derives (versions.yaml:33-41, retirement reason 1), so a pod claiming one could not be admitted at all; the forgeable-tier hazard needed the 2.0.2/3.0.1 backports, which were never cut. (b) The composed set is opt-in (composed-set.yaml:34-44) and is applied only on the ephemeral cluster the adopter's own drift-sample.yml:178 builds — no standing adopter cluster reconciles it — but that ephemeral cluster is the only in-force evidence the estate cites, so the cross-org "in force" proof behind principle 7 rests on the retired lines.

Fit impact, restated precisely: run 21's driftwood sample is not green — it FAILs fact 2 on cert-chain skew — but its true facts 3, 4 and 5 (16/16 objects, 19 inventory entries) are exactly what proves a deliberately-retired policy line in force, and e2e step 4, titled "flux reconciles the new cage spec onto the cluster", grades from that sample whenever the runner has no cluster. Unowned by any ticket; worse, the record states the opposite in REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:43 and in open ticket 71:10-11, both of which say the adopters pin composed v4.0.0.
EVIDENCE: driftwood/gitops/composed/composed-set.yaml:85-87 declares `- { version: "2.0.0" }`, `"2.0.1"`, `"3.0.0"`, with the in-file comment at :88-92: '4.0.0 -- the cage release (ticket 26) -- is NOT here yet on purpose. This array is reconciled from the tree at tag v1.1.0, and v1.1.0 carries composed/policies up to 3.0.0 only.' Run 21's sample confirms live: `last_applied_revisions: {composed-v2-0-0, composed-v2-0-1, composed-v3-0-0}`. `ls composed/policies/` at HEAD returns only v4.0.0. The retirement reason is in .scratch/ecosystem/map.md: '2.0.0, 2.0.1 and 3.0.0 are retired, not patched. They could never admit a pod ... the patched lines let a pod pick its own cage.' Second consequence: driftwood's HEAD composed/orphan-guard.yaml has `allowed: ['4.0.0']` while `git show v1.1.0:composed/orphan-guard.yaml` has `['2.0.0', '2.0.1', '3.0.0']`, so the pod at HEAD (claiming 4.0.0) would be denied by the guard Flux actually runs.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 4 ('Adopters pin by tag and commit'), principle 2, §4 step 4
OWNER: new none — Checked issues/ (esp. tickets 16, 26, 40, 60, 71), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (C1, M1-M18, Refuted claims), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, map.md, NORTH-STAR principles 4 and 7. Nothing owns this specific claim. Ticket 40/60 cover only whether the five-fact sample is a citable scheduled observation vs. a rehearsal - not which version array the ResourceSet reconciles. REVIEW C1 and open ticket 71 cover a different, adjacent defect (composed v4.0.0's CEL fails to compile under kyverno 1.19) that presumes v4.0.0 is what's installed; they never note that v4.0.0 isn't even in the tag (v1.1.0) the ResourceSet ranges from, so the cluster is reconciling the three deliberately-retired lines (2.0.0/2.0.1/3.0.0) instead. map.md's retirement note explains why those lines were retired but never flags they're still live. Ticket 16 Q5 (provisional) is the design decision that produces this outcome (ResourceSet ranges composed/policies/v<version> from the adopter's own signed tag) but closed before any tag carrying 4.0.0 existed, and nothing revisits the consequence. Not in REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's Refuted-claims list. This is a new, unowned finding.
FIT IMPACT: The cross-org 'in force' proof that principle 7 rests on is proving a year-old, deliberately-retired policy line in force, not the cage release the whole 2026-08-28 wave built. An observer reading the green sample would conclude the current cage is on the cluster.
REMEDY: Cut a driftwood tag whose tree carries composed/policies/v4.0.0, and in the same reviewed PR add the `{ version: "4.0.0" }` element and bump the driftwood-composed pin — the exact sequence the file's own comment prescribes. Then the five-fact sample proves the current line.

## [major] P3-1 Driftwood's own published response curve is inert: its largest signed price line is the platform's self-declared cage-efficacy table wearing the adopter's perspective label
(original title: The twin's priced entry under an adopter's perspective is computed from the platform's numbers, not the adopter's)
CLAIM: The *residual ladder* on driftwood's twin price entry — and therefore the rung it selects and the appetite sentence it records — is computed from platform/graded/cage.py's self-declared reduction table (0.30/0.70/0.92/0.98), not from driftwood's own published graded response curve (0.05/0.30/0.65/0.90 at costs £8k/£20k/£42k/£95k, each carrying an evidence_grade and a written basis). The entry's headline amount (£1,897,646.11) is NOT the platform's: it is fair.py's annualisation of driftwood's own lm triple [192000, 384000, 800000] with a frequency borrowed and named from feeds' threat-register (platform/fair/fair.py references no cage table). The material consequence is not the £171,600 the capture quotes — that is `impact*(1-reduce)+cost` at the mode impact of £384,000, one shock, a different scale from the entry's annualised residuals. It is this: the entry's recorded policy_basis says "loosest tier whose caged residual (37952.92 GBP) is within the tolerance (40000.00 GBP)", but substituting driftwood's own published mode reductions into the identical operation gives isolated = £189,764.61, 4.7x driftwood's own £40,000 band, with NO rung under tolerance — the same rung would be reached by failing closed (selection_policy.py:16,33), not by fitting. So driftwood's signed evidence asserts it is inside its own appetite on the strength of a table it did not author and explicitly disagrees with. The disclosure (residual_basis: platform-cage-tiers@1.0.0) and the rung-agreement guard are real and PASS on run 21, but pound_seam's own stated ceiling ("the per-rung reduction is not checkable... two unknowns behind it", pound_seam.py:443-445) is true only of the feed payload: driftwood's repo publishes reduction.mode and cost separately in responses/*.yaml, which emit-forward-intel.py:202-204 uses to build the curve. The substitution is a recorded decision (ticket 08 answer 4 "the curve never picks"; driftwood's own package docstring "residuals... from the estate's own pricing"), so the record it contradicts is not REGRILL 33 — whose perspective/currency/appetite discipline the entry does satisfy — but ADR-0021:18-20, which says the adopter's versioned selection package "turns the curve into one tier" when the shipped code turns the platform's residual ladder into a tier and only hashes the curve. Ownership: confirmed orphan. No ticket, ADR or map line names residual_basis or platform-cage-tiers; the check rode into the tree as an unmentioned rider on resolved ticket 36 (git log -S check_residual_basis -- verify/ -> 21b808d, "the insurer quote slice").
EVIDENCE: driftwood/composed/evidence.json prices[3]: {source: twin, kind: twin, perspective: driftwood, currency: GBP, amount: 1897646.1130078766, residual_basis: 'platform-cage-tiers@1.0.0', policy_version: 1.0.0, tail: bounded-pert}. driftwood/twin/orgs/driftwood/responses/*.yaml declare mode reductions 0.05/0.30/0.65/0.90 at costs 8000/20000/42000/95000; platform/graded/cage.py:82-111 TIERS declare 0.30/0.70/0.92/0.98 at 500/2000/6000/15000. Run 21's verify_pound-seam capture measures the gap: 'platform's tier table and driftwood's own graded curve still pick the same rung as cheapest on the same shock (isolated); they differ by up to 171,600 on the rungs themselves'. cage.py's own header (:71-79) concedes 'WHOSE NUMBERS THESE ARE. They are the PLATFORM's, self-declared, evidenced by nothing but this comment.'
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 3 ('One £ currency, proportionate to the org'), REGRILL 33 (one unit, many perspectives, no perspective privileged)
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (all 74), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (C1/M1-M18 + refuted claims), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, NORTH-STAR §6, and map.md Out of scope: none mention residual_basis, platform-cage-tiers, cage.py's TIERS table, or driftwood's own graded response curve disagreeing with it. Ticket 08 (the pound seam, resolved) and its build ticket 25 settled adjacent questions -- which engine annualises, perspective/currency labelling on every prices[] entry, no sum crosses perspectives -- but never decided whose tier-cost table backs a twin-perspective entry's residual pricing; ticket 08's own fact-finding pass flagged cage.py's 'WHOSE NUMBERS THESE ARE... self-declared' comment without resolving it, and no later ticket (09, 15, 25, 30) picks it up. Ticket 30 (open, blocked by 09/12) is about the twin's own runtime cage/spec/price, a different object, not this. REVIEW-2026-08-31's H3-01-adjacent items (M9, M10) are about mechanism triggers and feed pinning, not the tier-table authorship question. This is a new, unowned finding.
FIT IMPACT: The adopter's own signed evidence carries a six-figure line priced by a table the adopter did not author and explicitly disagrees with. The disclosure (residual_basis) is exemplary; the pricing is still the platform's perspective wearing the adopter's label.
REMEDY: Either price the twin entry from the adopter's own published curve (the artefact already carries it) and keep the platform table only as a fallback that names itself, or record an owner decision that the platform's calibration is the shared unit and the adopter's curve is evidence only. Either way, open a ticket — today it is disclosed and unowned.

## [major] P2-4 A second live refusal contradicts "there is no gate", and the glossary plus a green truth-surface check both certify a fix that never shipped
(original title: The orphan-guard is a live Deny that ADR-0022's 'one refusal' clause does not cover, and CONTEXT.md says it is not)
CLAIM: The estate ships three Deny-mode ValidatingPolicies where ADR-0022 blesses one. `policy-version-orphan-guard` carries `validationActions: [Deny]` in the platform ResourceSet (platform/distribution/versions.yaml:117), in its offline twin (render-orphan-guard.py:68) and in all three adopters' composed artefacts (driftwood|tuppence|ludlow composed/orphan-guard.yaml:13 and gitops/composed/composed-set.yaml), and `posture-trust-boundary` carries it too (platform/posture/policies/posture-trust-boundary.yaml:23, plus every served version dir at :15) — alongside the one refusal ADR-0022:41 blesses, `governed-namespace-requires-claim`. The estate's own gate grades the orphan refusal as correct: verify-orphan-guard.sh:49-50 makes "the pod was denied" the pass condition, and run 21 records `PASS: only versions the array declares can run` (talk/captures/.estate-clone_platform_distribution_verify-orphan-guard.out); graded/verify-graded.sh:520-525 would additionally require the refusal live, though that tail SKIPPED on run 21 for want of a KinD cluster, so run 21 proves the denial offline only. CONTEXT.md:221 states the doctrine ("the workload is not denied") and CONTEXT.md:227-228 does disclose the shipped Deny — but it claims that form is "superseded by ADR-0022", and it is not: ADR-0022 never mentions the orphan guard, and ADR-0014's own supersession banner (docs/adr/0014-...:5) limits the supersession to "the CREATE deny on a governed namespace ... The rest stands." Nor does the doctrine-conforming replacement exist: cage-tier.yaml:151-169 takes the tier from the namespace and nothing anywhere reads the version array to cage an orphan claim to `isolated`, so CONTEXT.md:218-221's mechanism is in no file. Ownership: ticket 02 wrote the prose and explicitly deferred the superseding ADR to ticket 09, whose ADR-0022 omitted the guard; tickets 26 (resolved), 27 and 63 do not mention it — no ticket owns converting the shipped guards from Deny to a cage.
EVIDENCE: platform/distribution/versions.yaml:117 `validationActions: [Deny]` on policy-version-orphan-guard, message 'policy-version not in the platform-declared version array (orphan): no ResourceSet element declares it, so it cannot run.' platform/distribution/verify-orphan-guard.sh:45-49 makes denial the pass condition: 'orphan pod (9.9.9) was NOT denied — a version outside the array could run'. Run 21 capture: 'PASS: only versions the array declares can run; the allow-list is the array.' CONTEXT.md:218-228: 'A deterministic catch-all that cages to **isolated** ... the workload is not denied ... The shipped `Deny` form is the July record, superseded by ADR-0022.' docs/adr/0022:37-43 blesses only governed-namespace-requires-claim: 'This is the one refusal the doctrine allows.' REGRILL reversal 5 is binding: 'Reverse: no refusal; the cage tightens until untenable.'
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 2 ('There is no gate'), REGRILL reversal 5, ADR-0022
OWNER: new none — Not owned. Grep of .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ for "orphan" hits only unrelated release-chain-orphan tickets (16, 18, 43, 53) plus ticket 09/58/12/15 which govern the *cage-tier* / *governed-namespace-requires-claim* mechanism, not the separate `policy-version-orphan-guard` ValidatingPolicy in platform/distribution/versions.yaml:117. Ticket 09 ("the cage ladder v2") is the decision behind ADR-0022 and ticket 26 ("the cage ladder lands")/27 (round 2) are its build tickets; grep of both build tickets for "orphan"/"versions.yaml" returns nothing, confirming they never touched policy-version-orphan-guard's shipped Deny. CONTEXT.md itself (rewritten 2026-08-28, ticket 09) documents the intended replacement narrative ("cages to isolated... the workload is not denied. ... The shipped Deny form is the July record, superseded by ADR-0022") but this is aspirational text about the mechanism ticket 09 decided, not evidence the orphan-guard artifact was actually changed — and the finding's cited versions.yaml:117 and verify-orphan-guard.sh:45-49 show the artifact and its own verify script still treat Deny/denial as the live, passing behavior. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md has no mention of "policy-version-orphan-guard", "two refusals", or "one refusal" anywhere (grep empty), so this was not raised, confirmed, or refuted in the prior review. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md's reversal table doesn't reference orphan-guard by name either. Net: a real, previously-undetected gap between documentation (CONTEXT.md/ADR-0022's "one refusal") and the shipped artifact — no ticket owns closing it.
FIT IMPACT: There are two live refusals where the doctrine permits one, and the record asserts the fix has already happened. A reader of CONTEXT.md would report principle 2 as satisfied; the cluster refuses.
REMEDY: Either convert the orphan-guard to a MutatingPolicy that renders isolated for an undeclared claim (what CONTEXT.md already says), inverting verify-orphan-guard.sh's pass condition; or amend ADR-0022 with a dated note admitting a second permitted refusal and its reason, and correct CONTEXT.md. This is an owner decision, not an implementation detail.

## [major] P2-6 The cage — the thesis's most distinctive claim — is proven only by presenter-run evidence that the north star's own citation rule forbids any document from using
(original title: The only live proof of the cage has never been on a citable run)
CLAIM: The cage's behaviour has never been observed on a citable run. Every live claim about the cage behaving — a pod admitted and Running in a caged Namespace, a forged tier label clobbered, an untiered governed Namespace falling closed to isolated, the isolated pod failing to connect to the API server ClusterIP:443 and 1.1.1.1:80, the baseline pod still reaching, a running isolated pod accepting an UPDATE — exists only in verify-graded.sh's live tail (units/platform/graded/verify-graded.sh:283-542), which has never produced a green line on any TRUTH run: the script graded FAIL on every recorded run from 4 to 13 (runs 4-11 on `cage-netpol generate matrix failed`, runs 12-13 on the kyverno 1.19 CEL skew) and has SKIPped for want of a cluster since run 15, because truth.yml's gate job installs kyverno/cosign/flux/gitsign and never creates a cluster. Ten other checks SKIP on the same missing cluster on run 21, including the wholly live distribution/verify-declared-versions-admit.sh, which is a second live cage proof. What the citable record does carry is weaker but real: runs 20 and 21 commit a five-fact sample taken on a genuine ephemeral KinD cluster (dsample-33558850420) grading true that all 16 rendered objects — cage-tier MutatingPolicy and cage-netpol GeneratingPolicy among them — are live, byte-equal to the offline render, and in the Flux inventory. So the surface can see the cage installed; it has never seen it bite. Under §5's citation rule the estate may not cite its strongest cage evidence, and at least one resolved ticket already breaches that: ticket 26's Answer asserts "The cage ladder is live on kind-driftwood at policy version 4.0.0" and cites "the TRUTH line of 2026-08-29", which is run 7, on which verify-graded FAILed. Ownership: ticket 56 (open) owns the surface seeing its clocks; ticket 52 asked for an in-gate ephemeral cluster and was resolved without one; ticket 60 (resolved) built the cluster-free sample path that carries reconciliation facts but no cage behaviour; no open ticket owns extending that path to the behavioural probes.
EVIDENCE: Run 21 capture .estate-clone_platform_graded_verify-graded.out ends 'SKIP (live tail): kind cluster driftwood is not listed by kind get clusters' / 'SKIP: offline proof holds; live tail could not look'. Nine other platform scripts SKIP for the identical reason on run 21 (access, currency-controller, engine, eud, federation, identity, posture-projection, verify-source-verification, declared-versions-admit), plus tuppence/reset/verify-reach-secrets. REVIEW-2026-08-31 C1 records the same script FAILing in runs 12 and 13 under the kyverno 1.19 pin. No TRUTH line in talk/truth.log has ever carried a green live cage observation.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 2, §5 ('One command, on a schedule, in CI, is the only source any document may cite for what works')
OWNER: new none — no existing ticket/ADR/REGRILL covers verify-graded.sh's live-tail cluster requirement — Checked .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (all), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (C1 list + Refuted claims), drift-review-2026-08-27/REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, NORTH-STAR §6, map.md Out of scope. Ticket 54 (resolved) fixed the kyverno 1.19 CEL-compile skew that caused verify-graded.sh to FAIL in runs 12-13 (REVIEW-2026-08-31 C1) — but that is a different failure mode (compile error) from the finding's claim (SKIP because no kind cluster named 'driftwood' exists to run the live tail against). Ticket 60 (resolved) built a real citable green cluster observation, but via a different mechanism (driftwood's ephemeral CI cluster + five-facts.py sampler checking composed-set/signature/reconciliation facts on TRUTH run 20) — not verify-graded.sh's specific isolated-pod-cannot-reach-API/internet-and-accepts-UPDATE live tail, which is what P2-6's evidence (run 21 SKIPs) is about. Ticket 56 (status: open) only covers whether the truth surface can see its own clocks ran, not whether it can see a live cluster's cage behavior — confirming the finding's own 'partly owned' framing. No REVIEW-2026-08-31 refuted-claim or REGRILL-ANSWERS entry addresses a local/ephemeral cluster requirement for verify-graded.sh's live tail. NORTH-STAR §6 and map.md's Out of scope list do not exempt this. Conclusion: this is a genuine, previously unticketed gap — no existing ticket, ADR, or owner decision owns or moots it.
FIT IMPACT: Under §5's own rule, the estate may not cite any of its strongest cage evidence, because none of it was produced by the truth surface. The cage is proven to an unusually high standard in a place the ambition says does not count.
REMEDY: Decide whether the citable run gets a substrate (an ephemeral KinD in the truth workflow, as drift-sample.yml already does for the adopters) or whether the cage's live tail is permanently a could-not-look and the estate says so in the record. Today it is neither decided nor named.

## [major] P1-1 The estate's only live answer to a would-be exemption is the priced cage; no served policy today offers the conditional rule anyone can meet
(original title: The conditional-policy arm of principle 1 has no living instance)
CLAIM: No currently-declared policy version carries a conditional-allowance branch, so principle 1's "conditional rule anyone can meet" arm has no *served* instance. The only such branch the estate ever released — `variables.nonroot || (variables.attested && variables.hardened)` in `distribution/policies/v2.0.1/require-nonroot.yaml:56`, shipped under signed tag policy/v2.0.1 on 2026-08-24 — was retired on 2026-08-29 when versions.yaml was cut to a single element (`versions.yaml:77`, 4.0.0 only); 4.0.0 and all three adopters' composed sets carry a flat non-root + read-only-fs rule with no widening branch, and no other served policy in any of the eight repos contains one. The beat still proves the rule offline every run against the frozen 2.0.1 tree (2-pass/2-fail matrix, kyverno test, residual priced at £21360/yr) and grades SKIP, not FAIL, so the shortfall is that the arm cannot be *installed or observed live*, not that it cannot be demonstrated at all. Two scope caveats: the access/break-glass plane ships a conditional-allowance mechanism of a different kind (graded ALLOW/STEP_UP/CAGE/DENY by factors × £, green offline at run 21) that the estate has not counted toward this arm — ticket 58 Q2 leaves "demonstrated another way" open; and the cage arm is *served* (cage-tier-4-0-0, composed into driftwood/tuppence/ludlow) though its live tail also skipped at run 21 for want of a cluster. Owned by ticket 63 (open; header still says "Blocked by: 58", comment says the blocker is resolved and it is ready to run), whose recorded decision — the isolated-flip cut becomes the second declared line 5.0.0 and re-carries root-if-attested — is PROVISIONAL, not ratified: ticket 58 records the owner's bare "Agree", which under the map's rule does not ratify.
EVIDENCE: platform/policy/verify-conditional.sh:73 emits, and run 21's capture records verbatim: 'SKIP (live tail): the root-attested conditional branch this beat proves lives only in require-nonroot-2-0-1, and 2.0.1 is retired (not in distribution/versions.yaml, 2026-08-29); the only currently-declared version (4.0.0) replaced it with a flat non-root+read-only-fs rule that has no root-if-attested branch, so no currently-declared version's live install can observe this claim'. distribution/versions.yaml:77 declares only 4.0.0. distribution/policies/v4.0.0/require-nonroot.yaml carries no conditional branch.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 1 ('An allowance is either a conditional rule anyone can meet, or a cage with a price')
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 63 (open, unblocked; decision recorded in ticket 58) — Finding is accurately current and already owned, not new. Ticket 58 (grilling, resolved 2026-08-31) recorded the owner's PROVISIONAL decision (bare "Agree", no reason, per map's rule) that ticket 63's isolated-default flip is the second declared version line AND re-carries the root-if-attested conditional branch (Q1a+Q2a). .scratch/ecosystem/issues/63-the-unlabelled-default-flips-to-isolated.md status=open, comment dated 2026-08-31 says "Blocker 58 is resolved; this ticket is ready to run" — i.e. charted but not yet built/shipped. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md M11 originally called the conditional arm an orphan; ticket 58's answer is an *update* to that finding (not a refutation in the "Refuted claims" list — checked, M11 is not listed there), confirming the gap is real and assigning it to ticket 63. Since distribution/versions.yaml:77 still declares only 4.0.0 (per P1-1's own evidence) and ticket 63 is still open/unbuilt, the finding's live-state claim remains true today; it is not a new discovery, it's the same known gap already tracked with a named, unblocked ticket and a provisional owner decision on how it will be resolved.
FIT IMPACT: Half of the sentence that replaces exemptions is currently unrepresented in any served policy, so the estate cannot demonstrate the alternative it offers to a carve-out.
REMEDY: Ticket 63 as written: cut 5.0.0 with the root-if-attested branch restored and relabel platform/policy/tests/conditional/resources.yaml to the new version (verify-conditional.sh:76 already refuses to trust a stale-fixture tail).

## [major] P4-1 The estate serves one policy line, so the thesis' "non-negotiable" multi-version runtime — coexistence, a retirement window, and the ±1 shift-left window — is unproven at runtime on every citable run
(original title: No older policy line is patchable, because only one line is declared)
CLAIM: Only one policy line is declared, so coexistence, retirement and the ±1 shift-left window have no multi-version subject, and all three checks are graded could-not-look on the citable run. distribution/versions.yaml:77 declares exactly one element (version 4.0.0, tag policy/v4.0.0, commit 64635df…, bump major) in platform at 46cd775, the tree TRUTH run 21 graded. Run 21's own CI log (gh run view 33616685427 --log) grades .estate-clone/platform/distribution/verify-coexistence.sh, .../verify-retirement.sh and .estate-clone/platform/shift-left/verify-shift-left.sh SKIP, each naming the one-version fact as its reason; verify-all.sh maps exit 3 to SKIP, and all three scripts genuinely exit 3.

Two corrections to the finding as written. First, patchability is undemonstrated, not impossible: the array mechanism exists and was exercised historically (platform commit ba64418, 2026-08-24, "add 2.0.1 to the version array -- patch on the 2.0.0 line"), the older trees and signed tags remain on disk (policies/v1.0.0…v3.0.0; tags policy/v2.0.0, policy/v2.0.1, policy/v3.0.0), and map.md:73 records that the repair those lines actually needed was correctly computed as major, so retirement — not a patch — was the honest move. Second, "unexercised on every run" holds only for retirement, which exits 3 before its first beat. verify-coexistence still runs and passes both offline beats, including a real two-version self-scoping matrix over the retired v2.0.0 and v3.0.0 bodies (distribution/tests/require-nonroot/kyverno-test.yaml), and verify-shift-left runs and passes three of four beats (the single-element ±1 window, the unversioned out-of-scope case, the orphan-target refusal); only the Audit→Deny flip beat skips.

The gap is real, already owned and already recorded: REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:69 (M11 item 1), map.md:9 owner decision (1), and open ticket 63, whose 2026-08-31 comment records that the isolated-default flip "IS the second declared line (the engine computes a major, so 5.0.0)". The honest SKIPs, not a false pass, are what keep the TRUTH number truthful.
EVIDENCE: platform/distribution/versions.yaml has one array element (line 77: version 4.0.0, tag policy/v4.0.0, commit 64635df..., bump major). Run 21 captures: verify-coexistence 'SKIP: ... distribution/versions.yaml declares one version (4.0.0)'; verify-retirement 'SKIP: distribution/versions.yaml declares one version (4.0.0), so a retirement would leave an empty allow-list'; verify-shift-left 'SKIP: distribution/versions.yaml declares one major line (4.0.0), so a target has no ±1 neighbour'. map.md records the reason: 2.0.0/2.0.1/3.0.0 were retired rather than patched because 'teaching an old line to read the Namespace tier is ADR-0022, which the engine computes as major, so it cannot ride on a patch'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 4 ('Older lines are patchable'); research/03-blogs-thesis.md's >=3-coexisting-version runtime requirement
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 63 (open, blocked-by 58 which is resolved-provisional) — Ticket 63 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/63-the-unlabelled-default-flips-to-isolated.md) is open and explicitly says the isolated-default flip cut "IS the second declared line (the engine computes a major, so 5.0.0)" — i.e. it is the designated fix for exactly the no-second-version-line gap the finding describes (coexistence/retirement/shift-left all SKIP because distribution/versions.yaml has one element). Its blocker, ticket 58 (grilling), is resolved with the relevant decision (Q1a) recorded PROVISIONAL only — the owner's reply was a bare "Agree" which per the map's rule does not ratify. So per the read-only rules bare agrees are not consent: the decision to make ticket-63's cut the coexistence subject is provisional, not executed, and the finding's underlying fact (only one version declared, three checks SKIP) remains true today. This is not a duplicate of a REVIEW-2026-08-31 finding still open in the same form — that review's M11 (closed by ticket 58) and M12 (closed by ticket 63) already cover this same gap, so P4-1 restates M11/M12 rather than reporting something new, but since ticket 63 is still open, status is owned-by-open-ticket rather than resolved.
FIT IMPACT: Three of the estate's most distinctive mechanisms — coexistence, retirement-by-deletion, and the shift-left +/-1 comparison — are unexercised on every run, and the record's own honest SKIPs are the only thing keeping the number truthful.
REMEDY: Cut 5.0.0 (ticket 63) so a second line exists; then decide separately whether a patch to a still-declared older line is ever demonstrated, since retirement has so far been the estate's answer to every defect.

## [major] P5-1 Principle 5's clock is evidenced only as a committed cron line: the citable number would not move if every schedule in the eco-system stopped firing
(original title: The truth surface cannot see whether any of its own clocks ran)
CLAIM: On every run whose capture is in git — runs 9 through 21, all twelve recorded — the live half of verify-schedules ("did each clock run inside its own period") records one SKIP per required clock and has never once graded. On run 21 that is 14 SKIP lines covering all 13 required clocks (driftwood propose-tier/renovate-run/twin-sweep, ludlow propose-tier/renovate-run, tuppence propose-tier/renovate-run, feeds/ico/insurer/nist/platform fetch, hub truth), every one reading "GitHub unreachable (Command ['gh','auth','status'] returned non-zero exit status 1.)". The cause is that the gate step carries no GitHub credential — deliberately, for a reason recorded at truth.yml:59-64,92-98: talk/verify-all.sh runs eight other orgs' unpinned verify scripts in the same process. It is NOT a structural limit on cross-org reads: all nine repos are public and both endpoints the check needs (workflow runs, workflow contents) return HTTP 200 with no credential at all, so the fact is readable today — driftwood's propose-tier clock last fired 2026-09-02T11:43:35Z, success. Closing this alone would not turn the script green: three publisher fetch.yml clocks SKIP for an unrelated reason ("opens no pull request anywhere", schedules.py:537-546), and grading is by exit code (verify-all.sh:62-66), not by the capture's last line. Owner: ticket 56 (open, unblocked), whose Question states the same defect in the same words. Consequence: a clock that stops firing produces no FAIL anywhere; the only indirect pressure is drift-sample.yml, whose stale sample turns e2e step 4 into a SKIP — never a FAIL — after 48h.
EVIDENCE: Run 21's verify_schedules capture carries twelve lines of the form 'SKIP: <unit>/<workflow>: GitHub unreachable (Command [gh, auth, status] returned non-zero exit status 1.) -- cannot look at whether this clock ran inside its period', covering driftwood propose-tier/renovate-run/twin-sweep, ludlow propose-tier/renovate-run, tuppence propose-tier/renovate-run, feeds fetch, ico fetch, insurer fetch, nist fetch, platform fetch and hub truth. The capture's last non-empty line is one of these SKIPs, so the whole script grades SKIP.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 5 ('Feeds refresh ... all on a schedule'), §5 ('The number and its date are recorded on every run')
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 56 — Ticket 56 ("The citable run can see whether the clocks ran"), status open, is a direct match: it describes verify-schedules' live half SKIPping on every CI run because the gate step deliberately carries no GitHub credential — the same root cause and same "gh auth status" mechanism P5-1 cites. Ticket 56 explicitly closes REVIEW-2026-08-31.md finding M6 ("verify-schedules blind, 3 confirmed findings"), and M6's text (.scratch/ecosystem/REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:59) is near-verbatim the same claim as P5-1: "the gate step deliberately carries no GitHub credential, so 'did each clock run inside its period' SKIPs on every CI run — structurally." Ticket 56 is still open (not resolved), so this is an owned-but-unfixed gap, not a duplicate finding needing a new ticket. No REGRILL-ANSWERS.md entry or NORTH-STAR §6/out-of-scope note supersedes it.
FIT IMPACT: A clock that stops firing produces no red anywhere. The estate's own memory records ~5h cron delays and three publisher fetch clocks failing on every run to date; none of that reaches the citable number.
REMEDY: Ticket 56's scope. The read is cross-org, so it needs either a read-only token scoped to the nine repos, or each unit publishing its own last-run observation into its lane for the hub to read without a credential.

## [major] P5-2 The £ has never moved a cage: the priced-intelligence loop has never closed into an enacted change, so the demonstration's pivot beat is unproven on real input
(original title: No proposal PR has ever opened, so the enactment arm of principle 5 has never run)
CLAIM: No proposal PR has ever opened, so NORTH-STAR §4 step 3 has never fired — and no machine-opened PR of any kind has ever been merged in an adopter repo. Across the complete PR history of the three adopters (48 PRs; numbering contiguous 1..22 / 1..14 / 1..12) there is not one `wargamer/retune-*` branch, and `gh api .../branches` confirms no such ref exists on driftwood, tuppence, ludlow or platform (the proposer's prefix is `wargamer/retune-{slug}`, wargamer.py:195,227). The only tier-related PRs are the three human "policy-composition: … propose-tier workflow" merges (driftwood #6, tuppence #5, ludlow #4), all authored and merged by chrisns on 2026-08-25. Stronger still: all 24 merged PRs are chrisns-authored and all 13 bot-authored PRs were closed unmerged — even driftwood #20, the one feed-pin bump that landed, was human-opened after Renovate's own PR-creation call errored ("prBlockedBy: Error", PR body). The arm itself is not idle but unfired, and unevenly so: driftwood's proposer ran successfully on its clock twice (2026-09-01T12:01Z run 33505428933; 2026-09-02T11:43Z run 33626099181) and returned `[]` both times — no band crossed; tuppence's and ludlow's scheduled proposers FAILED on both days (33515059588/33633036907, 33518603510/33638327430) at the feeds checkout, because their propose-tier.yml still pins `ref: ecosystem/thin-slice` (tuppence:182,189; ludlow:185,192), a deleted branch, so the re-compose and proposer steps never execute. Owner: ticket 74 (open), with ticket 62 owning the tuppence/ludlow pins. Fit impact: of the machinery downstream of the seam, the rejection ledger's live query path has run on real input (run 33626099181: "ledger derived from 0 closed-unmerged proposal PR(s) … half-life 30d, suppress at 0.5") and only its decay arithmetic against a real rejection is unexercised; the proposal's signature is built but unwired (P5-3); and the "human-merge attestation" is not untested but absent — no such mechanism exists anywhere in the eight units, only prose.
