EVIDENCE: `gh pr list --repo policy-as-versioned-{driftwood,tuppence,ludlow}/<repo> --state all --limit 60` returns 22, 14 and 12 PRs respectively; filtering for 'war-gamer', 'retune' or 'tier' in the title returns only the three human 'policy-composition: party artefact, governed namespace, propose-tier workflow' merges (driftwood #6, tuppence #5, ludlow #4), all authored and merged by chrisns. Ticket 60's own 2026-09-01 comment: 'the proposer returned [] — no band crossed, no proposal PR'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 5 ('proposals open ... A human merges'), §4 step 3
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 74 — Ticket 74 (open, "Step 3 happens once, for real") is the direct owner: it states the proposer is proven but "What has never happened is the event itself: a residual really crossing a band, the proposal PR opening... and a human merging it once" — matching P5-2's claim verbatim (no wargamer/retune-* proposal PR across the 48 PRs). Traces to REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:65 finding M9 ("no proposal PR has ever opened... Owning ticket: none → new ticket 60"), which graduated into ticket 74 per 74's Notes ("Graduated from ticket 60"). Ticket 60 itself (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/60-...) is resolved but only for its scheduling/sampler half — its own 2026-09-01 comment quoted in the finding ("the proposer returned [] — no band crossed, no proposal PR") is the evidence that the enactment half was explicitly NOT closed and was carried forward into 74. No refutation of this exists in REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's Refuted claims list, and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md was not found to address it. Not moot: NORTH-STAR §4 step 3 / principle 5 remain the ambition ref and are unmet per the ticket's own Done criteria (still open).
FIT IMPACT: The seam between the twin computing a tier and the estate enacting it as a PR is built, selfchecked and scheduled, and has produced nothing. Everything downstream of it — the rejection ledger with a half-life, the human-merge attestation, the proposal's signature — is untested on real input.
REMEDY: Ticket 74's scope, with P5-3's signing fix landed first so the first real proposal is attestable.

## [minor] P3-2 The heavy-tail seam ADR-0021 designed is unguarded and unexercised: no live £ figure crosses it yet, so the drift is latent, but the estate applies its own two-engines rule inconsistently
(original title: Two severity engines implement the same model in two repos with no equality check)
CLAIM: The lognormal-GPD severity model is implemented twice — platform/fair/severity.py (80 lines: a Monte-Carlo sampler plus a JSON-spec validator) and twin/severity.py (215 lines: closed-form survival/VaR/TVaR/loss-exceedance-curve) — and nothing anywhere compares them. I verified they sample the same distribution today (400k draws each at the estate's own heavy-tail parameters; tail mass and quantiles agree to Monte-Carlo error), so the duplication is real. Their contracts have already diverged, unguarded: platform refuses xi < 0 (severity.py:62-63) where twin explicitly supports it (twin/severity.py:87-89), and platform samples xi >= 1 where twin refuses TVaR as undefined (twin/severity.py:169-174) — the same declared spec is accepted by one engine and refused by the other. Nothing catches this: `grep -rn severity` over the hub's verify/ tree returns 0 hits across all 43 files, no ticket charts an equality check, and platform/fair/verify-fair-tail.sh only checks that the tail is named, out-prices a bounded PERT, and refuses a malformed spec. This is the exact risk the estate recognised for tier selection and guarded at pound_seam.py:503-518 ("Two implementations of one rule is a standing invitation to drift", :508). The duplication is also a silent deviation from its own ticket: ticket 08 decision 7 and ticket 25 both said fair.py would dispatch to twin/severity.py; the reason it reimplements instead (the two repos cannot import each other) is recorded only in platform/fair/severity.py:8-14, not in ticket 25's Built note or ADR-0021. CORRECTION to severity: the finding's claim that this sampler "is upstream of every £ figure" is wrong. The live path exists (composition.py:1745-1747 prices the forward-intel lm through fair.py) but no live payload takes it — driftwood's forward-intel feed emits a bounded triple, the run-21 composition capture records tail=bounded-pert, and the only lognormal-gpd number in any run-21 capture (VaR95=58953) comes from a fixture whose own note says it is standalone and illustrative, not fed by the live twin. Today the duplicated model is upstream of zero composed £ figures. It is a dormant path that becomes load-bearing the first time an adopter's twin emits a severity spec — which argues for building the guard before that happens, but places this below the major bar as a live defect.
EVIDENCE: platform/fair/severity.py (80 lines) and /Users/cns/httpdocs/controlplane/policy-as-versioned-flux/twin/severity.py (215 lines) both implement `S(x) = S_lognormal(u) * (1 + xi*(x-u)/beta)^(-1/xi)`; `diff` reports DIFFERENT. platform/fair/severity.py's header explains the duplication: 'It lives here, not at twin/severity.py, because twin/ is a python package in the HUB repo and fair.py runs inside the platform repo ... The spec crosses as JSON; the sampler lives beside the engine that consumes it.' `grep -rn 'fair/severity|severity.py'` over verify/ returns nothing; platform/fair/verify-fair-tail.sh mentions no cross-implementation comparison. Contrast pound_seam.py:499-520 (check 9), which exists specifically because 'two implementations of one rule is a standing invitation to drift'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 3 ('Every impact and every candidate response is priced in one currency')
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (all 74 tickets, via grep for severity|lognormal|GPD|drift guard|equality|compare|two implementations), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (no severity/lognormal hits at all), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md (no hits), NORTH-STAR §6 and docs/adr/ (no hits). Ticket 08 (the-pound-seam.md:27) and ticket 25 (build-the-pound-seam.md, resolved 2026-08-29) both describe fair.py dispatching a lognormal-GPD `lm` spec to twin/severity.py as part of building the pound seam — this is the origin of the duplication the finding describes, and it is treated there as the intended design (spec crosses as JSON, sampler lives beside consuming engine). Neither ticket, nor its Definition of Done / Answer, mentions any cross-implementation equality/drift check between platform/fair/severity.py and twin/severity.py — the ticket 25 Answer lists verify/pound-seam/ checks (twin entry, labels, policy pin, tail field, cross-perspective sums) with no severity-equality check among them. Ticket 13 (lift-or-retire) discusses a different "two implementations" case (label/pin drift between original and estate workloads), not this one. No ticket owns this specific gap, no REVIEW-2026-08-31 finding (C1/M1-M18 or Refuted list) covers it, and no REGRILL answer addresses it. Ownership check: NEW finding, not yet tracked anywhere.
FIT IMPACT: If the two samplers drift, the twin's forward-intel numbers and the estate's priced evidence describe different distributions under the same declared spec, and no check goes red. The estate already recognised this exact risk for tier selection and built the guard; the risk is larger here because the severity sampler is upstream of every £ figure.
REMEDY: Add a check that runs both severity modules over the same lm spec with the same seed and refuses a divergence beyond a declared tolerance — the same shape as pound_seam check 9. Cheap, and it makes the deliberate duplication safe.

## [major] P4-3 The computed release number is measured over a corpus the owner ruled too thin: three-way interactions are never exercised, so a bump can be under-computed and still pass
(original title: The binding 'run the full combination set' override is unimplemented and unowned)
CLAIM: REGRILL 6 (P050) is a recorded owner override — "Run the full set. Accept the runtime cost", listed under "Where the owner overrode the recommendation" (REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:12, :51) — and it is unimplemented and unowned. The shipped release, policy/v4.0.0 (tag on origin, the only live element in distribution/versions.yaml:77), records pairwise-only coverage in its signed evidence: coverage.pairwise_gap says "no three-way interaction was built", generated-corpus/manifest.yaml:12 says combination: pairwise over five axes, and the generator's own selfcheck asserts "never a full cross" — printed again in run 21's capture today. No three-way or sampled-cross mode exists in computed-semver. The named owner is ticket 18 (research/06-eslint-versioning-semantics.md:349), which is resolved with the requirement stated in its Question (:9) and in its "Already decided, not re-asked" list (:38) but absent from all five Answer items, from every Consequences build assignment (tickets 21, 25, 43), and from the map's ticket-18 entry; no other ticket among 01-74 and no ADR carries it. Ticket 18's own sizing of the work ("432 per subject ... roughly 15-45 minutes") is in its Facts-found section, not its Notes, and is stale: the generator now has five axes (13 predicates x 3 states x 2 pins x 5 tiers x 3 namespaces x 2 declares = 2,340 pods per subject), so the cost the owner accepted has never been re-measured against the corpus that actually shipped. The disclosure is honest; the decision is unhonoured and unassigned.
EVIDENCE: REGRILL-ANSWERS.md row 6 (P050): 'Pairwise vs full combination coverage | Run the full set. Accept the runtime cost.' — listed again under 'Where the owner overrode the recommendation'. platform/computed-semver/evidence/4.0.0.json coverage.pairwise_gap: 'axes were combined pairwise (predicate-expression x version-pin, predicate-expression x tier-label, and every pair among version-pin, tier-label, namespace and workload-declares), so no three-way interaction was built'. Ticket 18 (resolved) states the requirement in its Question and sized the work in its Notes ('432 per subject ... roughly 15-45 minutes: re-grill 6's accept the runtime cost is affordable'); none of its five Answer items covers it, and ticket 21 (resolved) was the named builder.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 4 (semver computed from measured verdict movement), REGRILL 6 (binding override)
OWNER: new tickets 18 & 21 (resolved, both silent on the build) — Confirmed unowned. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md row 6/P050 and the "overrode the recommendation" list bind "run the full combination set, accept the runtime cost." Ticket 18 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/18-the-publisher-release-under-cages.md) records this in its Facts (line 26, sizing the 432-per-subject full-cross job as affordable) and its "already decided" list (line 38: "run the full combination set, accept the runtime cost (re-grill 6)"), but none of the Answer's five numbered items (lines 84-92) or the "Consequences" list (lines 96-102, including "Ticket 21 builds: ...") assigns the build to anyone. Ticket 21 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/21-build-the-feed-contract.md), the ticket ticket-18 named as builder, has Question/Notes/Answer entirely about the feed-contract envelope, party.yaml, bump.yaml, and the H6-12 tag/array fix — no corpus/coverage/pairwise content at all. Grepped REVIEW-2026-08-31.md for pairwise/full combination/three-way: zero matches, so it is neither in the C1/M1-M18 list nor the Refuted-claims list. Grepped all 74 issue files for the same terms: only tickets 12, 32, 58, 68 (SPIRE trust-domain "pairwise" federation, an unrelated use of the word) and ticket 18 itself. No open ticket claims this build. Finding stands as new/unowned.
FIT IMPACT: The estate's own release-number evidence discloses a coverage shape the owner explicitly ruled against. The disclosure is honest; the decision is unhonoured, and a resolved ticket carries it.
REMEDY: Either build the full cross in corpus_generator and re-run the next release's gate against it (the sizing is already recorded), or put the pairwise gap back to the owner as a decision to reverse re-grill 6 with a reason. Do not leave a binding override sitting inside a resolved ticket.

## [major] P6-3 The twin is the one participant an adopter consumes without a pin or a signature: the estate's largest £ enters driftwood's evidence as a local file read, so the signed adopter artefact vouches for the copy, not the publisher
(original title: The twin's published feed carries a six-figure price line and is in no tag)
CLAIM: driftwood/twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json — proven to be the source of the GBP 1,897,646.11 twin entry in driftwood's composed evidence (its payload.curve hashes to prices[3].curve_hash sha256:70e5e9f6..., recomputed with composition.py:1685-1687's own algorithm) — exists in no git tag. `git tag --contains` on its commit f75509a is empty, and `git ls-remote --tags` on the real remote shows driftwood has only v1.0.0 and v1.1.0, neither of which contains any part of twin/. Under ADR-0019 point 2 ("The signature is the gitsign tag. No in-band signature field") the feed is unsigned by the estate's only signing mechanism, and the envelope carries no signature field of its own. Nothing in the gate names the absence: run 21's verify-feed-contract passes it three times purely on schema grounds, has zero "waiting for tag" SKIPs, and step 6's "80 published artefacts resolve to a tag" is not a tag check at all but `grep -c '^PASS:'` over feed_contract's output (verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:36-40). Correct the framing in three ways. (1) The number is seven-figure, not six-, and it is the largest of driftwood's four prices, not the second-largest. (2) The fit impact is wrong: the composed/evidence.json carrying the line is ALSO in no tag (last touched ea1c8db, 2026-09-01; `git tag --contains` empty), and the only signed evidence.json — v1.1.0's, 2026-08-25 — carries just two prices (ico 16,901,471.55 at tier "deny", platform 19,558.55) and no twin entry, so there is no signed copy and no signature-covers-the-copy-not-the-provenance asymmetry. (3) The gap is not twin-specific: driftwood has cut no release since v1.1.0 (2026-08-25), leaving 41 commits and 120 changed files outside every tag — including the party.yaml publishes[] record that declares forward-intel and the drift samples — and tuppence's and ludlow's composed evidence are untagged on the same footing. The real shortfall is that the adopter layer's entire current state, the twin feed among it, sits outside the signing mechanism while the gate reports it as tagged. Ticket 64 owns the twin half; the wider adopter-tag staleness is unowned.
EVIDENCE: `git ls-tree -r --name-only v1.1.0 | grep -c twin/forward-intel` -> 0. `git log --format=%H -1 -- twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json | xargs git tag --contains` -> empty. driftwood/composed/evidence.json prices[3] carries {source: twin, amount: 1897646.1130078766, curve_hash: sha256:70e5e9f6...}. The envelope itself carries no signature field (kind, name, version, published_by, published_at, payload_schema, payload only). Run 21's verify-feed-contract capture passes it three times on schema grounds ('envelope valid (feed/forward-intel 1.0.0)', 'payload vs twin/forward-intel/payload.schema.json', 'bump.yaml bump=none').
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 6 ('Every artefact carries a signature'), principle 4 ('Adopters pin by tag and commit')
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 64 (open); REVIEW-2026-08-31.md M16 — Finding P6-3 is a direct restatement of M16 from REVIEW-2026-08-31.md ("forward-intel feed... unsigned... outside every tag"), which is explicitly named as "Owning ticket: none → new ticket 64" (.scratch/ecosystem/REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:79). Ticket 64 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/64-the-twin-is-three-adopters-tagged-and-signed.md) is status: open, and its Done criteria explicitly include "twin/v0.1.0 and the forward-intel tag exist signed" — confirming this is unresolved and not a case of a resolved ticket falsely claiming the fix. No REGRILL answer or ADR moots it. Not a refuted claim in the review.
FIT IMPACT: The largest twin-sourced number in an adopter's signed evidence document has no signature behind it, while the document that carries it is signed — the signature covers the copy, not the provenance.
REMEDY: Ticket 64's scope: cut the twin release tag and the adopter's forward-intel feed under a signed tag, and make verify-feed-contract go amber (not silently PASS) for a published feed that resolves to no tag.

## [major] P7-1 "Proven in force across an org boundary" is true at a declared ceiling for the two publishers — but the citable capture prints it as three independent per-publisher proofs
(original title: The cross-org proof is a chain, not a direct observation of a publisher's policy in force)
CLAIM: The cross-org proof is a chain, and at run 21 the chain joins two different platform revisions. For the two publishers, fact 3 is a comparison of two files in the sampler's own checkout — composed/HEADER.yaml's parent sha against gitops/*-pin.yaml's commit — with no cluster read at all (five-facts.py:469-479; header_parents() and sources() both read the working tree). On the sampled ephemeral cluster no Kustomization consumes either publisher source, by deliberate design (drift-sample.yml:161-167 applies platform-pin.yaml only, "never -k gitops/platform/"); driftwood's tree does carry an opt-in Kustomization over GitRepository/platform (gitops/platform/platform-distribution.yaml:5-16), and nist has none anywhere. Worse than indirection: fact 3 reads HEADER from the working checkout (main), while facts 4 and 5 grade the objects rendered from COMPOSED_REF v1.1.0. At run 21 main's HEADER pinned platform 2.0.1/533dccb0 and the live source verified at fact 1 and 2 was v2.0.1/533dccb0, but every one of the 15 composed policy objects actually in force was built from platform 1.1.1/58ef9c5 (git show v1.1.0:composed/HEADER.yaml; all 15 files annotate inherited-from: platform@1.1.1). So the sample's own sentence — "the revision in force is the parent sha the composed set was built from" — is false as printed for platform; only nist's holds, because its sha is unchanged across the two refs. REGRILL 1's "a publisher's signed policy proven in force inside a consumer org" is therefore satisfied neither by direct observation nor, at present, by a sound inference: the publisher revision verified at the boundary is not the publisher revision the objects in force were composed from. The design choice traces to ticket 16 Q5 (issues/16:80, PROVISIONAL on the 2026-08-28 bare agree, issues/16:70); tickets 40 and 42 are resolved and no open ticket revisits it. The owner was shown Q5's mechanism as option (c), but has not been asked whether a chain answers "proven in force across an org boundary" — and the ref-skew above is a defect nobody has been shown at all.
EVIDENCE: Run 21's driftwood sample, verbatim: 'platform true fact_3_last_applied_revision_equals_the_pinned_commit: platform is a verified source only (ticket 16 Q5): no Kustomization reconciles it, so the revision in force is the parent sha the composed set was built from. HEADER 533dccb0a823 vs pin 533dccb0a823' — identical text for nist. drift/window.yaml's fact_3 claim explicitly writes this exception into the pre-registration. The choice traces to ticket 16 Q5, recorded 'provisional on a bare agree'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 7, REGRILL 1 (the re-scoped falsification test)
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 16 Q5 (resolved, PROVISIONAL) — Ticket 16's Answer §5 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/16-flux-rescoped-and-verified-at-the-cluster.md) is exactly this design: platform and nist kept on the adopter cluster "as verified sources only," with the sample asserting each resolved commit equals the parent SHA in composed/HEADER.yaml — i.e. the chain the finding describes, not a Kustomization reconciling the publisher source. The ticket itself weighed the alternative (option b: a ResourceSet over the adopter's composed tag with no platform/nist GitRepository at all, which "moves the org boundary off the cluster") and picked (c), the chain, "because it keeps the boundary observable at the cluster with no second policy install." Critically, Q5 is marked PROVISIONAL, not DECIDED like Q4 — the ticket's Answer preamble states the owner's 2026-08-28 response was a bare agree ("I can't find fault with a single one... Well done") which the map's process rule says "does not ratify," so Q1/Q2/Q3/Q5 stayed provisional pending a real re-ask. Ticket 40 (built from Q5) and ticket 60 (current TRUTH-line work) both operate the mechanism as designed but neither revisits whether the chain satisfies REGRILL 1's "proven in force" wording — ticket 60's only fact_3 mention (lines 204-206) is about a race/wait-ordering defect in the number, not the epistemics. No open ticket (73, 74, or others) revisits this. So: the finding is not new — the exact chain-vs-direct-observation shape is fully described and knowingly chosen in ticket 16 Q5 — but it is also not settled, since Q5 sits on a bare-agree "provisional" status the map's own rule says isn't ratification. This finding is best read as correctly identifying that the provisional Q5 decision has never been put back to the owner with REGRILL 1's specific "direct observation vs. chain" framing — i.e. it re-raises an owned-but-not-ratified provisional, which per the review's own convention (see REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's treatment of other provisionals) should route to the owner as a re-ask rather than as a brand-new finding.
FIT IMPACT: The re-scoped question is the one the owner chose to replace the drift-floor test with. Whether a chain (publisher tag verified at the boundary + composed set built from that sha + composed objects in force) answers 'proven in force across an org boundary' is a judgement the owner has not been asked to make with this wording in front of them.
REMEDY: Either put the chain-vs-direct question to the owner as a decision on the wording of REGRILL 1, or add a Kustomization that reconciles a small platform-owned object directly so fact 3 becomes an observation for at least one publisher. Do not leave the strongest claim resting on a provisional bare agree.

MINORS:
- P1-2 The record states the priced-holes doctrine as done while composition still refuses: CONTEXT.md asserts in the present tense that a new baseline hole 'moves the tier, never refuses', while composition.py still appends a new-hole refusal that sets the composition outcome to refused.
- P2-7 An isolated pod has full reach for about ten seconds after admission: The reach cage is generated by a background controller after admission, so the first caged pod in a brand-new governed namespace reaches everything for roughly one reconcile round-trip; the named upgrade path points at two tickets that were resolved without doing it.
- P2-8 An isolated pod can still mount the node filesystem via hostPath: hostNetwork, hostPID and hostIPC are clobbered shut, but a hostPath volume cannot be removed by server-side apply, so the bottom rung does not close node-filesystem access; the only bar is the forced runAsNonRoot.
- P3-3 The 'two-implementations guard' cannot detect the divergence that matters: pound_seam check 9 runs the adopter's selection package and cage.py over the SAME residuals, so it can never catch a disagreement about which curve produced those residuals; the check that would (check_residual_basis) compares only which rung is cheapest, not the per-rung numbers.
- P6-4 Step 6 counts schema passes as tag resolutions: The provenance check's line 'N published artefacts resolve to a tag on the publisher's real remote' counts every PASS line emitted by feed_contract.py, including envelope-schema and payload-schema passes, so the printed claim overstates what was observed.
- P6-5 The falsifier record carries the wrong pinned identity for the publishers: In every five-fact record, falsifier 2's ci_pinned_identity is driftwood's own cut-release regexp regardless of which party the record is about, while fact 2 in the same record correctly names each publisher's own.
- P6-6 The falsifier says the cluster verified nothing in a record where the controller returned a verdict: _falsifier_state treats any non-True SourceVerified as 'the cluster verified nothing', so when the gitsign-verifying controller runs and REJECTS a tag, the sample records a reason that is factually wrong.
- P7-3 The five-fact instrument is accruing the same coverage failure its predecessor recorded: The five-fact sample declares a daily cadence and a 0.9 coverage floor whose breach means UNMEASURED, and has three sample runs in the five days since it was declared; the predecessor instrument in the same file already reached unmeasured the same way, and nothing warns until the window closes.
- P1-3 The tighten-only adopter floor is implemented and never used: ADR-0022's adopter-declared tighten-only overlay floor is implemented in both selection engines but no adopter declares one, so the mechanism is unexercised on real data.
- P1-4 The revoked-pin-as-priced-hole arm is unexercised: The feed contract's revocation mechanism exists and is validated but every party's revoked[] is empty, so 'a pin to a revoked version is a priced hole, never a refusal' has never been demonstrated.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- Does 'everything is always caged' bind the permanently unversioned population — kube-system, Kyverno's own pods, Flux's controllers, cert-manager, and any COTS workload an adopter runs — or does the cage apply only to workloads that claim a policy version? | WHY: cage-tier.yaml:36-42 states in writing that a claim-less pod 'is not matched, and so is not caged', and calls the question 'a real, separate question, spun out to its own effort'. Inside a governed namespace the same pod is refused instead. So today principle 2 reads as 'every version-claiming pod is caged; everything else is outside policy or refused' — which is either a correct scoping the ambition should say out loud, or a hole. | CHANGES: If bound: the COTS shim (GAPS 3.14, 'decided four times, built zero times') becomes a build, ticket 63's unlabelled-default flip is necessary but not sufficient, and cage-tier needs a second matchCondition arm. If not bound: NORTH-STAR principle 2's sentence needs the scope written into it, and the governed-namespace Deny stops being an anomaly.
- Is the orphan-guard's Deny the doctrine's second permitted refusal, or must it become a fail-closed cage to isolated as CONTEXT.md already says it is? | WHY: ADR-0022 says the governed-namespace claim check is 'the one refusal the doctrine allows'. The orphan-guard is a second Deny, live in distribution/versions.yaml:117, and verify-orphan-guard.sh's PASS condition is that the pod is denied. CONTEXT.md:218-228 asserts the opposite is already true. Only you can say which document is the intent. | CHANGES: If it must cage: versions.yaml's guard becomes a MutatingPolicy, verify-orphan-guard.sh's pass condition inverts, all three adopters' composed orphan-guards re-render, and the engine computes a bump for the change. If the Deny stands: ADR-0022 needs a dated amendment naming two permitted refusals with reasons, and CONTEXT.md needs correcting.
- Whose numbers price an adopter's twin residual — the platform's calibration table, or the adopter's own published response curve? | WHY: driftwood's signed evidence carries a £1,897,646 twin line stamped residual_basis: platform-cage-tiers@1.0.0, computed from reductions of 0.30/0.70/0.92/0.98 at £500-£15,000, while driftwood's own published curve says 0.05/0.30/0.65/0.90 at £8,000-£95,000. The gate measures the gap at up to £171,600 a rung and grades it green because the cheapest rung still agrees. REGRILL 33 says no perspective is privileged; today the platform's is. | CHANGES: If the adopter's curve wins: composition re-prices every twin entry, cage.py's table becomes a fallback that must name itself, and the £ figures in every deck and capture move. If the platform's table wins: it needs to stop being 'self-declared, evidenced by nothing but this comment' and acquire a basis, and the adopters' response curves become evidence only.
- Must a demonstration of the eco-system include a running governed workload reconciled by Flux, or is the composed policy set proven in force sufficient? | WHY: Today no workload is under Flux anywhere: driftwood's only pod sits behind a Kustomization path (./apps) that does not exist in its tree, and tuppence and ludlow have no pod at all. The five-fact sample proves 16 policy objects in force and nothing running inside them. §4 step 4's sentence is 'The workload keeps running, caged tighter'. | CHANGES: If required: a one-line path fix on driftwood plus a pod in tuppence and ludlow's gitops/apps, and the five-fact sample's fact 4/5 set grows to include a workload — after which step 4 can be observed for the first time. If not required: §4 step 4's wording should say the cage spec, not the workload, and the demo narration follows.
- Must the proposal commit be gitsign-signed before step 3 is allowed to happen for real, or may the first real proposal land unsigned and be signed later? | WHY: Reversal 16 ('sign the proposal commit with the workflow Actions identity; add it to the expected-identity regexp') is recorded as decided in four tickets and is unbuilt: propose-tier.yml installs no gitsign and tier_pr.py commits unsigned, while wargamer.py hardcodes "signed": True and its own selfcheck asserts it. Ticket 74 is waiting for exactly this event and its text says nothing about signing. | CHANGES: If required first: ticket 74 gains a blocker, propose-tier.yml gets twin-sweep.yml's gitsign block, and each adopter's identity regexp grows a second workflow. If not: the estate's first real enactment leaves an unattributable commit that its own selfcheck will report as signed, and GAPS 1.9's remedy needs an explicit deferral note.
- Is re-grill 6's 'run the full combination set, accept the runtime cost' still binding for the next release, given the shipped 4.0.0 evidence records pairwise coverage with the gap disclosed? | WHY: You overrode the assistant's recommendation on this specific question. Ticket 18 wrote the requirement into its Question, sized the work in its Notes ('roughly 15-45 minutes: affordable'), and resolved without it in its Answer. The real release's evidence names the gap honestly, which is why it is visible at all — but a binding override sitting inside a resolved ticket is the shape the process rules exist to prevent. | CHANGES: If binding: corpus_generator builds the full cross, the release wall clock goes from 89 seconds to tens of minutes, and 5.0.0's evidence is the first to carry it. If reversed: the reversal needs recording with a reason, and coverage.pairwise_gap becomes the permanent, accepted shape rather than an open shortfall.

# demo-steps

VERDICT: Not fit yet for the purpose the map's Destination states, and the gap is honestly visible in the estate's own number rather than hidden by it. Of the seven §4 steps, two are real end to end (1: ico v3.0.0 signed and resolved on the real remote; 6: seven tags Rekor-verified by the real gitsign binary on the citable run), one is real once but only half (2: the pin moved by a real bot commit merged by a human in driftwood PR #20, but old_price == new_price so the money did not), one is real for one adopter and blocked by an instrument fault (4: driftwood's scheduled lane sample has facts 1/3/4/5 true and fact 2 false only because the in-cluster verifier rejects a tag that gitsign+Rekor verify fine), two have never happened at all (3: no proposal PR, no wargamer/* branch, today's real proposer returned []; and 5's "on its schedule": driftwood twin-sweep has fired twice and failed twice), and one is a reporting roll-up that correctly refuses to grade the others' results (7). The map's destination sentence — "run end to end once, on a clock, with driftwood, tuppence and ludlow consuming" — fails on three independent counts and is today not even a gradeable proposition, because verify-schedules cannot reach GitHub from the hub runner and every "did this clock run" question SKIPs. What IS fit is the instrument: in the seven steps I traced, the gate never turned an absence into a pass, step 4 has never once graded PASS, and the two false sentences I found (feeds' tags, step 2's word "merged") both understate or mis-frame rather than invent. Four things would make this dimension fit: push round 3 of the sampler wait-order fix so tuppence and ludlow reconcile at all (no owning ticket); land ticket 73 so driftwood's fifth fact turns; make one residual really cross a band (ticket 74); and fix the twin's re-render so its clock succeeds and step 5 asserts it (tickets 72, 64). Three of the four are charted; the first is not, and it is nearest to done.

STRENGTHS:
- Step 6 is genuinely, cryptographically real on the citable run: run-21 capture shows seven tags (driftwood v1.1.0, ico v3.0.0, insurer v1.0.0, ludlow v1.1.0, nist v1.1.0, platform v2.0.1, tuppence v1.1.0) verified by the real gitsign binary against real Fulcio certs with 'Validated Rekor entry: true', each against a regexp derived from that unit's own git remote (never a list in the script), with five negative shapes tested per unit (foreign org, foreign workflow, scheme prefix, suffix attack, non-main branch ref) — verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh parts 2 and 3.
- Step 1's remote check is a real network call, not a local read: verify/feed-contract/feed_contract.py:86 runs `git ls-remote --tags https://github.com/policy-as-versioned-<party>/<party>`, caches per run, and returns None (→ SKIP) rather than a pass when unreachable.
- Step 2's real-world event happened once and is graded from the real PR record, not a simulation: verify/renovate/verify-renovate-merged-feed-pr.sh PASSes on run 21 citing driftwood #20 — commit ea1c8db authored by github-actions[bot], moving party.yaml + composed/HEADER.yaml + composed/evidence.json in one commit, merged by a human at 2026-09-01T08:57:50Z. That is exactly the DoD ticket 61 set ('graded by a check that reads the PR record, not a simulation').
- The lane-sample grader is built to refuse: drift/five-facts.py:627-654 returns FAIL if drift/window.yaml no longer declares all three pre-registered falsifiers, SKIPs on a sample older than the freshness bound, and SKIPs via sample_provenance() on a hand-typed or unsigned sample — closing a documented real incident where three typed lines graded PASS.
- Step 3's PASS line was corrected against its own reader and now names itself: the dated 2026-08-29 note at step3_band.py:262-269 replaced wording that implied driftwood's real priced position had crossed its band, and the shipped line reads 'a SYNTHETIC residual placed either side of driftwood's own signed appetite band ... not driftwood's real priced position'.
- The proposer correctly proposes nothing: driftwood's real scheduled propose-tier run 33626099181 (2026-09-02T11:43Z, conclusion success) printed `note: ledger derived from 0 closed-unmerged proposal PR(s)` then `[]`. A machine that stays quiet when no band is crossed is worth as much as one that fires.
- Step 7's grading bites and proves it every run: verify-e2e-step7-honesty.sh:36-70 plants a hedged PASS, an exit-code/last-line mismatch, a non-conforming step, a missing step and a green whose transcript confesses mid-run, requires all four dishonest ones caught while an honest SKIP and FAIL pass through — and the no-argument path (lines 72-77) runs that selfcheck first, closing the 2026-08-28 finding that nothing ever invoked it.
- A red step 4 and a green step 7 in the same TRUTH line are both correct and not a contradiction: step 7 grades reporting, not results, and says so in its header, the README and its own verdict text — a genuinely well-drawn seam that stops one fact moving the number twice.
- The deck check caught the deck: run 21's verify/demo/verify-demo.sh FAIL is the mechanism working — the committed talk/deck.md records beats 1-6 as SKIP while a rebuild from run-21's own captures gives PASS,PASS,PASS,FAIL,PASS,PASS. The estate's one presentation artefact under-claims, and the gate says so.
- Step 4's substrate handling never turns absence into a pass: verify-e2e-step4-flux-reconciles-cage.sh:63-73 checks kubectl, kind, docker, the named cluster and the Flux CRD before any live read, and falls to grade_lane_sample (which itself can only PASS/SKIP/FAIL on a signed, fresh, run-id-bearing sample), never to a bare exit 0.

## [major] DS-F1 The honesty rule "a green that could not look is a red" is broken by the estate's own gate, on the one beat whose distinguishing words are "on its schedule" — and that clock has now failed both times it fired
(original title: Step 5 reads PASS on the citable run while the twin's only clock has failed on both firings)
CLAIM: Step 5 reads PASS on the citable run while both twin clocks are red and nothing on that run looked at either.

verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step5-twin-forecasts.sh grades on five file-presence checks, a scenario file count, and a delegate (verify/twin-evals/verify-twin-evals.sh) that is deliberately time-frozen (rss.run("1970-01-01T00:00:00Z"), signal_for(..., tag="placeholder-tag", commit="0"*40)). It never reads a run, a firing or a schedule. (It does read dates — twin/feed_signal.py:159-165 takes each envelope's event date or published_at — so the precise gap is the scheduled firing, not "a date".) Its run-21 capture ends PASS.

Meanwhile driftwood/twin-sweep.yml, the estate's only scheduled forecast job, has fired exactly twice and failed both times: 33508119299 (2026-09-01T12:31:05Z) and 33627910027 (2026-09-02T12:04:10Z), the latter logging "FAIL: twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json is not what the overlay renders" then exit 1. The hub's twin.yml clock (cron '17 5 * * *') has also concluded failure on each of its last five scheduled runs, so no twin clock in the estate is green.

The estate does own an instrument for this — verify/schedules/schedules.py:650-656 fails a clock whose last scheduled run did not conclude success — but on run 21 all thirteen of its live legs printed "GitHub unreachable (gh auth status returned non-zero exit status 1) -- cannot look at whether this clock ran inside its period", driftwood/twin-sweep.yml among them. So on the citable run the clock was unobserved by every check, honestly in verify-schedules' case and silently in step 5's.

The consequence is demonstrable, not hypothetical: talk/captures/verify_demo_verify-demo.out records a rebuild of talk/deck.md producing "beat step=5 status=PASS" against the committed SKIP, so the beat a viewer reads as "5 · The twin plays a dated signal forward and is scored" renders green while the twin has never once played a signal forward on its clock. The number 57/7 is right — the estate's gate does catch the underlying drift, at .estate-clone_driftwood_verify-twin-overlay.out — but the attribution is wrong.

Two further facts belong in the record. First, both firings died for a reason no ticket names: twin-sweep.yml's sweep step sets `set -uo pipefail` under GitHub's default `shell: /usr/bin/bash -e {0}`, which does not clear -e, so the intended rc==1 "moved=true" branch is unreachable and the clock can never open its proposal PR — an estate defect over and above the feed/overlay drift ticket 72 owns. Second, the correct NORTH-STAR citation for "a green that could not look is a red" is §3 principle 6 (NORTH-STAR.md:35), not §5; §4 step 5 (line 46) and §3 principle 5 are cited correctly. Ownership stands: ticket 64 (open) already promises to "harden step 5 to assert a dated scheduled sweep observation once twin-sweep first fires", and the firings have now happened, so that clause is due.
EVIDENCE: Script: verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step5-twin-forecasts.sh checks five paths with `want` then runs verify/twin-evals/verify-twin-evals.sh; no reference to a run id, timestamp or workflow anywhere in the file. Run 21 capture (origin/main:talk/captures/verify_e2e_verify-e2e-step5-twin-forecasts.out) ends 'PASS: the twin's overlay, feed, 6 scenarios and signal lookup are all present, and its own evals scored them'. Clock: `gh run list --repo policy-as-versioned-driftwood/driftwood --workflow twin-sweep.yml` → 33627910027 (2026-09-02T12:04:10Z, schedule, failure) and 33508119299 (2026-09-01T12:31:05Z, schedule, failure). `gh run view 33627910027 --log` → 'FAIL: twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json is not what the overlay renders' then '##[error]Process completed with exit code 1'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §4 step 5 ('The twin, ON ITS SCHEDULE, plays a dated external signal forward ... and publishes forward intelligence the platform consumes'); §3 principle 5; §5 ('a green that could not look is a red').
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 64 (open) — Ticket 64 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/64-the-twin-is-three-adopters-tagged-and-signed.md, open) explicitly includes: "harden step 5 to assert a dated scheduled sweep observation once twin-sweep first fires" — a verbatim match for DS-F1's ask. Its Notes line also cites REVIEW-2026-08-31's "minor step-5-presence-only" as a closed-finding precursor, confirming this exact gap was already charted, not newly discovered. DS-F1's "new evidence" (both twin-sweep firings — 2026-09-01T12:31Z and 2026-09-02T12:04:10Z — now failed) is the trigger condition ticket 64 named ("once twin-sweep first fires"), so it strengthens urgency but does not create new ownership; the underlying red is separately owned by ticket 72 (open, re-render defect) as the finding itself notes. No REGRILL answer or ADR moots this. Recommend: cite DS-F1 as confirming evidence that ticket 64's step-5-hardening clause is now actionable/overdue, not as a new ticket.
FIT IMPACT: The beat a viewer reads as '5 · The twin plays a dated signal forward' would render green on a rebuild while the twin has never once played a signal forward on its clock. The estate's gate does catch the underlying defect (driftwood/verify-twin-overlay.sh FAILs in run 21), so the number is right; the attribution is wrong.
REMEDY: Two lines in step 5: after the artefact checks, require (a) the committed forward-intel render to be reproducible from the overlay and (b) a dated sweep observation to exist; exit 3 naming ticket 72 until then, rather than exit 0.

## [critical] DS-F2 The one joint the thesis is actually about has never fired: no crossed band has ever opened a reviewed proposal PR, so "the eco-system ran end to end once" cannot be claimed
(original title: Step 3 has never happened; no proposal PR has ever opened on any adopter)
CLAIM: Step 3 has never happened. No `wargamer/retune-*` branch (the proposer's branch shape, platform/wargamer/wargamer.py:195,227) and no proposal pull request exists in any state on driftwood, tuppence or ludlow. Driftwood's proposer is fully operational and has run successfully four times — twice on its 06:47 clock and twice on PR events, including 3 seconds after the real Renovate feed bump PR #20 merged — and returned `[]` every time; today's scheduled run 33626099181 (2026-09-02T11:43Z, success) re-composed real prices at `--as-of 2026-09-02` showing `old_tier: isolated`, `proposed_tier: isolated`, `changed: false`, so the empty result is a genuine "no residual crossed a band", not a no-evidence no-op. Tuppence's and ludlow's propose-tier clocks have never once succeeded: every run fails at `actions/checkout` of feeds at the deleted ref `ecosystem/thin-slice` (ticket 62). The only exercise of step 3 anywhere is synthetic by construction — verify/e2e/step3_band.py:169-176 sets `under = band * 0.5` then loops `over *= 1.02` until the tier flips, and its own docstring (lines 11-15) states "Nothing is opened." Compounding it, driftwood already sits on the strictest rung (cage.py:127 `ORDER = [baseline, restricted, quarantine, isolated]`) with a caged residual of 37,952.92 GBP against its signed 40,000 GBP tolerance (party.yaml:44), so no price rise can move its tier at all; a real crossing needs roughly a 4x price fall or an appetite change. NORTH-STAR.md:44's load-bearing joint — price moving a cage through a reviewed PR — remains undemonstrated, and ticket 74 states the position accurately.
EVIDENCE: `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-{driftwood,tuppence,ludlow}/{driftwood,tuppence,ludlow}/branches --jq '.[].name'` — no wargamer/* branch on any of the three. `gh pr list --state all` on all three: no proposal PR. Driftwood's real scheduled run 33626099181 (2026-09-02T11:43Z, conclusion success) log: 'note: ledger derived from 0 closed-unmerged proposal PR(s) ...' then '[]'. tuppence propose-tier scheduled runs 33633036907 and 33515059588 = failure; ludlow 33518603510 = failure. The e2e probe is synthetic by construction: step3_band.py:181-190 sets `under = band * 0.5` then loops `over *= 1.02` until the tier flips.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §4 step 3 ('The £ crosses a band. The cage tier moves. A proposal PR opens, signed by the proposer's identity. A human merges.'); map.md:7 Destination.
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 74 (lineage: REVIEW-2026-08-31 M9 -> ticket 60 -> ticket 74) — Ticket 74 ".scratch/ecosystem/issues/74*.md" is open, Status: open, Blocked by: none, and its Question text matches DS-F2 verbatim in substance: "propose-tier runs on every adopter's clock ... correctly proposes nothing while no residual crosses a band" and "What has never happened is the event itself: a residual really crossing a band, the proposal PR opening ... and a human merging it once — NORTH-STAR §4 step 3, review finding M9." It also names the exact confound the finding cites (tuppence/ludlow propose-tier failing due to the deleted ecosystem/thin-slice checkout, owned separately by ticket 62) and states driftwood's proposer is otherwise operational, consistent with the finding's evidence (driftwood run returning `[]`). REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:65 (M9) is the original raise: "propose-tier failed both times it fired ... no proposal PR has ever opened; the £ crossed a band only in the SYNTHETIC dry-run. Owning ticket: none -> new ticket 60." Ticket 74's own Notes confirm graduation: "Graduated from ticket 60, whose grading half completed on TRUTH run 20." No REGRILL-ANSWERS entry or NORTH-STAR §6 decision moots this — it remains an open, unresolved ticket, not a resolved-but-still-true case. FIT IMPACT: The single most load-bearing joint of the demonstration — price moving a cage through a reviewed PR — exists only as a dry run. Without it the eco-system has never demonstrated the thing the thesis is about.
REMEDY: Ticket 74 as written. The concrete trigger: a real feed bump that pushes a caged residual across driftwood's £40,000 band (the step-3 probe shows the flip between £20,000 and £58,269), arriving by Renovate as step 2 already can, then a human merge.

## [major] DS-F3 The truth surface — the only record any document may cite — asserts a merged re-price that never happened, and the one bump that did merge moved no money, so step 2's second clause reads green on the board and is unmet in the estate
(original title: Step 2's PASS calls a synthetic bump 'merged', and the one real merged bump moved no money)
CLAIM: The re-price half of NORTH-STAR step 2 has never happened for real, and the e2e step-2 verdict line misdescribes an offline probe as a merged event.

(a) Exactly one Renovate PR has ever been merged anywhere in the estate — driftwood #20, head `renovate/feeds-threat-register-2.x`, single commit `ea1c8db` by `github-actions[bot]`, merged by a human 2026-09-01T08:57:50Z (checked across all eight units; the other seven have zero). It moved no money: the whole merge is 6 changed lines across 3 files, every one a version string, with `old_price` and `new_price` both `19558.549772440045`. That is an estate fact, not a composition defect — feeds `threat-register/v2` changes only the fintech scenario's `lef` (3,6,14 → 4,9,20) and its own changelog says "Other institutions unchanged." The shortfall is that the one adopter whose price v2 would move, tuppence, has never merged it: its four Renovate PRs (#1, #2, #4, #10) are all closed unmerged and its real `party.yaml:29` still pins v1.

(b) The step-2 verdict line nonetheless reads `PASS: a merged pin bump (threat-register v1 -> v2) re-prices tuppence's prices[] through composition (222,574.31 -> 326,139.13), offline, with no repo touched` (`step2_reprice.py:189-191`, unaltered by the shell wrapper, captured on run 21). Nothing was merged in tuppence. The prose headers of both the python (`step2_reprice.py:2-4`) and the wrapper (`verify-e2e-step2…sh:3-4`) get it right — "the one edit a merged Renovate PR makes" — and the script is scrupulous about the *number* at `step2_reprice.py:185-188` ("what moved is the recorded pin, not the number — not a re-price, looking on"). The compression into the verdict line drops the qualifier, and the verdict line is the only one of the three that travels: `talk/deck.html:107` headlines the beat-2 slide with it and `:278` repeats it in the verdict table. The wording was introduced 2026-08-28 (commit `63a9de7`), three days before any Renovate PR had ever been merged, so it never referred to the real event. Step 3 received exactly this correction on 2026-08-29 (`step3_band.py:276-284`, "a SYNTHETIC residual… not {org}'s real priced position"); step 2 did not.

(c) The deck is more honest than "only the narration note keeps it so": beat 2's `grep` filter in `talk/narration.json` includes `"note:"`, so `deck.html:108-119` shows the driftwood and ludlow "not a re-price, looking on" notes on the slide itself. The undisclosed half is the word "merged", which the narration ("A bot raises the pin in one adopter and a human merges it") sits beside tuppence's figures and reinforces.

(d) Ownership: none. `verify/renovate/verify-renovate-merged-feed-pr.sh` grades only that party.yaml and composed/ moved together under a human merge — it has no price test — and ticket 61 met its own DoD. No ticket and no line of REVIEW-2026-08-31 records that the one real merged bump moved no price or owns the step-2 wording. Remedy: the step-3 treatment applied to `step2_reprice.py:189-191` — name the adopter's bump as offline and unmerged in the verdict line, and cite driftwood #20 as the real-but-price-neutral event.
EVIDENCE: Real bump: `git show ea1c8db -- composed/evidence.json` in driftwood → `"old_price": 19558.549772440045` / `"new_price": 19558.549772440045`; only version strings changed. e2e PASS (origin/main:talk/captures/verify_e2e_verify-e2e-step2-renovate-pins-and-reprices.out, last line): 'PASS: a merged pin bump (threat-register v1 -> v2) re-prices tuppence's prices[] through composition (222,574.31 -> 326,139.13), offline, with no repo touched'. tuppence's Renovate PRs #1, #2, #4, #10 are all CLOSED unmerged (`gh pr list --repo policy-as-versioned-tuppence/tuppence --state all`); tuppence's real party.yaml still pins threat-register v1. The script is scrupulous about the number (step2_reprice.py:200-204, 'what moved is the recorded pin, not the number -- not a re-price, looking on') but not about the word 'merged'. Step 3 received exactly this correction on 2026-08-29; step 2 did not.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §4 step 2 ('Renovate raises the pin in one adopter. The composition RE-PRICES the adopter's exposure against its own size'); §3 principle 6 ('the record is falsifiable').
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (esp. ticket 61, the step-2 owner), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (M8's "step-2 gate check passes on a disclosed offline simulation" is the closest prior finding, closed by ticket 61), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, and NORTH-STAR/map out-of-scope. Ticket 61 (resolved) genuinely delivered a real merged Renovate PR — but on driftwood (PR #20, commit 27f1cf2/ea1c8db), not tuppence; its own Answer text is careful to say "v2 re-prices tuppence, not driftwood" and never claims tuppence merged anything. No ticket, review finding, or REGRILL answer addresses the specific defect DS-F3 raises: (a) the OLD offline-simulation check (verify-e2e-step2-renovate-pins-and-reprices) still emits a PASS line using the word "merged" without qualifying it as synthetic/simulated, and (b) the one real merged bump that does exist (driftwood ea1c8db) left old_price==new_price, i.e. moved no money — a fact no open ticket or resolved-ticket claim records. REVIEW-2026-08-31's M8 only diagnosed the general "passes on a disclosed offline simulation" problem, not this specific wording/no-money-moved gap, and ticket 61's closure of M8 added a separate new real-event check rather than touching the old simulation check's wording. This is a new finding.
FIT IMPACT: A deck reader of beat 2 ('Renovate raises the pin; the composition re-prices') would take the tuppence figures as a real merged event on a real adopter. Only the slide's narration note ('for two of them the new payload prices them exactly as the old one did') keeps the deck honest; the check's own verdict line does not.
REMEDY: Change step 2's PASS to the step-3 shape — 'a SYNTHETIC pin bump (no such PR has been merged on tuppence) ...' — and record, in ticket 74 or a successor, that no real bump has yet moved the £.

## [major] DS-F4 Step 6 has never Rekor-verified the intelligence publisher's tags and states in its PASS that it has none — the estate's one real cross-org bump consumes exactly that unverified artefact, and a not-looked is printed as an ok
(original title: Step 6's Rekor half silently excludes feeds and asserts the opposite in its PASS line)
CLAIM: Step 6 part 3 selects each unit's tag with `git tag -l 'v*.*.*'` (verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:88), a pattern whose anchored literal leading `v` excludes feeds' per-feed scheme (`*/v*.*.*`, feeds .github/workflows/release.yml:24). Feeds therefore falls into `queued` (:89) and the script states, falsely, "no signed tag yet … feeds" (:104) and "feeds have no signed tag yet" in the PASS line (:119) — in the same run in which verify-feed-contract names threat-register/v1.0.0 and threat-register/v2.0.0 on feeds and explicitly defers their signature check to step 6 ("existence, not signature -- step 6 checks the signature"). Both tags are real annotated tags that I verified myself with gitsign 0.17.1 against feeds' own regexp and issuer: "Good signature from […cut-release.yml@refs/heads/main]", "Validated Rekor entry: true" (tlog 2672987434, 2673492733). No recorded capture (runs 9, 15, 17, 21) has ever Rekor-checked a feeds tag, so the hub gate's provenance proof has never covered the one publisher using the per-feed scheme — though feeds' own release.yml did verify both tags in two successful CI runs on 2026-09-01, so the tags are unverified only by the citable gate, not absolutely. The same glob also silently skips platform's four `policy/v*` tags (only its highest plain tag, v2.0.1, is ever checked), though there it produces an under-count rather than a false sentence. The defect currently conceals a success (8/8 would verify), but a feeds tag that failed gitsign would be concealed identically. Orphan: no ticket owns the tag selector — the grep `grep -rl 'v\*\.\*\.\*' .scratch/ecosystem/issues/` does return issue 18, but that line concerns the policy repo's release trigger glob, not step 6; tickets 19, 26, 54 mention step 6 for unrelated reasons and ticket 57's Done criteria stop at the feed-contract SKIPs.
EVIDENCE: Script: verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh part 3, `tag="$(git -C "$ESTATE/$u" tag -l 'v*.*.*' 2>/dev/null | sort -V | tail -1)"`; empty → `queued+=("$u")`. Real tags: `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-feeds/feeds/tags --jq '.[].name'` → threat-register/v2.0.0, threat-register/v1.0.0. Glob check on the fresh clone: `git -C .../units/feeds tag -l 'v*.*.*' | wc -l` → 0. Run 21 capture: 'ok   no signed tag yet, honestly queued for cut-release.yml: feeds' and 'PASS: ... and feeds have no signed tag yet'. The publishers reader independently verified both feeds tags verify with gitsign against Rekor.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §4 step 6 ('every step above is verifiable in Rekor and in the artefact sidecars'); §3 principle 6.
OWNER: new DS-F4 — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ for the tag-glob (`v*.*.*`) or per-feed tag scheme (`threat-register/vX.Y.Z`): no ticket names it (grep for the glob only hits ticket 18, which is about a different repo's prerelease glob/semver mismatch, not feeds/step6). Ticket 57 ("feeds and insurer become runnable... first signed tags cut") is open/claimed and is the ticket that got feeds' real tags cut, and its Done criteria mention "adopter subscriptions' could-not-looks convert on a citable run" — but its text never mentions verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh, the tag-selection glob, or the false PASS-line wording, so it does not own this specific defect (silent exclusion of feeds from Rekor verification + an inverted-truth PASS line). REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's C1/M1-M18 list has no matching entry (M1 is about zero workflows/zero tags, already superseded by ticket 57's progress; M10 is about unpinned feed parents, a different defect), and its Refuted-claims list has nothing matching. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md has no relevant entry. NORTH-STAR §6 / map Out-of-scope do not exempt this. Verdict: genuinely new, unowned finding — no existing ticket, review entry, or owner decision covers it.
FIT IMPACT: A silent under-count dressed as an honest queue — the same failure class step 6's own 2026-08-28 comment ('$verified, not $units ... 8 asserted against 6 actually verified') was written to close, reappearing on the other side. The one publisher using the per-feed tag scheme is invisible to the estate's provenance proof, and the verdict line asserts the opposite of the truth.
REMEDY: Read each unit's tag shape rather than assuming vX.Y.Z — feed_contract.py already has tag_forms(entry, version) — or at minimum fall back to `git tag -l '*/v*.*.*'` and verify the newest per feed. One-line fix; removing the false sentence is the important part.

## [major] DS-F6 Flux has been proven to put policy in force on one adopter, not three, and the fix for the other two exists only in a local clone with no ticket owning it
(original title: tuppence and ludlow have never had their composed set in force, and the fix has no owning ticket)
CLAIM: tuppence and ludlow have never been **observed** with their composed set in force, and the fix has no owning ticket. Every five-fact sample either has ever appended — 9 records each, across 3 runs each, including the `tuppence-composed` / `ludlow-composed` sources — records fact_4 and fact_5 false. Ticket 60 (`Status: resolved`) attributes this to a sampler wait-ordering race and says a "round 3" fix is "committed and patched" on branch `ticket-60-wait-order`; that branch exists on no adopter remote (`gh api repos/.../branches` lists only `ticket-60-grade-the-lane-sample` and `ticket-60-sampler-waits` on all three), living only in the hub's local `.estate-clone/` (50fa2da / 6dcba87 / a396c3b) and as a patch under `.scratch/ecosystem/patches/ticket-60/`. Both adopters have now taken a post-round-2 **scheduled** sample that still shows the defect: tuppence run 33632084845 (event=schedule, 2026-09-02T12:49:19Z, head 19cd5087, after the PR #14 merge) and ludlow run 33636830681 (event=schedule, 2026-09-02T13:37:12Z, head ede531a, after the PR #12 merge), both reading "16 of 16 rendered objects are in no Flux inventory". `grep -rn 'wait-order|round 3'` across `.scratch/ecosystem/`, `talk/`, `docs/` and `NORTH-STAR.md` hits ticket 60 only; ticket 73 is scoped to fact 2, ticket 72 to the twin re-render, ticket 74 to step 3, and ticket 42 is resolved. Fit impact, corrected: two of the three adopters in the destination sentence (map.md:7) have never been observed with their composed set in force on any cluster, scheduled or dispatched, so "three adopters consuming" cannot become true until round 3 reaches their mains. But the fail arithmetic is smaller than stated: of run 21's seven fails, exactly **one** (`.estate-clone/tuppence/verify-reconcile.sh`) is blocked solely by this, and one more (`.estate-clone/ludlow/verify-reconcile.sh`) needs both round 3 and ticket 73. `verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step4-flux-reconciles-cage.sh` grades driftwood only (`ADOPTER="${E2E_ADOPTER:-driftwood}"`, line 46) and fails on driftwood's fact 2, as does `.estate-clone/driftwood/verify-reconcile.sh` — both are ticket 73's cert skew, untouched by round 3. Further, round 3 would turn tuppence's row FAIL → SKIP, not FAIL → PASS: its newest scheduled record reads `fact_2: None` ("nothing checked a signature at this source boundary"), which `drift/five-facts.py:648-653` grades as verdict 3.

Also note the root cause is an instrument fault by ticket 54's rule (the sampler applied the composed set before Kyverno's admission webhook served), not a fault in the estate's Flux distribution — which is why the claim is "never observed in force", not "never in force".

Same shape in every earlier tuppence and ludlow record. Ticket 60's closing note: 'Round 3 (branch ticket-60-wait-order, one commit per unit, order mechanically asserted ...) is committed and patched.' `gh api repos/.../branches` on all three adopters lists ticket-60-grade-the-lane-sample and ticket-60-sampler-waits only — no ticket-60-wait-order. Hub HEAD 7b92990 message: 'ticket 60: round-2 wait fix was mis-ordered; round 3 committed with asserted order'. `grep -rl 'wait-order\|round 3' .scratch/ecosystem/issues/` → only ticket 60, which is Status: resolved.
AMBITION: map.md:7 Destination ('with driftwood, tuppence and ludlow consuming'); NORTH-STAR §4 step 4; §7 principle 7 (Flux is the distribution arm).
OWNER: new ticket 60 (resolved, post-resolution comment only, no successor ticket) — Verified orphan. Ticket 60 (resolved 2026-09-01) is the only file mentioning "wait-order"/"round 3" (grep -rl confirms). Its Answer section explicitly graduates only the step-3/M9 concern to ticket 74; the post-resolution comment about round 3's mis-ordering fix is added AFTER the Answer and reopens nothing — Status stays resolved, no new ticket number assigned. Ticket 74 (step-3-happens-once-for-real.md) mentions tuppence/ludlow only re: ticket 62's feeds/insurer checkout naming, not the sampler wait-order race. Ticket 73 covers a distinct defect (cert-not-yet-valid on driftwood/ludlow composed tags), not the 16-of-16-absent race. MINORS:
- DS-F8 §5 bullet 1 is not literally satisfied: two verify*.sh are neither run nor excluded: The gate globs two directories rather than the tree, so two committed verify*.sh files under .scratch/ are neither run nor listed in the exclusions file, contrary to §5's stated invariant.
- DS-F9 Step 7 re-executes steps 1-6 with a 300s timeout against the gate's 900s: The table on deck beat 7 is a second, independent execution of steps 1-6, not a read of the gate's grades, and its per-step timeout is a third of the gate's, so a step running between 300s and 900s would be graded 'hung' by step 7 and pass in the gate.
- DS-F10 Step 7's PASS line lists seven verdicts for six steps: The verdict summary appends step 7's own grade to a list introduced as 'steps 1-6', so a reader counting positions mis-attributes which step is red.
- DS-F11 The committed deck is stale by six beats — in the safe direction: talk/deck.md on origin/main records beats 1-6 as status=SKIP with reasons from a tool-less local run, while a rebuild from run-21's own captures gives PASS,PASS,PASS,FAIL,PASS,PASS.
- DS-F12 Correction: the verify-schedules capture is not missing a wrapper verdict: A reader's map flagged the run-21 verify-schedules capture as anomalous for ending on a raw SKIP with no wrapper-composed verdict; it is not anomalous — the last line IS the wrapper's verdict, identical by construction to an earlier line.
- DS-F13 Correction: hub truth.yml's failures are the honest red, not observation-lane violations: The github-live map attributed hub truth.yml's continuous failures to '::error::the scheduled truth run left a change outside the observation lane'; on the runs I checked, including the one that produced TRUTH run=21, the observation-cage step succeeded and the only failing step is the intended 'fail if the gate failed'.
- DS-F14 The citable run cannot see whether any clock ran, so 'on a clock' is asserted, not graded: Every 'did this clock run inside its period' question in verify-schedules SKIPs on the hub runner because gh is unauthenticated there, so the truth surface has no direct evidence for the map's 'on a clock' clause.
- DS-F15 Step 1's tag was hand-dispatched and has no GitHub Release, and adopters' bump PRs link to release pages that do not exist: ico v3.0.0 was cut by workflow_dispatch with no subsequent release run and no GitHub Release, so Renovate's release-notes links in adopter bump PRs point at pages that do not exist.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- What does 'end to end once, on a clock' have to mean before you would call it done — must a single scheduled run of the whole fleet produce all seven steps green in one TRUTH line, or is it enough that each step has been observed on its own clock at some point, and the honesty command reports the set? | WHY: The seven steps run on eight different orgs' clocks with no promised ordering and a measured 4.5-5.5h GitHub cron delay, so a single synchronised end-to-end pass may be unreachable by construction. Today's gate can only ever grade 'each step, most recently observed', not 'one run through the chain'. | CHANGES: If you want one synchronised pass, a chaining or correlation mechanism has to be built (an id carried from the regulator tag through the bump PR, the proposal, the reconcile and the forecast) — that is a new ticket ahead of 74. If per-step is enough, the destination sentence should say so, and steps 1-7 need a per-step 'last observed on run N, date D' field instead.
- The one real merged Renovate bump (driftwood #20) raised the pin but left the price identical. Does step 2 count as done, or does 'the composition re-prices' require the £ to have actually moved on a real merge? | WHY: Ticket 61 is resolved on the strength of the pin+render moving together, and verify-renovate-merged-feed-pr PASSes on that. But NORTH-STAR §4.2's clause is about the exposure re-pricing, and that has only ever happened synthetically (tuppence, offline). Whether step 2 is green is a judgement about which half of the sentence is the point. | CHANGES: If the £ must move, step 2 reopens, the e2e step-2 PASS line needs the step-3 SYNTHETIC treatment, and a real price-moving feed bump becomes a prerequisite of ticket 74 rather than a separate concern. If the pin+render is the point, ticket 61 stands and only the wording changes.
- Round 3 of the sampler wait-order fix is committed but unpushed, and enact_guard is doing exactly what it was built to do. Do you want to push it, and do you want an open ticket cut for work that is blocked only on your push? | WHY: Two of the three adopters have never had their composed set in force, and today's post-round-2 scheduled tuppence sample still shows 12/16 objects absent. Ticket 60 is resolved and its closing note is the only record of round 3; nothing on the map tracks it, so it is currently invisible to the frontier list. | CHANGES: With the push, three of run 21's seven fails have a route to green and 'three adopters consuming' becomes reachable. Without it, the destination is blocked on something no ticket owns and no review will surface again. Either way the map needs a convention for owner-blocked, already-built work.
- Step 5's script grades artefact presence, not the schedule, and its clock has failed both times it fired. Is the intended fix to make step 5 assert a dated sweep observation (ticket 64), or should the twin's schedule be treated as out of scope for the demonstration until the twin has its own signed tag? | WHY: twin/VERSION is 0.1.0 with tag_cut: false, only driftwood has an overlay, and the hub's twin.yml has failed on 25 of 25 runs. If the twin is not yet a first-class publisher, requiring a scheduled forecast in the demonstration may be premature; if it is, step 5 is currently reporting green on an untrue claim. | CHANGES: Either step 5 gets hardened now (ticket 64) and goes SKIP until twin-sweep succeeds — moving the TRUTH line by one and making the deck's beat 5 honest — or step 5's sentence in NORTH-STAR §4 is amended to drop 'on its schedule' until the twin tags, and ticket 64 narrows.
- When a proposal PR finally opens (ticket 74), who reviews and merges it, and against what standard — is your merge the demonstration, or does the estate need a second reviewer for the proposal to mean what §3 principle 5 says it means? | WHY: Every human merge across all nine repos was authored and merged by the same identity (chrisns), and no repo has branch protection or rulesets on main. §4 step 3's clause is 'A human merges'; that is satisfied by a self-merge, but the thesis's claim is about reviewed change. | CHANGES: If a self-merge is the standard, ticket 74 can complete alone and step 3 goes green. If review is the standard, a second identity or a ruleset has to exist before the proposal PR is opened, which puts a prerequisite in front of 74 and gives the observation-lane rulesets (currently prepared but inert on public repos) a reason to land first.
- Should a check ever be allowed to say 'no signed tag yet' about a publisher whose tags it simply could not match — or should every 'queued / not yet' verdict be required to prove the absence rather than infer it from a failed lookup? | WHY: Step 6 reports feeds as having no signed tag because its glob is 'v*.*.*' and feeds tags as 'threat-register/vX.Y.Z'. The estate's own doctrine is that a green that could not look is a red; this is a green that looked in the wrong place and reported a confident negative. The same shape could recur anywhere a check infers absence. | CHANGES: A rule that 'absence must be observed, not inferred' would add a small assertion to step 6 and to any other check with a queued/not-yet branch, and would make the honesty hedge list catch this class (it currently cannot — 'no signed tag yet' contains no hedge word). Without the rule, F4 is a one-line fix and the class stays open.

# truth-surface

VERDICT: Fit in mechanism, unfit in framing. The mechanism is real and rare: glob discovery of 84 scripts, three graded outcomes by exit code, a self-checking exclusions file, a pinned toolchain, a working observation cage (21 CI runs, zero cage-step failures), and one dated number committed to git under a verifiable Sigstore identity (`git -c gpg.format=x509 -c gpg.x509.program=gitsign log -1 --show-signature a209496` → "Good signature from …/truth.yml@refs/heads/main, Validated Rekor entry: true"). Nothing about it is fake, and nearly every serious limit I found was already written down in the estate's own hand. It is unfit as a health measure for three compounding reasons. (1) The denominator is a fiction: 12 of 84 scripts can never exit 0 on the scheduled runner — 11 demand a persistent kind cluster named driftwood that truth.yml never creates, and verify-schedules demands cross-org GitHub auth the gate step deliberately withholds — and 5 more are dark because versions.yaml declares one version, so `pass=57 … total=84` is measured against a structural ceiling of 70 and a today-reachable 65 that nothing states or checks. (2) The composition is not what the framing implies: platform contributes 29 of 57 passes and ~26 of those are self-proofs; on my classification only 20 of 57 passes rest on a fact spanning more than one party or reaching live external state, 5 are simulations on synthetic material, and zero of 84 scripts read a live Kubernetes API — the only cluster evidence in run 21 is three JSONL lines recorded 13 hours earlier. (3) The surface cannot see its own instrument failing: seven scripts exit 0 after printing their own "could not look" (demonstrated), six real eco-system clocks are failing today while the gate reports one SKIP, a fall in the number blocks nothing, and four TRUTH lines (runs 1, 2, 3, 14) have gone missing unnoticed. Six cheap changes would make it fit: exit 3 not 0 in the seven scripts; publish the ceiling beside the number; give verify-schedules a token or its own job; pin the flux install; move the hub cron after the adopters' sample lanes; build ticket 59's two §5 bullets. None is architecture.

STRENGTHS:
- One command, on a clock, in CI, writing one dated, SIGNED, committed number. Verified: `git -c gpg.format=x509 -c gpg.x509.program=gitsign log -1 --show-signature a209496` → 'gitsign: Good signature from [https://github.com/policy-as-versioned-flux/policy-as-versioned-flux/.github/workflows/truth.yml@refs/heads/main] … Validated Git signature: true / Validated Rekor entry: true'. The number lives in git with its date and the nine commits it read.
- The exclusions mechanism is sound and cannot rot: talk/verify-all.sh:39-45 fails the gate both when a listed path has no reason AND when a listed path no longer exists. Both entries are provably executed by their parents in the same run — ico/verify-penalty-feed's capture shows 'Signature Verified Successfully' twice (v1, v2), and platform/honesty/verify-honesty's shows 'ok live feed signature verifies; a forged feed is rejected'.
- The observation cage works in practice. Across all 21 truth.yml CI runs, not one failed at the cage step: `gh run view <id> --json jobs` shows the failing step is 'fail if the gate failed' on runs 5-13 and 15-21, the tool install on run 14, and 'record the TRUTH line' on runs 2-3. No run has ever failed with 'left a change outside the observation lane' (this contradicts the github-live reader's note, which I could not reproduce).
- gitsign, kyverno and cosign are each pinned by version AND sha256 in .github/workflows/truth.yml:39-49, and the kyverno 1.18.2 pin carries its own written reason citing the 1.19.0 accident that reddened three checks for two runs.
- SKIP reasons are specific and actionable, never boilerplate — e.g. 'distribution/versions.yaml declares one version (4.0.0); coexistence needs two declared versions to show si…' and 'cs-16's cut-in-the-middle shape needs at least three declared versions and distribution/versions.yaml declares [4.0.0]'. A reader can act on every one of the 18.
- The reds are real estate faults, not instrument noise. Six of run 21's seven reds trace to four distinct estate defects with named owning tickets (73 cert-skew, 62/74 ludlow+tuppence objects absent, 72 twin feed re-render); only verify-demo is an instrument fault.
- The five-fact grader refuses to be fooled: drift/five-facts.py's grade() will not credit a sample unless its run field is a real GitHub Actions run id and the appending commit was authored and signed by the lane's own identity — closing a documented 2026-08-29 incident where three hand-typed lines graded PASS.
- The estate documents its instrument's limits against its own interest, in its own words: units/driftwood/.github/rulesets/README.md 'Amended 2026-08-28: this leg is NOT in force, and cannot be until the repos are private'; verify-e2e-step3's capture 'a SYNTHETIC residual … not driftwood's real priced position'; ludlow/verify-adopter-gate's 'NOT exercised here (CI-only, confirmed)'. Most of my findings extend self-criticism already written down.
- The 2026-08-31 review's remediation verifiably landed. C1 (kyverno pin), M2 (signature spine — source-verification now prints 'it ACCEPTS the real signed tag'), M3 (VERIFY_TIMEOUT 900, publisher-gate completes in 358s), M4 (corpus SIGPIPE, now a glob with the reason in a comment), M5 (jsonschema==4.23.0 installed), M7 (all three verify-reconcile scripts now grade the sample before the substrate check) and the cosign-unpinned minor are all fixed on run 21. The instrument is improving run over run.
- The cross-party contract layer IS genuinely observed: verify/feed-contract resolves 22 real cross-party pins against 8 real remotes plus ~60 envelope validations; verify/e2e/step6 verifies 7 real Fulcio cert subjects with Rekor entries validated; verify/party checks all 8 parties' own signed party.yaml; verify/renovate reads driftwood's real origin/main merge record for PR #20.

## [critical] TS-C1 The only instrument any document is allowed to cite can turn "could not look" into a green — including provenance's end-to-end PASS — so the project's one honesty claim is not true of the honesty machine itself
(original title: Seven scripts exit 0 after printing their own "could not look" — the gate grades them PASS)
CLAIM: Nine verify scripts convert absence into a pass. Seven print a SKIP line and exit 0 — verify-cage-engine.sh:12-15, verify-comparison-window.sh:11-14, verify-gate.sh:11-14, verify-rederive-bumps.sh:9-12, verify-generator-standing-check.sh:18-21 (whole script), plus verify-corpus-generator.sh:34-37 (step 3 only) and verify-witness-set.sh:68-71 (step 5, falling through to PASS at :117) — all in platform/computed-semver/. Two more, verify/proportionality/verify-proportionality.sh (:71-76 kyverno body proof, :94 live tail) and verify/provenance/verify-provenance.sh (:47-49 openssl, :66-72 Rekor, :80-90 SPIRE), have no exit-3 path at all and degrade to printed notes before asserting PASS; provenance's PASS text (:96-99) claims "workload+device+commit+human all root to one attestation … Provenance for every actor, end to end" for links §3/§4 did not observe. talk/verify-all.sh:62 grades exit 0 as PASS and prints no reason on a PASS row, so the truth surface cannot distinguish "observed true" from "could not look" for these — violating the instrument's own header contract (verify-all.sh:8-13, "a live tail that cannot see its cluster must exit 3, not 0") and NORTH-STAR.md:35 principle 6 ("a green that could not look is a red"). NORTH-STAR.md:57 (§5 bullet 2) is scoped to live tails and is the weaker citation here.

The defect is latent, not active: kyverno 1.18.2 is present locally, and run-21 captures show all seven really ran (e.g. "ok kyverno loaded 1 real policy rule(s)"). It is also not reachable on today's clock — both CI paths that run these scripts install kyverno pinned by version and sha256 under `set -euo pipefail` with no continue-on-error (.github/workflows/truth.yml:78-87; platform release.yml:66-79), so a failed install aborts before the gate. And the trigger the auditor names is the wrong one: the 1.19.0 accident put a *wrong* CLI on PATH, which reds loudly; this path needs the CLI *absent*. The real exposure is a local/offline `talk/verify-all.sh` run — a supported mode that emits its own citable `run=local` TRUTH line — or any future runner that omits the CLI.

The gap is genuinely unowned: no ticket names any of the nine (ticket 55 and REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:87 cover only render-version-tree, now correctly `exit 3` at verify-render-version-tree.sh:23-29), and no ADR mentions exit 3. The fix already exists and is two lines of reuse: platform/lib.sh:18 `skip()` and :43-46 `pass_line()`, which exits 3 whenever any tail was skipped. A second existing net, verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step7-honesty.sh, already fails exactly this shape (:9, :88-92) but its loop at :73 covers only the six e2e steps; widening it, or routing these nine through lib.sh, closes the class. Severity is better read as high-latent than critical-active.
EVIDENCE: Demonstrated live, read-only: `cd units/platform/computed-semver && env PATH=/usr/bin:/bin bash verify-cage-engine.sh; echo EXIT=$?` → `SKIP: kyverno CLI not found -- cage_engine's Track 1 (ValidatingPolicy admission) needs it` / `EXIT=0`; and with kyverno present `EXIT=0`. Both branches exit 0. Source: units/platform/computed-semver/verify-cage-engine.sh:12-15.
