Five siblings carry the identical whole-script form (`grep -n "command -v kyverno" -A4 verify-*.sh`): verify-comparison-window.sh:11-14, verify-gate.sh:11-14, verify-rederive-bumps.sh:9-12, verify-generator-standing-check.sh:18-21, verify-corpus-generator.sh:34-37 (step 3). verify-witness-set.sh:68-71 prints the SKIP text for step 5 and falls through to PASS. Two more shapes: verify/proportionality/verify-proportionality.sh and verify/provenance/verify-provenance.sh use `set -euo pipefail` with no exit-3 path at all — run-21 captures show '5. live dry-run tail skipped (no cluster with Kyverno CRDs reachable)' → PASS, and provenance's Rekor (§3) and SPIRE (§4) sections degrading to printed notes → 'PASS: … Provenance for every actor, end to end.'
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5 bullet 2 ("exactly three outcomes") and §3 principle 6 ("a green that could not look is a red")
OWNER: new ticket 55 (partial, unmerged) / no ticket for the remaining seven — Verified via direct search of .scratch/ecosystem/issues/, REVIEW-2026-08-31.md, and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md. Ticket 55 (status: prepared, not yet merged — enact_guard blocked the push, "waiting on the owner") fixes exactly one script: verify-render-version-tree's exit-0-on-missing-kyverno, explicitly named in its Answer as "the check-passes-on-absence class the 2026-08-25 incident came from." REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's own M1-M18 list and "Refuted claims" list name only that same one instance (line 87: "Silent-PASS-on-missing-kyverno in render-version-tree"). No ticket, ADR, GAPS row, or map.md scope entry names the six computed-semver siblings (verify-cage-engine.sh, verify-comparison-window.sh, verify-gate.sh, verify-rederive-bumps.sh, verify-generator-standing-check.sh, verify-corpus-generator.sh step 3), verify-witness-set.sh, verify-proportionality.sh, or verify-provenance.sh. Ticket 71 (open) is a different question — which kyverno engine *versions* the estate supports — not the skip-then-exit-0 instrument defect. So the finding's own "Claimed ownership: Partially" line is correct and this is new/unowned for 7 of the 8 scripts, and even the 1 nominally-owned fix is not yet live on the truth surface (ticket 55 unmerged).
FIT IMPACT: The instrument's single most important contract is that it never converts absence into a pass. It does, in at least seven places. Run 21's six computed-semver PASSes happen to be real (kyverno 1.18.2 was installed), so the defect is latent today — but a toolchain change of exactly the kind that already happened once (the 1.19.0 accident) would turn six greens into silent nothings with no signal.
REMEDY: Change `exit 0` to `exit 3` in the six computed-semver scripts and make witness-set step 5 / corpus-generator step 3 propagate a script-level SKIP; add an exit-3 path to verify-proportionality and verify-provenance for their live tails. Ten one-line changes; the pattern is already written correctly in distribution/verify-render-version-tree.sh, which cites the 2026-08-25 incident this class came from.

## [major] TS-M1 The deck that renders the demonstration is built from a local run at a superseded commit, so §4's story is shown to viewers as six SKIPs against a run that graded five PASS — and the red that says so is the one red no estate change can clear
(original title: verify-demo is structurally red on every scheduled run, and its own premise names a workflow step that does not exist)
CLAIM: verify-demo has FAILed on the last eight graded scheduled runs (13, 15-21; run 14 never reached the gate), and no hub-side mechanism keeps it green: talk/deck.md is outside truth.yml:38's OBSERVATION_LANE and the cage (truth.yml:126-144) fails the run on anything outside it, and no workflow builds the deck (`grep -rn "build_deck\|deck" .github/workflows/` → nothing; truth.yml's only gate step is `bash talk/verify-all.sh`) — contradicting talk/verify-demo.sh:31 "The committed deck is built by the scheduled workflow AFTER verify-all.sh finishes", and :44 "CI renders it on the scheduled run" (DECK_RENDER appears nowhere under .github/). It is not red by construction: runs 10-12 PASSed, because until run 12 the committed e2e captures were all-SKIP and matched the locally built all-SKIP deck; run 12 was the first run whose captures moved. Nor is it unpassable: verify/demo sorts before verify/e2e in the gate glob, so on the clock it grades the committed deck against the previous recorded run's captures, and a plain local `python3 talk/build_deck.py` over origin/main's captures reproduces the clock's beats exactly (PASS PASS PASS FAIL PASS PASS PASS) — committing that file would make the next run green. It would then go red again the run after: a deck built from run-20 captures passes --check against run-20 captures and fails against run-21 captures on five step-4 figures, because step 4's capture embeds a per-run cluster id and an OpenSSL error pointer. So this red is a hub instrument fault, unclearable by any estate change and re-appearing within one run of any manual rebuild, owned by open ticket 66 (M18); it is one of the seven reds in TRUTH run 21 (exactly seven committed captures end in FAIL). The shipped deck is consequently understated — beats 1-6 all render SKIP with laptop reasons where the clock observes five PASS and one FAIL — though deck.md:27's "records no run of the truth surface at this commit" is accurate as generated, since the clock writes each TRUTH line at the parent of the commit that carries it.
EVIDENCE: Grade history, read from the capture at each `truth: record run N` commit (`git show <sha>:talk/captures/verify_demo_verify-demo.out | tail -1`): runs 10, 11, 12 PASS; runs 13, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20, 21 all `FAIL: talk/deck.md has been hand edited or is stale; run python3 talk/build_deck.py`. The run-21 capture shows the committed deck's beats 1-6 all `status=SKIP` against a rebuild of `PASS,PASS,PASS,FAIL,PASS,PASS`. Cause 1: .github/workflows/truth.yml:38 `OBSERVATION_LANE: "talk/truth.log drift/samples.jsonl talk/captures observations"` — deck.md is not in it and the cage step fails the run on anything outside. Cause 2: `grep -rn "build_deck\|deck" .github/workflows/` → no matches; there are exactly two workflows (truth.yml, twin.yml) and neither builds the deck. Yet verify/demo/verify-demo.sh:33-36 states "The committed deck is built by the scheduled workflow AFTER verify-all.sh finishes". Cause 3: run 21's commit a209496 changed 12 capture files, so any hand-rebuilt deck goes stale within one run.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5 (the surface's reds must be real and finishable) and §4 (the demonstration the deck renders)
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 66 (open); REVIEW-2026-08-31 M18 — Ticket 66 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/66*, Status: open) is exactly this finding: quotes the same premise ("scheduled workflow rebuilds and commits the deck" is false), same cause (truth.yml has no deck step; cage refuses talk/deck.md), same consequence (reds every scheduled run whose grades moved). It explicitly closes REVIEW-2026-08-31 finding M18, which matches TS-M1 verbatim in mechanism. Ticket is open, not resolved, so it genuinely owns this rather than falsely claiming a fix — no reversal needed. TS-M1 adds fresh evidence (runs 13-21 grade history, run-21 deck showing all-SKIP beats vs rebuild PASS/PASS/PASS/FAIL/PASS/PASS) but the finding and root cause are the ticket's own stated scope, not a new discovery.
FIT IMPACT: One of the seven reds is permanently unfixable by any estate change, so fail=0 is unreachable for an instrument reason. It also means the shipped talk/deck.md is stale: line 27 currently reads 'talk/truth.log records no run of the truth surface at this commit' and every beat shows SKIP, so the deck a viewer sees is pessimistically wrong rather than optimistically wrong.
REMEDY: Either add a deck-build step to truth.yml after the gate and add talk/deck.md to OBSERVATION_LANE (a declaration the clock may write, which is a real ADR-0024 decision, not a config tweak), or make the two scheduled-only checks compare the committed deck against the captures at ITS OWN commit rather than this run's. Correct verify-demo.sh:33-36's header either way — it currently describes machinery that does not exist.

## [major] TS-M2 Principle 5 is untrue today — feeds do not refresh, the twin does not sweep, proposals do not open — and the one citable surface records it as a single SKIP
(original title: verify-schedules can never PASS, its ruleset check prints nothing at all, and six real clocks are failing unseen)
CLAIM: verify-schedules can never PASS inside the citable run, its server-side-ruleset half emits no line at all, and five real clocks are red today unseen by it.

Never PASS, for two independent reasons: (a) 13 distinct liveness questions SKIP on every CI run because the gate step carries no GitHub credential — .github/workflows/truth.yml:65-66 checks out `persist-credentials: false`, :92-98 gives the gate only `{VERIFY_TIMEOUT: '900'}`, and GH_TOKEN reaches only the cage step at :109-110, so `gh auth status` fails and schedules.py:507-512 sets live=False; (b) three unconditional D2 SKIPs (ico/nist/platform fetch.yml carry no `gh pr create`; schedules.py:538-546) would force exit 3 even with a working token. Any SKIP returns 3 (schedules.py:658-662) and the wrapper exits 3 (verify-schedules.sh:41-50), landing on the truth surface as exactly one SKIP (talk/verify-all.sh:61-64) inside run 21's skip=18. The run-21 capture carries 14 "GitHub unreachable" lines but only 13 distinct clocks: verify-schedules.sh:47 echoes the first SKIP back as its own summary.

The vanishing check: schedules.py:561 is `if live and os.path.isdir(...)`, so with live=False none of its four branches (:563-574) can emit. All eight unit clones carry `.github/rulesets/observation-lane.json`, so eight server-side-cage questions produce no line at all — `grep -c -i ruleset` on the capture is 0. That is a fourth outcome against NORTH-STAR.md:57 ("exactly three outcomes … could-not-look prints as SKIP with the reason"), and the wrapper's one summary SKIP names a liveness question, never a ruleset, so nothing on the truth surface says these were not asked.

The consequence, read live 2026-09-02 with read-only `gh run list --event schedule`: of the 13 clocks the checker questions, 5 are red on their most recent scheduled run — feeds/fetch 07:54 failure, insurer/fetch 09:45 failure, driftwood/twin-sweep 12:04 failure, tuppence/propose-tier 12:59 failure, ludlow/propose-tier 13:51 failure. hub/truth is also failure but documented-exempt (schedules.py:70-72, :651). Two further clocks the checker does not question, tuppence/drift-sample and ludlow/drift-sample, were red on 2026-09-01 and green on 2026-09-02 — the likely source of the "six". The reds are estate faults, not instrument faults: feeds/fetch dies in its own observation cage on stray `__pycache__/*.pyc`; ludlow/propose-tier dies in checkout fetching policy-as-versioned-feeds/feeds at ref `ecosystem/thin-slice`, which no longer exists. schedules.py is the only script anywhere in the hub or the eight units that reads workflow run history, so nothing else could have caught them.

Severity, ambition ref (NORTH-STAR §3 principle 5 and §5 bullet 2) and ownership (ticket 56 open; REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:59 M6) are all as the auditor stated.
EVIDENCE: verify/schedules/verify-schedules.sh:38-48: any per-clock SKIP makes schedules.py return 3 and the wrapper exits 3. The run-21 capture (talk/captures/verify_schedules_verify-schedules.out) carries 14 lines of the form "SKIP: <unit>/<workflow>: GitHub unreachable (Command ['gh','auth','status'] returned non-zero exit status 1.) -- cannot look at whether this clock ran inside its period". The ruleset check at verify/schedules/schedules.py:561 is guarded `if live and os.path.isdir(...)`, so with live=false it prints nothing: `grep -c -i ruleset talk/captures/verify_schedules_verify-schedules.out` → 0 — a fourth outcome, neither PASS, FAIL nor SKIP. Independently verified with read-only gh today: `gh run list --repo policy-as-versioned-feeds/feeds --event schedule --limit 2` → 2026-09-02T07:54 failure fetch, 2026-09-01T08:37 failure fetch; insurer/insurer → 2026-09-02T09:45 failure fetch, 2026-09-01T10:16 failure fetch; driftwood → 2026-09-02T12:04 failure twin-sweep; tuppence → 2026-09-02T12:59 failure propose-tier, 2026-09-01T13:32 failure drift-sample; ludlow → 2026-09-01T14:17 failure propose-tier, 2026-09-01T14:07 failure drift-sample.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 5 ("Intelligence re-prices on a clock"), §5 bullet 2 (three outcomes), §5 as the only citable source
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket-56 — Ticket 56 (open, .scratch/ecosystem/issues/56-the-citable-run-can-see-whether-the-clocks-ran.md) owns this exact defect: verify-schedules' live half structurally SKIPs every CI run because the gate step carries no GitHub credential (a recorded security decision) — precisely TS-M2's root cause. Ticket cites REVIEW-2026-08-31 M6 (line 59: "verify-schedules' live half can never look from inside the citable run... Owning ticket: none → new ticket 56") and is still open (not resolved-but-still-true). TS-M2's additional evidence (the vanishing ruleset check emitting zero lines under live=false, and the six now-actually-failing clocks confirmed via gh run list) is new corroborating/aggravating detail, not a distinct unowned defect — ticket 56's scope ("grade clock health... Done = 'ran inside its period' grades for real on a scheduled run without exposing a credential") already covers fixing exactly this. No REGRILL-ANSWERS entry moots it; not in REVIEW-2026-08-31's Refuted claims list.
FIT IMPACT: Principle 5 is materially broken today — six clocks red — and the instrument that exists to notice cannot. The consequence has now landed, which it had not when M6 was written. The vanishing ruleset check is separately a §5 bullet-2 violation: a check that is neither run nor reported.
REMEDY: Either (a) move verify-schedules into a second job in truth.yml that carries a narrowly-scoped cross-org read token and report its verdict into the same TRUTH line, or (b) have each unit's own clock publish a liveness observation into its own observation lane which the hub then reads offline. Independently and cheaply: change schedules.py:561 so the ruleset question emits a SKIP with its reason when live is false, instead of nothing.

## [major] TS-M4 The honesty instrument cannot tell a new break from a standing one, and the tracker has already gone false about the estate (NORTH-STAR §5, principle 6)
(original title: Neither of §5's last two bullets exists: no fall blocks anything, and ticket Status is not derived from a check)
CLAIM: Of §5's last two bullets, one and a half are unbuilt. Bullet 5's first sentence IS built — `talk/truth.log` on origin/main holds 18 dated, run-numbered, SHA-stamped lines, and run 21 was recorded even though the run concluded `failure`. Its second sentence, "A fall is a blocking event", is unbuilt: `grep -rn "truth.log" talk/ verify/ .github/` returns 21 hits in 10 files, all of them writers (`.github/workflows/truth.yml:99-102`), OBSERVATION_LANE allow-list declarations, prose, or `talk/build_deck.py`, which selects only the line matching this commit (`build_deck.py:137-154`) and asserts only that a quoted line exists and is same-commit (`:391-400`). No code anywhere in the hub or in the eight fresh unit clones reads two TRUTH lines. The only blocking step, `truth.yml:164-166`, fires on the gate's own exit, and 16 of 21 recorded runs fail there (20 of 21 conclude failure; runs 1 and 14 died at the CLI install, 2 and 3 at the record step, run 4 is the sole success) — so the real fall from run 17 (`pass=59 fail=1`, truth.log line 14) to run 18 (`pass=57 fail=3`, line 15) was indistinguishable from the standing red. Note that a fall-detector does exist for a different number: `verify/twin-evals/verify-twin-evals.sh:62-76,143` grades a twin skill score that fell against its last recorded value as FAIL; the correct statement is that no fall in the TRUTH number blocks anything.

Bullet 4 is entirely unbuilt: `grep -rn "ecosystem/issues"` across every `.py`/`.sh`/`.yml` in the hub returns nothing — no tool reads the 74 ecosystem tickets, and the nearest analogue (`twin/invariants/harness.py:93-103,153-172`) runs the other direction on a different tracker. A live instance: ticket 55 (`.scratch/ecosystem/issues/55-...md:4,29`) still reads `Status: prepared` and "They are NOT on the platform repo", while its branch was merged to platform `main` as `46cd775` on 2026-09-01 05:09:41 +0100 — the exact platform SHA in run 21's TRUTH line and in every line since run 16, so the ticket has been stale for ~29 hours across six recorded runs. The fixes are visibly live (`computed-semver/verify-corpus-generator.sh:40`; the run-21 capture `talk/captures/.estate-clone_platform_verify-source-verification.out:13-14` now reads "it ACCEPTS the real signed tag" / "VERIFIED"). `.scratch/ecosystem/map.md:97-98` carries the same stale claim. Ticket 59 (`Status: open`) owns both bullets and confirms neither mechanism is built; its (b) as written targets *resolved* tickets only, so as specced it would not have caught this `prepared` one.
EVIDENCE: `grep -rn "truth.log" talk/ verify/ .github/` returns only writers (truth.yml:5,38 and the record step) and talk/build_deck.py, which quotes the newest line. No consumer compares two lines. Empirically: run 17 → 18 fell from pass=59 fail=1 to pass=57 fail=3 (talk/truth.log lines 14-15) and both runs' workflow conclusion is `failure` for the same reason as every other run — the signal is saturated (17 of 21 CI runs fail at the step 'fail if the gate failed'). For derived Status: `git -C units/platform log --oneline -2` → `46cd775 Merge pull request #8 from policy-as-versioned-platform/ticket-55-instrument-faults` / `04184df Every red on the clock is real, explained, and finishable (ticket 55)`; 46cd775 is the platform SHA in run 21's TRUTH line, and the fixes are visibly live (verify-source-verification's capture now reads 'it ACCEPTS the real signed tag'; verify-corpus-generator.sh:40 now says 'A glob, not `ls | head -1`'). Yet .scratch/ecosystem/issues/55-every-red-on-the-clock-is-real-explained-and-finishable.md still reads `Status: prepared` and 'They are NOT on the platform repo.'
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5 bullets 4 and 5
OWNER: duplicate-of-0831-finding-still-open ticket 59 (open); REVIEW-2026-08-31.md M14 — Ticket 59 ("Build the two unbuilt §5 bullets: a fall blocks, Status is derived") is open and charts precisely this gap — closing REVIEW-2026-08-31's M14 (fall-is-blocking and derived-Status). Its Question text matches TS-M4's claim near-verbatim: no consumer diffs two TRUTH lines, and Status lines aren't derived from a named check. Ticket 55 (Status: prepared) is a separate, related-but-different ticket about instrument faults (corpus SIGPIPE, publisher-gate timeout, source-verification chain build) — its Answer's live-instance detail (46cd775 merged to platform main while ticket 55 still reads Status: prepared, Answer still says fixes aren't on platform) is a fresh, still-true empirical observation not mentioned in ticket 55, ticket 59, REVIEW-2026-08-31, or REGRILL-ANSWERS.md. So: the ambition-level claim (no fall-blocking, no derived Status) is a duplicate of ticket 59/M14; the concrete ticket-55-tracker-drift instance is new and not owned by any open ticket — worth flagging separately as evidence supporting ticket 59's urgency, but the core finding as stated is already charted.
FIT IMPACT: Two fifths of §5 is documentation, not build. Without fall-blocking, a regression is indistinguishable from the standing reds; without derived Status, the tracker drifts from reality within days, and I found a live instance today.
REMEDY: Build ticket 59 as written: (a) truth.yml diffs the new TRUTH line against the previous and raises a distinct failure on any fall in pass or rise in fail, with a committed-reason escape hatch mirroring the exclusions file; (b) map each resolved ticket to its named gate check and flag Status from the scheduled run. Immediately and for free: correct ticket 55's Status and Answer, since its work is merged and proven.

## [major] TS-M6 The only citable measure of "what works" cannot tell a loosely coupled eco-system apart from one party testing itself: 20 of 57 passes cross a party boundary, and the composition is nowhere labelled
(original title: Half the headline pass count is the platform grading its own fixtures; only 20 of 57 passes rest on a cross-party or live fact)
CLAIM: Of the 57 passes on TRUTH run 21, platform owns 29 (platform's rows are 29 PASS / 15 SKIP / 1 EXCLUDED, 45 of the 84 discovered scripts) and 24 of those 29 are the platform grading its own modules against its own fixtures — the remaining five being three cross-party observations (compose/verify-composition against real driftwood, oscal/verify-claims against nist's real catalogue, distribution/verify-infra-declaration against driftwood/ludlow/tuppence's composed policies) and two simulations (verify-cut-release-tags, oscal/verify-upflow). On a per-script classification — where a pass counts as an eco-system observation only if its verdict turns on an artefact published by a party other than the script's owner or on live external state — roughly 17 to 20 of the 57 passes qualify (the auditor counted 20; an independent re-classification under the same rule lands at 17, so the shortfall is if anything understated). Five passes are simulations on synthetic material, each self-declared in its own capture, and one (verify/e2e/step7) is a meta-check on the other steps' verdicts. The rest are a party grading its own code, fixtures or artefacts. None of this means a script is lying — they are real regression tests, several excellent — but a reader taking "pass=57 of total=84" as "68% of the eco-system works" is misled by the framing, especially as the four questions NORTH-STAR §4 poses grade SKIP (clocks), FAIL on a day-old replay (set in force), SKIP (version coexistence) and PASS-on-synthetic (step 3, which ticket 74 records has never happened for real). The composition of the number is analysed in no ticket, in the map, or in the previous review.
EVIDENCE: Grades by owning repo, re-derived from the run-21 log: platform 29 PASS / 15 SKIP / 1 EXCLUDED (45 of 84 rows); hub 13 PASS / 1 SKIP / 2 FAIL; driftwood 1/0/3; feeds 3; ico 3 (+1 excluded); insurer 1 PASS 1 SKIP; ludlow 2 PASS 1 FAIL; nist 3; tuppence 2 PASS 1 SKIP 1 FAIL. Classification is per-script, from reading all 82 run-21 captures. Self-proofs include all 10 computed-semver scripts, fair, tcor, honesty, wardley, wargamer, risk, party-artefact, break-glass, the four cert-identity-regexp scripts and twin-evals. Simulations, each self-declared in its own capture: platform/verify-cut-release-tags (throwaway repos under /tmp/tmp.lnaTmCv8ez), ludlow/verify-adopter-gate (throwaway platform+ludlow repos), platform/oscal/verify-upflow (self-generated PolicyReports), verify/e2e/step3 ('a SYNTHETIC residual … not driftwood's real priced position'), verify/provenance (a narrative chain over committed fixtures). The 20 genuine eco-system observations include verify/feed-contract (22 real cross-party pins vs real remotes), verify/e2e/step6 (7 real Fulcio certs + Rekor), platform/compose/verify-composition (real driftwood vs 5 real pinned parents), verify/renovate (driftwood's real merge record for PR #20) and ico/verify-penalty-feed §8 (real git ls-remote against nist).
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §1 ("the eco-system operating") and §5 as the only citable source for what works
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (grep for self-proof/grading its own/composition/headline/pass=57/68%/synthetic/meta-check terms; also opened 03-the-truth-surface.md and 67-the-record-matches-the-surface.md), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (both its confirmed-findings section and its "Refuted claims" list), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, and NORTH-STAR/map Out-of-scope. Nothing addresses a per-script composition breakdown of what the 57 passes actually rest on (self-proof vs simulation vs cross-party/live fact). The closest adjacent item is ticket 67(d), which only requires that any pass/fail figure map.md quotes exist as a truth.log line (a citation-accuracy check) — it does not touch the finding's claim that the headline number's *composition* is misleading even when every cited figure is accurate. REVIEW-2026-08-31's refuted-claims list covers different claims entirely (chain never ran, no clock fired, ticket 53 blocker, etc.) — none about pass-count composition. No ticket, ADR, or REGRILL answer has ever charted or decided this. Treat as new.
FIT IMPACT: A reader taking pass=57 of total=84 as '68% of the eco-system works' is misled by the framing rather than by any script lying. Meanwhile the four questions NORTH-STAR actually poses are answered SKIP (clocks), FAIL on a day-old replay (set in force), SKIP (version coexistence) and PASS-on-synthetic (step 3, which ticket 74 says has never happened for real).
REMEDY: Tag each script in a committed manifest with its class (estate-observation / self-proof / simulation) and emit the split in the TRUTH line, e.g. `pass=57 [observed=20 self=31 simulated=5 meta=1]`. The classification is cheap because most scripts already say which they are in their own headers.

## [major] TS-M8 Step 5 of the demonstration can grade green off artefacts two other checks in the same run prove wrong: the e2e harness never consults the owning repo's verdict on the artefact it names
(original title: verify-e2e-step5 PASSes on file presence while two other scripts prove the same artefact wrong in the same run)
CLAIM: In TRUTH run 21, E2E step 5 reports PASS and prints `ok driftwood/twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json (the feed the estate consumes)` while, in the same run, `.estate-clone/driftwood/verify-twin-overlay.sh` and `.estate-clone/driftwood/twin/verify-twin-scenarios.sh` both FAIL because that exact file is not what the overlay renders (its `derived_from` still names threat-register version 1; party.yaml has pinned v2 since 2026-08-28, and signals.yaml still carries only a v1 row). Step 5's `ok` on that path is a bare `[ -e ]` presence test (verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step5-twin-forecasts.sh:16-20, 26); step 5 as a whole is not presence-only — it also runs verify/twin-evals/verify-twin-evals.sh and grades on its exit code (lines 55-62) — but that harness checks skill scores, beats, determinism and the hub-side pin→signal table, never whether driftwood's feed matches its overlay, so nothing in step 5 can see the defect. The consequence is not yet on the committed deck (talk/deck.md still shows beat 5 as SKIP, built before run 21 and itself one of the seven reds), but a rebuild renders it green: run 21's verify-demo capture prints the rebuild diff `> beat step=5 status=PASS`. The underlying estate defect is open ticket 72; the instrument shortfall was already recorded as the REVIEW-2026-08-31 minor "step 5 grades file presence not the scheduled forecast" and is closed by open ticket 64 (not 66). The run's aggregate TRUTH line is red (fail=7) with both reds named, so what reports green over a fact the run contradicts is the step-5 beat, not the surface headline.
EVIDENCE: verify_e2e_verify-e2e-step5-twin-forecasts.out: 'ok driftwood/twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json (the feed the estate consumes)' … 'PASS: the twin's overlay, feed, 6 scenarios and signal lookup are all present, and its own evals scored them'. Same run: .estate-clone_driftwood_verify-twin-overlay.out → 'FAIL: emit-forward-intel.py --check: FAIL: twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json is not what the overlay renders' / 'TOTAL: could not read the feed; nothing further was observed'; .estate-clone_driftwood_twin_verify-twin-scenarios.out → 'FAIL: the overlay with its six scenarios still loads and re-renders the feed byte-identically: FAIL: twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json is not what the overlay renders' plus a second fail on the threat-register v2 pin having no signal row.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §4 step 5 (the twin forecasts) and §5 (the surface must not report a green over a known-false fact)
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket-64 (open, misattributed as 66 in the finding) + ticket-72 (open) — Finding's own "Claimed ownership" line is wrong: it says the minor was folded into ticket 66, but ticket 66 (open) is entirely about verify-demo's on-clock deck-check premise and never mentions verify-e2e-step5 or presence-vs-content grading. The actual owner, per both REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:87 ("step 5 grades file presence not the scheduled forecast ... folded into tickets 54, 55, 59, 64, 66, 67") and ticket 64's own Notes ("Closes review findings: ... minor step-5-presence-only"), is ticket 64 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/64-the-twin-is-three-adopters-tagged-and-signed.md), Status: open, whose body explicitly says "make the gate name the absence until then (verify-e2e-step5 hardcodes driftwood today)" and "harden step 5 to assert a dated scheduled sweep observation." The underlying content-mismatch defect that produces the two FAILs (overlay/scenarios vs the stale threat-register v1/v2 pin) is separately owned by ticket 72 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/72-a-feed-bump-re-renders-the-twin-s-derived-artefacts.md), Status: open. Neither ticket is resolved, so the finding's underlying gap is real and still open — but ownership should be re-pointed from ticket 66 to ticket 64 (presence-check hardening) plus ticket 72 (content-correctness fallout), not left citing 66.
FIT IMPACT: The demo deck's beat 5 renders green over a provably broken artefact. This is the one place in the run where the surface actively reports something the run itself contradicts.
REMEDY: Make step 5 consume the twin-overlay and twin-scenarios verdicts (the same way step 7 reads other steps' captures) so a red on the artefact reds the beat, instead of asserting presence.

MINORS:
- TS-m1 Two verify*.sh are neither run nor excluded — the §5 glob is scoped to two directories, not "every": NORTH-STAR §5 bullet 1 says the surface "discovers every verify*.sh by glob and fails if any is neither run nor listed in a committed exclusions file with a reason", but the glob covers only .estate-clone and verify/, leaving two committed scripts unrun and unexcluded.
- TS-m2 clone-estate.sh's stated pinning precondition has fired and was not acted on; the gate still clones unpinned default branches: clone-estate.sh says it clones default branches only because no signed tag exists yet and should be pinned once one lands; every one of the eight units now carries signed tags, and the gate still grades whatever is on each unit's main at clone time.
- TS-m3 verify-provenance misreports which signing tools are present on the runner: verify/provenance/verify-provenance.sh prints "no rekor-cli/cosign to query the log" while cosign 3.1.3 is installed on the runner, because the detection tests rekor-cli only.
- TS-m4 Four TRUTH lines are missing and nothing detects the gaps: talk/truth.log has no line for runs 1, 2, 3 or 14; the record is discontinuous and nothing in the estate notices a day with no number.
- TS-m5 Unchecked checks are printed as NOTE and graded green: The party-artefact checker converts an unresolvable declared version pin into a NOTE and exits 0, so insurer/verify-insurer-party.sh reports PASS with four of its checks not run.
- TS-m6 map.md still publishes a health claim the truth surface never recorded, with a correction that is itself stale: The live wayfinder map still states "65 pass, 0 fail, 16 could-not-look of 83 … Nothing is red" as body text, and the correction beneath it cites run 13 — two days and eight runs old.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- What is the truth surface FOR — a daily regression alarm for you, or the citable evidence a skeptical audience is shown? The two want opposite denominators and opposite failure modes. | WHY: An alarm wants a stable, reachable ceiling and a loud diff between runs; it does not care that half the checks are the platform grading itself. Evidence for an audience wants the opposite: it must foreground the ~20 cross-party observations and say plainly that 11 scripts cannot look on this runner. Today the single number tries to be both and misleads as either. | CHANGES: If it is an alarm: build ticket 59's fall-checker, publish a ceiling line, and stop treating fail=0 as the done bar. If it is evidence: split the TRUTH line into observed / self-proved / simulated (TS-M6), and lead with the 20.
- Is a persistent Kubernetes cluster in scope for the daily clock, or never? Eleven scripts hard-code a kind cluster named `driftwood` that truth.yml has never created and contains no code to create. | WHY: Those eleven can only ever be SKIP or FAIL on the clock, which is 13% of the discovered set permanently unpassable. Their offline halves do real work and are being thrown away in the tally. | CHANGES: If in scope: truth.yml gains a KinD bring-up (minutes of runtime) and the ceiling rises to ~81. If never: the eleven should move to a separately-graded live suite invoked by talk/up.sh, and the daily number gets a reachable ceiling of 70 that a reader can hold you to.
- Is "at least three policy versions coexisting" still a required property of this estate, or did the retirement to a single 4.0.0 line supersede it? | WHY: Five checks (coexistence, retirement, shift-left, conditional, publisher-gate part C) exist only to observe it and are all dark. The talk makes it the crux; the mea culpa does not revise it; ADR/versions.yaml retired down to one with good, live-observed reasons. | CHANGES: If required: re-declaring a second and third line turns five SKIPs into real observations and gives the thesis's headline claim green evidence for the first time. If superseded: those five need an exclusion entry or a stated ceiling reason, and CONTEXT.md:153-155 ("the crux of the original implementation") needs revising.
- Should the hub's clock move to fire AFTER the adopters' drift-sample lanes, so step 4 grades today's cluster rather than yesterday's? | WHY: Hub cron is 05:47; driftwood 06:20, tuppence 08:22, ludlow 09:16. The hub is deterministically first, so the three reconcile checks and step 4 grade a recording that is always at least a day old. Run 21 (10:11Z) graded samples from 2026-09-01T21:0x while driftwood's own 09-02 sample succeeded at 11:20Z. | CHANGES: A one-line cron change collapses the feedback loop on tickets 72/73/74 from a day to hours, and makes a fix landing in the morning visible the same evening. If you prefer the current ordering, the deck and the tickets should say the step-4 verdict is a day-old replay by design.
- What counts as "green"? fail=0 alone, or fail=0 AND every SKIP either owned by a ticket or listed with a reason? | WHY: There has never been a fail=0 line in 18 recorded runs, and given TS-C2 there cannot be one that also means much: 12 scripts can only be SKIP. Without a definition, "the truth surface is green" has no test, and process rule 4 ("done is the truth surface, never the demo") has no terminating condition. | CHANGES: It determines whether TS-C2's ceiling line is cosmetic or load-bearing, and whether the SKIP list needs the same reason-or-fail discipline the exclusions file already has. It also settles what ticket 59's fall-checker should block.
- Should a unit repo be able to move one of its own checks from red to green between two TRUTH lines? clone-estate.sh clones unpinned default branches, and its own comment says to pin once signed tags exist — which they now all do. | WHY: For a daily health read, grading main is right. For a citable claim, it means the observed thing changed without a reviewed release, and the only record is a short SHA. All eight units now carry signed tags, so the file's stated precondition has fired and was not acted on. | CHANGES: Pinning to signed tags makes the number reproducible and matches what Flux actually runs, at the cost of the surface lagging merged fixes by a release. Not pinning is defensible but the stale comment in clone-estate.sh:35-38 must go, replaced by a stated decision.

# pound-engine

VERDICT: Against what NORTH-STAR.md:32 actually asks — "one £ currency, proportionate to the org … so a pay rise, a hardening control, a cage tier, an insurance transfer and a strategic play are comparable" — the engine is close to fit and is the greenest surface in the estate: every £ verify script (fair-tail, tcor, risk-tuned, composition, pound-seam, honesty, penalty-feed, insurer-party, party) PASSes on TRUTH run 21, and none of run 21's seven FAILs is in this dimension. I reproduced three published numbers exactly by hand from signed artefacts (driftwood's uk-gdpr exposure £1,787,177.0751717847 to 2 ulp, the insurer's £113,403.30 premium exactly, the twin's lm triple byte-for-byte). The perspective/currency partition is watertight and enforced in code, not documentation; the hole breakdown is a strict partition refused unless the weights sum to 1.0; the multi-source correlation model in fair.py is actuarially correct and proved by its own selfcheck. Where it is not fit is that the estate publishes these figures with no qualifier, and a second party has priced a real insurance layer off them. On the estate's own numbers and its own engine, the expected loss to the 3m-xs-40k layer the insurer wrote for driftwood is £2,470,722 against a £113,403 premium — an implied loss ratio of 2,179% — so driftwood's signed exposure and the insurer's signed quote cannot both be believed. Underneath that, the frequency that annualises everything is an editorial constant asserting ~2.17 ICO monetary penalties a year against one £86m retailer and cannot draw a loss-free year at all; the magnitudes rest on n=2 published fines rescaled by a ratio that is neither the decided rule (ticket 07 §1's min()) nor the statute (Art 83(4)'s "whichever is higher"); one loss-magnitude table is a platform-held fixture of exactly the class ADR-0021 retired; and the estate's own back-test instrument, which runs and reports "under-prices, 40% VaR95 exceedance" for driftwood and "over-prices, ratio 0.127" for ludlow, is wired to nothing and grades a different number from the one that gets published. What would make it fit is small and mostly editorial: state on the artefact what the number is (an ordinal, auditable, traceable comparison instrument, not an expected annual loss), publish the insurer's implied loss ratio against the exposure it prices and let it be red, get size onto tuppence and ludlow so "proportionate to the org" is true of more than one adopter of three, aggregate the retained residuals against the one band instead of spending it once per line, and wire the calibration verdict to something.

STRENGTHS:
- Three published numbers reproduce exactly by hand from signed inputs. driftwood's uk-gdpr exposure: I re-implemented pert/simulate from their docstrings (not by importing them) off units/ico/penalty-schema/v3/feed.json (rate 0.02, cap_gbp 8,700,000, examples 7,552,800 and 275,000) and units/driftwood/party.yaml size.turnover 86,000,000, and got 1787177.0751717845 against a published 1787177.0751717847. The insurer premium reproduces exactly: (3,704,381.737952101 - 1,072,306.2451030708 - 40,000) x 0.035 x 1.25 = 113,403.30. The twin's lm [192000.0, 384000.0, 800000.0] = 3,200,000 x [0.06, 0.12, 0.25] byte-for-byte.
- The perspective/currency partition is enforced in code, not by discipline. fair.py:67-93 (sum_prices) raises on any mix of perspective or currency; composition.py:1704-1711 refuses a forward-intel feed pricing another party's balance sheet; insurer/pricing/quote.py refuses a quote insuring another adopter, a premium booked on another sheet, and a layer across two currencies. Run-21 capture verify_pound-seam: 'driftwood/ludlow/tuppence: every list of amounts in the document is one perspective in one currency' and 'the estate's own summing helper refuses to add this quote's layer to its premium'.
- premium is structurally excluded from exposure. composition.py:1875-1879: EXPOSURE_KINDS = ('feed', 'twin'), with the reason stated -- 'folding it in would make the premium an input to the formula that computes it'. That is the right call, enforced by a tuple rather than by care.
- The hole breakdown is a strict partition, refused otherwise, and its keys are real. composition.py:1467-1472 raises Refused if published control_weights do not sum to 1.0 (abs_tol 1e-9); ico/verify-penalty-feed.sh:170-206 independently re-checks sum, duplicates, range and regime/violation-type existence. I confirmed all 18 weight ids across all four regimes resolve in nist's real 1,196-control OSCAL catalogue. Run-21: '4 priced hole(s) sum to its total 1,787,177.08 GBP'. No double counting exists within the priced regime.
- The multi-source correlation model is actuarially correct and proved. fair.py:106-131 draws one frequency per year and sums magnitudes WITHIN each event across obligation sources, rather than simulating each regime independently; the selfcheck at fair.py:300-310 constructs the naive independent sum and asserts the correlated combination's TVaR sits above it. This is the one place in the estate that reasons like an actuary and it is right.
- Two selection engines are cross-checked at the band boundary and a disagreement is a hard FAIL. verify/pound-seam/pound_seam.py:566-596 runs the adopter's own selection_policy.py and platform/graded/cage.py:select_tier over 60 constructed cases (each rung's exact band boundary +/- 1e-6, every rung tried as a floor). Run-21: 'driftwood: platform/graded/cage.py and driftwood's own selection-policy package pick the same rung in all 60 cases'. A rigged adopter policy cannot pass.
- Missing-instrument discipline is coherent and asymmetric in the right direction. A missing or stale size widens to the publisher's statutory cap and never refuses (composition.py:1477-1506); a publisher declaring no currency, or an FX rate absent for the date, refuses and emits nothing (composition.py:1548-1555, :1437-1446). Run-21 verifies both directions live: 'GBP->AUD on 2026-08-15 is 1.936, the rate the signed fx feed publishes' and 'a date the fx feed does not publish (2025-08-15) refuses as a missing instrument, and prices nothing'.
- Nothing in the pound path is red on the citable run. From `gh run view 33616685427 --log`: platform/fair/verify-fair-tail.sh PASS, platform/tcor/verify-tcor.sh PASS, platform/risk/verify-risk-tuned.sh PASS, platform/compose/verify-composition.sh PASS, verify/pound-seam PASS, platform/honesty/verify-honesty.sh PASS, platform/wargamer PASS, ico/verify-penalty-feed.sh PASS, insurer/verify-insurer-party.sh PASS, verify/party PASS. None of run 21's seven FAILs touches this dimension.
- The self-referential pricing cycle was found and cut, correctly. driftwood/twin/emit-forward-intel.py:230-239 records that the insurer's quote -- priced off the exposure whose largest line derives from this very feed -- used to be named in derived_from, 'and it made the premium and the forecast mutually self-supporting'. Removed, with the reasoning kept.
- The ponytails are honest, dated and name their own upgrade path: quote.py:31-38 on rate-on-line vs E[layer loss], composition.py:1894-1901 on sum-of-means vs an aggregate distribution, cage.py:71-79 on the table being 'the PLATFORM's, self-declared, evidenced by nothing but this comment'. Almost none of my criticism is 'this was hidden'.

## [major] PE-01 Principle 3's named comparable fails at its only instance: the estate's one insurance transfer is priced at 1/22 of the risk it takes, so the £ cannot yet be used to choose between a transfer, a control and a cage tier — and the gate reports it green
(original title: The insurer's premium is 1/22 of the expected loss on its own layer, on the estate's own numbers)
CLAIM: The insurer's premium for driftwood is 1/22.9 of the expected loss on the layer it writes, and this is readable straight off the quote's own published fields, not only from a simulation. `units/insurer/quote/driftwood/v1/feed.json` publishes `layer = 2,592,075.49` and `premium = 113,403.30`; premium/layer = 0.04375 = rate 0.035 × (1 + load 0.25) exactly (`quote.py:191-192`, `terms/driftwood.yaml:24-25`). Because `layer` is computed off `exposure.total` (`quote.py:189`), which is a sum of annualised expected losses — I reproduced all three driftwood exposure lines from `platform/fair/fair.py` to within 2e-10 of their signed amounts — the layer IS an expected-annual-loss quantity. Simulating the three lines year by year at n=200,000 and applying the terms' 60% pl-2/ra-3 exclusion and the 3,000,000-xs-40,000 aggregate layer gives E[loss in layer] = 2,469,572: an implied loss ratio of 2,178% and a burn cost of 82.3% of the aggregate limit.

Two of the estate's own pricers therefore contradict each other on the same concept: `platform/tcor/tcor.py:116-117` prices the transfer move as `premium = ale_warn * (1.0 + load)` with `DEFAULT_LOAD = 0.40` (`:61`) — expected loss × 1.4 — while the insurer repo charges expected loss × ≈0.044, a gap of ~32×. `NORTH-STAR.md:22` records the ambition's own shape of the insurer as the tcor one ("I cut it to a 40% load"). Worse, the disclosed upgrade path does not rescue it: `quote.py:35-37` says formula 2.0.0 will price `(1 + load) * E[loss in layer]`, which on these numbers is 1.25 × 2,469,572 = £3,086,965 — above the £3,000,000 aggregate limit, i.e. the cover is uneconomic at a technically adequate price.

Stated precisely, the two signed artefacts are each internally consistent £ quantities (I reproduced all three premiums to the penny), but they cannot both be defensible: either the £2.63m insured expected annual loss for an £86m-turnover retailer is far too large, or the £113k premium is far too small. £113k is a plausible market price for a 3m-xs-40k cyber aggregate on a firm that size, so the exposure is the more likely culprit — which ties this finding to the frequency defect rather than making it purely an insurance defect.

Ownership: the FORM is owned in two places, one of them signed — `quote.py:31-38` and `terms/driftwood.yaml:19-23` both say `layer * rate` is a rate-on-line and NOT the expected loss between attachment and limit, and the magnitude is arithmetically inferable from the disclosed rate. What is unowned is that the knob is off by a factor of 22 rather than being a refinement, that the named 2.0.0 path breaches the limit, and that tcor.py contradicts it. Nothing grades any of it: a grep for loss-ratio / burn-cost / adequacy language across all eight unit clones returns only the formula id and the two ponytail comments, and `verify-insurer-quote.sh` grades reproduction, attachment-equals-appetite, control ids, perspective separation and booking only. Ticket 37 covers sub-limits and aggregation, not magnitude.

Two corrections to the supporting evidence: (a) ludlow's layer is £2,121,187.98, not £4,000,000 — the £4m is its limit, which the layer does not reach; only tuppence's layer clamps at its limit (£5,000,000 for a £195,000 premium). Layer-to-premium ratios are driftwood 22.9, tuppence 25.6, ludlow 16.5. (b) On citable run 21 the driftwood reproduction check SKIPped because the quote is priced against a superseded exposure sha, though `exposure.total` is unchanged at both 6cf0671 and HEAD, so the arithmetic still lands.

Severity note: the harm is currently latent. `composition.py:1875-1879` deliberately keeps `premium` out of `EXPOSURE_KINDS`, no recovery is netted against the exposure, and no crossover consumes the insurer's quote — so the estate publishes the incoherence but has not yet made a decision on it.
EVIDENCE: Ran offline in units/platform/fair: `fair.simulate((1,2,4),(54367.816091954024,773782.5287356322,1791836.6896551724),n=200000,seed=42)` for the ico line, `((2,4,9),(1000,4000,9000))` for threat, `((2,4,9),(192000.0,384000.0,800000.0))` for twin; summed year by year; insured = 0.4*ico + threat + twin (terms/driftwood.yaml excludes pl-2+ra-3 = 60% of the ico line); layer = min(max(x-40000,0),3e6). Output: mean summed annual loss 3,696,478 (signed total 3,704,381.737952101 in units/driftwood/composed/HEADER.yaml); mean insured 2,627,358 (signed 2,632,075.49); E[loss in layer] 2,470,722; implied loss ratio 2179%; burn 82.4% of limit. Premium 113,403.30 from units/insurer/quote/driftwood/v1/feed.json and units/insurer/pricing/quote.py:192 `premium = round(layer * rate * (1+load), 2)` with rate 0.035 / load 0.25 from units/insurer/terms/driftwood.yaml:24-25. Same shape for the others: talk/captures/.estate-clone_insurer_verify-insurer-quote.out records tuppence's layer 'capped at the 5,000,000.00 limit' for a 195,000.00 premium and ludlow's at 4,000,000.00 for 128,862.17.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:32 principle 3 (an insurance transfer must be comparable in the one £); NORTH-STAR.md:22 (the insurer row, added after ratification)
OWNER: new ticket 14 (resolved, formula 1.0.0 ratified as interim) / ticket 37 (open, does not cover magnitude) — Ticket 14's Answer §3 ratifies formula 1.0.0 (rate-on-line over the layer) as the interim premium mechanism and quote.py:31-41 itself names the FORM defect and a 2.0.0 upgrade path (premium = (1+load)*E[loss in layer]) plus "until then the rate is the calibration knob" — so the shape of the gap is acknowledged. But nothing checked or ticketed the resulting MAGNITUDE: no ticket computes E[loss in layer] against the signed premium, no check grades it, and ticket 37 ("insurance round 2", open) scopes only sub-limits/aggregation/insurer-own-size/hole-vs-exclusion, not this. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's M1-M18 and Refuted-claims lists contain no loss-ratio/premium-magnitude item, and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md has no matching entry. Treat as a new finding; ticket 37 is the closest existing home but does not currently own it — recommend either widening 37's question or opening a new ticket for "formula 2.0.0 / magnitude check."
FIT IMPACT: The estate's one demonstration that a priced exposure can be transferred to a counterparty is internally incoherent by a factor of 22. Any reader who does the arithmetic concludes that at least one of the two signed artefacts is not a £. It undermines the comparability claim precisely at the seam NORTH-STAR names ('an insurance transfer').
REMEDY: Compute the implied loss ratio (E[layer loss] / premium) inside verify-insurer-quote.sh and print it on every run, red above some declared band. It costs one function and forces the owner to say which of the two numbers is the wrong one, rather than publishing both.

## [major] PE-02 The regulator publishes the fine but the platform invents how often it lands — so the estate's "annualised" £ is not an annual loss, and no participant owns the number that would make it one
(original title: The frequency that annualises every price is an editorial constant, and the model cannot draw a loss-free year)
CLAIM: driftwood's published 1,787,177 GBP uk-gdpr exposure is the annual-loss expectation of a fair.py simulation whose frequency is an editorial constant, DEFAULT_WARN_LEF = (1, 2, 4) at units/ico/schema/to_fair_scenario.py:38, asserting a mean 2.165 ICO monetary penalty events per year against one 86m-turnover retailer. The schema publishes no frequency and says so (:19-22); the whole headline is exactly linear in the constant (halving the triple halves the ALE to 0.500x; doubling gives 2.006x), and the ALE reproduces to the last digit offline (1787177.0751717845 vs evidence.json's old_price 1787177.0751717845). The schema can cite only five real UK GDPR monetary penalty notices in total across both tiers since 2019. Separately, fair.py:133 uses the drawn PERT rate directly as the year's event count with no Poisson/Bernoulli count layer, so there is no zero-inflation anywhere in the engine: for every composed prices[] entry that carries a frequency -- all seven have a lower bound of 1 or more -- P(a year with no loss) is exactly 0.0, where a Poisson count on the same rates would give 13.2% for lef=(1,2,4) and 2.1% for (2,4,9). (Two corrections to the original wording: the zero-free property comes from the triples' lower bound, not from round(), which actually creates zeros where lo=0; and it is not true of "every priced line" -- the deny states at (0,0,1) draw 97.2% loss-free years and break-glass driftwood-read at (0,1,2) draws 10.1%.) No ticket among the 74 owns getting a grounded frequency -- ticket 24 settled size, not frequency -- though the drift review did record the gap as "thin or stubbed" at .scratch/drift-review-2026-08-27/appendices/F-code-maps.md:475 without graduating it into GAPS.md or a ticket.
EVIDENCE: units/ico/schema/to_fair_scenario.py:38 `DEFAULT_WARN_LEF = (1, 2, 4)   # plausible regulatory-incident frequency, events/yr`; :19-22 'lef is NOT in the schema ... ponytail: a flat editorial per-regime warn-LEF'. units/platform/fair/fair.py:133 `freqs = [max(0, round(f)) for f in pert(*lef, n, rng)]`. My re-derivation of the 10,000 frequency draws for lef=(1,2,4) seed 42: {1: 1188, 2: 6055, 3: 2674, 4: 83} -- mean 2.1652/yr, minimum 1, zero years with no event. The schema itself (units/ico/penalty-schema/v3/feed.json) can cite only five real UK GDPR monetary penalty notices in total across both tiers since 2019.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:32 'Regulator penalties enter as published schemas applied to the adopter's own size' -- the schema publishes no frequency and the constant supplying it is not a published fact
OWNER: new none — No ticket, ADR, or prior review owns this. Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md for LEF/frequency/ponytail/DEFAULT_WARN_LEF: only hits are ticket 24 (size-beyond-turnover, resolved), ticket 08 (pound-seam, resolved), tickets 13/15/19/14/22 — all about loss-MAGNITUDE (lm) size-scaling or engine seams, none touch the loss-EVENT-FREQUENCY (lef) constant or fair.py's round()-induced zero-inflation loss. Ticket 24's Q1-Q5 scale the (lo,mode,hi) severity triple by turnover/data_subjects/relevant_revenue for HIPAA/PCI/FCA; it never revisits DEFAULT_WARN_LEF=(1,2,4) or the frequency draw at all — 24's own facts-found even quotes fair.py's shared-frequency-draw line without touching the frequency source. Ticket 08's Answer 1 says 'the twin has no frequency ... fair.py is wired to prices[]' but this is about which engine assigns frequency (twin vs estate), not whether the estate's own frequency constant is grounded in a schema or fact. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md has zero matches for LEF/frequency/rounding/zero-inflation/ponytail (grep returned nothing) — its C1/M1-M18 and Refuted-claims lists do not cover this. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md and GAPS.md likewise have zero matches. NORTH-STAR §32 ('Regulator penalties enter as published schemas applied to the adopter's own size') is about size-proportionality of magnitude, which ticket 24 already addresses — it does not speak to frequency grounding, and §6 out-of-scope list does not exclude this either. The to_fair_scenario.py docstring's own honesty ('lef is NOT in the schema... ponytail: a flat editorial per-regime warn-LEF') plus lef_basis labelling in composition.py shows the gap is disclosed but never ticketed as a defect to fix. Conclusion: PE-02 is a genuinely new, unowned finding — the rounding/zero-inflation half in particular (freqs = [max(0, round(f)) for f in pert(...)] eliminating P(no-loss-year)=0) appears nowhere in any prior document.
FIT IMPACT: Every headline £ in the estate is linear in this constant. It is the single largest source of error in the whole engine, it is not a modelling choice with a defensible range, and the rounding removes the zero-inflation that dominates a low-frequency regulatory risk. It is the reason the smallest honest claim cannot be 'a defensible expected annual loss'.
REMEDY: Either require the publisher to ship an lef with a cited base rate and a stated denominator (events per controller-year), and refuse as a missing instrument otherwise; or rename the output on the artefact so it stops claiming to be annualised. A third, cheap option: make frequency a counting distribution (Poisson with the PERT as the rate prior) so a loss-free year is at least possible.

## [major] PE-05 The appetite is not an org budget: nothing sums what driftwood retains, so every line reads "inside the band" while the org carries 2.18x it — and each new priced parent raises the retained total with every line still green
(original title: No aggregation: each line is tiered against the full band, and driftwood's retained residuals sum to 2.18x its own appetite)
CLAIM: Cage tier selection runs independently per prices[] entry against the whole appetite band: composition.py:1934-1981 passes the same scalar `tolerance` to price_parent (once per feed edge, 1964-1967) and to price_twin (1977), and cage.py:182-187 select_tier — plus driftwood's own selection-policy/selection_policy.py:88 — compares ONE line's caged residual to the full band. Driftwood's three exposure lines each genuinely satisfy that test against 40,000 GBP (units/driftwood/party.yaml appetite.tolerance) while the residuals they retain sum to 87,387.44 GBP: ico isolated 1,787,177.075 x 0.02 = 35,743.54; threat-register baseline 19,558.550 x 0.70 = 13,690.98; twin isolated 37,952.92 as recorded on the entry. That is 2.18x the appetite — 1.84x on the most conservative reading, which drops the threat-register line as double-counting the twin's borrowed lef ("lef_from": "threat-register", same [2,4,9]), though the estate itself sums all three into composed/HEADER.yaml exposure.total 3,704,381.74. Nothing anywhere tests an aggregate against the band: exposure_section aggregates only the UNCAGED amounts, tcor.py:141-160 balance_sheet sums residuals but never compares the total to tolerance (and still spends the full band per row at tcor.py:110), and verify/pound-seam asserts no aggregate. The defect is worse than "every added line is still green": price_parent records no basis string and cage.py:217-221 appends "(fits band £X)" unconditionally, so a line over the band is indistinguishable from one under it — tuppence already retains 185,247.31 against a 15,000 appetite (12.35x) and ludlow 187,160.42 against 5,000 (37.43x), both with over-band lines printing as a bare proposed_tier. These are proposed tiers over an empty cages[], so "retained" is the model's word (cage.py:167), not a live workload state. Ownership: composition.py:1895 owns only the adjacent exposure-total shape and names fair.py's portfolio aggregate as the upgrade path; nothing names the per-line band spend — yet the org-level annual-aggregate reading of `tolerance` is already the estate's recorded decision (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/14-insurance-and-the-insurer-party.md:79, CONTEXT.md:425-427), and CONTEXT.md:308-312 states both the org-level framing and the per-line mechanism in one glossary entry without reconciling them, against NORTH-STAR.md:32.
EVIDENCE: composition.py:1934-1981 (compute_prices) calls price_parent/price_twin once per edge, each passing the same bare `tolerance`; cage.py:171-181 select_tier compares one line's caged_residual to the full band. From units/driftwood/composed/evidence.json and cage.TIERS reduce values: ico proposed_tier 'isolated' -> 1,787,177.075 x 0.02 = 35,743.54; threat-register proposed_tier 'baseline' -> 19,558.550 x 0.70 = 13,690.98; twin proposed_tier 'isolated' -> residuals.isolated = 37,952.92 recorded on the entry itself. Sum 87,387.44 against units/driftwood/party.yaml appetite.tolerance 40,000.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:32 'proportionate to the organisation: ... its declared appetite'
OWNER: resolved-ticket-but-still-true ticket 14 (resolved, Q2 decision (a)) / ticket 25 (resolved 2026-08-29, build) — Ticket 14 (resolved 2026-08-28, grilling "Insurance and the insurer party") already found and named this exact architectural gap in its Facts: "There is no portfolio-level aggregate annual loss list anywhere; the per-risk lists are never summed year by year" (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/14-insurance-and-the-insurer-party.md:23). Its Q2 decision (a) explicitly resolved to build "an `exposure` section... computed by summing the per-risk annual loss lists year by year... a new aggregate `fair.py` does not have," and assigned the build to ticket 25: "Ticket 25: appetite.{attachment, limit, exclusions}, the exposure aggregate in fair.py, the premium line in the one prices[] pass, ORDER rewrite" (14:94). Ticket 25 (resolved 2026-08-29, "Build the pound seam") lists what it actually built — prices[] schema pass, per-hole partitioning, forward-intel as source:twin, fair.py tail/lognormal-GPD dispatch, appetite on party.yaml, FX feed — and contains no mention of "aggregate" or "exposure" anywhere (grep on 25-build-the-pound-seam.md returns nothing). So the aggregate that ticket 14 decided on and assigned to ticket 25 was never actually built; PE-05's evidence (driftwood's three lines each individually 'fit' but sum to 2.18x appetite) is the still-live symptom of that unbuilt aggregate. This is not in REVIEW-2026-08-31's C1/M1-M18 or Refuted-claims lists, and it is not named in map.md's Out of scope. Status: the underlying gap is owned by a resolved decision (ticket 14) whose assigned build (ticket 25) did not deliver it — so it is resolved-ticket-but-still-true, not a fresh unowned finding. A new/reopened ticket against 25's build gap (or reopening 25) is the right home, not a brand-new ticket duplicating 14's analysis.
FIT IMPACT: The appetite is presented as an org-level budget and is spent once per line. The estate can tell an adopter 'every one of your workloads is inside your risk appetite' while the org carries more than twice it.
REMEDY: Once fair.py gains the portfolio aggregate that composition.py:1894-1901 already plans, compare the summed retained residual to the band and record the headroom on the exposure section. Even before that, printing the sum of selected-tier residuals beside the band would make the breach visible.

## [major] PE-06 The one £ input NORTH-STAR names by name — a regulator's penalty applied to the adopter's own size — under-prices every adopter's statutory ceiling by about 5x, and the estate's own check certifies the wrong rule as correct
(original title: The turnover-sizing rule diverges from the decided rule and from the statute, and its selfcheck enforces the divergence)
CLAIM: ico's converter scales the whole loss triple by (rate x turnover)/cap with neither a min nor a max (units/ico/schema/to_fair_scenario.py:68-71), while the decided rule (ticket 07 section 1, echoed at map.md:32) is hi = min(rate x turnover, cap) with the examples scaled by hi/cap. The converter's selfcheck at :167-172 asserts the uncapped ratio exactly, so a passing test locks the divergence in, and no open ticket owns it — the build shipped under ticket 25 (commit 9aa89df, not ticket 21 as first stated), and tickets 07, 21, 24 and 25 are all resolved. Two corrections to the impact. First, the code-vs-decision divergence bites only above turnover = cap/rate = 435,000,000; below it the min() is a no-op, so at driftwood (the only adopter declaring a size; tuppence and ludlow carry no size block and price unsized at 9,039,791) the code emits hi = 1,791,836.69 against the decided rule's 1,720,000 — a 4.18% overstatement of one adopter's ceiling, not the 20,835,310 figure, which requires a 1bn-turnover adopter the estate does not have. Second, the 5x understatement of the statutory ceiling belongs to the decided rule, not to the code: UK GDPR Art 83(4) / DPA 2018 s157 sets the standard maximum as the HIGHER of 8,700,000 or 2% of turnover, so driftwood's statutory ceiling is the full 8,700,000, and ticket 07's min() yields 1,720,000 while the code yields 1,791,837 — the code is marginally closer to the statute than the decision it diverges from. The finding is therefore two defects that should be separated: (a) an unowned build divergence from a recorded decision, real but currently worth 4% on one adopter, and (b) an owner-ratified decision that inverts the statute (min where the statute says higher-of), which is the larger of the two, applies to all three adopters, and is recorded nowhere as a known deviation.
EVIDENCE: units/ico/schema/to_fair_scenario.py:68-71 `cap, rate = float(f.get('cap_gbp') or 0), f.get('rate'); if turnover is not None and cap and rate: scale = (float(rate) * float(turnover)) / cap; lo, mode, hi = lo*scale, mode*scale, hi*scale` -- no min, no max. Ran against the shipped v3 schema: turnover unset -> (275,000, 3,913,900, 9,063,360); turnover 86,000,000 -> (54,368, 773,783, 1,791,837); turnover 1,000,000,000 -> (632,184, 8,997,471, 20,835,310) with cap_gbp = 8,700,000. Selfcheck at :167-168 `assert big[2] > unsized[2]` and :172 asserts the exact un-capped ratio. Decided rule: .scratch/ecosystem/issues/07-org-size-obligations-and-currency.md:19 'pct_of_global_turnover becomes hi = min(rate x turnover, cap); the real examples keep their shape and scale by hi / cap'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:32 'Regulator penalties enter as published schemas applied to the adopter's own size'
OWNER: new tickets 07/21/24/25 (all resolved) — The decided rule (ticket 07 §1, reaffirmed unchanged by ticket 24's answer item 3 consequences: "Ticket 07 §1 ... gain optional relevant_revenue" — uk-gdpr's min(rate×turnover,cap) is untouched by ticket 24) was to be built by ticket 21 ("composition passes size and drops the hard-coded uk-gdpr / lower-tier") and finished by ticket 25 ("regime entry partitioned into per-hole amounts from ico v3 weights"). Both tickets are Status: resolved, but neither Answer mentions implementing the min(...,cap) ceiling, and the finding's direct read of units/ico/schema/to_fair_scenario.py:68-71 (checked against the fresh unit clone) shows scale = rate*turnover/cap with no min — confirmed still diverging at v3 turnover=1e9 (hi=20,835,310 vs cap 8,700,000). REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's C1/M1-M18 list, minor-polish list, and "Refuted claims" section contain no mention of turnover/cap_gbp/to_fair_scenario/scale, so this specific build-vs-decision divergence was not previously raised or refuted. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md has no hits for turnover/to_fair_scenario/cap_gbp either. Per the "resolved ticket whose Answer claims fixed does NOT own it if finding says still true" rule, tickets 21/24/25 do not own this — matching the finding's own "Claimed ownership" line. This is a new, unowned finding.
FIT IMPACT: The one place NORTH-STAR names by name -- a regulator schema applied to the adopter's own size -- implements neither the statute nor the estate's own recorded decision, and its test suite locks in the divergence. For driftwood specifically the effect is a 5x understatement of the statutory ceiling (its real Art 83(4) maximum is the full 8.7m, not 1.79m).
REMEDY: One line: apply the decided min() (or, if the owner prefers the statute, max(cap, rate*turnover)) and update the selfcheck assertion at :167-172 to the chosen rule. Record which of the two the owner wants in ticket 07's answer.

## [major] PE-07 The money half of the threat line is neither published nor proportionate: loss magnitude is an unsigned, adopter-keyed constant in the platform's repo, so that exposure is the platform's opinion about an adopter rather than a priced consequence of signed facts
(original title: The threat line's loss magnitude is a platform-held per-adopter fixture -- the exact class ADR-0021 retired -- and the publisher ships no converter)
CLAIM: The threat line's loss magnitude is a platform-held per-adopter fixture — the same pattern ADR-0021 retired — and the migration that is owned would leave it intact.

`platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py:34-38` hard-codes `THREAT_LM_GBP` keyed by adopter name (driftwood 1k/4k/9k, tuppence 5k/25k/90k, ludlow 20k/100k/400k) and `:44` reads it. The feeds publisher's own `threat-register/v2/feed.json` payload carries only `threat`, `flavour`, `lef` and `source` per institution — no magnitude — so the number that turns the threat line into money is written and signed by the platform, not by the feed's publisher, and is not derived from any adopter's signed `size` (driftwood declares turnover 86m GBP and 240,000 customers yet prices at a 4,000 GBP mode; tuppence and ludlow declare no `size:` at all). The band does vary by adopter name; what it does not do is come from a signed size fact or from the publisher.

The feeds repo ships `converters/fx.py` but no converter for threat-register, cve or eol, so `composition.py:542-550` falls through to the platform copy — which is what produced the 19,558.55 GBP threat line in `driftwood/composed/evidence.json`. Contrast `platform/risk/enforce.py:17-23` ("a party's band is now declared by the party that carries it, next to its size, and nobody else") and `:170-171` (`assert not os.path.exists(.../appetite.json)`), and ticket 24 item 4 ("Each publisher ships its converter beside its feed ... Composition calls it with the adopter's signed `size`. Platform holds no per-regime arithmetic.").

Ownership, corrected: the missing publisher-side converter IS named in the record — `feeds/converters/README.md` calls it "the one honest gap in the table" and `platform/feeds/README.md`'s moved-notice assigns the deletion to tickets 25 and 29 — but that same README states "Nothing about it changes in the move", so the owned migration is an explicit no-op on the fixture. No ticket names `THREAT_LM_GBP` at all; ticket 24:25 records only the per-org LEF triple as "the only per-org differentiation today", missing the LM dict (an omission the 2026-08-27 drift review already flagged, G-drift-findings.md:320, without it reaching a ticket).

Fit impact, corrected: one of driftwood's three exposure lines (`EXPOSURE_KINDS = ("feed", "twin")`, composition.py:1879) is priced by a magnitude its publisher never wrote — the ico line is publisher-signed and scaled to driftwood's signed 86,000,000 GBP turnover, and the twin line's lm is written by driftwood itself (it lacks a signed tag, a separate gap ticket 64 owns). The visible absurdity holds and is worse than a scoping quibble: `driftwood/composed/evidence.json` carries the threat line and the twin line with the identical borrowed `lef [2,4,9]` and identical `lef_basis`, at a 4,000 GBP mode and a 384,000 GBP mode respectively, in one document.
EVIDENCE: units/platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py:34-37 `THREAT_LM_GBP = {'driftwood': (1_000, 4_000, 9_000), 'tuppence': (5_000, 25_000, 90_000), 'ludlow': (20_000, 100_000, 400_000)}` and :44 `lm = THREAT_LM_GBP.get(institution, ...)`. units/feeds/threat-register/v2/feed.json payload.institutions carries only threat/flavour/lef/source per institution -- no magnitude. The feeds repo ships no to_fair_scenario.py (find across the clone returns only platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py), so composition.py:542-550 falls through to the platform copy. Contrast units/platform/risk/enforce.py:17-23 ('a party's band is now declared by the party that carries it ... and nobody else') and :170-171 (`assert not os.path.exists(.../appetite.json)`). Ticket 24 item 4: 'Each publisher ships its converter beside its feed, as ico does today.'
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:24 'each participant ... signs its own artefacts, and is consumed only through a pinned, signed dependency'; NORTH-STAR.md:32 'proportionate to the organisation'; ADR-0021
OWNER: new n/a — No ticket, ADR, or review entry owns this. Searched: (1) .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ — grep for THREAT_LM_GBP/to_fair_scenario/magnitude/appetite.json across all tickets found no ticket naming the platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py magnitude fixture. Ticket 21 (Build the feed contract, resolved) explicitly kept "the platform copies" of threat-register etc. in place for gate-script reasons (issues/21:23, decision 5) but never discusses the magnitude constant or a missing publisher-side converter for it. Ticket 24 (Size beyond turnover, resolved-provisional) Q4/item-4 decided "each publisher ships its converter beside its feed, as ico does today" (issues/24-size-beyond-turnover.md:124) — this is the exact sentence the finding quotes — but that decision is scoped to regime/obligation-pricing converters (ico-style, ticket 07/24 vocabulary: size facts, obligations, rate/turnover), not to the threat-register's lef->money magnitude. Nothing in ticket 24 or 21 names THREAT_LM_GBP or commits to giving the feeds repo its own to_fair_scenario.py. Ticket 25 (Build the £ seam, resolved) explicitly retired the one fixture ADR-0021 named, platform/risk/appetite.json (issues/25 Answer: "platform/risk/appetite.json is retired") — but THREAT_LM_GBP is a different file/fixture never mentioned there or in ADR-0021 itself (read in full: only appetite/twin/selection-policy/tail are in scope). (2) REVIEW-2026-08-31.md — grep for THREAT_LM_GBP/to_fair_scenario/magnitude found zero hits in both its Confirmed-shortfalls list and its Refuted-claims list, so it was neither raised nor refuted previously. (3) REGRILL-ANSWERS.md — grep for threat/magnitude/LM_GBP/appetite found only unrelated hole-pricing rows (P123, P191, P194), nothing about this fixture. (4) NORTH-STAR §6 (What is explicitly out) and map.md Out of scope — neither excludes publisher-side threat-magnitude conversion; NORTH-STAR:24's signed-artefact principle and ADR-0021's party-band precedent, both cited by the finding, remain live and unaddressed by this specific gap. Conclusion: PE-07 is a new finding, not a duplicate, not resolved, not refuted, not ruled out of scope.</note>
</invoke>

FIT IMPACT: Two of the three exposure lines are priced by numbers the publisher never signed, keyed by adopter name in a third party's repo. It also produces the visible absurdity that the same named event costs 4,000 GBP mode as a threat line and 384,000 GBP mode as a twin line in one document.
REMEDY: Move THREAT_LM_GBP into the feeds publisher's own feed payload (per institution, beside the lef it already ships) and move to_fair_scenario.py into the feeds repo, so composition's converter resolution finds the publisher's copy. That is what ticket 24 item 4 already decided.

## [major] PE-08 The £'s proportionality is exercised on one adopter of three: tuppence and ludlow publish no size, so NORTH-STAR §2's adopter description is untrue of two of the three named example consumers
(original title: "Proportionate to the org" is true of one adopter of three: tuppence and ludlow price identically to the penny)
CLAIM: Two of the three adopter parties declare no `size` block at all (only units/driftwood/party.yaml:33-38 does), so tuppence's and ludlow's uk-gdpr exposure is the publisher's unsized statutory-cap read — both exactly 9,039,791.01976426 GBP, hole for hole — against driftwood's 1,787,177.0751717847 (the same figure times its 1,720,000/8,700,000 turnover scale), and every per_customer restatement in both is null (composition.py:935-949; :1487-1506). Their other prices[] line, threat-register, does differ (tuppence 222,574.31 fintech, ludlow 318,229.78 healthcare), so the two are not identical overall — only on the regulator line. NORTH-STAR.md:43 step 2 scopes itself to "one adopter" and driftwood satisfies it; the shortfall is against principle 3 (NORTH-STAR.md:32), whose four levers land unevenly: declared appetite is exercised on all three (verify-proportionality and verify-pound-seam, run 21), turnover and customers on one, and regulators on none — ticket 07 §2's `obligations` list is absent from every party.yaml and from platform/party/schema.json, so tuppence prices no FCA and ludlow no HIPAA. Ticket 07 §1 (resolved) and spec.md:131 do require size of "the adopter's" party artefact generally; what is missing is any ticket scheduling the adopter-by-adopter build, and any check that would notice — `size` is absent from schema.json's `required`, and party_artefact.py:306-307 returns no error when it is absent, so the gate stays green.
EVIDENCE: `grep -l '^size:'` across units/{driftwood,tuppence,ludlow}/party.yaml returns driftwood only. units/tuppence/composed/evidence.json and units/ludlow/composed/evidence.json both carry `ico feed penalty-schema 9039791.01976426`, identical to the last decimal, versus driftwood's 1787177.0751717847 (= 9,039,791.01976426 x 0.19770114942528735, the turnover scale factor). composition.py:1487-1506 `_sized_turnover` returns None with no size and the publisher then 'prices at its own statutory cap, the widest read'. Run-21 capture verify_pound-seam confirms both: 'ludlow regime entry (penalty-schema): 4 priced hole(s) sum to its total 9,039,791.02 GBP' and the same figure for tuppence.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:32 'The price is proportionate to the organisation: its turnover, its customers, its regulators, its declared appetite'; NORTH-STAR.md:43 step 2 'The composition re-prices the adopter's exposure against its own size'
OWNER: new none — No ticket owns building tuppence's and ludlow's size declarations. Ticket 07 (resolved) only decided the size-block *shape* generally (party.yaml gains size:{turnover,customers,data_subjects,headcount,as_of}); ticket 24 (resolved, provisional) built the size-*reading* mechanism (HIPAA provisions, FCA relevant_revenue, per_customer field, publisher converters) but its 'Consequences' list (24-size-beyond-turnover.md:128-132) assigns only mechanism work to tickets 21/25/10/19/11 -- no line assigns an adopter-by-adopter party.yaml build, and grep confirms only driftwood/party.yaml carries a size: block today. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's M15 flags the same driftwood-only pattern but for twin files, not size/pricing -- it does not cover this claim and its 'Owning ticket: none -> new ticket 64' targets twins, not size. Not in the Refuted claims list. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md has no entry on this. NORTH-STAR/map Out-of-scope sections do not exempt tuppence/ludlow's size facts. This is a new, unowned finding.
FIT IMPACT: A fintech and a healthcare provider carry penny-identical uk-gdpr exposure. The estate's headline proportionality claim is demonstrated once, on the one adopter that also happens to be the only one with a twin, an insurer parent, a selection policy and a Flux-wired workload.
REMEDY: Add a signed size block to tuppence's and ludlow's party.yaml. It is a five-line change per repo and it converts the estate's central claim from one instance to three.

## [major] PE-10 A signed regulator feed publishes an editorial apportionment as if it were published regulatory fact, and the carrier cites it as authority for retaining 60% of the adopter's largest exposure line
(original title: The control weights carry no citation, and they set both the hole prices and 60% of the insurance exclusion)
CLAIM: ico's control_weights carry no stated derivation anywhere in the feed envelope, its changelog, its payload schema (which forbids one: weight items are `additionalProperties: false` over source/id/weight) or its verify script — unlike the fine schedule beside them, where every regime carries an authority and a statute and three of the four carry dated public penalty notices (pci-dss cites undated, unnamed acquirer bands and says so). The values are round multiples of 0.05, and unlike other admitted guesses in the same repo (the editorial LEF, the demo signing key, the carrier's rate and load) they carry no `ponytail:` honesty flag.
