They nonetheless fix all four of driftwood's uk-gdpr hole prices (composition.py:1474, amount x weight) and are the sole determinant of the SIZE of the insurer's carve-out: pl-2 + ra-3 = 0.6 x £1,787,177.08 = £1,072,306.25 excluded (insurer/quote/driftwood/v1/feed.json:40), which is 60% of the uk-gdpr line — the largest regulator line, though not driftwood's largest exposure line (the twin's forward-intel is £1,897,646.11) — and 28.9% of its £3,704,381.74 total exposure. The carrier states its own underwriting reason for choosing pl-2 and ra-3 (governance-of-record failures are retained); what it cannot source is the 60%. The mechanism (a partition that must sum to 1) is sound and enforced by verify-penalty-feed.sh:170-206 and pound_seam.py; only the values are unsourced. Ticket 15 (resolved) decided that a regulator publishes weights; ticket 38 (open) owns refusal deletion and the ungoverned ramp. No ticket, ADR, spec story or verifier owns provenance for the weight values, so a regulator or carrier asking "why is the system security plan 30% of the GDPR exposure?" has no answer in the artefact.
EVIDENCE: units/ico/penalty-schema/v3/feed.json payload.control_weights['uk-gdpr']['lower-tier'] = pl-2 0.3, ra-3 0.3, ca-2 0.2, ir-8 0.2 (every regime's weights are round tenths). The changelog states only what the field IS, not where the numbers come from. units/ico/verify-penalty-feed.sh:170-206 checks sum-to-1, duplicates, range (0,1] and regime/violation-type existence -- never provenance. Downstream: units/driftwood/composed/evidence.json holes = 536,153.1225515354 / 536,153.1225515354 / 357,435.41503435693 / 357,435.41503435693 (composition.py:1473-1474 `amount * float(w['weight'])`); units/insurer/terms/driftwood.yaml excludes `{regime: uk-gdpr, control_ids: [pl-2, ra-3]}` = 1,072,306.25 of a 1,787,177.08 line.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:32 'Regulator penalties enter as published schemas' -- a published schema whose apportionment is unsourced
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (ticket 15 "Price everything that was counted", ticket 38 "Priced holes in composition", ticket 21 "Build the feed contract"), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md, and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md — none mentions provenance, derivation, or citation of the control_weights values. Ticket 15's Answer item 1 and ticket 21's build note establish THAT ico publishes per-violation control weights and pins nist as a controls parent, and ticket 38 owns wiring the weight-multiplication mechanism into composition.py, but neither ticket's Question, Facts, or Answer text asks where the weight numbers (0.3/0.3/0.2/0.2 etc.) themselves come from, nor requires a citation/statute/penalty-notice source for them — only that they sum to 1 and are schema-versioned as a payload_schema major (ADR-0019). NORTH-STAR §6 (out of scope) and map.md's Out of scope list carry no carve-out touching this (portability/lock-in, covert sensing, real surveillance data, video-as-deliverable — none relate). No ADR (checked via grep across docs/adr/ for weight/provenance terms in related tickets) and no REGRILL-ANSWERS entry addresses it; REGRILL-ANSWERS #34 ("provenanced") concerns a twin trade-off-curve pick under P193, an unrelated mechanism. This is a genuinely new finding: the mechanism (partition-sums-to-1, schema-versioned) is owned/built, but the specific claim — that the weight VALUES carry no stated derivation anywhere, unlike sibling fines which cite an authority/statute/penalty notice — is not raised or answered by any existing ticket, ADR, or owner decision.
FIT IMPACT: The mechanism (a partition that must sum to 1) is sound and enforced. The values are an assertion carrying a six-figure carve-out. A regulator or a carrier asked 'why is the system security plan 30% of your GDPR exposure?' has no answer in the artefact.
REMEDY: Add a `basis` string per weight entry in the payload schema, the way the fines already carry `source`, and have verify-penalty-feed.sh refuse a weight with no basis. Even 'editorial, unsourced' as an explicit value is better than silence.

## [major] PE-11 The transfer arm has no working clock: the insurer's quote is provably stale against the exposure it prices, and the only mechanism that could refresh it has refused on every run it has ever had
(original title: The daily re-pricing clock is dead: all three insurer requote jobs fail every scheduled run)
CLAIM: Every scheduled insurer requote job has failed on both of its runs to date (2026-09-01 33496526156, 2026-09-02 33615860064; all three matrix legs, at step "re-price and compute the bump") with "REFUSED: missing instrument: .adopters/<adopter>/composed/HEADER.yaml carries no `exposure` section". The cause: fetch.yml checks each adopter out at the tag insurer/party.yaml pins, v1.1.0, and v1.1.0's tree predates the exposure section — the section landed in driftwood at commit ceac84b on 2026-08-29, four days after tag v1.1.0 (eacae33, 2026-08-25); same for tuppence and ludlow, and no adopter has cut a tag above v1.1.0. party.yaml's own signed comment asserting v1.1.0 is "the one whose tree carries the exposure section" is false. No automated mechanism can recover: the insurer's renovate.json manages only gitops/platform/platform-pin.yaml, not party.yaml's three exposure pins, and there is no newer adopter tag to bump to; recovery needs a human to cut an adopter v1.2.0 and merge a pin-bump PR. Zero PRs of any state have ever been opened on the insurer repo. Beyond staleness, the standing quote's provenance is false: quote/driftwood/v1/feed.json records priced_against version "v1.1.0" with exposure_sha256 397abe81d6cb, but that digest is the exposure section at untagged main commit ceac84b — v1.1.0 has no exposure section at all, and driftwood's current HEAD signs 5822260bab86 (the SKIP on the citable run). The `fetch` job of the same workflow passes; it is the re-pricing half that is dead. Ticket 57 owns registration (that half is genuinely done) and ticket 62 owns branch-ref pins; no ticket in .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ contains the word "requote".
EVIDENCE: `gh run list --repo policy-as-versioned-insurer/insurer` shows fetch runs 33496526156 (2026-09-01) and 33615860064 (2026-09-02), both conclusion failure. `gh run view 33615860064 --repo policy-as-versioned-insurer/insurer --log` shows all three jobs failing identically: 'requote (driftwood) REFUSED: missing instrument: .adopters/driftwood/composed/HEADER.yaml carries no `exposure` section -- there is no signed exposure to attach a layer to' and the same for tuppence and ludlow. Consequence recorded on the citable run: talk/captures/.estate-clone_insurer_verify-insurer-quote.out 'SKIP: quote-driftwood: priced against exposure sha256:397abe81d6cb but ../driftwood/composed/HEADER.yaml now signs sha256:5822260bab86 -- the insured re-signed its exposure and a re-quote PR is due (the clock opens one; a human merges it)'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:34 principle 5 'Intelligence re-prices on a clock ... cages re-price, and proposals open, all on a schedule'
OWNER: new none — No open ticket, no REVIEW-2026-08-31 M-item, and no REGRILL answer names this specific failure mode: insurer's requote jobs checking out each adopter at its pinned tag (v1.1.0) whose tree predates the exposure section, so `.adopters/<adopter>/composed/HEADER.yaml` carries no `exposure` section and the job REFUSEs.

Checked and ruled out as owners:
- Ticket 57 (feeds/insurer become runnable, claimed) — owns workflow registration and first signed tags for feeds/insurer repos themselves; both are now registered and tagged (v1.0.0 cut 2026-09-01), so ticket 57 is resolved and does not name this failure.
- Ticket 62 (feed parents consumed pinned and signed, open) — owns the *opposite* direction: adopters (driftwood/tuppence/ludlow) checking out feeds/insurer/ico parents unpinned. This finding is insurer's CI checking out the *adopters* at a stale pinned tag — a different pin relationship, not covered by ticket 62's question or notes.
- REVIEW-2026-08-31.md M1 (feeds/insurer unrunnable) — about zero registered workflows/tags, now fixed by ticket 57; does not mention exposure-section content drift.
- REVIEW-2026-08-31.md M9/M10, and the Refuted-claims list — none reference requote, quote-driftwood, exposure sections, or v1.1.0 tag staleness.
- Tickets 36/37 (insurer quote slice, insurance round 2) — describe the intended pricer mechanism and open aggregate/sub-limit questions but do not address a stale pinned-tag tree causing REFUSED runs.
- REGRILL-ANSWERS.md — only touches insurer topics via P123 (pricing baseline holes), unrelated to this pin-staleness mechanism.
- Ticket 74 (step 3 happens once for real) is the nearest recent/adjacent ticket but concerns propose-tier band-crossings, not the insurer requote clock's REFUSED failures.

Conclusion: this is a genuinely new, unowned finding. Evidence in the finding (gh run view logs, the citable SKIP capture) is consistent with what these documents show and is not contradicted or refuted anywhere.
FIT IMPACT: The priced-transfer line is the one place the estate demonstrates a counterparty re-pricing off signed facts on a clock. It has never once succeeded, and the SKIP in the citable run is honest about a mechanism that cannot recover on its own.
REMEDY: Either bump the insurer's exposure pin to a tag whose tree carries the exposure section, or have the requote job resolve the pin to the newest tag that does and record which. The verify script already detects the staleness; the fix is a pin, not code.

## [major] PE-12 The transfer arm is not priced on the same footing as the other three, so the board's Total Cost of Risk overstates ceding a risk and the "one currency, comparable moves" principle is not true where the estate books it
(original title: tcor.py's transfer move double counts the deductible and charges the full ALE, biasing the four-move crossover)
CLAIM: tcor.py:117-118 prices a transfer as premium = full do-nothing ALE x (1+load) with residual = the deductible's face amount, so the sub-deductible layer is charged to the carrier and retained again. The overstatement is exactly d + load x E[retained], strictly positive whenever a deductible exists, so the transfer arm is systematically dearer than a correct layer decomposition: for driftwood's third-party-fulfilment risk the shipped £21,150 should be ~£16,135 (annual-aggregate grain, the grain ticket 14 chose) or ~£15,005 (per-occurrence), inflating the captured board line from £42,923 to the £47,921 in run-21's .estate-clone_platform_tcor_verify-tcor.out, ~12%. Two corrections to the finding as filed. (1) It IS owned: .scratch/ecosystem/issues/14-insurance-and-the-insurer-party.md:20 names the formula verbatim and its Consequences say the insurer layer "supersedes tcor.py's ALE-times-load premium", with the per-risk transfer move deleted and ORDER becoming fix/cage/isolated. The live defect is that the decision is half-executed: ticket 36 built insurer/pricing/quote.py:192, which correctly nets the attachment out of the layer, but nothing touched tcor.py; wargamer.py:51 and wardley.py:52 still import it, wargamer.py:291-296 still asserts transfer wins on the hyperscaler risk, verify-tcor.sh is still graded on the truth surface, and no open ticket carries the rewrite. (2) No shipped crossover is actually wrong today: with the transfer arm repriced, all 3 rows of driftwood-portfolio.json and all 6 of wargamer/human-device.json keep the same chosen move, and the bias is monotone so it could only move rows toward transfer. The residual-grain half of the original claim is also weak: under the annual-aggregate grain the estate chose, E[min(annual loss, 4000)] = 3,991, so booking the 4,000 face is near-exact; premium-on-full-ALE is the entire error. Finally there are two transfer formulas in shipped code, not three, and quote.py:31-41 already owns its own ceiling and names the correct target formula.
EVIDENCE: units/platform/tcor/tcor.py:116-118 `premium = ale_warn * (1.0 + load); out['transfer'] = line(deductible, 0.0, premium)`. Correct decomposition is premium = (1+load) x E[loss above deductible] and residual = E[loss below deductible]; at ~2.17 events/yr the expected retention alone is roughly 2.17x the per-event deductible, not 1x. Run-21 capture .estate-clone_platform_tcor_verify-tcor.out: 'balance sheet £47921 = residual £27271 + controls £3500 + premium £17150' -- the 17,150 premium is the third risk's full ALE (~13,713) x 1.25, and its residual line is the 4,000 deductible from tcor/scenarios/driftwood-portfolio.json. Separately this is a third transfer formula in one estate: units/insurer/pricing/quote.py:192 uses layer x rate x (1+load); nothing reconciles the two.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:32 'a hardening control, a cage tier, an insurance transfer ... are comparable' -- comparability requires the transfer arm be priced on the same footing as the others
OWNER: resolved-ticket-but-still-true ticket 36 (graduated from ticket 14) — Ticket 14 (resolved, provisional) explicitly names this exact defect as a Consequence: "Supersedes tcor.py's ALE-times-load premium and costs.transfer.{load, deductible} in driftwood-portfolio.json," and prescribes the fix (aggregate exposure, TVaR/layer-priced quote, premium as a cost line under the adopter's perspective). Ticket 36 ("the insurer quote slice") graduated from that answer and is marked Status: resolved with Answer "Built 2026-08-29 ... The premium reaches the adopter prices[] as the contract-cost line ticket 25 reserved." So an open ticket does not own this finding — ticket 36's own Answer claims it already fixed the tcor.py decomposition. But per the ownership rule, a resolved ticket claiming a fix does not own a finding that says the defect is still true, and it is still true: the fresh clone at units/platform/tcor/tcor.py:114-117 (checked directly) still reads `premium = ale_warn * (1.0 + load); out["transfer"] = line(deductible, 0.0, premium)` — the identical per-risk ALE×load formula with deductible-as-residual that ticket 14 said would be superseded, not the layer/TVaR-based insurer-quote premium ticket 36 claims was wired in. ORDER also still lists "deny" rather than "isolated," contradicting ticket 09/36's stated consequence, reinforcing that tcor.py itself was never edited despite the ticket's Answer. Not addressed in REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's C1/M1-M18 or Refuted list (checked, no match), and no REGRILL-ANSWERS.md entry moots it — ticket 14's own entry (P177, reversal 20) is the source of the prescribed fix, not a refutation. So: the fix is a decided owner position (ticket 14), a build ticket (36) claims it landed, but the code contradicts that claim — PE-12 should be re-opened against ticket 36, not filed as new.
FIT IMPACT: The headline claim of the module is that the fix/cage/transfer/deny crossover is computed rather than asserted. One of the four arms is mispriced in a known direction, so the crossover is computed on a biased field.
REMEDY: premium = (1+load) * (ALE_warn - E[retained below deductible]); residual = E[retained below deductible]. Both are one extra simulate() call away, since fair.py already returns the annual loss list.

MINORS:
- PE-13 The twin's whole exposure line rests on one elasticity with no stated basis: The elasticity {min 0.06, mode 0.12, max 0.25} that turns 3.2m GBP of quarterly checkout revenue into driftwood's 1,897,646 GBP twin line -- 51% of its signed exposure total -- carries no basis field, unlike every response reduction beside it in the same overlay and unlike the valuation it multiplies.
- PE-14 A hole's price does not depend on whether the hole is open: The per-hole breakdown is a partition of the whole regime exposure across every published weight regardless of whether the adopter has that control open, so closing pl-2 changes no number anywhere and the living-£ loop does not run on the hole dimension.
- PE-15 Three of the four published regimes are size-blind or absurd, and the decisions to fix them were made but not built: Run against the shipped v3 schema, HIPAA, FCA and PCI produce identical triples at any turnover -- FCA's ceiling is 122,595,840 GBP (1.2x Standard Chartered's fine) for a firm of any size -- and ticket 24's provisional decisions to size HIPAA by data_subjects x provisions and FCA by relevant_revenue were assigned to ticket 21, which is Status resolved with none of them shipped.
- PE-16 The adopter's own selection policy has zero degrees of freedom: verify/pound-seam FAILs on any disagreement between the adopter's published selection_policy.py and platform/graded/cage.py:select_tier across 60 boundary cases, so ADR-0021's 'whose money is at risk decides how much of it to carry' currently means the adopter is free only to reimplement the platform's rule exactly.
- PE-17 The reduction table that prices the tier is unevidenced, the adopter's measured one is not used, and the check grades the wrong question: Tier residuals are computed from platform/graded/cage.py's reduce values, which the file itself says are 'the PLATFORM's, self-declared, evidenced by nothing but this comment', while driftwood publishes materially different measured reductions -- and the gate compares only which rung is CHEAPEST on net_cost_of_risk, not which rung the selection returns.
- PE-18 Tier selection is saturated and no real £ has ever crossed a band: Eight of the nine priced tier selections across the three adopters sit on the bottom rung, every entry records changed: false with old_price == new_price, and the only demonstration of a band crossing is an explicitly synthetic residual the script itself labels as 'not driftwood's real priced position'.
- PE-19 No counting distribution, no aggregate cap, no correction for overlapping shock windows: Per-event magnitudes are drawn i.i.d. within a modelled year with no aggregate cap, so the twin's shock -- defined as suppressing checkout conversion 'for a quarter' -- can be drawn nine times in one year, and nothing caps a line at the revenue it is exposed to.
- PE-20 The mode of the uk-gdpr triple is a two-point median, and the min is a small firm's fine rescaled by a large firm's ratio: lm_triple sets mode = median(real_examples), and uk-gdpr lower-tier has exactly two examples, so the mode is their midpoint (3,913,900 GBP) -- a figure no regulator has ever imposed, 14x one datum and half the other -- carrying beta-PERT weight lambda=4, and the same turnover scale is applied to the minimum, rescaling Doorstep Dispensaree's small-pharmacy fine down to 54,368 GBP.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- What is the £ FOR: an ordinal decision instrument (rank control options, make a dependency bump visible as a number in a reviewed PR), or a balance-sheet quantity a CFO books and a carrier cedes against? | WHY: NORTH-STAR.md:32 asks for 'comparable' and 'proportionate', which the estate largely delivers. It never asks for a defensible expected annual loss. If the ambition is comparability, then PE-01, PE-02, PE-09 and PE-20 are mislabelling problems, not modelling problems, and the fix is one sentence on the artefact. If the ambition is a bookable quantity, they are the top of the build queue and the frequency work is unavoidable. | CHANGES: Whether the remedy is editorial (qualify exposure.total on HEADER.yaml and in the deck, publish the calibration verdict beside it) or structural (a grounded frequency, an aggregate loss distribution, a repriced insurance layer). It also decides whether PE-01's loss ratio is a defect or a deliberate simplification worth stating.
- Is appetite.tolerance one economic quantity or three? Today the same signed 40,000 GBP is a threshold on the marginal benefit of a control (enforce.py), a threshold on retained mean annual loss (cage.py), and an aggregate insurance attachment (exposure_section, consumed by the insurer's layer). | WHY: These have different units of meaning, and whichever the owner intended, the other two readers are computing something the party did not declare. It is also what PE-05 turns on: as a mean-loss threshold it is a per-line test; as a retention it is an org-level budget being spent three times. | CHANGES: Whether party.yaml gains two more signed fields, whether the cage ladder compares to a per-line or an aggregate figure, and whether the insurer's attachment is the same number at all. It changes driftwood's selected tiers if the aggregate reading wins.
- Is the insurer a real second opinion whose quote must be defensible, or an illustrative counterparty demonstrating the shape of a signed cross-org transfer? | WHY: NORTH-STAR.md:22 records the insurer as 'Does not exist yet' at ratification and the row as your ask, cut to a 40% load. If it is illustrative, PE-01's 2,179% loss ratio is a scale artefact of a demonstration and belongs in a note. If it is meant to be a real independent view, the estate publishes two mutually contradictory signed numbers and one of them has to move. | CHANGES: Whether quote.py's formula goes to 2.0.0 off an aggregate loss distribution (its own stated upgrade path), whether the rate/load are recalibrated, and whether verify-insurer-quote.sh gains a loss-ratio check that can go red.
- Is the twin's forward-intel line a further consequence of the same breach the ico line prices (a lost-conversion loss alongside a regulatory fine), or a distinct event? Its own payload declares claim_scope.included = ['uk-gdpr']. | WHY: It decides whether driftwood's signed exposure.total of 3,704,381.74 is a legitimate sum or a double count of one regime under two names -- and whether the insurer's uk-gdpr exclusion should reach the twin line as well as the ico line. fair.py already implements the correct treatment for the 'same breach, several consequences' case (one shared lef, summed within the event); the composition does the opposite at portfolio level. | CHANGES: Either the twin's lm moves inside the ico entry's lm list under one shared frequency (which lowers the total and removes the 2.17-vs-4.5 inconsistency), or claim_scope is corrected and the exclusion keying is revisited. Both change the number the insurer prices from.
- Should tuppence and ludlow declare size before the estate claims proportionality, and where should a loss FREQUENCY legitimately come from? | WHY: Two of three adopters price identically to the penny because neither signs a size, so NORTH-STAR section 4 step 2 is demonstrated once. And the frequency that annualises every line is a converter constant with no denominator -- if an editorial DBIR midpoint is acceptable to you, PE-02 is a labelling issue; if a frequency must carry a cited base rate, it is publisher-side work in ico and feeds. | CHANGES: Adding size to two party.yaml files is a five-line change per repo and converts the estate's central proportionality claim from one instance to three. The frequency answer decides whether ico's converter keeps DEFAULT_WARN_LEF, whether the feed gains an lef with a stated denominator, and whether a missing frequency becomes a missing instrument that refuses.
- Should the adopter's selection-policy package be allowed to differ from platform/graded/cage.py, and should platform's unevidenced reduce table or the adopter's measured curve price the residuals? | WHY: verify/pound-seam refuses any disagreement across 60 boundary cases, so 'whose money is at risk decides how much of it to carry' currently means the adopter must reimplement the platform's rule exactly. Meanwhile cage.py:71-80 says its own reduce values are 'evidenced by nothing but this comment' and driftwood publishes measured reductions of 0.05/0.30/0.65/0.90 against the table's 0.30/0.70/0.92/0.98 -- and the estate prices with the unevidenced set. | CHANGES: Whether the pound-seam check narrows (agree on ordering and fail-closed rung, not on every selection), whether composition prices twin residuals off the adopter's own published curve, and whether ADR-0021's autonomy claim is restated to match what the gate actually permits.

# twin-validity

VERDICT: Not fit yet — for one fixable set of reasons and one that is a decision, not a fix. The twin is neither theatre nor intelligence: it is a genuinely strong, self-auditing apparatus for HOLDING A BELIEF TO ACCOUNT (real Brier/log-loss, a real git-tree rewind that withholds post-T facts by construction, three information regimes with no default, structural refusal to score Netflix/Intel, an AST-verified planter/detector split, a grading system that refuses hand-typed grades, 1550 tests and 70/1/3 invariants green on a live runner today) wrapped around a forecast that is a hand-typed constant. `twin/verbs.py:932-956` emits `"probability": float(beliefs[proposition_id])` — nothing anywhere infers a probability from a signal, and the package prints that fact in its own artefact at run time. The estate seam is wired but not load-bearing: the twin's £1,897,646 line is driftwood's largest price and does reach an enacted Namespace cage tier, but via a hand-typed label no check verifies, from a feed that sits outside every signed tag driftwood has cut (v1.1.0 contains zero paths under twin/), produced by a clock that has fired twice, failed twice, and never written an observation line. What would make it fit: (a) the owner decides whether the twin must derive a probability or whether the north-star row is restated to say it scores recorded beliefs; (b) require provenance (evidence_grade + basis) on a world model as the schema already does for a valuation; (c) close the four already-owned tickets — 72 (the `bash -e` sweep bug), 64 (tags, signatures, three adopters, step-5 hardening), 51 (the scored headline), 30 (the twin's own cage); (d) three small unowned fixes: refuse when a declared `publishes[]` feed is absent, one check binding composed `proposed_tier` to `posture.acme.io/tier`, and a non-saturated skill eval.

STRENGTHS:
- A forecast really has been scored against a real, dated, cited outcome and the answer is honestly red. I reproduced it: `PYTHONPATH=. python3 -P -m twin backtest --repo <fixture> --org royal-mail --scenario would-the-twin-have-flagged-it --regime as-consumed --at 2018-06-01` then `twin score --outcome royal-mail-concedes-the-automation-shortfall-2019` printed `market-consensus-2013 p=0.05 brier=0.9025 log-loss=2.9957 [as-consumed]`. The outcome (twin/fixtures.py:4395-4416) cites Royal Mail's real 2019-05-23 c.GBP1.8bn investment announcement with a URL; the rewind is real (bundle's regime.gate.ingestion_history.committed = 2018-05-17T09:00:00Z, before T, so the October profit warning is absent rather than filtered).
- The subjects' facts are real, not authored to produce a verdict. `grep -o "https://[^ \"']*" twin/fixtures.py | sort -u | wc -l` → 81 distinct real citations (SEC EDGAR, FCA, commonslibrary.parliament.uk, the Royal Mail prospectus on data.parliament.uk, BaFin, trade press), plus 18 example.invalid URLs confined to the two deliberately-synthetic hand-computable fixtures. GAPS 2.14's 'authored fixture written to produce the narrated verdict' critique targets platform/honesty/incidents.json and does not transfer to the twin's eleven firms.
- The engine refuses to score what it cannot honestly score: Netflix (too famous to distinguish anticipation from recitation) and Intel (unresolved until 2026H2/2027H1) carry no answer key and `twin score` structurally refuses; Kodak and Maersk carry no outcome by design. Invariant 37 in today's CI asserts the worst score is printed first: 'the worst is brier 0.9025 and is printed first, worse than a coin flip and still the headline'.
- The invariant harness is real, runs daily on a live runner, and is honestly red for a pre-registered reason. Hub twin.yml run 33615039125 (2026-09-02T09:36Z): `RESULT: 70 passed, 1 failed, 3 skipped (0 pending invariants, 3 skipped and not faked)` and `1 failed, 1550 passed in 115.13s`. The single failure is `flux_coverage_floor_is_still_reachable`, whose own message says 'This guard staying red is the finding, not a defect in it'. Three determinism jobs (x86_64-linux, aarch64-linux, arm64-darwin) plus reproduce-elsewhere all pass.
- Capability grades are computed and non-fakeable, not self-declared. `PYTHONPATH=. python3 -P -m twin grade` → `aggregate: 73 of 73 across 13 capabilities, 13 at full`, reproduced locally; twin/grades.py:150-188 refuses a hand-typed `grade:` key and refuses a capability whose checklist text or count has drifted from the owning ticket's live acceptance criteria. Materially stronger than the skill-eval mechanism and should not be conflated with it.
- Several anti-gaming mechanisms are structural rather than prose: twin/detector.py imports nothing naming `planter` (AST-checked on real source) and detect() returns byte-identical output with a decoy `plants` key spliced in; misuse.log_removal() accepts no float parameter so a constraint-removal number can only come from re-running the real pre-filter; evolution_judge.judge() hard-codes evidence_grade 5 and evidence-ladder.yaml sets pricing_threshold 2, so no skill output can ever price; enact_guard.py records two real dated self-caught bugs (2026-08-29 permissive default, 2026-08-31 _push_target cwd) rather than presenting itself as sound.
- The self-disclosure is specific and, at the sharpest point, is exactly the finding an auditor would write. `twin regimes` printed live: 'model residual not computed: … A forecast here reads a world model's declared belief and nothing infers it from a signal, so the three probabilities are identical by construction and a computed residual of zero would read as "the model is fine" rather than as "nothing consumes a signal".'
- Test discipline is high and behavioural: 33,872 lines of twin source against 18,623 lines of tests, 1,326 `def test_` functions, 1,550 collected tests — roughly one test per 22 lines — and the tests run the CLI end to end, AST-scan real source, and construct negative controls that must fail.

## [critical] TWIN-01 The twin — one of the eco-system's six named participants — does not forecast: its scored number is an authored constant transcribed from YAML, so the flagship "we can prove when we're wrong" beat exercises the scoring plumbing and never the twin's judgement
(original title: Nothing in the twin infers a probability; every scored forecast is a transcription of an authored constant)
CLAIM: Every probability the twin scores under a proper scoring rule is a verbatim read of an authored `beliefs:` entry: `twin.verbs.run()` emits `"probability": float(beliefs[proposition_id])` (twin/verbs.py:948) and nothing in the forecast path derives that number from a signal, a fact, an edge or a component's position. On the flagship falsifiability beat the scored number is the 0.05 authored at twin/fixtures.py:4242, and the run's own stdout concedes it: "A forecast here reads a world model's declared belief and nothing infers it from a signal, so the three probabilities are identical by construction". The five real, cited, dated Royal Mail signals are loaded and admitted (they appear in the bundle only in the gate's `admitted.signals` list) and reach no field of the forecast, so the three-regime diagnostic can localise a failure only to sensing or interpretation and the model residual is structurally uncomputable. Consequences: NORTH-STAR.md:34 principle 5 ("Feeds refresh, the twin re-forecasts") is structurally unreachable, not merely unbuilt, because a feed refresh cannot move a number read out of a `beliefs:` map; and the twin's row at NORTH-STAR.md:19 claims feeds as an input the forecast never consumes. NORTH-STAR.md:35 principle 6 is satisfied in letter — an authored, pre-registered prior scored under Brier is a pre-registered forecast scored under a proper rule.

Three corrections to the finding as written. (1) Drop "nothing in the twin infers a probability": twin/severity.py:127-137 computes exceedance probabilities from a lognormal/GPD splice whose parameters twin/anchoring.py:42-66 fits in closed form from cited, dated quantiles — computed, not transcribed, though they are probabilities of a loss magnitude and none is ever scored. (2) Drop "change 0.05 to 0.95 and the red result goes green with nothing else in the estate moving": harness guard `a_scored_forecast_is_never_silently_dropped` (twin/invariants/harness.py:2795-2800) raises Violated when the worst brier on this key is <= 0.25, naming that exact attack ("the beat's red result has been tuned away"); it passes today at 0.9025 and would fail at 0.0025, and it runs in .github/workflows/twin.yml on any change under twin/**. The correct statement is that the guard pins the demo to staying red without making the number earned. (3) Ownership: no open ticket owns closing the gap, but the limitation is disclosed and accepted in committed ACs (.scratch/twin/build/36:35-37 and /72:44-49, both ticked) as well as in twin/regimes.py:305-308, twin/verbs.py:1000 and twin/README.md:2910-2911 — a declared design state, not a concealed defect.
EVIDENCE: twin/verbs.py:932-956, the only probability expression in the package (grep for `probability` in twin/pricing.py returns nothing). Reproduced live: `PYTHONPATH=. python3 -P -m twin backtest … --at 2018-06-01` then `twin score …` printed `market-consensus-2013 p=0.05 brier=0.9025`; 0.05 is literally the `beliefs:` value in orgs/royal-mail/world_models/market-consensus-2013.yaml inside twin/fixtures.py. `twin regimes` on the same scenario printed `as-consumed 5 fact(s) admitted` and `model residual not computed: … A forecast here reads a world model's declared belief and nothing infers it from a signal, so the three probabilities are identical by construction`.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:35 principle 6 ('Forecasts are pre-registered and scored against reality under proper scoring rules') and NORTH-STAR.md:19 (the twin's row: 'Priced forecasts … scored against reality').
OWNER: new none — No ticket, ADR, REGRILL-ANSWERS entry, or REVIEW-2026-08-31 item owns or addresses this specific claim (that the scored probability is a verbatim YAML transcription rather than an inferred quantity). Ticket 46 (open) is about who runs the publisher-reliability scorer and feed signing — a different question. Ticket 51 (open) is about Wardley `needs`-edge propagation and pre-registration dates for the forecast book — also different. Ticket 11's Answer (resolved 2026-08-28) covers overlay location, perspective/currency binding, signal-lookup mapping, the standing scenario library, and which evals run in the gate; it never touches how `twin.verbs.run()` derives a probability, and none of its five decided items or amendments (C4/C5/C13/C14/C19) bear on this. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's Refuted-claims list contains two adjacent-but-distinct refutations: 'Forecast scoring machinery does not exist' and 'No live forecast is scored' — both refuted on the grounds that twin/scoring.py, twin/forecast_book.py and twin/verbs.py's score() (with the Carillion/NMC/Wirecard/Royal Mail/Enron backtest fixtures) constitute a real, exercised scoring mechanism. Those refutations concede the *scoring machinery* runs; they say nothing about whether the *probability being scored* is itself computed or merely copied from an authored `beliefs:` constant in twin/fixtures.py — which is exactly TWIN-01's claim. So TWIN-01 is not refuted by, and does not duplicate, those two refuted claims; it is a narrower and previously unexamined defect one layer beneath them. NORTH-STAR §6 (What is explicitly out) and the map's Out of scope list neither exempt this nor mention it. No C1/M1-M18 item in REVIEW-2026-08-31 covers it either. Searched: .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (grep for probability/Brier/beliefs/forecast/backtest/world_model — tickets 11, 46, 51 and a few unrelated feed tickets matched; none owns this), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (grep clean, plus full read of Refuted-claims and C1/M1-M18 headers), drift-review-2026-08-27/REGRILL-ANSWERS.md and GAPS.md/REPORT.md (grep clean for probability/Brier/beliefs/forecast/world_model), DRIFT_FINDINGS.json/G-drift-findings.md (found the two related-but-distinct refuted claims discussed above), NORTH-STAR.md §6, map.md Out-of-scope section.
FIT IMPACT: The flagship demonstration ('we can prove when we're wrong') is determined by one YAML digit: change 0.05 to 0.95 and the red result goes green with nothing else in the estate moving. The five real, cited, dated Royal Mail signals are loaded, admitted, and consumed by nothing. The three-regime diagnostic can localise only to sensing or interpretation; the model residual is structurally uncomputable.
REMEDY: An owner decision, not a patch. Either build a signal→probability path (which reopens twin 11 Q1's ordinal-arithmetic and grade-5 rulings), or amend the north-star twin row and the deck's beat-5 title to say the twin scores RECORDED beliefs under proper scoring rules — a smaller, fully true claim.

## [major] TWIN-02 Every flagship back-test scores a number the artefact attributes to a real third party ("Market consensus, pre-crisis") with nothing recording how that belief was established — so the twin's headline red is a claim about a market it never sourced
(original title: The schema requires provenance on every fact except the number that scores)
CLAIM: A `signal` document requires `source` and `provenance` (twin/schema.py:615-624), a `valuation` requires `evidence_grade` and `basis` (:232), a causal edge requires an `evidence_grade` (:857-860) — but a `world-model`, whose belief is the sole origin of every scored probability in the shipped pipeline (twin/verbs.py:935-948; `forecast_book.emit` has no production caller), requires only `id`, `name`, `beliefs` (:611-614). Stronger than first stated: the schema is closed (:461-485), so a world model cannot carry a source, provenance, evidence grade or calibration even if its author wanted to — `Schema.validate` refuses each with "The schema is closed — it accepts beliefs, credence, id, name, note and nothing else." No invariant or harness check requires a witness for a belief. Measured over the fixture corpus: 25 world-model documents (24 unique paths, not 20), exactly one containing a URL, and that one cites Cowgill & Zitzewitz for the mechanism its belief is about while deriving no number from them; driftwood/twin/world/world_models/reference-map.yaml carries 13 unsourced beliefs (0.45 … 0.18). The one optional field that could carry trust in a stance, `credence`, is copied into every forecast body (verbs.py:944) and read by nothing in twin/ or tests/. Against NORTH-STAR.md:35 principle 6, the artefact does not disclose the gap either: a forecast bundle's depth block is the min over domain-model, provenance and scenario-engine, all three fully checked (13/13 capabilities at n/n, `does_not_do.register()` returns 0 entries over 73/73 criteria), so the artefact announces `full` with nothing unchecked. Ownership is not "none found": twin/README.md:2918 ("Forecast probabilities are read from a world model's declared belief. Nothing infers them. This is the honest stub: the plumbing is real, the judgement is authored"), :287, and .scratch/twin/build/08:31 disclose it — but only in prose, which is exactly the "side table" twin/evidence-ladder.yaml:7 says a grade must never live in. Related and unchanged: driftwood's price-eligible edge cart-pii-loss-cuts-checkout-revenue declares grade 2 with a one-line note, no calibration and no observations, and `pricing_threshold: 2` (evidence-ladder.yaml:20) lets it into the £.
EVIDENCE: twin/schema.py:611-614 (world-model Schema: required {id, name, beliefs}; optional {credence, note}) vs twin/schema.py:615-624 (signal: required {id, date, steep, source, statement, provenance}) vs twin/schema.py:232 (valuation requires `basis`). `grep world_model twin/invariants/*.py` finds only forecast-shape checks. Measured over the fixture corpus: 20 world_models/*.yaml documents in twin/fixtures.py, exactly 1 contains any URL, and that one cites Cowgill & Zitzewitz in support of the narrative, not of its belief numbers. driftwood/twin/world/world_models/reference-map.yaml carries 13 beliefs (0.45, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.15, 0.35, 0.08, 0.2, 0.12, 0.3, 0.4, 0.1, 0.18) with no source. Related: twin/schema.py:790-806 makes `calibration` optional on a causal edge and schema.py:177-185 validates `evidence_grade` only as an integer 1-5, so driftwood's pricing edge cart-pii-loss-cuts-checkout-revenue declares grade 2 ('repeated historical co-movement … observed moving together across several instances') with a one-line note and no observations.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:35 principle 6 ('Every artefact carries a signature that says what it does and does not assert'); the twin's own evidence-ladder discipline that 'the grade travels WITH the claim rather than in a side table'.
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (grep world_model|credence|beliefs|provenance|calibration|evidence.grade): only ticket 11-an-org-twin-each.md matches, and it addresses the signal schema's source/provenance requirement (twin/schema.py:595-604) plus the ingest heuristic's grade-5-by-construction issue — it never discusses world-model's required/optional fields or that world-model is exempt from any provenance requirement. Searched REVIEW-2026-08-31.md for world_model/credence/beliefs: zero matches, so this gap is not in its C1/M1-M18 list nor its Refuted-claims list. Searched REGRILL-ANSWERS.md: no hits on world-model schema exemption; the only nearby item (re-grill 34/P193, trade-off curve provenance) is unrelated to world-model belief provenance. NORTH-STAR principle 6 (line 35, "every artefact carries a signature that says what it does and does not assert") is the ambition this finding argues is violated, not a decision that refutes or owns it. No ticket, review finding, or owner decision claims ownership of "world-model requires no source/provenance/evidence-grade for its beliefs." Finding is new.
FIT IMPACT: A system that refuses a hand-typed capability grade, a hand-typed constraint-removal number and a claim that names its own grade accepts a hand-typed probability with no witness at all — and that probability is the one number the whole scoring apparatus exists to grade.
REMEDY: Add `evidence_grade` and `basis` as required fields on the world-model schema, exactly as `valuation` already requires them, and require a `calibration` block whenever a causal edge declares a grade inside the pricing threshold. Both are small, closed-schema changes the loader already has machinery for.

## [major] TWIN-03 The twin is the one participant whose output nobody can verify — and it is the source of the adopter's single largest price, so "every actor is attestable" is false exactly where the money is
(original title: Everything the twin publishes sits outside every signed tag, contradicting the adopter's own signed party artefact)
CLAIM: Everything driftwood's twin publishes sits outside every signed tag, and so does the record that declares it. driftwood's newest tag v1.1.0 (eacae33, 2026-08-25) contains 60 paths and zero under `twin/`; `twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json` was added 2026-08-28 (055e340, ticket 25) and last changed 2026-08-29 (f75509a, ticket 29), so the feed, its `bump: none` rule, and the `publishes[]` entry that declares it all live only on untagged main — v1.1.0's party.yaml has no `publishes:` block at all. The hub has zero tags locally and on origin, so the `twin/v0.1.0` that PIN.yaml pins (`tag_cut: false`) does not exist either. Every other publisher's declared feed path IS inside its newest gitsign-signed tag (nist v1.1.0/catalog, ico v3.0.0/penalty-schema, feeds threat-register/v2.0.0, insurer v1.0.0/quote, platform v2.0.1/distribution/policies), which makes the twin the sole publisher whose output no tag covers. NORTH-STAR.md:19 ("signed by an agent identity") and principle 6 (NORTH-STAR.md:35) are unmet for the twin at the tag level. Two corrections to the original framing: (1) the "the tag is what signs it" assertion at party.yaml:62-66 is NOT in the signed artefact — it is on untagged main, so the contradiction is between main and itself, not between main and a signed party artefact; (2) the absence of an in-envelope signature is not a twin gap — ADR-0019:16-18 rules out in-band signatures for every feed, and ico's, feeds' and insurer's envelopes carry the identical seven keys with no signature field, while twin/sign.py's HMAC is never invoked by emit-forward-intel.py and bears on the twin package's own artefacts rather than this feed. Blast radius is also narrower than stated: no party.yaml in the estate inherits forward-intel; the only consumer is driftwood's own composition (one `source: twin` price in HEAD composed/evidence.json), and the insurer pins driftwood's `exposure` at v1.1.0, which predates the twin. Owned by ticket 64 (open), closing M16.
EVIDENCE: `git -C units/driftwood ls-tree -r --name-only v1.1.0 | grep -c '^twin/'` → 0. v1.1.0 is dated 2026-08-25 (`git log -1 --format='%ci' v1.1.0`); twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json was added 2026-08-28 (commit 055e340, 'ecosystem ticket 25'). v1.1.0's composed/evidence.json carries prices from ['ico','platform'] only. driftwood/party.yaml:62-66: 'Signed by the same gitsign tag as everything else here (ADR-0012), so an untagged feed.json on a branch is never a signature.' twin/forward-intel/bump.yaml reads `bump: none`. twin/VERSION=0.1.0 with twin/PIN.yaml and VENDORED.md both recording `tag_cut: false`. The twin package's own artefact signing (twin/sign.py) is HMAC from TWIN_SIGNING_KEY, which its README calls 'a shared key [that] proves possession, not identity'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:19 ('signed by an agent identity') and principle 6 (every artefact carries a signature).
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 64 — Ticket 64 (Status: open) at .scratch/ecosystem/issues/64-the-twin-is-three-adopters-tagged-and-signed.md explicitly closes M16 ("The twin's release is untagged and unsigned, and driftwood's forward-intel feed is outside every tag") from REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:79, which is the same claim as TWIN-03: v1.1.0 lacks twin/ paths, PIN.yaml tag_cut:false, forward-intel unsigned. Ticket 64's Done criteria ("twin/v0.1.0 and the forward-intel tag exist signed, and PIN.yaml's tag_cut flips true") directly targets this gap and it is not yet done. No REGRILL answer or ADR moots it. TWIN-03 is a duplicate of an already-owned, still-open finding — not new.
FIT IMPACT: The north-star's 'signed by an agent identity' is unmet at both levels — no tag signature on the published feed, and no keyless/identity-bound signature inside the envelope. Every other publisher in the estate (nist, ico, feeds, insurer) publishes under verifying gitsign tags; the twin is the exception.
REMEDY: Cut twin/v0.1.0 on the hub (needs the release workflow truth.yml lacks; the owner dispatches) and cut a driftwood release covering twin/forward-intel v1 by queueing its bump.yaml — exactly what ticket 64 already specifies. Until then, add a gate row that goes amber naming the absence.

## [major] TWIN-04 The twin participant has published nothing on its schedule: the twin's clause of principle 5 and step 5 of the demonstration rest on a sweep whose move path cannot execute, and the citable run cannot see it
(original title: The twin's clock has fired twice, failed twice, and never written a single observation)
CLAIM: driftwood's twin-sweep workflow has fired twice on its schedule (33508119299, 2026-09-01T12:31Z; 33627910027, 2026-09-02T12:04Z), failed both times, and has never written an observation or opened a proposal PR. The cause is exactly as stated: the sweep step runs under GitHub's default `shell: /usr/bin/bash -e {0}` while its body opens only `set -uo pipefail`, so `python3 twin/emit-forward-intel.py --check` exiting 1 — the very "the feed moved" signal the job exists to act on — aborts the step before `rc=$?` (twin-sweep.yml:88-91). Both downstream steps show as skipped in the run, proving `moved` was never set; `observations/` does not exist on main and no `twin/forward-intel-*` branch exists. Ticket 72 (open) names this cause precisely. One clause needs narrowing: `twin sweep` is not "run by nothing" — twin/cli.py:375 is invoked from twin/beat-intel.sh:93 via twin/beat-sequence.sh:39, reached daily by the hub's twin.yml (cron '17 5 * * *', :84), but only against the intel fixture; what is true, and what ticket 11:30 already records, is that no per-adopter sweep runs `twin sweep` anywhere, and driftwood's clock still runs emit-forward-intel.py despite ticket 29 — whose completion its own comment (twin-sweep.yml:30-33) made the precondition for switching — being marked resolved.
EVIDENCE: `gh run list --repo policy-as-versioned-driftwood/driftwood --workflow twin-sweep.yml` returns exactly two runs, both `schedule`, both `failure`: 33508119299 (2026-09-01T12:31Z) and 33627910027 (2026-09-02T12:04Z, after run 21's TRUTH line). `gh run view 33627910027 --log-failed` shows `shell: /usr/bin/bash -e {0}` while the script body opens `set -uo pipefail`, then `FAIL: twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json is not what the overlay renders` followed immediately by `##[error]Process completed with exit code 1` — the `moved=true` branch never runs. `ls units/driftwood/observations/` → No such file or directory. The workflow's own comment (twin-sweep.yml:30-33) also states 'the sweep is the forward-intel re-render, the only twin output this repository has today… Ticket 29 lands the scenario library and the scored sweep; when it does, this job runs `twin sweep`' — ticket 29 is marked resolved and `twin sweep` is run by nothing.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:34 principle 5 ('Intelligence re-prices on a clock … the twin re-forecasts') and NORTH-STAR.md:46 §4 step 5 ('The twin, on its schedule …').
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 72 — Ticket 72 is open and its 2026-09-01T12:31Z note already identifies the exact root cause TWIN-04 restates: the twin-sweep step runs under GitHub's default `bash -e`, so the `rc=$?` branch mapping exit 1 to `moved=true` is unreachable and any real feed move aborts the step — fix is `set +e` (or `|| rc=$?`) matching verify-reconcile.sh's pattern. Source: /Users/cns/httpdocs/controlplane/policy-as-versioned-flux/.scratch/ecosystem/issues/72-a-feed-bump-re-renders-the-twin-s-derived-artefacts.md, Notes section. TWIN-04's second run (33627910027, 2026-09-02T12:04Z) postdates the ticket and confirms the same failure recurred, but adds no new causal claim. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md has no mention of twin-sweep/bash -e/ticket 72 (grepped, no hits), so this is not a refuted or previously-reviewed claim — it's a fresh confirmation of an already-owned, still-open ticket.
FIT IMPACT: The clock half of the north star has produced zero evidence. The sweep exists to re-render a moved feed and open a PR; the one thing it was built for is the one path it cannot execute.
REMEDY: `set +e` around the `--check` call, or `python3 twin/emit-forward-intel.py --check || rc=$?` — the same pattern verify-reconcile.sh already uses. Then re-derive the signal-lookup rows too, per ticket 72 finding 2.

(original title: NORTH-STAR §4 step 5's named stimulus is structurally excluded from the clock and has never been played forward)
CLAIM: NORTH-STAR §4 step 5's named stimulus has never been played forward, and the truth surface grades step 5 green anyway on a presence check. Binding it to a pin here would be the twin deciding on the clock, which is the one thing this table exists to prevent.' Run-21 capture talk/captures/.estate-clone_feeds_verify-news-headline-skill.out: 'ok no adopter repo carries a claim file yet -- the skill has not been run for real'. `find units/driftwood/twin -type d -name outcomes -o -name '*outcome*'` returns nothing. NORTH-STAR.md:46: 'The twin, on its schedule, plays a dated external signal forward (the niobium headline) on the value chain, emits a scored forecast …'
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:46, §4 step 5 of the demonstration order.
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 51 (open) + ticket 23 (resolved, Answer item 1) — The core claim (step 5 has never run end-to-end; no driftwood forecast is scoreable) is explicitly owned by open ticket 51 ("The supply-constraint actor path and the scored headline forecast", blocked by 11/23) — matching the finding's own "Claimed ownership" line. The mechanism reversal (niobium moves out of the feed into the twin's own scenario library per ticket 11, "step 5 plays it from there") is recorded, not silent, as ticket 23's resolved Answer item 1 (PROVISIONAL, 2026-08-28), and REVIEW-2026-08-31's refuted-claims list already confirms "No live forecast is scored" is refuted only as to mechanism-exists, while conceding "the remaining decisions are open tickets 51 and 46's own text." So TWIN-05 is not a new orphaned finding on that front. However, the narrower sub-claim — that NORTH-STAR.md:46 itself still reads the pre-reversal mechanism ("plays forward... emits a scored forecast") and nothing found owns updating that sentence to match the ticket-23 reversal — is not covered by ticket 51's scope (which is the actor path/forecast build, not editing NORTH-STAR) and was not located in any ADR, REGRILL-ANSWERS item, or REVIEW-2026-08-31 entry. Recommend downgrading TWIN-05 to flag only that residual: NORTH-STAR §4 step 5's prose needs a restatement note/footnote pointing at ticket 23's reversal, since the "step 5 never executed" and "mechanism never reversed" parts are already owned/answered.

## [major] TWIN-06 The citable number counts north-star step 5 as observed-true when the north star itself records that step as never having run, and the deck's twin beat rests on that verdict
(original title: The step-5 gate check grades file presence and passed green in the same run in which the same artefact failed twice)
CLAIM: verify-e2e-step5-twin-forecasts.sh grades six `[ -e ]` path-existence checks plus a count of six scenario YAMLs, then runs verify/twin-evals/verify-twin-evals.sh — whose seven skill metrics all score 1.000 against both threshold and prior, and whose feed assertion is over the hub's own published envelopes "with a placeholder tag/commit", never driftwood's rendered feed. Nothing it runs looks at the artefact's content. In TRUTH run 21 (verified in the CI log for run 33616685427, not only the captures) it printed `ok driftwood/twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json` and PASSed, in the same run in which .estate-clone/driftwood/verify-twin-overlay.sh FAILed with "twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json is not what the overlay renders" and .estate-clone/driftwood/twin/verify-twin-scenarios.sh FAILed 2 of 16, on that same feed's re-render and on its signal-lookup rows. Step 7's honesty table then carries that green upward, tabulating "5 twin forecasts PASS" and concluding "steps 1-6 each report one honest verdict". Two corrections to the original: (a) the TRUTH line is not the uncandid surface — run 21 records fail=7 and both twin reds are inside it; the overstatement lives in step 5's verdict word and step 7's roll-up. (b) The committed deck is not green on this beat: talk/deck.html is gitignored, and talk/deck.md (footer `run=local · hub=5b1a891 · 2026-08-31T17:04Z`) renders beat 5 as status=SKIP, "could not look". The green beat exists in the untracked local deck.html and in any rebuild, since build_deck.py:77-87 derives the beat status from the capture's last line — so this compounds the already-known stale-deck defect rather than standing on the committed deck. Ownership is split: ticket 64 (open) owns hardening step 5 and its Notes name "minor step-5-presence-only"; ticket 72 (open) owns the artefact defect itself and already records both reds as real estate faults. Severity is better read as major-on-the-instrument, since the estate's own truth surface does report both underlying reds.
EVIDENCE: verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step5-twin-forecasts.sh header: 'This script does not pretend to look at a forecast; it looks at the five things step 5 needs'. Run-21 captures on origin/main: verify_e2e_verify-e2e-step5-twin-forecasts.out → 'ok driftwood/twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json (the feed the estate consumes)' … 'PASS'; .estate-clone_driftwood_verify-twin-overlay.out → 'FAIL: twin/forward-intel/v1/feed.json is not what the overlay renders'; .estate-clone_driftwood_twin_verify-twin-scenarios.out → 'TOTAL: 14 pass, 2 fail', both fails on that same feed and its signal-lookup rows. talk/narration.json beat 5 title: 'The twin plays a dated signal forward and is scored'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:59 ('Ticket Status: is derived from a named check') and the estate's own rule that a green that could not look is a red.
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 64 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/64-the-twin-is-three-adopters-tagged-and-signed.md) — Ticket 64 is open and explicitly folds in REVIEW-2026-08-31's 'minor step-5-presence-only' finding (ticket 64 Notes: "Closes review findings: M15 ... M16 ... minor step-5-presence-only"; REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:87: "step 5 grades file presence not the scheduled forecast ... All folded into tickets 54, 55, 59, 64, 66, 67"). Ticket 64's Question explicitly calls for hardening step 5 beyond presence checking ("harden step 5 to assert a dated scheduled sweep observation once twin-sweep first fires") plus authoring the missing tuppence/ludlow overlays and tagging/signing the twin. It is still Status: open with no Answer — nothing has actually fixed the presence-only grading yet. TWIN-06's run-21 evidence (step5 PASS on feed.json while verify-twin-overlay.sh and verify-twin-scenarios.sh FAIL on that same file in the same run) is a fresh, concrete instantiation of exactly the design flaw ticket 64 already owns, not a new unowned defect. Confirmed not in REVIEW-2026-08-31's Refuted-claims list, and grepping REGRILL-ANSWERS.md for step-5/presence/twin-forecast turns up nothing that moots this.
FIT IMPACT: The beat that narrates the twin on the deck is green on the strength of `ls`, in a run where the artefact it names is demonstrably wrong. The script's own prose is candid; the TRUTH line and the deck beat are not.
REMEDY: Make step 5 run `emit-forward-intel.py --check` itself (or read verify-twin-overlay's verdict) so the beat cannot be green while the feed it names is stale, and change the beat title to what the script proves.

## [major] TWIN-07 The demo's own proof that the twin is "not decoration" is a number that cannot fall: the gate's only graded twin-quality signal scores a heuristic against the corpus it was fitted to, and in one case against its own lookup table
(original title: The only twin quality metric on the truth surface is saturated, tautological in at least one case, and its regression guard has never had two data points)
CLAIM: The truth surface carries exactly one twin quality metric (verify/twin-evals/verify-twin-evals.sh), and all seven of its metrics sit at the 1.000 ceiling against a baseline recorded once, on 2026-08-13, in a single commit (f91a41c) — so the metric can only ever move down, and its recorded bar has never been refreshed. At least one of the seven is tautological, and worse than the record admits: for evolution-judge, all FOUR corpus labels (Carillion 0.62, NMC 0.55, Wirecard 0.8, Enron 0.45) are bit-identical values in `_MATURITY_KEYWORDS` (twin/evolution_judge.py:71-85), and in every case the firing phrase sits in the component's own NAME (twin/evolution_judge.py:257-262), so the accumulated evidence is never consulted — the eval asks whether a lookup table returns its own value. Ticket 23:29 under-records this as "three of four". The disclosure GAPS 3.22 asked for has landed (skill-thresholds.yaml, module docstrings) but stops at "fitted to the four backtest orgs"; it never says the labels ARE the table's values. No ticket owns removing the circularity.

Three corrections to the original framing. First, the corpora are NOT one pooled set from four subjects: only 27 of the 46 metric-item pairs come from Carillion/NMC/Wirecard/Enron (signal-classify 23, evolution-judge 4); causal-claims is netflix×3 + intel, gameplay-lens is intel/netflix/pocket, substrate-generator is three synthetic schedule recipes, ethics-gate is five sensor-admission cases with no org at all. And 46 double-counts: causal-claims and causal-claims-grade-accuracy share one 4-item corpus (digest 6c8f7f4a…), so there are 42 distinct items. The tautology charge lands on evolution-judge, not on the set.

Second, the regression guard is not the thing that skips. verify-twin-evals.sh:107-119 compares each live score against the 2026-08-13 recorded value on every gate run — it has its one data point and uses it, and its own comment (:110-112) explains it deliberately avoids detect_regression() precisely because that comparison is mute with a single recording. The "fewer than two committed versions" SKIP belongs to skill_score_log_is_append_only (twin/invariants/harness.py:735-776), an append-only guard against editing a recorded entry, which is a @harness_check absent from twin/invariants/manifest.yaml's 16 invariants and runs only in .github/workflows/twin.yml — a workflow verify-twin-evals.sh:5-7 itself notes is never cited by any document, and therefore not on the truth surface.

Third, ticket 29's "a fall in any score is a fail" HAS been exercised: verify-twin-evals.sh:70-73 asserts verdict(0.9, 0.8, 1.0) == "fell" on every gate run, with the comment "a comparison that is only ever exercised by scores that all pass cannot tell 'correct' from 'always says pass'". What has never happened is a real fall in a real score.

Net: the finding survives as a genuine twin-validity gap — one saturated metric, a stale baseline, and a demonstrably self-answering evolution-judge eval — but it is a ceiling-and-circularity problem in one skill, not a dead instrument across 46 items, and the guard machinery is better built than the finding credits. Severity major is still defensible on the NORTH-STAR.md:19 "scored against reality" test; the fit-impact sentences should be rewritten.
EVIDENCE: Run-21 capture talk/captures/verify_twin-evals_verify-twin-evals.out: seven lines all reading `score=1.000 … last=1.000`. twin/skill-scores.jsonl: exactly 7 lines, all `recorded_at 2026-08-13T00:00:00Z`, corpus totals 23, 4, 4, 4, 3, 3, 5 = 46 items; `git log --oneline -- twin/skill-scores.jsonl` → one commit, f91a41c. twin/evolution_judge.py:71-85 `_MATURITY_KEYWORDS` contains payment-acquiring/merchant acquiring (Wirecard), construction/support-services (Carillion), hospital operations/healthcare (NMC), mark-to-market/energy trading (Enron). twin/skill-thresholds.yaml discloses the pattern for every entry ('the heuristic scores 1.0 on its own N-item corpus… set below that'). Today's CI: invariant 15 `skill_score_log_is_append_only` → SKIP, 'the score-over-time log has fewer than two committed versions; nothing to compare yet'. The circularity is already recorded in ticket 23's facts section ('three of four corpus labels are literal keyword-table values', H5-08, GAPS 3.22).
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:19 (the twin is 'scored against reality'); the deck's own narration for beat 5: 'a forecaster that is never scored is decoration'.
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket .scratch/ecosystem/issues/23-the-news-feed-and-the-headline-skill.md (Facts, referencing GAPS.md:81 item 3.22) — Ticket 23 (status: resolved, but its Facts/Q5 are about a separate downstream question — headline-to-coordinate claim-scope propagation) explicitly records the circularity as real: "the evolution-judge 1.0 score is circular: three of four corpus labels are literal keyword-table values (G-drift-findings.md:679-684, H5-08). GAPS 3.22 asks to 'disclose the circularity in the skill card'" (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/23-...md:29). GAPS.md:81 (item 3.22) prescribes three actions: "Rename or build the model step; disclose the circularity in the skill card; reopen doctrine and climate" — disclosure is only one of three, and none of the three is "fix the tautology" or "give the regression guard two data points." No ticket's Answer claims the circularity itself, the saturated-at-1.000-on-fitted-corpus problem, or the never-exercised regression guard (invariant 15, still SKIP per today's run) is fixed — REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's C1/M1-M18 and Refuted-claims lists were also checked and contain no entry naming this. So: the disclosure sub-task is owned/landed (skill-thresholds.yaml + docstrings, matches the finding's own "Claimed ownership" line), but the circularity/tautology/never-moved-regression-guard defect itself remains unowned by any ticket — same conclusion the finding already states. Nothing in REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, NORTH-STAR §6, or the map's Out-of-scope section addresses or excludes this. Recommend: this is a legitimately open, undeclared defect — could be filed as a new ticket, but ticket 23 is the closest existing anchor since it already carries the facts.
FIT IMPACT: verify-twin-evals.sh is the twin's only quality signal on the truth surface, and it is structurally incapable of moving. Ticket 29's promise that 'a fall in any score against the last recorded value is a fail' has never once been exercised.
REMEDY: Either hold out a corpus the heuristic was not fitted on (even 5 items from a fifth org would break the tautology), or reclassify the seven metrics on the surface as harness-mechanism checks rather than skill scores, so a reader of the TRUTH line is not told the twin's judgement was graded.

## [major] TWIN-09 Nothing ever checks that the cage a workload actually runs in is the cage the £ selected — the estate can enact a tier no price justifies and still grade green
(original title: The £ to cage last mile is a hand-transcribed comment that nothing checks)
CLAIM: The last mile from the £ to the cage is unbound: no check anywhere in the estate compares a party's composed `proposed_tier` to the `posture.acme.io/tier` label on its governed Namespace, so ADR-0022's ratified sentence — the tier is "declared on the Namespace manifest ... rendered from the signed composed artefact" (docs/adr/0022:15-16) — is satisfied today only by a hand-typed label and a hand-written comment (driftwood gitops/apps/namespace.yaml:22-30, last written by commit 348b97c, 2026-08-28). The estate is not inert here: driftwood's scheduled `propose-tier.yml` runs `platform/wargamer/tier_pr.py run` for real every day and succeeded on 2026-09-01 and 2026-09-02 (runs 33505428933, 33626099181) — but it printed `[]` both times, because its only trigger is `wargamer.py:174` `"drift": bool(price.get("changed"))`, and `changed` is computed against the previously committed `composed/evidence.json` (composition.py:1790-1792 and 1599-1601, `_previous_prices()` at 1056-1066), never against the declaration. Two consequences follow: the proposer fires only for the single compose cycle after a price moves the tier, so an un-merged tier PR leaves a divergence that goes permanently silent; and even when it fires, `apply_tier_declaration()` (tier_pr.py:210-235) overwrites the label without ever reading it. The surviving checks stop short on both sides — `verify/pound-seam/pound_seam.py` never mentions a Namespace at all, and the live step4 proof F (verify-e2e-step4:136-142) asserts only that the label is non-empty and on the ladder. The related check ticket 09 actually resolved (item 5: only a `platform`-role party may declare `infra`) IS built and gated by `platform/distribution/verify-infra-declaration.sh`; the "Namespace-level check at compose time" sentence the auditor quoted comes from ticket 09's explicitly SUPERSEDED first draft (line 52), not its Answer, and that citation should be dropped. All three adopters currently declare `isolated` and all three price `isolated` as their strictest, so this is a missing instrument, not an observed wrong cage. Adjacent ownership exists: ticket 27 (open) holds "how a tier move prices against the ticket 08 prices[] entry" and its definition of done includes wiring a check into talk/verify-all.sh.
EVIDENCE: units/driftwood/gitops/apps/namespace.yaml sets `posture.acme.io/tier: "isolated"` with a comment reading '"isolated" is the strictest proposed_tier across today's composed/evidence.json prices[] for this party'. `grep -rln "proposed_tier" units/driftwood --include='*.py' --include='*.sh' --include='*.yml'` → no matches. On the hub, only verify/e2e/step2_reprice.py and step3_band.py read the field, and both operate on a tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() copy that is not a git repository. platform/compose/composition.py mentions `posture.acme.io/tier` only at line 3179 to assert it is ABSENT from a pod section. Ticket 09's own resolution says 'The validating deny of an unentitled tier is then a Namespace-level check at compose time' — that check does not exist.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR.md:31 principle 2 ('Everything is always caged … The £ selects the spec') and the demonstration order's claim that the twin's forward intelligence is what the platform consumes.
OWNER: new TWIN-09 — Ticket 09 (resolved) designed the Namespace-as-declaration mechanism and explicitly named "the validating deny of an unentitled tier ... a Namespace-level check at compose time" (issues/09-the-cage-ladder-v2.md:52,143). Ticket 26 (resolved, build) implemented only the runtime half: cage-tier reads namespaceObject and renders the label onto pods, fails closed to isolated, tighten-only mutation, per-tier cage-netpol reach (issues/26-the-cage-ladder-lands.md:44). Neither ticket's Answer, nor any other ticket, nor REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (no "hand-transcribed" or tier-consistency finding present), nor REGRILL-ANSWERS.md (only tangential P143 fail-closed row), mentions a compose-time check that the Namespace's posture.acme.io/tier equals the composed evidence.json proposed_tier for that party. Confirmed absent in code: grep -n "posture.acme.io/tier" on units/platform/compose/composition.py shows only an assertion the label is ABSENT from a pod section (line 3179) plus prices[] bookkeeping of proposed_tier (lines 1600/1790-1792/2774/2838/3480/3509/3544) -- never a write-to-Namespace or verify-against-Namespace step keyed on the computed tier. The named compose-time validating-deny check was never built, and ticket 26's definition-of-done/TRUTH line does not cover it. This is a real, currently-unowned gap distinct from what tickets 09 and 26 actually closed.
FIT IMPACT: The seam the whole dimension turns on — the twin computes a price, the price selects a cage, the estate enacts it — is completed by an unverified human transcription. The enactment beyond that point is real (driftwood's five-fact sample, run 33624104359, reports 16/16 rendered objects present, byte-equal and in a Flux inventory), which makes the unchecked link the weakest joint in an otherwise-observed chain.
REMEDY: One check, in the adopter's shift-left or in compose: read `proposed_tier` from composed/evidence.json, read `posture.acme.io/tier` from gitops/apps/namespace.yaml, and refuse when the label is looser than the strictest priced tier.

MINORS:
- TWIN-10 The twin's contribution to the enacted tier is redundant, and the appetite band admits only the bottom rung: Removing the twin's price line would not change driftwood's enacted cage tier, and driftwood's declared tolerance sits above only the tightest of the four residuals, so the £ ladder has exactly one admissible answer.
- TWIN-11 Only driftwood has a twin, and ticket 29 remains resolved claiming three: tuppence and ludlow carry no twin overlay of any kind, while ticket 29's Answer states 'the six standing scenarios exist per adopter'.
- TWIN-12 The honesty register cannot currently be shown to work: `does_not_do.py`'s generated register is empty because all 13 capabilities are `full`, and its own invariant skips rather than exercising it, so an empty register is indistinguishable from a register that would be empty regardless of state.
- TWIN-13 The twin's only real statistics module prices nothing in the estate: `twin/severity.py` (lognormal body spliced to a GPD tail, closed-form TVaR) is genuine and tested but is reachable only through a payload shape no adopter emits, so it contributes no number to any price.
- TWIN-14 `twin grade` still prints a green aggregate with no reference to the suite's state: GAPS 2.13 asked for the aggregate to be stamped SUITE RED when `twin verify` fails; that half was never implemented, so `twin grade` reports 73/73 full while the invariant suite is red in CI.
- TWIN-15 The deck's honesty slide names the wrong standing red: talk/narration.json's 'Honesty over green' slide says the twin's standing red is a stale drift-window sample; that invariant passes today and the actual standing red is the Flux coverage floor.
- TWIN-16 `author: "ai-generated"` is still stamped by pure arithmetic with no model call, after three tickets closed around its retirement: platform/wardley/wardley.py labels records produced by a single multiplication as machine-authored, no model call exists anywhere in the estate, and the wargamer selfcheck enforces the label's presence.
- TWIN-17 A truth-surface PASS line reads as a live Polymarket observation of an illustrative fixture: verify-news-headline-skill.sh's passing capture states a dated Polymarket price move as an observation, while the corpus and the published envelope both declare the readings illustrative and not a fetch.
- TWIN-18 GAPS 2.14's remedy is only one third applied in the estate's sole £ back-test: platform/honesty/incidents.json's top note now discloses that the loss figures are authored, but the per-org `source` fields still assert real registers for fictional companies.
- TWIN-19 The sweep's own observation record hardcodes a self-description that is already false: twin-sweep.yml writes `twin_ref: "policy-as-versioned-flux@main -- the twin package does not self-version yet (ticket 29)"`, but the twin does self-version and the gate asserts it.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- Must the twin DERIVE a probability from signals, or is 'a recorded belief, scored honestly against reality' the thing you actually want? Today `twin run` reads a YAML number and nothing infers one, and the package says so in its own output. | WHY: This single answer decides whether the twin is 70% complete or 20% complete against the north star. If derivation is required, the work reopens twin decision 11 Q1 (no arithmetic on ordinal scales), the grade-5 pricing ban, and the 'reasoning is a skill a human runs' reversal — it is a multi-month direction, not a ticket. If a recorded belief is enough, the twin is close to done and the shortfall is documentary. | CHANGES: If derivation is required: a new capability, a new eval that is not corpus-circular, and probably a model call inside a reviewed-PR skill, plus TWIN-02's provenance requirement becomes urgent. If not: NORTH-STAR.md:19 and :35 are amended to say 'recorded beliefs scored under proper scoring rules', the deck's beat-5 title changes, and TWIN-01 stops being a finding at all.
- Should a world model's beliefs carry a required evidence grade and basis, the way a valuation and a causal edge already do? Today the schema will accept any probability with no source, and 19 of 20 world models in the corpus carry none. | WHY: It is the one place the twin's otherwise-uniform 'how we know travels with the claim' discipline has a hole, and it is the hole under the only number the scoring apparatus grades. It is also cheap: the closed-schema machinery already exists. | CHANGES: Adding it forces a grade and a basis onto reference-map.yaml's 13 driftwood beliefs and Royal Mail's market-consensus 0.05 — which will make several of them visibly grade 4 or 5, and grade 5 may not price. That is either exactly the disclosure you want or a change to what the pricing threshold means for beliefs; both are decisions.
- Is it acceptable for the twin's forward-intel feed to remain outside every signed tag and outside `inherits[]`, so that its absence is silent and its bytes carry no gitsign signature — while it produces driftwood's largest price line? | WHY: It is the only price in the estate that can vanish without a refusal, and driftwood's own signed party.yaml already asserts that the tag is what signs it. The estate's whole contract with adopters is that a missing instrument refuses. | CHANGES: If not acceptable: cut twin/v0.1.0 and a driftwood forward-intel release (ticket 64), and make composition raise `Refused` when a declared `publishes[]` feed is absent. If acceptable as a deliberate exception for a self-published feed, that exception should be written into ADR-0021 and named on the gate, because today it reads as an oversight rather than a decision.
- What cage does the twin itself run inside, on the ticket 09 ladder, at what price? Ticket 30 is open and frontier, and today there is no twin cage in any adopter — the Article 22 floor exists only as `enact_guard.py`'s command-pattern net and the absence of a merge function. | WHY: NORTH-STAR principle 2 says everything is always caged, the twin included, and that the £ selects the spec. The twin is the one actor in the estate whose cage is a Python hook on the operator's laptop rather than a priced, versioned, enacted spec — and the hook's own docstring records two dated bugs where it silently admitted exactly what it exists to refuse. | CHANGES: It determines whether ticket 30 is a small artefact (a twin-self cage spec plus a price in the adopter's overlay, wired into verify-all.sh) or a real piece of infrastructure (a caged runtime for the agent). It also determines whether the Article 22 floor is a property the estate can demonstrate or only assert.
- How do you want the twin's judgement quality graded, given that all seven metrics score 1.000 on 46 items drawn from the same subjects the heuristics were fitted to, and evolution-judge's eval is literally a lookup table being asked to reproduce its own lookup? | WHY: verify-twin-evals.sh is the twin's only quality signal on the truth surface and it is structurally incapable of moving. The disclosure is exemplary; the measurement is not a measurement. | CHANGES: A held-out corpus (even five items from a fifth org) breaks the tautology and makes the metric able to fall — which is what ticket 29 promised. Alternatively, relabel the seven metrics on the surface as harness-mechanism checks, which is honest but removes the twin's only claimed quality evidence and should be a deliberate choice, not a drift.
- Should NORTH-STAR §4 step 5 be restated? The build deliberately reversed its mechanism — driftwood's signals.yaml declares niobium unbound precisely so the twin cannot decide on the clock — but the ambition document still describes the twin playing the niobium headline forward on its schedule, and the deck's beat 5 is titled accordingly. | WHY: The reversal is well argued and recorded in ticket 23, but the ambition and the demonstration order were never updated, so the estate is measured against a step it decided not to build. That is exactly the drift class the 2026-08-27 review existed to catch. | CHANGES: If restated: step 5 becomes 'a human runs the classify-and-judge skill and the reviewed PR lands a claim', ticket 51's scope narrows, and verify-e2e-step5 can be rewritten to check that a real claim file exists — which would currently be red and honest. If not restated: the clock must gain a scoring path, which collides with the 'reasoning is a skill a human runs' reversal.

# security-spine
