VERDICT: The artefact-signing spine is genuinely fit and is the strongest thing in the estate: 24 real keyless tags with Rekor entries, per-repo anchored identity pins that are negatively tested and enforced at four independent places (release.yml, the adopter gate, five-facts.py, the cluster boundary), no key material committed, and a cluster-side verifier whose design decisions — verify at the signed tagger time, refuse an unanchored regexp before using it, never turn could-not-look into a verdict, prove by AST walk that it cannot sign — are better than most production systems. Against NORTH-STAR §2's "consumed only through a pinned, signed dependency", the platform→adopter and nist→adopter legs are proven on the citable run. The enforcement spine is not fit yet, for three non-overlapping reasons. First, the one control that actually gates enactment is broken in a way its own proof cannot see: verify_gitsign.py evaluates the Fulcio chain at the tag's tagger second, which is systematically at or below the certificate's notBefore, so it rejects 5 of 24 genuine estate tags (including ico v3.0.0, driftwood v1.1.0 and ludlow v1.1.0, all live pins) while its own fixture happens to be delta-0 and passes. Second, everything about actor attestation — SPIRE, mTLS, SPIFFE authz, OpenBao, device SVIDs, human login — has never been observed running on any citable run (all six scripts SKIP on "kind cluster 'driftwood' is not listed"), and federation is one trust domain with no peer and the peers' anchors held as literals in the platform's own tree, which is the tenant shape §2 forbids. Third, the governance layer principles 4 and 5 rest on — "bump only by reviewed PR", "a human merges" — has no enforcement point anywhere: zero rulesets and zero branch protection on all nine repos, verified live, plus an accepted release/<M>.<m>.x signing path that touches main at all and that nothing covers. What would make it fit, in order: (1) fix the tagger/notBefore race and add a delta-−1 tag to the fixture corpus — a day's work that unblocks four of run-21's seven reds; (2) protect main and release/*.x on all nine repos with a required review, converting principles 4 and 5 from convention to mechanism; (3) pin the Flux install and the Renovate install like every other tool — hours; (4) sign the proposer's commit and delete the hardcoded signed:True before step 3 ever fires, so no fabricated provenance can enter a real proposal; (5) either land the party/schema.json patch so federation becomes a party-owned signed fact, or write down that federation is out of scope for this build. Items 2 and 5 are owner decisions; the rest are mechanical and the estate already does each of them correctly somewhere else in the same repository.

STRENGTHS:
- 24 of 24 estate tags are real keyless gitsign signatures with real Rekor entries under per-repo anchored identity pins. Re-derived on one: `cd units/ico && gitsign verify-tag v3.0.0 --certificate-identity-regexp='^https://github\.com/policy-as-versioned-ico/ico/\.github/workflows/cut-release\.yml@refs/heads/(main|release/[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.x)$' --certificate-oidc-issuer=https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com` -> `Validated Git signature: true / Validated Rekor entry: true / Validated Certificate claims: true`.
- The identity pins are correct, per-repo, and negatively tested, and all six identity-regexp checks are PASS on the citable run 21. Every units/<u>/.github/workflows/release.yml carries the identical anchored shape with only the org/repo substituted. units/driftwood/scripts/verify-identity-regexp.sh:32-46 runs REAL `gitsign verify-tag` against every real v*.*.* tag and fails the script if any is rejected — an estate observation, not a selfcheck. Run-21 capture .estate-clone_driftwood_scripts_verify-identity-regexp.out ends `PASS: EXPECTED_IDENTITY_REGEXP matches main + release/<major>.<minor>.x only, anchored to this repo.`
- verify_gitsign.py's trust decisions are principled: it evaluates the chain at the tagger timestamp inside the SIGNED payload rather than 'now' (lines 20-25, 191-196); it REFUSES an unanchored identity regexp before using it (lines 213-217, 'an unanchored pattern matches a substring of any identity and pins nothing'); it carries a genuine tri-state where CouldNotLook is never a pass and a gate it holds stays held while it cannot look (lines 88-92, 300-308, 335-341); and units/platform/verify-source-verification.sh:57-140 proves it never signs by an AST walk over its own subprocess argv — added 2026-08-29 after a real `subprocess.run(["git","tag","-s",tag])` walked past the previous shell-shaped grep.
- A pod cannot pick its own tier, and three named live defects in that area were found and closed. units/driftwood/composed/policies/v4.0.0/cage-tier.yaml:30-37 takes the tier from `namespaceObject` and CLOBBERS the pod's own posture.acme.io/tier label; the fallback is fail-closed (`variables.nsTier in [...] ? variables.nsTier : (variables.nsGoverned ? 'isolated' : 'baseline')`); the mutation is tighten-only (ORed hardening booleans, quantity() min on cpu/mem, hostNetwork/hostPID/hostIPC clobbered false); posture-trust-boundary-4-0-0 Denies a mismatched posture label; governed-namespace-requires-claim Denies a claim-less pod in a governed namespace (units/platform/distribution/versions.yaml:155-176). driftwood's governed namespace declares posture.acme.io/tier: "isolated" (gitops/apps/namespace.yaml:30) and its pod carries no tier label at all.
- The observation cage is real code that demonstrably bites. .github/workflows/truth.yml:108-162 does `git reset -q` FIRST (fixing a reproduced 2026-08-28 defect where staged-and-clean entries rode along), stages only OBSERVATION_LANE, asserts the STAGED SET against the same env list rather than a second regex, then asserts the tree clean outside the lane. Proof it bites rather than rubber-stamps: hub truth.yml has failed 17 consecutive runs on `::error::the scheduled truth run left a change outside the observation lane`.
- Tool supply chain is pinned by version AND sha256 nearly everywhere: gitsign 0.17.1/69213a8a…, kyverno 1.18.2/cb2feb83…, cosign 3.1.3/4629c757… (truth.yml:38-49); kind, flux, kyverno and flux-operator all version+digest in units/driftwood/.github/workflows/drift-sample.yml:47-61, whose header states the rule outright ('A `curl | bash` installer would put an unreviewed script inside the identity that signs this repository's observation commits'). Only two exceptions exist estate-wide, both named as findings.
- No private key material or credential literal is committed anywhere. `grep -rln "BEGIN .*PRIVATE KEY"` over all eight unit clones and the hub (excluding .git) returns zero; units/ico/schema/keys/ and units/platform/feeds/keys/ hold only *.pub.pem; no ghp_/github_pat_/AKIA literal found.
- twin/enact_guard.py:94 now fails closed (`DEFAULT_MODE = "operations"`), flipped 2026-08-29 after discovering that thirteen tests asserting guard behaviour passed only because an autouse fixture forced a mode the shipped default did not use. The module names its own ceiling honestly rather than dressing the keyword screen up as a proof (docstring lines 30-35).
- truth.yml:59-66 deliberately withholds the write credential from the untrusted scripts: `persist-credentials: false` with a written rationale, and GH_TOKEN scoped to the cage step's env: only. This is a real, deliberate mitigation and better than the GitHub default, even though its residual (SS-07) is that the separation is process-level rather than a boundary.

## [major] SS-02 Only the artefact half of "every actor is attestable" is proven on the clock; the actor half — workloads, humans, devices, the agent — has never been observed anywhere, and a resolved ticket rests on a check that has never been able to look
(original title: The entire identity substrate is asserted structurally and has never been observed running on any citable run)
CLAIM: No citable TRUTH run has ever carried a green live identity tail: on run 21 (and on every capture back to run 9) five identity-plane scripts — platform/identity/verify-identity.sh, platform/identity/verify-federation.sh, platform/access/verify-access.sh, platform/eud/verify-eud.sh and platform/verify-source-verification.sh — grade SKIP with the same substrate reason, "kind cluster 'driftwood' is not listed by kind get clusters", so no SVID, mTLS handshake, SPIFFE-principal authorization, live OpenBao jwt config, Pomerium/Dex login, device SVID or gitsign-verifier pod has ever been observed on a source NORTH-STAR §5 permits a document to cite. The sixth script named in the original finding, break-glass/verify-break-glass.sh, grades PASS and makes no live claim, so "all six grade SKIP" is wrong. The substrate is not merely "asserted structurally": it has been observed running off-clock — a real pod held spiffe://acme.internal/posture/2.0.0/ns/tuppence-reset/sa/teller-current on 2026-08-20, reach was proven both ways (200/403), and three bugs were found by running it (verify-identity.sh:91-93, :116-118, :261-263; G-drift-findings.md:1245) — but that evidence predates the truth surface and is uncitable under §5. The missing-cluster SKIP is an instrument condition shared by ten scripts on run 21, not an identity-specific one; what is identity-specific is that no cluster-free substitute and no cluster-bearing lane was ever built for it. Ticket 16 Q3(a)'s ephemeral-KinD lane installs no identity components (grep for spire/istio across units/driftwood/.github/workflows/ returns nothing), ticket 60 built cluster-free grading only for the reconcile and step-4 facts, and ticket 68 (open) explicitly accepts "names the absent cluster as a could-not-look" as done — so the statement "the identity substrate has never been observed live on a citable run" is unowned, while the underlying no-cluster fact is owned and decided (ticket 20 facts 2026-08-28, decision D4). Fit impact stands as written: artefact attestation is proven on the clock, actor attestation is not, so principle 6 is half-demonstrated and the §4 ordering cannot reach steps needing a live identity plane. The SKIP lines are honest per §5 — they open with "SKIP: offline proof holds; live tail could not look" and are themselves the 2026-08-28 fix for a former false PASS — so the criticism of their trailing declarative clause should be dropped to a note that the clause is the unmade claim and is easy to misquote out of context.
EVIDENCE: Last lines from origin/main:talk/captures/, run 21: `.estate-clone_platform_identity_verify-identity.out` -> `SKIP: offline proof holds; live tail could not look: kind cluster 'driftwood' is not listed by kind get clusters — SPIRE is Istio's CA, mTLS STRICT, authz by SPIFFE principal…`; `.estate-clone_platform_identity_verify-federation.out` -> same SKIP; `.estate-clone_platform_verify-source-verification.out` -> same SKIP; `.estate-clone_platform_access_verify-access.out` -> same SKIP; `.estate-clone_platform_eud_verify-eud.out` -> same SKIP; `.estate-clone_platform_break-glass_verify-break-glass.out` -> `verify-break-glass: done (offline; rides on the access plane…)`.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §1 ('every actor is attestable'), §3 principle 2 ('a workload, a human, a device, a model action and the twin itself each run inside a cage'), §3 principle 6, §5 ('every live tail has exactly three outcomes')
OWNER: new SS-02 — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (12, 26, 32, 03, 16, 20, 57, 60), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (C1, M1-M18, Refuted claims), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, NORTH-STAR §6, map's Out of scope, and docs/adr/. No hit owns the specific claim "the identity substrate has never been observed live on any citable TRUTH run."

Closest candidates, all distinguishable:
- Ticket 32 ("Build the identity substrate package") is resolved 2026-08-29, and its Answer claims OpenBao JWT login was proven live once and the checks were wired into verify-all.sh with "the TRUTH line of 2026-08-29" as the record. But that one-time proof run is not the same as the SS-02 claim, which is about the ongoing citable CI truth surface (run 21, and presumably every other CI run) structurally lacking a kind cluster, so all six identity verifiers SKIP every time. Ticket 32's Answer does not address, and is not falsified by, "no citable run has it running" — it just doesn't own that statement. This is a resolved-ticket-adjacent-but-not-owning case, not a resolved-but-still-true case, since ticket 32 never claimed the CI/citable lane itself would show identity live.
- REVIEW-2026-08-31 M11(3) notes "Federation has no peer: no second SPIRE trust domain anywhere" — narrower (federation only, one of six scripts) and already folded into ticket 58/26.
- REVIEW-2026-08-31 M5/M6 document other structural could-not-look defects in the citable lane (missing jsonschema install, no GitHub credential) but do not mention the identity/kind-cluster SKIPs at all.
- Ticket 12's "Facts found" section (2026-08-28, pre-dates ticket 32's build) documents live identity substrate defects (CrashLoop, Pomerium never installed, no device attestation, OpenBao dev mode) against a `.estate-clone/` live cluster read — this is about defects in a live cluster the reviewer could reach, not about the citable-run SKIP pattern SS-02 describes.
- No GAPS/REGRILL entry names "identity has never been observed on a citable run" as a decided/accepted scope item; the closest analog (three publisher clocks "observe themselves," refuted-claims list) is a different, already-decided scope carve-out unrelated to identity.

Net: this is a new finding. It is not contradicted by any Refuted-claims entry, not superseded by a REGRILL answer, and not covered by an open or resolved ticket.
FIT IMPACT: Artefact attestation is proven on the clock; ACTOR attestation (workloads, humans, devices) is not proven anywhere citable. Principle 6 is therefore half-demonstrated, and NORTH-STAR §4's demonstration ordering cannot reach steps that depend on a live identity plane. The SKIPs are honest per §5, so this is a coverage shortfall rather than a dishonesty defect — but the SKIP reason lines end in declarative sentences ('SPIRE is Istio's CA, mTLS STRICT…') that read as claims when a capture is grepped for its tail.
REMEDY: Decide whether the identity plane is in scope for the citable surface at all. If yes, give it an ephemeral-cluster lane of the shape drift-sample.yml already proves works (fresh KinD per run, bounded waits, delete on always) so the live tails can grade for real. If no, record that decision in NORTH-STAR §6 and change the SKIP reason lines so they state the absence rather than restating the claim.

## [minor] SS-03 Anti-tenancy is proven where §2 makes it load-bearing (signed artefacts, pinned dependencies); the unbuilt workload-identity layer leaves the platform holding unsigned facts about three adopters — declared, refused-to-pass, and owned by ticket 68
(original title: Federation is aspirational and the peers' trust anchors are literals held in the platform's own tree — the tenant shape §2 forbids)
CLAIM: Federation is declared, not live, and the peers' trust anchors are literals in the platform's own tree. There is one trust domain, `acme.internal` (units/platform/identity/spire/helmrelease.yaml:55), with no `federation` key in the SPIRE values at all, so no bundle endpoint is served. Only driftwood has ever run SPIRE: verify-federation.sh:21-23 records that platform runs no live cluster in this estate and tuppence/ludlow carry no SPIRE CRDs — three of the four "cluster-running" parties, not two. Because party/schema.json is `additionalProperties: false` and party_artefact.py:185-188 reads its allowed top-level keys straight from it, a party.yaml carrying the three decided fields is REFUSED today (verified by running validate_schema: "unknown top-level field 'trust_domain'", "unknown top-level field 'bundle_endpoint'"); none of the eight party artefacts carries them, so all twelve ClusterFederatedTrustDomain objects — each peer's trust_domain and bundle_endpoint — are authored by the platform in platform/identity/federation/, about parties that never signed them. NORTH-STAR §2's "each participant signs its own artefacts" is unmet in the one place identity is meant to prove it, and units/tuppence/reset/up.sh:12 (`CTX=kind-driftwood`) makes it sharper still: tuppence's flagship sensitive service runs on driftwood's cluster under driftwood's trust root, a literal tenancy.

The estate's honesty here is exemplary: verify-federation.sh refuses to pass, exits 3, and names all three blockers in code. Two corrections to the original finding's framing. First, the two cited assertions (verify-identity.sh:50 and verify-federation.sh:85-98) are OFFLINE structural checks over YAML files, not live checks — they still assert mutually exclusive worlds, in that realising federation would fail line 50, but no cluster is read by either. Second, and materially: the fix is NOT unowned. Ticket 68 ("Federation gets its peer", Status: open, Type: task (AFK)) names the patch verbatim — "Add `trust_domain`, `bundle_endpoint` and `federates_with[]` to party/schema.json … declare both domains on the two signed party.yaml files, stand up the second trust domain on driftwood's cluster, and add a verify script that grades the live bundle exchange with three outcomes" — and map.md:100 records it as graduated from ticket 58 on 2026-08-31. The auditor cited REVIEW-2026-08-31's then-current "Owning ticket: none → ticket 58, then builds" as if it still stood. The residual is a tracked, specified, open build ticket resting on a decision the owner took only on a bare "Agree" — which argues for downgrading this from major to a tracked gap, not for treating the schema patch as an orphan. Also: TRUST_DOMAIN is at tuppence/reset/reach.py:29, not :16; and the run-21 SKIP reason is the absent kind cluster, not the three federation blockers, so that capture does not evidence them.
EVIDENCE: units/platform/identity/README.md:74-86 and units/platform/identity/federation/driftwood.yaml:12-31 name three blockers: driftwood's live SPIRE still runs the estate-wide `acme.internal`; tuppence and ludlow run KinD with no SPIRE; `spire-server.federation.enabled` is off so no bundle endpoint is served. Confirmed in code that the two live checks assert mutually exclusive worlds: units/platform/identity/verify-identity.sh:50 asserts `td == "acme.internal"` (single domain) while verify-federation.sh:86-97 asserts twelve objects across four `<party>.acme.internal` domains. Schema blocker, quoted from identity/README.md:90-99: 'party/schema.json does not accept them yet — it is additionalProperties: false and party_artefact.py reads its allowed top-level keys straight out of that file, so adding the keys today makes every party artefact REFUSED… which is exactly the "demand from a literal" shape H8-05 already names as a defect.' Independently confirmed: `grep -rn trust_domain` across all three adopter clones returns nothing; the only trust-domain-like constant is `TRUST_DOMAIN = "acme.internal"` at units/tuppence/reset/reach.py:16. verify-federation.sh exits 3 rather than passing (run-21 capture confirms SKIP).
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2 ('Nothing in the eco-system is a tenant of anything else'; 'each participant … signs its own artefacts'), §3 principle 6
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 68 — Chain: REVIEW-2026-08-31 M11(3) (federation orphan gap, incl. party/schema.json refusing trust_domain/bundle_endpoint/federates_with) -> ticket 58 (grilling) Q3, recommendation (a) driftwood becomes second SPIRE trust domain paired with platform, three fields landed on both signed party.yaml files -> owner replied bare "Agree", recorded PROVISIONAL per map's bare-agree rule (issues/58-...md:27-31) -> graduated to ticket 68 "Federation gets its peer" (issues/68-federation-gets-its-peer.md), Status: open, Blocked by: none. Ticket 68's Question text names exactly the finding's blocker: "Add trust_domain, bundle_endpoint and federates_with[] to party/schema.json ... declare both domains on the two signed party.yaml files, stand up the second trust domain on driftwood's cluster, and add a verify script that grades the live bundle exchange". So SS-03 is not new and not unowned as claimed in "Claimed ownership" — ticket 68 is the specific build ticket the finding says doesn't exist. Note the decision is still PROVISIONAL (bare agree, not ratified), so the finding's characterization of urgency/§2 violation remains substantively true, but the ownership gap it alleges (no build ticket owns the schema patch) is factually wrong: ticket 68 owns it and is open/unblocked, i.e. not stalled behind anything unnamed.
FIT IMPACT: The estate's honesty here is exemplary — verify-federation.sh refuses to pass and names all three reasons. The residual is that the one small, named change that would make federation a party-owned signed fact (rather than a platform-authored literal about someone else) is unowned and blocking, so §2's anti-tenancy rule is violated in the one place identity is supposed to prove it.
REMEDY: Either (a) patch party/schema.json to accept trust_domain, bundle_endpoint and federates_with[], migrate the four party.yaml files, and delete the literals from platform/identity/federation/ — small and named; or (b) record in NORTH-STAR §6 that federation is explicitly out for this build, and mark ADR/README accordingly so the aspiration stops reading as pending work. Which one is an owner call.

## [major] SS-04 "Reviewed PR" and "a human merges" are unverifiable assertions, not checkable properties — and ADR-0001's recorded risk acceptance rests on a forge-immutability leg that exists on none of the nine repos
(original title: The signature attests a workflow path, not a review — and there is zero branch protection and zero rulesets on all nine repositories)
CLAIM: The signature attests a workflow path, not a review — and there is zero branch protection, zero rulesets, zero environment protection and zero tag protection on all nine repositories, a regression from the 2022-thesis org which still has both.

Anyone with push access to a unit repo can rewrite cut-release.yml, push it to main unreviewed, dispatch it, and produce a tag whose certificate every consumer's identity pin accepts; nothing on GitHub prevents this in any of the nine repositories. Verified live: `rulesets` -> `[]` on all nine, including with `?includes_parents=true` (so no org-level ruleset applies either); `branches/main/protection` -> 404 "Branch not protected" on all nine; `environments` -> total_count 0 and `tags/protection` -> 404 on all eight units. The pin is a path, verified from a real certificate rather than from doctrine: the SAN of the cert on platform's signed tag v2.0.1 is `URI:https://github.com/policy-as-versioned-platform/platform/.github/workflows/cut-release.yml@refs/heads/main`, and the only flags any verifier passes are `--certificate-identity-regexp` and `--certificate-oidc-issuer` (release.yml:99-100, adopter-gate.py:255-257) — the cert's Build Config Digest (OID 1.3.6.1.4.1.57264.1.19) is present but read by nothing. The in-workflow publisher gate is no defence, because it lives in the file the attacker rewrites and signs with the same identity.

Two corrections to the original finding. (a) The PR corroboration is overstated: 46 is the closed non-Renovate count across the nine, but only 35 were actually merged — of those 35, all 35 have author == mergedBy (`chrisns`). (b) The blast radius today is one human plus any workflow holding `contents: write`: platform and driftwood each list exactly one collaborator.

One amplification that removes the obvious defence. Public visibility is not the reason there are no rulesets — that limitation applies only to PUSH rulesets, as units/*/.github/rulesets/README.md:21-23 already states correctly. In the same org as the hub, the public repo `fleet` carries an active branch ruleset `require-pr-gate` on refs/heads/main (required status check `gate`, strict), and policy, ledger, api, storefront and reports each carry an active tag ruleset `protect-release-tags` on refs/tags/v* (deletion, non_fast_forward). So the enforcement the nine lack is applicable, and was in force in the 2022 thesis org. This also names a second unguarded surface: with no tag ruleset, a release tag on any of the nine can be deleted and re-pushed to a different commit. Additionally, the prepared observation-lane ruleset would not have closed this even if applied, because its `bypass_actors` grants the org admin an always-bypass (rulesets/README.md:58-60).

Fit impact stands as written: principles 4 and 5 are conventions with no enforcement point. The identity pin does real work against a FOREIGN signer — driftwood/drift/five-facts.py:764 tests exactly that, against `someone-else/x` — and no work against the repo's own writer. No ticket owns branch or tag protection across the nine; tickets 70, 10 and 57 are all scoped to the scheduled identity's observation lane.
EVIDENCE: Verified live, read-only, on all nine: `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-<u>/<u>/rulesets` -> `[]` for platform, nist, ico, driftwood, tuppence, ludlow, feeds, insurer AND the hub; `gh api repos/.../branches/main/protection` -> `404 {"message":"Branch not protected"}` for all nine. The GitHub OIDC subject is the workflow FILE PATH at a ref (units/platform/.github/workflows/release.yml:54 pins `…/cut-release\.yml@refs/heads/(main|release/…)$`), not the workflow's content, so the certificate attests only that a run executed that path on that branch. Corroborating (not re-derived by me): the github-live map records all 46 closed non-Renovate PRs across the nine repos as authored AND merged by the same identity.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 4 ('bump only by reviewed PR'), §3 principle 5 ('A human merges. Nothing timed ever changes a verdict on its own'), §4 step 3
OWNER: new none — No ticket, ADR, or REGRILL answer owns this. Searched: .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (grep for branch protection/rulesets hits only 70, 10, 57 — none address write-path/push-access branch protection); REVIEW-2026-08-31.md M14 mentions "hub main has no protection" but only in the unrelated context of a TRUTH-line fall not blocking release (owned by open ticket 59) — not about push access to cut-release.yml or the OIDC-subject-is-path-not-content gap. M11(4)/ticket 58 Q4/ADR-0023/ADR-0024/units/*/.github/rulesets/README.md cover only the observation-lane server-side cage scoped to the scheduled CLOCK identity (explicitly acknowledged by the finding's own "Claimed ownership" note) — they say nothing about the human/writer identity or general branch protection on main across the nine repos. REVIEW-2026-08-31's "Refuted claims" list (grep, none matched: "reviewed PR", "branch protection", "push access", "OIDC", "identity pin", "workflow path"). NORTH-STAR §3 principles 4/5 and §4 step 3 are cited by the finding itself as the ambition these gaps violate, not as coverage. No GAPS.md or REPORT.md row addresses it either. This is a genuinely new, unowned finding.
FIT IMPACT: Principles 4 and 5 are, today, conventions with no enforcement point. The identity pin does real work against a FOREIGN signer (a different org cannot mint an accepted tag) and no work against the repo's own writer. Every claim of the form 'bumped only by reviewed PR' in the tickets and the deck is an assertion about how the owner works, not a property of the system.
REMEDY: Apply branch protection or a branch ruleset on main in all nine repos requiring a pull request and at least one approving review from someone other than the author, and cover release/*.x (see SS-05). If a single-operator estate cannot satisfy 'a reviewer other than the author', say so explicitly in NORTH-STAR §6 rather than leaving principle 5 reading as enforced.

## [minor] SS-05 The maintenance-branch signing path principle 4 deliberately admits is unexercised, stale and governed by nothing — fixing unprotected `main` without covering `release/*` would be half a fix
(original title: release/<M>.<m>.x is an accepted signing branch, one exists, and nothing covers it)
CLAIM: Every unit's EXPECTED_IDENTITY_REGEXP accepts a tag signed from a release/<major>.<minor>.x branch — in each unit's own release.yml and in the three adopters' cross-repo pins (driftwood/tuppence/ludlow platform-pin.yaml and shift-left.yml) — platform has such a branch today (release/2.0.x, carrying a working workflow_dispatch cut-release.yml with no ref guard), and no ruleset or branch protection exists on any ref of any of the eight repos. A dispatch on that branch still runs the publisher gate but needs no review and touches main not at all, producing a tag whose signature every consumer accepts. Two things the gate does not do: it never checks who may write to or dispatch from that ref (verify-certificate-identity-regexp.sh:23 names release/2.0.x only to assert the regexp ACCEPTS it, and grades that PASS), and it never reads that branch at all — clone-estate.sh:38 clones default branches only, and run 21 records platform=46cd775 = origin/main, not release/2.0.x = 860e744 — so such a release is invisible to the truth surface, a present-tense difference from an unprotected main rather than a gap conditional on SS-04 being fixed. It is not an automatic path into a cage: all three adopters set renovate automerge:false, so a pin bump still needs a human merge.
EVIDENCE: All eight release.yml pins end `@refs/heads/(main|release/[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.x)$` (units/platform/.github/workflows/release.yml:54 and the seven siblings). Live: `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-platform/platform/branches --jq '[.[].name]|join(",")'` -> `ecosystem/thin-slice,main,observations,policy-composition/tickets-09-16-wip,release/2.0.x,renovate/configure,repair/…` — release/2.0.x exists. cut-release.yml is workflow_dispatch, which accepts any branch. SS-04's ruleset query returns [] for every ref in every repo, so the branch class is covered by nothing.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 4 ('Older lines are patchable' — the reason the pattern is right — and 'bump only by reviewed PR' — the reason the gap matters)
OWNER: new none — No ticket, ADR, REGRILL answer, or REVIEW-2026-08-31 item owns this. Searched: .scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md for release-branch/ruleset/EXPECTED_IDENTITY_REGEXP language — only ticket 57 (feeds/insurer registration, mentions the regex as an existing fact while fixing the default-branch mismatch) and ticket 58 (four architectural gaps) surface, but ticket 58's ruleset decision item (4) is scoped to the observation-lane cage on public repos (ADR-0024/ADR-0023, `units/*/.github/rulesets/README.md`), a different ruleset than main/release-branch signing protection. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md M14 says "hub main has no protection" but that's about the hub's own drift-fall detection signal, not unit-repo signing-branch coverage, and its Refuted-claims list has nothing matching. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md #29 addresses author/merger identity separation for machine changes reaching main, not branch-protection/ruleset enforcement of the accepted signing-branch class, and doesn't mention release/N.M.x at all. NORTH-STAR §3 principle 4 ("bump only by reviewed PR") is the ambition this finding is measured against, matching the sibling finding SS-04 in this same review pass (units/*/.github/rulesets/, gh api .../rulesets and .../branches/main/protection, both confirmed empty/404 across all 9 repos) — SS-05 extends SS-04's "no branch protection anywhere" claim with the concrete live release/2.0.x branch and cut-release.yml's unrestricted workflow_dispatch. SS-04 itself also appears unowned (not in any ticket, not in the Refuted list) — worth flagging alongside SS-05 to whoever aggregates this review, since SS-05 is effectively SS-04's sharper, evidenced instance rather than a fully independent gap.
FIT IMPACT: Even if SS-04 were fixed by protecting main, this would remain an unprotected signing path that every consumer trusts. It is a second front door with the same key and no lock, and no check in the 84-script gate names it.
REMEDY: Extend whatever protection SS-04 lands to the release/*.x ref pattern, and add an assertion to the existing verify-certificate-identity-regexp.sh family that every branch matching the accepted pattern in the live repo is protected — so the pin and the protection cannot drift apart.

## [major] SS-06 The gate breaks the estate's own "everything the gate observes with is pinned" rule inside the very identity that signs the only citable number
(original title: truth.yml pipes an unpinned remote script to sudo bash in the job that signs and pushes the estate's only citable artefact)
CLAIM: truth.yml:91 pipes an unpinned remote installer to root — `curl -s https://fluxcd.io/install.sh | sudo bash` — inside the single `gate` job that, eight steps later, gitsign-signs and pushes talk/truth.log with `github.token` (truth.yml:153-163). It is the only curl-to-shell in the hub or in any of the eight unit repos, and it sits directly under its own step comment (truth.yml:79-81) that "Every tool the gate observes with is pinned by version AND checksum", between two steps that do verify sha256sums (84-89). Three unit workflows state the opposite rule and implement it: driftwood/.github/workflows/drift-sample.yml:44-46 ("A `curl | bash` installer would put an unreviewed script inside the identity that signs this repository's observation commits"), pinning FLUX_VERSION 2.9.3 with FLUX_SHA256 eae4e860... at :49-50 and checksum-verifying at :103-104 (same text at ludlow and tuppence :45). The unpinnedness is live, not theoretical: run 21's log (run id 33616685427, hub 7b92990) shows the installer resolving `releases/latest` and "Using 2.9.5 as release", so the gate ran flux 2.9.5 against sample lanes pinned to 2.9.3. Two corrections to the original framing: (i) no verify script in the hub or in any of the eight units invokes the flux CLI (verify-all.sh runs only verify*.sh; `flux <subcommand>` appears only in up.sh files that no verify script calls), so the TRUTH number does not today vary with the flux version — the reproducibility harm is latent, and the concrete defect is a step named "the flux CLIs the offline proofs call" installing a tool nothing calls, at root, unpinned; (ii) the installer does verify its own tarball against upstream checksums, so the unreviewed element is the version-selecting script itself, not the binary. Additionally, `curl -s` carries no `--fail` and no `-L`, so an error-page body would be executed by root's bash and an empty body would leave flux absent with the step still green. No ticket owns it; ticket 54 ("The gate observes with the estate's own toolchain", resolved) pinned kyverno and cosign under exactly this rule and never named flux. Severity major stands, on the root-before-the-signing-and-push mechanism (SS-07) rather than on demonstrated grading variance.
EVIDENCE: .github/workflows/truth.yml:80-92. The step's own comment reads 'Every tool the gate observes with is pinned by version AND checksum, like gitsign above. An unpinned tool makes the number unreproducible: the same estate grades differently on two days for a reason no TRUTH line records.' The step then does `echo "${KYVERNO_SHA256}  kyverno.tgz" | sha256sum -c -`, `echo "${COSIGN_SHA256}  cosign" | sha256sum -c -`, and finally `curl -s https://fluxcd.io/install.sh | sudo bash`. The estate's own rule is stated at units/driftwood/.github/workflows/drift-sample.yml:48-50: 'A `curl | bash` installer would put an unreviewed script inside the identity that signs this repository's observation commits.' The correct pinned form already exists in the estate: drift-sample.yml:53-56 pins `FLUX_VERSION: 2.9.3 / FLUX_SHA256: eae4e8608c0ade2bf4e8dec1669dbb6b0c28b5822b252d97feccfb4fb1181fd2`.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5 ('The number and its date are recorded on every run' — reproducibility), §3 principle 6 ('a signature that says what it does and does not assert')
OWNER: new issues/54-the-gate-observes-with-the-estate-s-own-toolchain.md (adjacent, does not cover) — Ticket 54 (resolved 2026-08-31) fixed kyverno pinning (1.18.2+sha256) and cosign pinning (3.1.3+sha256) in this exact same workflow step, and its own Done= criteria and Answer only mention kyverno/cosign/jsonschema — it never touches the flux line. Verified current truth.yml:91 (checked out tree, matches evidence's cited line numbers 80-92) still runs `curl -s https://fluxcd.io/install.sh | sudo bash`, unpinned, three lines below the pinning comment that ticket 54's Answer says now governs "every tool" — so the comment's claim is inaccurate re: Flux. Not in REVIEW-2026-08-31's Refuted-claims list (grep for flux/install.sh/curl found nothing there or in REGRILL-ANSWERS.md). No ticket search hit (grep -rl "truth.yml" across issues/ gave 12 tickets; none of their content, including 54's, addresses Flux install). This is a real, currently-true gap that escaped ticket 54's otherwise-matching fix — new finding, not owned.
FIT IMPACT: Two consequences. (a) The TRUTH number is not reproducible with respect to the Flux CLI version, contradicting the comment immediately above it and undermining §5's premise that the number is the citable thing. (b) A compromise of that installer reaches root on the runner before the cage step uses GH_TOKEN to push the hub's main, which is the concrete mechanism behind SS-07.
REMEDY: Copy drift-sample.yml's pinned tarball form verbatim — the version and digest are already known in this estate. This is a five-line change and removes both the reproducibility gap and the root-code-execution step.

## [major] SS-07 The only citable number is signed and pushed from the same process that just ran eight other orgs' unpinned code, while the clock records a separation guarantee it does not have
(original title: The hub's write credential is separated from 84 untrusted foreign scripts by process convention, and the comment claims a boundary that does not exist)
CLAIM: The hub's write credential is separated from the gate's untrusted foreign scripts by process convention, not by a boundary, and the comment asserts a property the design does not have. truth.yml runs one job: the gate step (.github/workflows/truth.yml:92-98) runs `bash talk/verify-all.sh`, which discovers 84 verify*.sh scripts — 68 of them cloned unpinned off eight other orgs' default branches into .estate-clone/ (clone-estate.sh:22,28,58), 16 the hub's own under verify/ — and runs each as plain `bash` (talk/verify-all.sh:47,60), unsandboxed, as the runner user, in a job whose earlier steps use passwordless sudo (truth.yml:75,86,90,91). A later step in that same job holds `GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}` (line 110, `contents: write`) and pushes with it (lines 162-163), under the comment "The credential is handed to THIS command only; nothing the gate ran could reach it" (lines 160-161). persist-credentials:false removes the token from .git/config; it does not isolate the process, and the cage's `git status --untracked-files=all` guard inspects only the hub working tree, so nothing in $RUNNER_TEMP, $HOME, PATH, or the gitignored .estate-clone/ is examined. No exploit is claimed — all nine repos share one owner and no malicious script was read — but the stated guarantee is stronger than the mechanism, on the estate's most-quoted artefact. Ticket 56 (open) owns only the read-credential half; the write credential and the overclaiming comment are owned by nothing. Separately confirmed: verify-schedules produces 14 `GitHub unreachable … cannot look at whether this clock ran inside its period` SKIPs and zero live passes on run 21, a permanent could-not-look on the citable surface.
EVIDENCE: .github/workflows/truth.yml:158-160: `# The credential is handed to THIS command only; nothing the gate ran could reach it (see persist-credentials above).` followed by `git -c http.extraheader="AUTHORIZATION: basic $(printf 'x-access-token:%s' "${GH_TOKEN}" | base64 -w0)" push origin HEAD:"${GITHUB_REF_NAME}"`. The gate step (truth.yml:93-100) runs `bash talk/verify-all.sh`, which discovers and runs every verify*.sh across .estate-clone (full clones of eight orgs' default branches, per clone-estate.sh) — 84 scripts on run 21. sudo is available and used in the same job (SS-06). persist-credentials:false removes the token from .git/config; it does not isolate the process. Corroboration that the estate half-knows this class: ticket 56 (open) is written as 'a separate workflow step or job that runs before the gate … or an equivalent design that keeps the token out of the eight orgs' unpinned scripts' — but scoped to the READ credential verify-schedules needs, not the WRITE credential the cage step already holds. Related and confirmed: origin/main:talk/captures/verify_schedules_verify-schedules.out ends every clock with `SKIP: <unit>/<workflow>: GitHub unreachable (Command '['gh','auth','status']' returned non-zero exit status 1.) -- cannot look at whether this clock ran inside its period`.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5 ('One command, on a schedule, in CI, is the only source any document may cite'), §3 principle 6
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 56 (read-credential half only); write-credential half + overclaiming comment: unowned — Ticket 56 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/56-the-citable-run-can-see-whether-the-clocks-ran.md, from REVIEW-2026-08-31 M6) is open and confirmed still true (matches SS-07's own "Claimed ownership" split). But its scope is explicitly limited: "Grade clock health with a read-scoped credential isolated from the untrusted verify scripts ... keeps the token out of the eight orgs' unpinned scripts" — this is about the verify-schedules READ token, not the truth.yml push-step WRITE credential. I found no ticket, ADR, or REGRILL-ANSWERS entry addressing: (1) the write credential (GH_TOKEN used for the git push in truth.yml:158-162) coexisting in the same job as the 84 unpinned verify*.sh scripts and passwordless sudo, or (2) the specific overclaiming comment "nothing the gate ran could reach it" (truth.yml:160-161). Grepped .scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md and docs/adr/*.md for "SS-06", "passwordless sudo", "sudo -n", "NOPASSWD" — no hits. REVIEW-2026-08-31's Refuted-claims list (lines 89-101) contains nothing matching this claim (closest are process-isolation/observation-lane items, none about the push-credential/sudo/unpinned-script coexistence). So: the read-credential sub-finding duplicates ticket 56 (still true, not resolved-away); the write-credential sub-finding and the overclaiming-comment sub-finding are new and unowned.
FIT IMPACT: I am not claiming an exploit exists — all nine repos are owned by the same person and I read no malicious script. I am claiming the property asserted in the comment is not the property the design has, and the estate's most-quoted artefact (the TRUTH line) is produced under a stated guarantee that is stronger than the mechanism. Separately, 'did the clocks run inside their period' is a permanent could-not-look on the citable surface.
REMEDY: Either move the cage/commit/push into a separate job that does not run foreign scripts (artifact-passing the transcript), or soften the comment to state what persist-credentials:false actually buys. Widen ticket 56 to cover the write credential, not only the read one.

## [major] SS-08 Step 3's signing half is unbuilt while the proposer's own record asserts it: the one artefact authored by an agent rather than a human carries a manufactured Rekor claim, and a discovered verify script grades that literal as a pass
(original title: The proposer's commit is unsigned while its own record hardcodes a gitsign/Rekor identity claim)
CLAIM: The proposer's commit is unsigned while four places in the estate assert a hardcoded gitsign/Rekor claim about it, and the derived word "signed" is already printed to humans in the citable run's committed captures.

Mechanics: units/driftwood/.github/workflows/propose-tier.yml:184-185 is the only git config in the file and sets user.email/user.name alone — the file contains no gitsign install and none of `gpg.format x509`, `gpg.x509.program gitsign`, `commit.gpgsign true`, unlike twin-sweep.yml:211-213, drift-sample.yml:251-253 and the hub's .github/workflows/truth.yml:153-157 (the finding cited 145-149). Ludlow (:233) and tuppence (:223) are identical. units/platform/wargamer/tier_pr.py contains zero occurrences of "gitsign" and commits with a bare `_git("commit", "-q", "-m", p["title"])` at :350 before `_git("push", "--force", ...)` at :351.

The assertion: wargamer.py:199-200 and :231-232 carry `"identity": "gitsign keyless (OIDC -> Fulcio) -> Rekor transparency log"` and `"signed": True`. The comment on :200 ("stamped at commit time by propose-policy-pr.sh") is itself false — propose-policy-pr.sh:5 says it "NEVER commits, pushes, opens or merges the PR itself". Four sites test the literal against itself: wargamer.py:324, wardley/wardley.py:452, wardley/verify-wardley.sh:73, and transitively wargamer/verify-wargamer.sh. All are inside the gate — talk/verify-all.sh:45 discovers every verify*.sh by find, and verify-exclusions.txt excludes only two unrelated scripts — so two of TRUTH run 21's 57 passes are earned by a self-referential assertion.

Correction to the finding's mitigation: the claim is NOT latent. tier_pr.py:283's _pr_body indeed never renders signed/identity, and no wargamer/* branch exists on any of the three adopters (gh api repos/.../branches), so no PR body has ever carried it. But run-21 captures committed on the hub's origin/main do: talk/captures/.estate-clone_platform_wargamer_verify-wargamer.out prints "proposed 4 signed PR(s), 0 merged" and "PASS: ... a signed policy PR proposed"; .estate-clone_platform_wardley_verify-wardley.out prints "5 signed PR(s), 0 merged, all gated" and "to a signed, gated, never-merged PR". These narrate simulated proposals, not real PRs, but they are human-readable, on the default branch, in the citable run.

Correction to ownership: the gap is decided-and-undelivered rather than unowned. GAPS.md:29 names it exactly. Ticket 28 (resolved) asked for "bot commits are signed" alongside scheduling propose-tier and its Answer records no signing work. Ticket 12 (resolved) settled that "the proposer signs with the workflow Actions identity added to the expected-identity regexp" (:37, answer 4 at :96). Neither half was built: driftwood's EXPECTED_IDENTITY_REGEXP (release.yml:47) is anchored to cut-release.yml only, so a signed proposer commit would not verify against the estate's own pin either. Ticket 74 owns step 3 firing and says nothing about signing.

Nothing will catch the first firing: driftwood/.github/rulesets/observation-lane.json carries only file_path_restriction, its README records that required_signatures was deliberately removed because GitHub cannot verify gitsign, and no ruleset is applied to any repo. NORTH-STAR §4 step 3 ("A proposal PR opens, signed by the proposer's identity") and §3 principle 6 remain unsatisfiable as written, and the first real crossing will push an unsigned commit while the estate's own evidence keeps saying "signed".
EVIDENCE: units/driftwood/.github/workflows/propose-tier.yml:184-185 is the ONLY git config in the file: `git -C driftwood config user.email "wargamer-proposer@policy-as-versioned-driftwood.invalid"` / `git -C driftwood config user.name "wargamer proposer"` — no `gpg.format x509`, no `gpg.x509.program gitsign`, no `commit.gpgsign true`, unlike truth.yml:145-149, drift-sample.yml's cage step and twin-sweep.yml, all three of which set exactly those three lines with a written explanation. units/platform/wargamer/tier_pr.py:350-351 then runs a plain `_git("commit", "-q", "-m", p["title"])` and `_git("push", "--force", …)`. The false claim: units/platform/wargamer/wargamer.py:199-200 and :231-232 carry `"identity": "gitsign keyless (OIDC -> Fulcio) -> Rekor transparency log"` and `"signed": True,  # stamped at commit time by propose-policy-pr.sh`; wargamer.py:324 asserts `assert p["signed"] is True and "Rekor" in p["identity"], p` — a selfcheck that tests a hardcoded literal against itself. Mitigations I verified: _pr_body() (tier_pr.py:283-309) does NOT render signed/identity into the PR body; and no proposal has ever fired — `gh api repos/.../branches` shows no wargamer/* branch on any of the three adopters.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §4 step 3 ('A proposal PR opens, signed by the proposer's identity'), §3 principle 6 ('Every artefact carries a signature that says what it does and does not assert')
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 12 (identity-as-spine), owner decision re-grill 16 / P142 — Exact match to GAPS 1.9 ("the only real PR opener does not sign; the one that claims signed: True never commits"), re-cited as still-present inside ticket 12's own body: ".scratch/ecosystem/issues/12-identity-as-spine.md:33" says "The war-gamer's identity: \"gitsign keyless ...\", signed: True is a string literal (platform/wargamer/wargamer.py:199-200; GAPS 1.9)." The owner already recorded the fix direction in REGRILL-ANSWERS.md line 79: "16 | P142 | Proposer commit signing | Reverse: sign the proposal commit with the workflow Actions identity; add it to the expected-identity regexp." Ticket 12's "Already decided" block (line 37) restates this: "The proposer signs with the workflow Actions identity added to the expected-identity regexp; author and merger are different identities for now ... (reversal 16; re-grills 29, 37)." This is a settled owner decision but NOT yet built: ticket 12 still has open questions (Q4/Q5) that build on top of "proposer = workflow Actions identity (reversal 16, settled)" as a premise, and nothing marks propose-tier.yml's missing gitsign config or wargamer.py's literal-assertion selfcheck as actually fixed. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's C1/M1-M18 and Refuted-claims lists do not mention tier_pr.py/wargamer.py signing at all, so this finding is not refuted there. Conclusion: SS-08 is a real, still-true restatement of GAPS 1.9; its remediation direction is an owner-recorded decision under ticket 12, so it should be logged as evidence against ticket 12 rather than opened as a new ticket — but ticket 12 does not yet claim the defect is fixed, so the finding stands as open/unbuilt.
FIT IMPACT: NORTH-STAR §4 step 3 cannot be satisfied as written. Today the false field is latent (not rendered to humans, and the proposer has never run), so it is a loaded gun rather than a discharged one — but the first time step 3 fires for real, an unsigned commit will carry a data record claiming Rekor provenance, which is precisely the class of defect this estate has hunted and fixed twice elsewhere (the insurer's fabricated v1.2.0 pin; the hand-typed drift samples).
REMEDY: Add the three gitsign config lines to propose-tier.yml alongside the existing two, and derive wargamer.py's `signed`/`identity` from the actual commit (`git log --format=%G?` / the certificate SAN) rather than a literal — or delete both fields. Do it BEFORE ticket 74 fires step 3.

MINORS:
- SS-09 npx --yes renovate@44.37.1 is the second exception to the estate's pinning rule, in a job holding contents+PR+issues write: Three adopters install Renovate at run time via npx with the version pinned but the transitive dependency tree resolved live, in a job with contents: write, pull-requests: write and issues: write.
- SS-10 A gate PASS line asserts the one thing its own function's docstring says the checker cannot assert: verify-schedules emits 'nothing it runs is opaque to this checker' for every clean scheduled job, while the same module's docstring names `npx renovate` as exactly the opaque case it cannot read — and it prints that line for the renovate job and for the truth job that runs curl|sudo bash and 84 foreign scripts.
- SS-11 Committed demo credentials, and a live manifest comment the package's own README calls false: The Dex and OpenBao manifests carry three labelled demo credentials, and dex-helmrelease.yaml narrates its static account as 'the SAME subject as the gitsign committer' — a claim identity/README.md states in terms is false.
- SS-12 The cluster-side check is strictly weaker than the CI-side check: it never consults Rekor, so enforcement happens where verification is weakest: verify_gitsign.py verifies the CMS signature, the Fulcio chain, the URI SAN and the issuer extension, but not the Rekor signed entry timestamp or an inclusion proof — so a signer holding a Fulcio cert for the pinned identity who never logged it passes at the Flux source boundary and fails real gitsign.
- SS-13 ADR-0023's two retirement triggers have both fired and the second signing scheme is still in the tree: ADR-0023 names two key-based signers with exact retirement triggers; both triggers fired (feeds cut threat-register/v1.0.0 and v2.0.0, ico cut v3.0.0) and none of the named deletions has happened, so two signature schemes remain live over overlapping artefacts.
- SS-14 Two stale comments on trust-critical files now assert the opposite of what is true: driftwood's platform pin comment names the wrong tag as the source of its pinned SHA, and drift-sample.yml claims the gitsign verifier is absent from the pinned platform tag when it is present and actively producing verdicts.
- SS-15 'Agent signatures attest the absence of a human' has no implementation, and every signed tag is in fact human-triggered: No mechanism anywhere attests the absence of a human, and because every cut-release.yml is workflow_dispatch-only, every signed tag in the estate was initiated by a human choosing a commit.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- Should a gitsign tag be trusted when the Fulcio certificate's validity window opens one second AFTER the tagger timestamp the tag itself signs? Put differently: is the correct trust instant the tagger's claimed time, the certificate's notBefore, or the Rekor signed entry timestamp? | WHY: This single choice decides whether the estate's only enforcing signature check accepts or rejects 21% of its own genuine tags. Strict-at-tagger is defensible cryptographically (the tagger time is inside the signature) but is empirically wrong here, because git stamps the tagger line before gitsign requests the certificate — so the race is structural, not incidental. Choosing Rekor also closes the transparency-log gap (SS-12) in the same change; choosing a skew tolerance is cheaper but leaves that gap open and needs a named, tested bound. | CHANGES: SS-01's remedy and severity, SS-12's remedy, the fixture corpus in platform/distribution/verify/testdata/, and at least four of run-21's seven fails (three adopter verify-reconcile scripts and verify-e2e-step4). If the answer is Rekor, the gitsign-verifier gains a network dependency at the cluster boundary and needs its own could-not-look path, which changes the controller's failure semantics.
- Is this estate meant to demonstrate that policy-as-a-versioned-dependency is enforceable against a repository's own writer, or only against foreign parties? Concretely: should main and release/*.x be protected with a required review across all nine repositories, given you are the only human in the estate? | WHY: Right now the identity pins do real work against a foreign signer and no work against the repo's own writer, because there is zero branch protection and zero rulesets anywhere (verified live on all nine). NORTH-STAR principles 4 and 5 ('bump only by reviewed PR', 'A human merges') are therefore conventions with no enforcement point. A single-operator estate cannot honestly satisfy 'reviewed by someone other than the author' — so either the principle needs an achievable restatement, or the estate needs a second reviewing identity, or the gap needs recording in §6. | CHANGES: SS-04 and SS-05's severity and remedy. If protection is applied, several tickets that stand resolved on 'bumped by reviewed PR' become genuinely proven rather than asserted; if the answer is 'this is a single-operator demonstration', then principles 4 and 5 need an amendment in NORTH-STAR §8 and every document quoting them needs the qualifier, which is a documentation sweep rather than a build.
- Is the SPIRE / Istio / OpenBao / Pomerium / EUD identity substrate in scope for the citable truth surface, or is it a designed-and-shelved package? And if in scope, is federation (four trust domains, twelve objects) part of the ambition for this build or explicitly later? | WHY: Six scripts covering the entire actor-attestation half of principle 6 grade SKIP on every citable run for the same reason (no kind cluster on the runner), and federation cannot be true until party/schema.json accepts three fields it currently refuses. Today the peers' trust anchors are literals in platform's tree, which is the tenant relationship §2 forbids. These are two different decisions — 'observe the substrate live' and 'make federation real' — and they have different costs: the first is an ephemeral-cluster lane of a shape drift-sample.yml already proves works; the second is a schema patch plus a trust-domain migration that re-mints every SVID. | CHANGES: SS-02 and SS-03's severity and whether they are shortfalls or out-of-scope. If in scope, it adds an identity lane to the truth surface and a party/schema.json build ticket; if out, NORTH-STAR §6 gains two entries, verify-federation.sh's SKIP reason changes from 'not yet' to 'out of scope', and principle 2's 'a human, a device' clause needs a qualifier.
- Should the proposer's commit be gitsign-signed, and should wargamer.py's `signed`/`identity` fields be derived from the actual commit or deleted? Is this a blocker for ticket 74 firing step 3 for real? | WHY: NORTH-STAR §4 step 3 says the proposal PR is 'signed by the proposer's identity'. Today propose-tier.yml sets only user.name and user.email, tier_pr.py does a plain git commit, and wargamer.py hardcodes `"signed": True` with a Rekor identity string that its own selfcheck asserts against itself. The claim is currently latent — it is not rendered into the PR body and no proposal has ever fired — but the first real firing turns it into fabricated provenance inside a live proposal, which is the exact defect class this estate has caught and fixed twice before. | CHANGES: SS-08's severity (latent vs discharged) and whether ticket 74 should be blocked on a signing fix. If the answer is 'sign it', the three gitsign config lines go into propose-tier.yml and the two literals become derived values; if 'the proposer needs no signature', the fields must be deleted and §4 step 3 amended, because a hardcoded True is worse than an honest absence.
- Is the hub's truth workflow allowed to execute 84 unpinned scripts from eight other organisations' default branches in the same job that holds a contents:write token and runs an unpinned installer as root — or should the gate and the commit be separated into different jobs? | WHY: persist-credentials:false is a real mitigation and better than the default, but the comment beside the push asserts a boundary ('nothing the gate ran could reach it') that process-level separation does not provide. Ticket 56 already reasons in exactly these terms for the READ credential verify-schedules needs; the WRITE credential the cage step already holds is the larger version of the same question and is owned by nothing. Answering it also settles whether the curl|sudo bash Flux install is acceptable in that job at all. | CHANGES: SS-06 and SS-07's remedy and whether ticket 56 should be widened. If the answer is 'separate them', the truth workflow becomes two jobs with an artifact handoff and the overclaiming comment can be replaced with a true one; if 'this is acceptable in a single-operator estate', the comment must still be softened, because it currently states a guarantee the estate does not have.
- Given that both of ADR-0023's retirement triggers have now fired, do the two ed25519 signing paths get deleted, or does 'one signature' become 'one signature per artefact class'? | WHY: ADR-0023 wrote its own deletion lists and said the estate 'cannot say "one signature" without this paragraph beside it' until the tags existed. feeds cut threat-register/v1.0.0 and v2.0.0; ico cut v3.0.0. The tags exist and the files do not: ico/schema/sign.sh, both keys/ directories and 10+ .sig files are still on the default branches, along with the openssl blocks in five named consumers. Deleting them touches verify-feeds.sh, wardley/, honesty/, wargamer/ and the hub's verify/provenance — five consumers that may still read those .sig files. | CHANGES: SS-13's remedy and whether the ADR's amendment paragraph is now stale documentation or still live doctrine. Deleting also changes what platform/feeds/ can serve, which interacts with the feeds-migration duplication the platform-engines reader flagged (old copies retained because five consumers are not repointed).

# engineering-quality

VERDICT: Fit for demonstrating the thesis on a citable clock; not yet fit for the adoptable eco-system NORTH-STAR §2 implies. The measured estate is 122,479 lines of code and test (66,100 python, 13,732 shell, 24,024 yaml, 18,623 test-python) plus ~53,500 lines of tracked prose. Its agent-legibility is genuinely excellent — 0.37-0.82 prose-to-code across the core engines, 180 dated in-code incident notes, 48 READMEs, zero dead modules found in either the hub or the eight units, 88% return-type annotation in the hub, zero bare except handlers in 261 handlers, and verified determinism (fair.py byte-identical across runs; the three-architecture determinism matrix green in the same CI run where three other jobs failed). The hub's twin suite is 1550/1551 green locally and in CI, failing only on one pre-registered owned invariant. Against that, two structural blockers stop a human team picking this up: all eight unit repos (28,490 python + 11,246 shell) have zero tests in any framework, zero type checking and zero lint, standing on 7,000 lines of monolithic in-module selfcheck() functions (gate.py's is 53% of the file) that a test runner cannot address; and the three adopters are copy-pasted, with only 15 of 39 shared code files identical after party-name normalisation and 24 genuinely diverged — including drift/five-facts.py, the engine that decides the estate's central drift claim, where driftwood has a WINDOW_CLUSTER guard tuppence/ludlow lack and tuppence/ludlow have an _org() fix driftwood lacks, so neither copy has both. Two instrument faults sit on top: six computed-semver scripts print SKIP and exit 0 (graded PASS, latent only because kyverno is pinned and installed on the runner), and the clock never provisions a cluster so 12 of run 21's 18 SKIPs can never be graded there. Making it fit for a human team is a short, specific list: wire pytest+mypy over the existing selfcheck seams in platform, publish the shared adopter logic as a pinned artefact the way the estate already publishes policies, change six exit 0 to exit 3, and put build/test/run instructions in a README that currently mentions none of them.

STRENGTHS:
- 1550 of 1551 pytest tests pass, and the identical single failure appears locally (`.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` -> `1 failed, 1550 passed in 693.95s`) and in CI (`gh run view 33615039125 --log-failed` -> `1 failed, 1550 passed in 115.13s`). The failure is `flux_coverage_floor_is_still_reachable`, a pre-registered finding owned by build ticket 70. The invariant job reports `70 passed, 1 failed, 3 skipped` — one better than the twin map's recorded 70/2.
- Determinism is real and proven across architectures. Every stochastic path is explicitly seeded (platform/fair/fair.py:132,324; feeds/fetch/market-moves.py:62; twin/substrate.py:125; twin/pert.py:225; twin/benchmark.py:205), the one os.urandom (twin/substrate.py:137) is the deliberately non-deterministic generator. Verified empirically: two `python3 fair/fair.py summary fair/scenarios/driftwood-cart-pii.json` runs diff'd IDENTICAL including ale=19558.549772440045. CI's `determinism` matrix passed on aarch64-linux, x86_64-linux and arm64-darwin in run 33615039125 alongside `reproduce-elsewhere`, while three sibling jobs failed.
- No dead code. I checked every python module in the hub's twin/ and in all eight unit clones for any reference outside its own file (.py/.sh/.yml/.yaml/.md): zero orphans in either. For a 66k-line estate built in two months by one person that is unusual.
- No bare exception handlers anywhere: 261 `except` clauses across hub and units, zero `except:`. Of the 45 that swallow, 29 are the negative-test idiom inside twin/invariants/; I read the four production ones individually and only two are worth reporting (both minor).
- compose() is a genuinely deep module, not a shallow wrapper: platform/compose/composition.py:2005 takes two arguments (adopter_dir, parent_trees), returns (evidence dict, path->content mapping), and its docstring ends 'Writes nothing to disk -- that is the CLI's job.' Reading the body confirms it: the whole party.yaml -> resolve_sha -> merge -> restatements -> holes -> prices pipeline is pure.
- fair.py is the best-shaped module in the estate: 390 lines, pure stdlib, three CLI verbs (summary/compare/selfcheck), one file read, no writes, fully deterministic, and its selfcheck asserts real properties including that a sum crossing a perspective or a currency REFUSES rather than adding ('no number in this estate is a lie by addition').
- Guards are structural, not name-matching. wargamer/tier_pr.py's disposing_calls() does an AST walk, adopted after a real planted regression (a rewritten _open_or_update_pr calling `gh pr merge --squash --admin`) passed the previous attribute-name-only check — recorded in the module's own docstring at lines 82-203.
- The generated artefacts really are generated, not copy-pasted: all three adopters' composed/policies/v4.0.0/*.yaml are byte-identical to platform/distribution/policies/v4.0.0/ modulo the composed-for label and two provenance annotations (inherited-from: platform@2.0.1, source-path: distribution/policies/v4.0.0/cage-tier.yaml), and scripts/render_composed.py is byte-identical across all three after party-name normalisation.
- No code vendoring into adopters: driftwood/twin/ is 62 files / 1,446 lines of data and config only — no hub twin/*.py module appears in it (checked by basename against the hub's twin/) — with the pin declared in PIN.yaml and VENDORED.md.
- Comment density is high and load-bearing: measured prose:code ratios of 0.82 (gate.py), 0.80 (verbs.py), 0.73 (composition.py), 0.63 (tier_pr.py). 180 comment lines name a specific past defect, usually with a date and an incident reference; 48 READMEs across the estate. This is what makes the codebase agent-navigable.
- Type discipline: 882/994 hub functions (88%) and 610/873 unit functions (69%) carry return annotations; mypy runs over 159 source files in CI and is one stale `type: ignore` from clean.
- The tool supply chain is pinned by checksum, not just version: .github/workflows/truth.yml:39-49 pins gitsign 0.17.1, kyverno 1.18.2 and cosign 3.1.3 each with an explicit SHA256. That discipline is currently the only reason EQ-02's latent false-pass is not live.
- The honesty check is correctly scoped and says out loud what it cannot do: verify-e2e-step7-honesty.sh PASSed in run 21 while step 4 FAILed, which is right — its claim is 'every claim above is reported honestly', not 'every claim above is true' (lines 12-15) — and its own comment states that nothing at its level can detect a fabricated claim carrying no hedge word.

## [major] EQ-01 The platform's policies carry pass/fail tests but the platform's own 19,066-line machinery carries none, is type-checked nowhere, and has no CI on a change at all — so the "adopter organisation operates it" role in NORTH-STAR §2 is not honestly on offer
(original title: Zero automated tests, types or lint in all eight unit repos, and none over the gate itself)
CLAIM: The eight unit repos' 28,490 lines of python and 11,246 of shell, plus the 3,738 lines of python in the hub's verify/ and talk/, are covered by no python test framework, no type checker and no linter. Verified: `def test_` appears in zero files across all eight clones; no pyproject/ruff/mypy/flake8/setup.cfg/tox/pre-commit/pytest.ini/conftest.py exists anywhere in any unit tree; no unit workflow mentions pytest, mypy, ruff, shellcheck, flake8, pylint or black. The hub's pytest.ini pins `testpaths = tests`, all 1,551 collected tests import twin.* only, and .github/workflows/twin.yml:64 runs `mypy twin tests conftest.py`, so the gate's own code is unchecked by the one type checker the estate owns; truth.yml, which runs the gate, has no test, type or lint step. The python substitute is in-module `selfcheck()` functions with no case selector (`argv[1] == "--selfcheck"` or a bare argparse flag), fail-fast and graded only by exit code: platform 5,171 of 19,066 python lines across 31 such functions (27%), ludlow 443/2,270, tuppence 410/2,422, driftwood 410/2,649, feeds 162/935, ico 67/373, insurer 77/436, nist 25/339 — composition.py's is a single 1,009-line function (lines 2617-3625). Two corrections to the original wording: (1) the selfchecks are not the *only* automated coverage — the gate discovers 84 verify*.sh scripts (68 in the units, 16 in the hub, matching `total=84` in the TRUTH line), some asserting in shell independently, and platform carries five declarative `kyverno test` matrices with per-case expectations for the policy layer, so failure isolation exists at 84-script granularity even though it does not exist within any one script; (2) the per-file blast radius varies — composition.py (3,654 L) is reached by exactly one gate script, but computed-semver/gate.py (1,134 L) is reached by five. Ownership: no ADR and no ecosystem ticket owns adding tests, types or lint, but `--selfcheck` is a deliberate, repeatedly specified acceptance harness (.scratch/ecosystem/spec.md:183; policy-composition issues 11, 13, 14) — an owned choice with no owned remediation. The estate's own record already shows the substitute missing real defects: .scratch/policy-composition/issues/17:100-121 documents passing selfchecks alongside a cross-adopter CLI mismatch and a function that "never existed anywhere in the codebase".
EVIDENCE: `grep -rl 'def test_' --include='*.py'` across the eight fresh clones returns 0 files (no pytest, no unittest). `ls -a <unit> | grep -icE 'ruff|mypy|flake8|pyproject|pre-commit'` = 0 for all eight; `grep -rl 'pytest|mypy|ruff|shellcheck' <unit>/.github/workflows/` = 0 for all eight. In the hub, `grep -rl 'verify/|talk/|build_deck|pound_seam' tests/*.py` returns no matches — all 1,551 collected tests import twin.* only — and /Users/cns/httpdocs/controlplane/policy-as-versioned-flux/.github/workflows/twin.yml:64 runs `mypy twin tests conftest.py`, omitting verify/ and talk/. AST measurement of the substitute: platform 5,171 of 19,066 python lines are inside selfcheck functions (27%, 31 functions); ludlow 443/2,270; tuppence 410/2,422; driftwood 410/2,649; feeds 162/935; ico 67/373; insurer 77/436; nist 25/339.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2 (an eco-system other organisations adopt and operate) and §3 principle 6; the thesis's claim that policy machinery is ordinary versioned software a team can own.
OWNER: recorded-owner-decision REGRILL-ANSWERS.md row 30 (P179 "Test seams"), cited at issues/18-the-publisher-release-under-cages.md:38 ("publisher release is one of six test seams, Python stays (re-grill 30)") — No ticket owns EQ-01 as stated, and REVIEW-2026-08-31 does not list or refute it. The closest relevant fact is an owner decision bearing directly on it: REGRILL answer 30 (P179 "Test seams") re-opened and decided "one seam per eco-system joint (composition, publisher release, adopter gate, cage tier move, feed fetch, twin-to-estate tier handoff). Python stays." That is a deliberate, narrower answer to "what automated testing should exist" than EQ-01's framing (full pytest/mypy/ruff coverage of all 28,490 python lines) — the owner chose six integration seams at eco-system joints, not a full unit-test/type/lint regime for the unit repos.

This does not moot the finding: (1) even the narrower six-seam decision is unbuilt — `grep -rl 'def test_'` across all eight fresh clones still returns 0, so no seam has been implemented as an actual test; ticket 18 (the seam-30 citation) resolved only the degraded-publish semver semantics, not the test itself, and no other open or resolved ticket claims to have built any of the six seams. (2) The decision is silent on linting and type-checking in the eight unit repos (ruff/mypy/flake8/pre-commit/shellcheck) and on covering the hub's verify/ and talk/ under twin.yml's mypy line — those parts of EQ-01 are genuinely new and unowned by any ticket, ADR, or regrill answer.

Net: this is a recorded owner decision that narrows the ambition (no full test-framework mandate was ever made), but it is not a decision that the finding is wrong or already fixed — the six seams and the lint/type gap both remain live, unbuilt, and untracked by any open ticket.
FIT IMPACT: A change to composition.py (3,654 L), gate.py (1,134 L) or adopter-gate.py (1,087 L) can only be validated by running one shell script whose entire verdict is one exit code — no failure isolation, no selectable assertion, no coverage figure, no static check that a refactor preserved types. An adopting platform team inherits 28,490 lines of python with no way to make a safe change. This is the single largest obstacle to the codebase being maintainable by anyone but its author.
REMEDY: Each selfcheck() is already a sequence of independent assert/print('ok ...') blocks, so the cheapest honest fix is a one-line `test_selfcheck.py` per module that invokes it, plus pytest+mypy jobs in platform's CI — a day's work that buys a runner and failure isolation without rewriting logic. Then add `verify talk` to the hub typecheck job's existing mypy argument list.

## [major] EQ-02 The gate's own "a green that could not look is a red" rule is unenforced in six of its checks: with kyverno absent, they report proof instead of could-not-look
(original title: Six verify scripts print SKIP and exit 0, which the gate grades PASS)
CLAIM: Six verify scripts in platform/computed-semver/ exit 0 after printing a SKIP line when the kyverno CLI is absent, and talk/verify-all.sh:62 grades exit 0 as PASS ("observed true"), so on any runner without kyverno all six are counted as observed-true — the defect class the estate names as its cardinal sin (NORTH-STAR.md:35 "A green that could not look is a red"; :57 the three-outcomes rule). The six, at the cited lines: verify-gate.sh:11-13, verify-cage-engine.sh:12-14, verify-comparison-window.sh:11-13, verify-rederive-bumps.sh:9-11, verify-generator-standing-check.sh:18-20 (each `echo "SKIP: …"; exit 0`), and verify-corpus-generator.sh:34-36 (`SKIP (step 3):`, after two steps of real offline work). For the first four, nothing at all is verified and the script becomes a silent no-op graded PASS. A seventh, verify-witness-set.sh:68-70, is worse: its SKIP sits inside an `if/else` with no exit at all, so it falls through to :117-119 and its last line is an affirmative `PASS: …`. The six also escape `--live` (verify-all.sh:63 turns exit 3 into FAIL, but not exit 0), so a --live run on a kyverno-less host would report them green. The fix already exists in a sibling directory: platform/distribution/verify-render-version-tree.sh:23-28 exits 3 with a comment naming this exact class and the 2026-08-25 incident. The orphan is deliberate-looking: the ticket-55 patch (.scratch/ecosystem/0001-Every-red-on-the-clock-is-real-explained-and-finisha.patch:25-27 and its first hunk at :39-54) fixed render-version-tree while editing verify-corpus-generator.sh three lines below that file's own `exit 0` guard, and left it; REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:87 names only render-version-tree, and no ticket owns the six. Two citation corrections: the kyverno pin is at truth.yml:46-47 but the SHA256-checked install step is :78-87; and the gate's graded surface is 84 scripts (68 unit + 16 hub verify/), not 86 — the 86 figure counts talk/verify-all.sh and talk/verify-demo.sh, which the gate never discovers, and neither is among the six. Live impact on TRUTH run 21 is nil: kyverno 1.18.2 was installed and the captures show it really ran (verify-corpus-generator.out:8, verify-witness-set.out:23), so pass=57 fail=7 skip=18 stands. The defect is an armed false-green in the citable instrument, not a wrong number today.
EVIDENCE: talk/verify-all.sh:61-62 reads `case $rc in 0) printf '%-70s PASS\n' "$s"; pass=$((pass+1));;`, and the same file's header lines 6-13 state 'Nothing is faked: a live tail that cannot see its cluster must exit 3, not 0.' The six: platform/computed-semver/verify-gate.sh:11-13, verify-cage-engine.sh:12-14, verify-comparison-window.sh:11-13, verify-rederive-bumps.sh:9-11, verify-generator-standing-check.sh:18-20, verify-corpus-generator.sh:34-36, each `if ! command -v kyverno …; then echo "SKIP: …"; exit 0`. An automated sweep over all 86 verify*.sh (SKIP echo followed within 4 lines by exit 0) returns exactly these six. verify-witness-set.sh:68-70 is a seventh, worse variant: it prints its 'SKIP (step 5)' mid-file and falls through to exit 0, so the SKIP is not even the last line. The fix already exists twenty lines away: platform/distribution/verify-render-version-tree.sh:23-27 reads 'exit 3, not 0. verify-all.sh grades 0 as PASS, so exiting 0 here reported "the coexistence proof holds" on any runner without the CLI -- a check that passes on absence, the exact class the 2026-08-25 incident came from.' Live impact today is nil: .github/workflows/truth.yml:46-47 installs kyverno 1.18.2 pinned by SHA256 and run 21's gate log shows all six PASS with real kyverno output.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5's three-outcomes rule (observed-true / observed-false / could-not-look) and §3 principle 6; the estate's stated cardinal sin that absence must never read as a pass.
OWNER: new none — orphan of ticket 55 — REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:87 named the minor "Silent-PASS-on-missing-kyverno in render-version-tree" and folded it into ticket 55. Ticket 55's own Answer (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/55-every-red-on-the-clock-is-real-explained-and-finishable.md:62-63) confirms the fix was scoped to exactly one script: "verify-render-version-tree exited 0 when kyverno was absent... It exits 3 now." Nothing in ticket 55, 54, 59, 64, 66, or 67 mentions computed-semver/ or verify-witness-set.sh, and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md and the Refuted-claims list (REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:89-100) are silent on this too. Ticket 55 is also still Status: prepared with the fix sitting on an unmerged branch (.estate-clone/platform ticket-55-instrument-faults, commit 04184df), not on platform main — so even the one script it covers is not yet live. The six computed-semver/ siblings plus the worse verify-witness-set.sh variant are a genuinely new, unowned finding: no ticket, GAPS row, or ADR claims them.
FIT IMPACT: No number on TRUTH run 21 is wrong. But the citable instrument carries an armed false-green in six checks, triggered the first time the clock runs without kyverno — and the estate's whole claim rests on the instrument not being able to lie about absence.
REMEDY: Change `exit 0` to `exit 3` in six files, and make verify-witness-set.sh exit 3 when its step 5 could not look. Seven one-line edits.

## [major] EQ-04 The estate's only correctness instrument now returns "red" regardless of the code's state, and a real regression has already slipped through it unseen — the thesis's "silent failure on main" reproduced in the project's own CI
(original title: The only pytest/mypy gate has failed 25 runs running, and one of its three reds is a one-line fix that arrived unnoticed)
