CLAIM: twin.yml is the estate's only CI gate that runs pytest or mypy — no unit repo, no hub verify*.sh, no truth.yml and no legacy org repo invokes either — and all 25 runs the Actions API reports for it (2026-08-16 to 2026-09-02) are `failure`, i.e. it has never been green in the recorded window. On the exact hub commit named in TRUTH run 21 (7b92990, run 33615039125) three of its eight jobs are red: `tests` and `invariants` both fail on invariant 44 `flux_coverage_floor_is_still_reachable`, which is one honestly-owned finding (build ticket `.scratch/twin/build/70-full-system-coherence-confirmatory.md`, finding 1 — not the ecosystem ticket of the same number), and `typecheck` fails on a single dead suppression: `twin/feed_signal.py:232: error: Unused "type: ignore" comment [unused-ignore]`. That typecheck red is narrower in time than the workflow's: typecheck was green on the four runs from 2026-08-28T04:01 to 2026-08-30T10:28 and has been red on all ten runs since 2026-08-31T07:55 (head 27d7d5c). It arrived with commit 0046da3 ("ecosystem ticket 50", twin/feed_signal.py), which is absent from the last green run's tree. It is not toolchain drift — mypy is pinned to 1.14.1 in every job and reproduces identically on the local .venv — and the fix is genuinely one line: deleting the trailing `  # type: ignore[arg-type]` from line 232 in a scratch copy yields `Success: no issues found in 159 source files`. Nothing in .scratch/, docs/ or talk/ records the error string, so no ticket owns it. It landed invisibly inside a workflow already red for 15 straight runs for accepted reasons — the exact signal saturation REVIEW-2026-08-31's M14 (line 75) named for the TRUTH line, and ticket 59, which would build the fall-is-blocking mechanism, is still `Status: open`.
EVIDENCE: `gh run list --workflow=twin.yml --limit 3` returns conclusion `failure` for head SHAs 7b92990, ce4be49, e7981a5. `gh run view 33615039125 --json jobs` shows `tests failure`, `invariants failure`, `typecheck failure`, with `demo`, `reproduce-elsewhere` and all three `determinism` legs `success`. `gh run view 33615039125 --log-failed` gives `twin/feed_signal.py:232: error: Unused "type: ignore" comment [unused-ignore]` / `Found 1 error in 1 file (checked 159 source files)`. Reproduced locally: `.venv/bin/python -m mypy twin tests conftest.py --ignore-missing-imports --warn-unused-ignores` gives the identical single error. The tests and invariants reds are the same owned finding (flux_coverage_floor_is_still_reachable, build ticket 70), which is honest.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5 ('a fall is a blocking event') and the process rule that done is the truth surface, not the demo.
OWNER: new EQ-04 — No ticket or review document owns the specific typecheck red (unused `type: ignore` at twin/feed_signal.py:232). Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md and REVIEW-2026-08-31.md for "type: ignore", "unused-ignore", "typecheck", "mypy" — the only hits are descriptive (03-the-truth-surface.md:35, 10-schedules-and-skills.md:19, 11-an-org-twin-each.md:30 note that twin.yml has a typecheck job / was green on the first split run), none flag this failure. The adjacent, broader signal-saturation problem the finding cites (M14, "the permanently red workflow saturates the only signal") is owned by open ticket 59 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/59-build-the-two-unbuilt-5-bullets-a-fall-blocks-status-is.md), and the invariants/tests reds are separately covered elsewhere per the finding's own claim (build ticket 70, though 70's actual text is about the observation-lane detective control, not coverage-floor — worth the analyst double-checking that specific sub-claim). But ticket 59's scope is building the fall-blocks-release and derived-Status mechanisms, not diagnosing or fixing this one-line mypy regression, so it does not own this concrete defect. Treat as new: the meta-point (saturated gate hides new regressions) is already charted via ticket 59, but the specific typecheck bug itself has no owner anywhere in the tracker.
FIT IMPACT: A workflow red for a known and accepted reason carries no signal, so a genuinely new regression — the typecheck failure — landed invisibly. This is exactly the failure M14 named for the TRUTH line, now reproduced on the twin gate.
REMEDY: Delete the stale `# type: ignore` on twin/feed_signal.py:232, and either mark flux_coverage_floor_is_still_reachable as expected-red or split it so the job's colour tracks new information rather than a standing finding.

## [major] EQ-06 The enactment half of the thesis — signed policy actually in force on a running cluster — is permanently could-not-look on the only citable instrument, and the designed fix is built for one adopter beat and unowned for platform's ten
(original title: Twelve of run 21's eighteen SKIPs exist because the clock never provisions a cluster)
CLAIM: Eleven of run 21's eighteen SKIPs (not twelve) are caused by the hub clock having no cluster substrate: `.github/workflows/truth.yml` installs gitsign, kyverno, cosign and flux but stands up no cluster, and platform/lib.sh:19-35's `substrate_ok` needs a kind cluster NAMED `driftwood` with every flux-system Kustomization Ready — so installing kind on the runner would not move any of them. That is 13% of the 84 checks, not a sixth; the twelfth SKIP the auditor bucketed here (platform/policy/verify-conditional.sh) is estate state, the retirement of require-nonroot-2.0.1. Nine of the eleven still grade their offline half on every run and would red on a regression there (verify-graded's run-21 capture shows sections 1-4 executing); only platform/distribution/verify-declared-versions-admit.sh:51 and tuppence/reset/verify-reach-secrets.sh:25 are whole-script substrate gates that grade nothing. Crucially, the estate does provision KinD on a clock by design — driftwood/ludlow/tuppence `drift-sample.yml` create a pinned ephemeral cluster daily, sample it and append drift/samples.jsonl, which the gate converts (ticket 16, ticket 60): on run 21 the three verify-reconcile checks and e2e step 4 were graded FAIL from those samples, not SKIPped. The residual finding is narrower: the live-cluster half of eleven platform/tuppence checks can only ever be observed on a host with the seeded named substrate, no sampler path exists for them, and no open ticket owns building one (tickets 03, 16, 26, 52 and 60, the only ones naming a cluster on a clock, are all resolved).
EVIDENCE: Run-21 grades extracted from `gh run view 33616685427 --log`: PASS=57 FAIL=7 SKIP=18, matching the TRUTH line exactly. Bucketing the 18 SKIP reasons: 12 cite `kind cluster 'driftwood' is not listed by kind get clusters` or `kind cluster 'driftwood' does not exist`; 3 cite `distribution/versions.yaml declares one version (4.0.0)`; 1 cites `GitHub unreachable (Command '['gh', 'auth', 'status']' returned non-zero exit status 1.)`; the remaining 2 are verify-publisher-gate part c and verify-insurer-quote's staleness SKIP. `grep -nE 'kind create|kind |cluster|flux install|docker' .github/workflows/truth.yml` returns nothing — the workflow installs gitsign/kyverno/cosign/flux binaries but stands up no substrate. verify/e2e/lib.sh:23 has a cluster_up() that would create an ephemeral pav-e2e cluster but opens `command -v kind >/dev/null && docker info … || skip "substrate absent (kind/docker)"`. The fact is recorded at .scratch/ecosystem/issues/03-the-truth-surface.md:50 ('truth run 5 … no cluster'), but ticket 03 is Status: resolved, as are the only two other tickets naming an ephemeral cluster (16 and 52).
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5 (the truth surface is the only citable source of what works) and §4 steps 4-5, which are cluster-observed claims.
OWNER: new none — Not owned. Ticket 03 (resolved) records the no-cluster fact as accepted design for the hub's citable run (talk/verify-all.sh grades cluster-free by design, "could-not-look" as SKIP is the honest outcome) but never proposes provisioning a cluster on the hub clock — so it doesn't own fixing the SKIPs, only documents why they're honest. Tickets 16/40/52/60 built the actual cluster-side verification architecture, but it lives in each ADOPTER's own scheduled repo clock (ephemeral KinD there, per ticket 16 Q3 "measurement stays inside the consumer org, on its own clock"), which produces samples the HUB's truth.yml then grades cluster-free (ticket 60, confirmed by TRUTH run 20/21 both showing skip=18, down from 22 after ticket 60 wired verify-e2e-step4 + 3x verify-reconcile to read samples instead of needing a live cluster). That accounts for only 4 of the 18 SKIPs. The remaining ~12 "kind cluster 'driftwood' is not listed" SKIPs the finding cites are a distinct, still-live gap: no open ticket (checked 54 jsonschema-resolved, 56 schedules-open-but-different-cause, 72/73/74-different causes) names provisioning a cluster inside .github/workflows/truth.yml itself, and REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's M5/M6/M7 and its Refuted-claims list are all different root causes (jsonschema install, missing GH token, sample-conversion — M7 is now closed by ticket 60). No REGRILL-ANSWERS entry or ADR addresses provisioning a cluster on the hub clock specifically, and NORTH-STAR §6 / map Out-of-scope say nothing about it. This looks like a genuinely new, unowned finding.
FIT IMPACT: About 14% of the surface (12 of 84) can never move off SKIP as the clock is configured, so a regression in any of those twelve is undetectable from the citable number. A reader of the TRUTH line cannot tell 'we looked and it was fine' from 'we structurally cannot look here'.
REMEDY: Either install kind and stand up one ephemeral cluster in truth.yml (verify/e2e/lib.sh already knows how to create and tear one down), or state in NORTH-STAR §5 that the clock grades offline only and live tails are graded on a separate named lane — so a permanent SKIP becomes a declared property rather than an unexplained gap.

## [minor] EQ-07 The semver and composition mechanisms are verified all-or-nothing by an undocumented convention, so the estate's most load-bearing code is harder to change safely than the thesis's "ordinary software a team owns" promise — no claim it makes is untrue today
(original title: selfcheck is a monolith, not a suite: 53% of gate.py is one untestable function)
CLAIM: In the ~32,000 Python lines that have no test suite — the eight unit repos (28,490 L) plus the hub's own gate instrument, `verify/` and `talk/` (3,738 L) — the substitute for unit tests is a single in-module `selfcheck()` function, one per module, 43 of them across the units and 6 more in hub `verify/`+`talk/`. None of them can be run selectively (`--selfcheck` is a bare flag with no name selector anywhere), and all of them abort at the first failing assert, so the assertions after it are never exercised. The pattern is at its worst in `platform/computed-semver/gate.py`, where `selfcheck` is 597 of 1,134 lines (53%), packs 106 asserts behind exactly one `print`, takes 15s wall (63s CPU) to run, and emits its entire result as a single 2,626-character line carrying 24 semicolon-separated claims — which is the whole of `talk/captures/.estate-clone_platform_computed-semver_verify-gate.out` on origin/main, one line long. Two corrections to the original framing: (1) this is not "the estate" — the hub's `twin/` (33,872 L, 51% of all estate Python) has a genuine pytest suite of 82 files and 1,326 test functions across 18,623 lines of test code, which is selective and reports per-assertion; (2) "cannot report per-assertion results" is false for 8 of the 43 unit selfchecks, including the two largest that the finding itself cited — `compose/composition.py`'s selfcheck (1,009 L, the biggest in the estate) emits 57 progressive `OK <claim>` lines and its capture is 1,568 readable lines, and `party/party_artefact.py` emits 30 for a 39-line readable capture. The single-print shape is the majority (35 of 43) but not the rule, and the readable minority shows the fix is already practised inside the estate. Also: on failure the run does not report "one bit" — an induced late assertion failure produced a line-precise traceback (gate.py:1002), the failing expression, and the actual value; what is lost is which of the 106 passed and whether more would fail, not the identity of the first failure. No ADR owns the convention (`grep -ril selfcheck docs/adr/` returns nothing), so "no claimed ownership" stands.
EVIDENCE: AST measurement of the longest function per module: platform/computed-semver/gate.py selfcheck = 597 of 1,134 lines (53%); platform/compose/composition.py selfcheck = 1,009 of 3,654 (28%) against compose() itself at 298 lines; computed-semver/coverage.py selfcheck = 374 of 976 (38%); cage_engine.py selfcheck = 343 of 1,492; wargamer/tier_pr.py selfcheck = 248 of 792; party/party_artefact.py selfcheck = 237 of 793; verify/schedules/schedules.py selfcheck = 213 of 890; twin/invariants/harness.py's longest check is 282 lines. The consequence is visible on origin/main: talk/captures/.estate-clone_platform_computed-semver_verify-gate.out ends with the whole selfcheck's result as a single ~1,500-character sentence carrying about twenty semicolon-separated claims, so a reader cannot tell which was exercised or which would fail first.
AMBITION: The thesis's claim that this is ordinary versioned software a team can own and change; NORTH-STAR §3's insistence that a claim is checkable.
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (all 74 ticket files, keyword and full-text grep for selfcheck/monolith/untestable/test suite/function length/unit test), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (its full C1/M1-M18 list and its Refuted claims list), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md/NORTH-STAR §6, and the map's Out of scope section (docs/adr/ also has no ADR titled or indexed toward test structure). No ticket, review finding, refutation, or owner decision addresses selfcheck-function monolithism, per-assertion test reporting, or gate.py's 53%-in-one-function shape. The only hits were false-positive keyword matches (e.g. tickets 09/11/19/23 use "testable"/"test" in unrelated contexts about kyverno CEL evaluation or Renovate pinning, not about the selfcheck harness's own structure). NORTH-STAR §6's "out of scope" items (talk/video, power layer, covert sensing, surveillance data, original-org-as-system) do not cover this either — it is a code-structure/testability concern, not excluded by ambition. This finding is new and unowned.
FIT IMPACT: Not a correctness defect — the assertions are real and they run — but it makes the code hostile to incremental change: a one-line edit to gate.py can only be validated by running 597 lines of assertions that report one bit, and the capture that records the result is unreadable.
REMEDY: Each selfcheck is already a sequence of independent assert / print('ok ...') blocks, so splitting it into def check_<name>() functions with a driver loop is mechanical and yields per-check reporting for free. Start with gate.py (highest ratio).

## [major] EQ-08 The human governance layer the mea culpa names as the thing v1 missed has never had more than one party in it: proposer, reviewer, merger and signer are one identity in all nine repos
(original title: Bus factor 1: no second engineer has ever read this code)
CLAIM: Bus factor 1: no second person has ever committed to, reviewed, or been a member of this estate. All nine live repositories have exactly one human author (hub 408 commits by Chris Nesbitt-Smith, 17 by the truth-surface bot; platform 98, driftwood 89, tuppence 58, ludlow 51, nist 14, ico 14, feeds 8, insurer 7 human commits, every other identity a bot), as do all 15 legacy 2022-thesis org repos. Every one of the nine orgs has exactly one member, chrisns. No branch protection and no rulesets anywhere: `branches/main/protection` 404s and `rulesets?includes_parents=true` returns [] on all nine (org-level rulesets could not be read directly for want of admin:org scope, but an inherited ruleset would have appeared in that result). Of all 62 pull requests across the estate, zero have any review of any kind — not 46, all 62, Renovate included — and all 35 merged PRs have merged_by == author == chrisns. There is no CODEOWNERS file in any repo. CORRECTION TO THE ORIGINAL FINDING, part 1: the unreviewed-self-merge half IS owned, twice. The estate self-corrected this exact observation on 2026-07-18 (".scratch/faithful-floor/issues/12-pr-ci-gate.md:44-50: GitHub's own record shows zero reviews and the same account (chrisns) as both author and merger, the same self-merge pattern as every other PR in this solo-dev project"), and the owner ratified a forward rule on 2026-08-28 — "author and merger must be different identities for now" (REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:35, re-grill 29 against P175), now carried as settled in three live tickets. What is genuinely unowned is only the legibility half: nothing in NORTH-STAR.md, the map, or HISTORY.md addresses handover, onboarding or whether a second engineer could pick this up. CORRECTION, part 2: "no second engineer has ever read this code" overstates — reading leaves no trace, all nine repos are public, and one external account (sublimino) has starred the hub. THE SHARPER GAP THE FINDING MISSED: the owner's own ratified rule that author and merger must be different identities is met by zero of the 35 merged PRs, and 29 of those were merged after the rule was set on 2026-08-28 (platform#8, driftwood#16/18/19/20/21/22, tuppence#13/14, ludlow#11/12 among them). Nothing in the tree enforces it. Fit impact stands: this is the honest state of a solo research estate, not negligence, but the codebase's legibility to the platform team the thesis targets has never been tested, and comment density is an untested proxy for it.
EVIDENCE: `git shortlog -sne --all` on the hub: 408 commits by Chris Nesbitt-Smith <chris@cns.me.uk>, 17 by the 'truth surface' bot, no other human. Per-unit author counts: platform 98 human + 4 release bot; driftwood 89 + 3 sampler + 1 github-actions; tuppence 58 + 2; ludlow 51 + 2; nist 14; ico 14; feeds 8; insurer 7 — one human identity in every repo. Corroborated by the github-live map: `repos/O/R/rulesets` returns [] and `branches/main/protection` returns 404 on all nine repos, and every one of 46 examined closed non-Renovate PRs was authored and merged by the same account.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2's eco-system of adopting organisations, and the thesis's implicit claim that a platform team can take this on.
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/, REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (its Major list M1-M18 and its Refuted-claims list), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, NORTH-STAR §6, and map.md's Out of scope — none address solo authorship / bus-factor-1 / absence of a second human reviewer or branch protection as a legibility/fitness risk. Nearest adjacent item: REGRILL answer 29 (P175, REGRILL-ANSWERS.md line 35) says 'author and merger must be different identities for now' — but that's a narrower, differently-scoped decision aimed at preventing unreviewed machine (bot) self-merge, not a statement about human peer review or codebase legibility to a second engineer. REVIEW-2026-08-31 M14 separately notes 'hub main has no protection' but only in service of a fall-detection/CI-signal argument, not a bus-factor or review claim. No ticket, ADR, or refutation covers this finding.
FIT IMPACT: This is the honest state of a solo research estate and I do not read it as negligence. But it bears directly on fitness: the codebase's legibility has never been tested by the audience the thesis targets, and the high comment density (the real mitigation, and it is real) is an untested proxy for that.
REMEDY: No code change. The owner's decision: either test the 'a second engineer can pick this up' claim by having someone actually try, or stop making it in the pitch material.

MINORS:
- EQ-09 platform/graded/README.md names a resolved ticket as the owner of unbuilt work: At exactly the run-21 platform commit, graded/README.md says the Kyverno half of the cage ladder is 'Not yet served' and that eco-system ticket 26 will land it; ticket 26 is Status: resolved.
- EQ-10 An unreadable workflow file makes the identity-regexp cross-check silently not fire: drift/five-facts.py swallows OSError when reading the CI workflow for EXPECTED_IDENTITY_REGEXP, returns empty strings, and the caller then skips the comparison because the value is falsy — turning 'could not look' into 'nothing to check'.
- EQ-11 currency.py's fallback default names policy versions the estate has retired: platform/currency-controller/currency.py falls back to the literal '1.0.0,2.0.0' when it cannot read distribution/versions.yaml, but that array declares only 4.0.0 today and 2.0.0 was retired as unsafe.
- EQ-12 152 known gaps hide behind an undefined private marker while the estate shows zero TODOs: TODO/FIXME/XXX/HACK appear zero times in 66k lines of python and 14k of shell; known gaps are marked with the project-private word 'ponytail:', 152 times, which is not defined in CONTEXT.md.
- EQ-13 The README does not tell a new engineer how to build, test or run anything: README.md mentions none of pytest, .venv, clone-estate.sh, verify-all.sh or the truth surface; it describes the ADRs as 'ADR-0001…0010' when 24 exist; and there is no CONTRIBUTING or DEVELOPING file.
- EQ-14 The developer environment is not the CI environment and nothing pins it: The checked-in .venv is Python 3.14.6 while twin.yml pins 3.12, the same suite takes 6x longer locally than in CI, and no lockfile or dependency manifest exists outside the CI workflow's inline pip install.
- EQ-15 345 MB of tracked audio and video inflate the hub's history: The hub tracks 170 .wav, 76 .mp4 and 143 .png files under .scratch/talk-spec/, about 345 MB, in a repository whose .git is 257 MB and whose CI clones are deliberately full rather than shallow.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- Is the eco-system meant to be operated by someone other than you — a client platform team taking these nine repos and running them — or is it a reference implementation whose job is to be read and cited, not adopted? | WHY: The two purposes have opposite engineering-quality bars. A reference implementation that is read is well served by what exists: 0.37-0.82 prose-to-code, 180 dated in-code incident notes, no dead code, and a daily gate. An estate that is operated by a second team needs the thing that is missing: a test runner, types and lint over the 28,490 lines of unit-repo python, and one copy of the adopter logic instead of three. | CHANGES: If it is read-only, EQ-01 and EQ-03 drop from critical to minor and the remaining work is EQ-02, EQ-05 and EQ-13. If it is adopted, EQ-01 and EQ-03 are the two things that must be built before anything else on the frontier, and several resolved tickets that shipped triplicated code should be reopened.
- Is 'three adopters' meant to be three genuinely independent organisations that each own their own tooling, or three instances of one shared, pinned adopter package? | WHY: Right now it is neither cleanly: the composed policies and render_composed.py are genuinely rendered from a pinned platform artefact (a real strength), while five-facts.py, verify-reconcile.sh, adopter-gate.py and shift-left.yml are hand-maintained triplicates that have diverged 61 to 1,752 lines. The thesis's own doctrine says shared machinery should be a pinned signed dependency; the estate does not apply that doctrine to its own tooling. | CHANGES: If they are meant to be independent, the divergence is a feature and EQ-03 becomes a documentation task (say so, and stop presenting the three reconcile FAILs as comparable evidence). If they are meant to be instances, EQ-03 is the largest build on the board, and the mechanism to do it already exists in platform's publish/pin path.
- Should the daily clock stand up an ephemeral Kubernetes cluster, or should the TRUTH line explicitly declare itself an offline-only grade? | WHY: Twelve of run 21's eighteen SKIPs are one cause: truth.yml installs no kind and creates no cluster, so those twelve checks can never move off could-not-look. verify/e2e/lib.sh already contains a working cluster_up/cluster_down pair. Right now the TRUTH line does not distinguish 'we looked and it was fine' from 'we structurally cannot look here', and a regression in any of those twelve is invisible. | CHANGES: Provisioning a cluster converts about 14% of the surface from permanently-SKIP to genuinely graded, and probably moves the pass/fail counts materially in both directions. Declaring the clock offline-only instead makes the twelve SKIPs a stated property, lets NORTH-STAR §5 be honest about what the number covers, and requires a separately named live lane before any §4 step 4/5 claim can be cited.
- Is 'a second engineer could pick this up' a claim the estate makes, or one it should stop making? | WHY: Every human commit across all nine repos is by one identity, no repo has branch protection, and no pull request has ever been reviewed by a second person. The mitigation — unusually high comment density, 48 READMEs, dated incident notes — is real, but it is an untested proxy: nobody else has tried. | CHANGES: If the claim stands, the cheapest test is to have one engineer attempt to add a check and cut a release from the README alone, and to fix what they hit (which today would start at EQ-13, since the README names none of the operating machinery). If the claim is dropped, EQ-08 and EQ-13 stop being findings and become recorded properties of a solo research estate.
- Should the twin CI workflow's colour track new information, or is a permanently red gate the intended honesty signal? | WHY: twin.yml has failed 25 consecutive runs. Two of the three red jobs are the same owned, pre-registered finding (flux_coverage_floor_is_still_reachable, ticket 70), which is honest. The third — a one-line unused `type: ignore` in twin/feed_signal.py:232 — arrived and was invisible because the workflow was already red. This is REVIEW-2026-08-31's own M14 saturated-signal problem, reproduced on the twin gate; ticket 59 is still open. | CHANGES: If red-means-red, the standing finding needs an expected-red channel (amber) so a new regression is visible, and the same treatment probably applies to the TRUTH line's seven standing fails. If a permanently red gate is the intended statement, then the estate needs some other mechanism to notice a genuinely new regression, because at present it demonstrably does not.
- Is the in-module selfcheck() function the intended permanent test strategy, or a scaffold that a real suite was meant to replace? | WHY: 7,000 lines (24%) of unit-repo python sit inside single monolithic selfcheck functions — 53% of gate.py, 28% of composition.py — with no runner, no failure isolation and an all-or-nothing exit code. The assertions are genuinely good (the tier_pr AST guard, fair.py's mixed-currency refusal, gate.py's rederive checks), so this is a packaging question, not a substance one. | CHANGES: If it is the intended strategy, then EQ-01 and EQ-07 are design decisions to be recorded in an ADR and defended, and the fix is limited to making the captures readable. If it is a scaffold, then a one-line test_selfcheck.py per module plus pytest/mypy jobs in platform's CI is a day's work that turns 7,000 lines of existing assertions into an addressable suite, and should be sequenced ahead of most of the open frontier.

# operability-adoptability

VERDICT: Four audiences, four different answers. (a) Conference: PARTLY FIT. The seven-beat, grade-carrying, generated-not-authored deck is the estate's single best idea and beat 1 opens in 0.15s with zero dependencies — but the committed deck.md on origin/main reads seven "could not look" slides while run 21's own captures grade five of those checks PASS, the thesis's headline runtime claim (>=3 versions coexisting) is undemonstrable by construction because distribution/versions.yaml declares exactly one version, and the only documented live bring-up reconciles a locally-fabricated in-cluster git server while the runbook tells the presenter to say it is the real signed GitHub remote. (b) Adopter organisation: NOT FIT. Nine GitHub orgs, 37 workflows, 84 verify scripts, nine controllers on one KinD cluster, ~2,300 lines of your own Python, three divergent forks of the adopter gate (1087/661/1213 lines), zero onboarding documents anywhere in eight repos, and no measured or stated time-to-first-cage. (c) Regulator or intelligence publisher: PARTLY FIT — the best-designed adopter surface in the estate. The feed envelope is seven closed fields with a well-argued no-in-band-signature rule, the publisher contract is one readable page, and a publisher's own verify passes offline in seconds on a stock laptop (I ran ico/verify-penalty-feed.sh: PASS). But the contract has no independently fetchable home (feeds' own verify SKIPs without a sibling platform checkout), there is no SDK or starter repo, and ico's own README still teaches the retired ed25519 mechanism rather than the envelope adopters pin. (d) ControlPlane asset: NOT FIT as reusable, fit as an argument — the hub, which holds the thesis, PRD, 24 ADRs, NORTH-STAR, the truth surface and 56,233 lines of Python, carries no licence at all (gh api .../license -> 404) while all eight units carry Apache-2.0. What would make it fit: a LICENSE file, a second declared policy line (an owner decision, ticket 58), a rebuilt deck, an onboarding page per audience, and honesty in the runbook about what up.sh actually reconciles from.

STRENGTHS:
- The seven-beat deck discipline is genuinely novel and works: build_deck.py generates deck.md from one capture per check and verify-demo.sh refuses the deck if a figure is not in the capture behind it, if a beat status disagrees with the run, or if a TRUTH line is quoted from another commit. It caught its own staleness in run 21 ('FAIL: talk/deck.md has been hand edited or is stale', talk/captures/verify_demo_verify-demo.out on origin/main) rather than shipping a green lie.
- Beat 1 is instantly demonstrable on any laptop with no dependencies: `python3 platform/fair/fair.py summary platform/fair/scenarios/driftwood-cart-pii.json` returned ale=19558.5, var95=30947.9, tvar=34086.7, carried=34958.4 in 0.155s wall clock, run directly in this review.
- The publisher contract is small, closed and defensible: platform/feeds/schema.json is 7 required fields with additionalProperties:false and an explicit argument for why there is no in-band signature field ('a signature cannot cover itself'). feeds/README.md:20-70 documents file layout, the bump ladder, tag convention and catalogue-free discovery in one page. `bash ico/verify-penalty-feed.sh` PASSes offline in seconds on a stock laptop, including a real `git ls-remote` against nist's remote.
- Real live-cluster evidence does exist on a clock, in the adopters' own lanes: driftwood/drift/samples.jsonl's newest line (run 33624104359, 2026-09-02) records an ephemeral KinD cluster kind-dsample-33624104359 in which fact_1_ready_at_the_pin_on_the_real_remote and fact_2_tag_signature_verified_at_the_source_boundary were observed true against https://github.com/policy-as-versioned-nist/nist at v1.1.0@33a05df.
- The gate's toolchain is pinned by version AND SHA256 (gitsign 0.17.1, kyverno 1.18.2, cosign 3.1.3 at .github/workflows/truth.yml:39-49) with the kyverno-1.19 false-red incident written into the comment — REVIEW-2026-08-31's critical finding C1 is fixed and none of run 21's seven fails is a cage-tier CEL compile error.
- The platform engines are more reusable than the demo suggests: only three hard-coded adopter sets exist estate-wide (platform/identity/verify-federation.sh:47, computed-semver/gate.py:967, comparison_window.py:347, the last two inside selfchecks), and the 77 adopter-name mentions in the 3,654-line compose/composition.py are all comments or fixtures below `def selfcheck()` at line 2617.
- clone-estate.sh:40-56 documents, with the observed failure for each, why both --depth 1 and --filter=blob:none were rejected ('a gate that grades differently depending on how its inputs were fetched is not a gate') — an example of the estate reasoning about its own instrument honestly.
- Ticket 60's reorder made the three verify-reconcile.sh scripts grade the five-fact sample BEFORE checking for a local cluster, which is why they moved SKIP->FAIL in run 21. That is a better outcome, not a regression: the gate now reports a real red where it used to report a comfortable grey. It fixes REVIEW-2026-08-31 finding M7.
- REVIEW-2026-08-31's M1 is substantially fixed: feeds and insurer now have registered workflows and real signed tags (git -C units/feeds tag -l -> threat-register/v1.0.0, threat-register/v2.0.0; units/insurer -> v1.0.0).

## [major] O3 The one instrument that proves "every actor is attestable" is blind to the estate's only multi-feed publisher, and reports that blindness as an honest queue note
(original title: The provenance beat structurally cannot see the feeds publisher's signed tags)
CLAIM: verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:88 selects a unit's newest tag with `git tag -l 'v*.*.*'`, a glob that cannot match the feeds repo's tag convention `<feed>/vX.Y.Z` — documented at feeds/README.md:33-37 and enforced unconditionally by feeds/.github/workflows/cut-release.yml:99,111,114, which builds every tag as `${{ inputs.feed }}/v${{ inputs.version }}`. feeds' two real tags (threat-register/v1.0.0, threat-register/v2.0.0) do verify: gitsign verify-tag against release.yml:38-39's own anchored regexp and issuer returns "Good signature from [.../cut-release.yml@refs/heads/main]" and "Validated Rekor entry: true". Step 6 nevertheless prints "ok   no signed tag yet, honestly queued for cut-release.yml: feeds" and passes with "7 of 8 anchored identity regexps matched" (run-21 capture). No future feed release can change this. The fault is self-evident inside the same capture: part 1 reports "80 published artefacts resolve to a tag on the publisher's real remote; 0 honestly queued" because feed_contract.py:65-71 knows both tag shapes, while part 3, six lines later, calls feeds queued. It is an instrument fault whose fix already exists in the estate's own code, it silently understates provenance by one of four publishers, and it obscures the "at least one signed tag each" done criterion of ticket 57 (Status: claimed). No ticket owns it (`grep -rn "tag -l" .scratch/ecosystem/issues/` is empty). Secondary: the same selector picks platform's plain v2.0.1 and never checks its policy/vX.Y.Z tag line against Rekor.
EVIDENCE: verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:88 `tag="$(git -C "$ESTATE/$u" tag -l 'v*.*.*' 2>/dev/null | sort -V | tail -1)"` followed by `[ -n "$tag" ] || { queued+=("$u"); continue; }`. feeds/README.md:38-41: 'a release tag names the feed: `<feed>/vX.Y.Z`, e.g. `cve/v2.1.0`. A single-feed publisher (`ico`, `nist`) stays on a plain `vX.Y.Z`.' `git -C units/feeds tag -l` -> threat-register/v1.0.0, threat-register/v2.0.0. Run 21 capture (origin/main:talk/captures/verify_e2e_verify-e2e-step6-provenance.out) prints 'ok   no signed tag yet, honestly queued for cut-release.yml: feeds' and the PASS line says '7 of 8 anchored identity regexps matched ... and feeds have no signed tag yet'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR section 4 step 6 (provenance: every step verifiable in Rekor); principle 6 (every actor is attestable)
OWNER: new none — No ticket, REVIEW-2026-08-31.md entry, or REGRILL-ANSWERS.md decision owns this. Searched: grep across .scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md for "verify-e2e-step6-provenance" (zero hits) and for the tag-glob/multi-feed-convention terms (only feed-contract/renovate/composer tickets 04,11,19,21,22,24,57,60,61,62,72 turned up, none touching step6's tag selection). REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's M5 (owned by ticket 54, resolved) is the only place step6 is mentioned at all, but M5 is about the clock never installing jsonschema causing step6 to hard-SKIP — a different fault in the same script. Ticket 54's Answer confirms it fixed exactly that: "verify-e2e-step6 both PASS once [jsonschema] is present" — it made step6 actually run, which is what exposes this tag-glob bug; ticket 54 never touches the `tag -l 'v*.*.*'` line. Ticket 57 (feeds/insurer runnable, first signed tags cut) converts a different script's SKIPs (`verify-feed-contract`'s "waiting for tag threat-register/v1.0.0"), not step6-provenance's "no signed tag yet, honestly queued" line — ticket 57's Done criteria never mention step6 or its tag glob. No entry in REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's Refuted-claims list or REGRILL-ANSWERS.md addresses the feeds multi-feed tag convention vs. step6's plain-v glob. NORTH-STAR §4 step 6 / principle 6 and the map's Out-of-scope were not searched line-by-line but nothing in the ticket/review corpus references this instrument fault, so it is genuinely new and unowned.
FIT IMPACT: The audience-facing provenance slide understates the estate by one of its four publishers, and the understatement is silent by construction: no number of feed releases will ever change the verdict. It is an instrument fault masquerading as an honest 'queued' note.
REMEDY: Widen the glob to `'*v[0-9]*.[0-9]*.[0-9]*'`, or better, resolve each unit's expected tag shape from its own party.yaml `publishes[]`, and re-grade.

## [major] O4 The presenter's bring-up page would have the stage narrate a signed-GitHub reconcile the cluster never performs — it reconciles an unsigned tag it created seconds earlier from a git server inside itself, and the runbook's own honesty banner is where the claim is repeated
(original title: The presenter's runbook misdescribes the only live bring-up it documents)
CLAIM: talk/RUNBOOK.md:74-77 tells the presenter that `.estate-clone/driftwood/scripts/up.sh` points Flux at the real `policy-as-versioned-driftwood` GitHub repo and that "mo-09 retired the in-cluster git-server this used to seed". At the default-branch HEAD a presenter actually clones (clone-estate.sh:57, run by talk/up.sh:60), that script does the opposite: it git-inits a throwaway seed from the local gitops/ tree and tags it with an unsigned annotated tag (up.sh:30,36), builds and loads the git-server image (up.sh:74-75), applies and restarts `deploy/git-server` (up.sh:78,84-87), and applies a GitRepository at `url: ${GIT_URL_IN_CLUSTER}` (up.sh:97) = `http://git-server.flux-system.svc.cluster.local/cgi-bin/git/driftwood.git` (lib.sh:12). tuppence and ludlow carry the identical arrangement (their lib.sh:12, up.sh:76,86-89,99,127), so the mo-09 removal has landed in none of the three committed trees. The runbook is also internally inconsistent: its §4 still says `reset.sh soft` "re-seeds the git source", which is the git-server world §1 says is gone. This is a hub-documentation fault, not an instrument fault: driftwood's README.md:19,31-33, gotk-sync.yaml:10-11, drift-sample.yml:12-16,160 and verify-reconcile.sh:57-66,76-94 all describe the git server accurately and grade whichever world the live url picks. Two corrections to the finding as filed: (1) ownership is not absent — `.scratch/ecosystem/issues/16-...:20` names this exact regression and ticket 42 (`:9`) scopes "re-land the mo-09 git-server removal in all three up.sh" yet is marked `Status: resolved` without having done it, i.e. the defect is owned and falsely closed; (2) a real-remote reconcile does exist, in the scheduled sampler lane on an ephemeral CI cluster (run-21 capture: `fact_1_ready_at_the_pin_on_the_real_remote` true for driftwood-composed, nist, platform) — so the runbook misattributes the sampler lane's property to the presenter's local bring-up rather than inventing it outright.
EVIDENCE: talk/RUNBOOK.md:57-60: '`.estate-clone/driftwood/scripts/up.sh` — KinD `driftwood` + Flux, pointed at the real `policy-as-versioned-driftwood` GitHub repo (mo-09 retired the in-cluster git-server this used to seed) + reconcile.' Against that: driftwood/scripts/up.sh:32-38 git-inits a throwaway seed from the local gitops/ tree and tags it v1.0.0; :74 `docker build -q -t "$IMAGE" "$WORK/ctx"`; :78 `kubectl apply -f "$GITSERVER_DIR/deployment.yaml"`; :93-101 apply a GitRepository with `url: ${GIT_URL_IN_CLUSTER}`, defined at driftwood/scripts/lib.sh:11 as `http://git-server.flux-system.svc.cluster.local/cgi-bin/git/driftwood.git`. `grep -n 'github.com' driftwood/scripts/up.sh` matches only a comment (line 57). `git -C units/driftwood log --oneline -- scripts/up.sh` shows three commits, none of which is a mo-09 retirement.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR principle 4 ('Adopters pin by tag and commit') and principle 6 ('Every artefact carries a signature that says what it does and does not assert'); NORTH-STAR section 4 step 4
OWNER: resolved-ticket-but-still-true ticket 42 (Question: "re-land the mo-09 git-server removal in all three up.sh"); underlying fact first recorded in resolved ticket 16 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/16-flux-rescoped-and-verified-at-the-cluster.md:20) — Not new. Ticket 16 (resolved 2026-08-28) already documented the identical fact verbatim: "driftwood/scripts/up.sh ... still seeds a local repo, tags it unsigned ... and points Flux at the git-server" and deferred the fix to ticket 42, whose Question is literally "re-land the mo-09 git-server removal in all three up.sh." Ticket 42 (resolved 2026-08-29) claims this was built, but its Answer only describes tuppence/ludlow gaining a sampler, falsifiers and scheduled lane — it never states driftwood's git-server line was removed. I independently re-verified at the fresh clone HEAD (units/driftwood/scripts/up.sh, lib.sh): GIT_URL_IN_CLUSTER is still http://git-server.flux-system.svc.cluster.local/... and up.sh still builds and applies the git-server Deployment — the mo-09 removal was never actually landed for driftwood despite ticket 42's resolved status, matching finding O4's evidence exactly. (Ticket 40's Answer separately claimed driftwood was "reconciling the real remotes," and ticket 60's 2026-09-01 correction already withdrew that OBSERVED figure as unsupported — but for a different reason, missing sample data, not the URL/git-server fact.) No ticket, REVIEW-2026-08-31.md, or REGRILL-ANSWERS.md addresses RUNBOOK.md's own prose (the mo-09-retired-git-server claim at RUNBOOK.md:74-76) as a documentation defect in its own right — that presenter-doc framing is undocumented anywhere — but the code-level fact it rests on is already tracked, and its claimed fix (ticket 42) is already shown by this finding's own evidence not to have landed for driftwood.
FIT IMPACT: The live provenance beat, if a presenter ran it and narrated it as the runbook instructs, would claim a signed-remote reconcile the cluster is not doing. driftwood's own README describes the git server accurately, so the code and the unit doc agree; the hub's presenter-facing runbook is the wrong one — which is the document a presenter reads under pressure.
REMEDY: Either correct RUNBOOK section 1 to state what up.sh does, or add a `--remote` mode that points the GitRepository at the real GitHub URL and signed tag and lets the gitsign-verifier gate it — which is the beat the talk actually wants.

## [minor] O5 The eco-system's own repository is legally un-reusable: the hub's docs, ADRs, truth surface and twin are all-rights-reserved, while every artefact a consumer actually pins is Apache-2.0
(original title: The hub — the most valuable half of the estate — carries no licence)
CLAIM: The hub repo `policy-as-versioned-flux/policy-as-versioned-flux` is public and carries no licence — no LICENSE/COPYING file has ever existed on any ref in its history (`git log --all --diff-filter=A -- 'LICENSE*' 'COPYING*'` → empty), `gh api .../license` returns 404, and the repo's `license` field is `null` — while all eight unit repos (each in its own org, e.g. `policy-as-versioned-platform/platform`) are public and carry a 202-line Apache-2.0 LICENSE that GitHub recognises as `Apache-2.0`. The unlicensed hub holds NORTH-STAR.md, docs/PRD.md (472 lines), all 24 ADRs, CONTEXT.md, verify/, talk/, twin/ (33,872 Python lines) and tests/ (18,623 Python lines). The gap is wider than the hub alone: all 14 other repos in the same org (fleet, policy, ledger, storefront, reports, api, datastore, cloud, governance-agent, handbook-generator, pr-gate-action, c2p-collector, readiness-collector, renovate-config) are also public with `license: null`, so the divide is the original thesis org (15 repos, unlicensed) versus the eight new unit orgs (Apache-2.0). No ticket, ADR, prior review or HISTORY entry mentions licensing anywhere (`grep -riln 'licen' .scratch/ecosystem/` → no matches), so it is unowned. Impact: as "all rights reserved", the hub's content cannot lawfully be reused, modified or redistributed in a client engagement, a proposal, or as a ControlPlane reference architecture — though GitHub's ToS does still permit viewing and forking a public repo, so "cannot be forked" should be dropped. The NORTH-STAR portability line (NORTH-STAR.md:65) is an adjacency, not a direct ambition breach: it sits under "6. What is explicitly out" and concerns adopter feeds being re-derivable from pins and switching cost being priced, not source-code licensing. Remedy unchanged: add Apache-2.0 LICENSE to the hub (and, if intended, the 14 legacy org repos) plus a README line.
EVIDENCE: `git ls-files | grep -i license` in the hub returns no output. `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-flux/policy-as-versioned-flux/license` -> `{"message":"Not Found", ... "status":"404"}`. `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-flux/policy-as-versioned-flux --jq .visibility` -> `public`. `ls units/*/LICENSE | wc -l` -> 8. The unlicensed hub holds NORTH-STAR.md, docs/PRD.md, all 24 ADRs, CONTEXT.md, verify/, talk/, twin/ (33,872 lines of Python) and tests/ (18,623 lines).
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR section 6 ('A power layer, except portability... feeds are re-derivable from pins a departing adopter keeps') — portability is explicitly in scope, and an unlicensed public repo is the opposite of portable
OWNER: new none — No ownership found. `grep -ril 'licen' .scratch/ecosystem/issues/` returns nothing (confirmed independently). `grep -ni 'licen' .scratch/ecosystem/REVIEW-2026-08-31.md` and `.scratch/drift-review-2026-08-27/REGRILL-ANSWERS.md` both return nothing — no C1/M1-M18 item, no refuted claim, no re-grill answer touches licensing. NORTH-STAR §6 "What is explicitly out" lists portability-as-priced-lock-in (re-grill 38) but this is about switching-cost economics, not repo licensing — it does not cover or moot this finding. map.md's "Out of scope" section header exists at line 118 but grep for licen there also returned nothing. This is a genuinely new, unowned finding.
FIT IMPACT: Nothing in the hub can legally be lifted into a client engagement, quoted in a proposal, forked by a prospect, or reused by ControlPlane as a reference architecture. For audience (d) this alone is disqualifying, and it is the cheapest finding in this report to fix.
REMEDY: Add LICENSE (Apache-2.0, matching the units) at the hub root and a one-line note in README.md.

## [major] O7 The estate's own README claims a shared apparatus it does not have — the thing that is genuinely inherited is the policy, and saying otherwise is the overclaim this project exists to avoid
(original title: The adopter surface is copy-paste, contradicting the config-base claim)
CLAIM: The estate publishes no shared adopter apparatus: the same responsibility (cs-28's adopter gate) exists as three independently written implementations — driftwood/.github/scripts/adopter-gate.py 1087 lines, tuppence/.github/scripts/adopter-gate.py 661, ludlow/.github/scripts/adopter_gate.py 1213 (renamed), sharing only 260-340 identical lines — over differing workflow sets (8/6/6) and 2,270-2,422 lines of adopter-side Python each, with no shared action, reusable workflow, template repo or onboarding guide anywhere (the only `uses:` across all 20 adopter workflows are actions/checkout@v4 and actions/setup-node@v4). What is genuinely inherited is the policy layer: composed/policies/v4.0.0, kind/ and scripts/lib.sh are identical modulo the adopter's own name — not byte-identical as originally stated. The divergence has consequences beyond duplication: only ludlow pins the Sigstore trust root (--trusted-root + trusted_root.json), so cs-28's "identity-pinned and offline" criterion is met by one of three gates while tuppence's docstring still claims offline; and ticket 42's Flux path fix (./gitops/apps), found by running driftwood's own lane against the real remote, was applied to tuppence and ludlow but never back-ported to driftwood, whose gotk-sync.yaml:40 still says ./apps against a tree that has no root apps/ — applied unedited in driftwood's scheduled sampler (drift-sample.yml:158-161) and invisible to the five-fact instrument, which samples only the nist party. The estate acknowledges the drift ("evolved independently per adopter", read-two-pins.py:6-9) and platform declares the CI layer institution-owned by design (shift-left/ci-workflow.example.yml:1-3), but no ticket owns consolidating it, and the 2022 org's own pr-gate-action composite action shows the shared-artefact shape existed and was not carried forward. The correct ambition reference is platform/README.md:11-13's own unqualified "inherited rather than copy-pasted" sentence, which reads wider than what is actually inherited — not NORTH-STAR principle 4, whose pinned-signed-composed policy claim does hold.
EVIDENCE: platform/README.md:11-14 states the config-base promise. `wc -l` on the gates: driftwood/.github/scripts/adopter-gate.py 1087, tuppence/.github/scripts/adopter-gate.py 661, ludlow/.github/scripts/adopter_gate.py 1213 (note the third even renames the file). Adopter-side Python totals (excluding twin/): driftwood 2,321, tuppence 2,422, ludlow 2,270. Workflow counts: 8/6/6, with twin-sweep.yml and verify-identity-regexp.yml only in driftwood. `grep -n 'path:' */gitops/flux-system/gotk-sync.yaml` -> driftwood `./apps`, tuppence `./gitops/apps`, ludlow `./gitops/apps` — ticket 42's fix was never back-ported to the repo where the bug was found. What IS shared: composed/policies/v4.0.0/*.yaml and the kind/ and scripts/lib.sh templates are byte-identical.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR principle 4 ('Adopters pin by tag and commit, compose from several parents like class inheritance'); the config-base pattern claim in platform/README.md
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (all 74 tickets, incl. 16 "Flux rescoped and verified at the cluster" and 42 "Widen the Flux slice to tuppence and ludlow"), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (Critical/Major/Minor list and Refuted claims), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, map.md Out-of-scope, and NORTH-STAR §6. No ticket, prior-review finding, owner decision, or out-of-scope item addresses adopter-gate divergence (1087/661/1213-line forks), workflow-set drift, or the gotk-sync.yaml path inconsistency (driftwood `./apps` vs tuppence/ludlow `./gitops/apps`). Ticket 16's own facts-found (2026-08-28) independently note driftwood's Kustomization reconciles `./apps` (line 21) but never frames this as a cross-adopter divergence needing backport, and ticket 42's Answer covers only samplers/reconcile/engine-install parity, not the gotk-sync path or the adopter-gate.py line-count/workflow-set divergence — so the finding's own attribution of the path fix to "ticket 42" is not supported by that ticket's actual scope/Answer. REVIEW-2026-08-31 M11(4) is the closest prior material but addresses a different gap (observation-lane server-side cage on public repos), not adopter-gate consolidation; that review's text explicitly says "No ticket proposes consolidating the adopter gate or publishing it as a pinned artefact" is itself absent — i.e. this exact point was not raised before. Treat O7 as a genuinely new, unowned finding.
FIT IMPACT: A fourth adopter must fork ~2,300 lines of Python and 6-8 workflows by hand, choosing which of three divergent gates to copy, with no template repo and no guidance. This is the largest single obstacle to the estate demonstrating anything other than itself, and it undercuts the doctrine it is meant to prove.
REMEDY: Decide whether the adopter gate is platform-published code — in which case publish it as a versioned artefact adopters pin, which is the estate's own doctrine applied to itself — or genuinely per-adopter, in which case stop calling it the config-base pattern. Either answer is defensible; the current state is neither.

MINORS:
- O9 The ratified ambition and fifteen other places still describe a six-participant estate: NORTH-STAR section 2 still records the intelligence publisher and the insurer as 'Does not exist yet', though both are real repos with real gitsign-verifying tags; and fifteen lines across five files still say 'six units' / 'six-org' / 'the six parties', including CONTEXT.md's very first glossary entry.
- O10 There is no onboarding document of any kind, for any audience: No repository in the estate contains a getting-started, onboarding, quick-start or how-to-adopt document; a prospective adopter or publisher has nothing to read except three demo repos to reverse-engineer.
- O11 The documented prerequisites are incomplete; a newcomer's first run silently degrades to SKIPs: talk/RUNBOOK.md's required-CLI list omits gitsign, cosign and the two Python packages the clock installs, so a newcomer following the runbook on a stock laptop gets SKIPs whose cause the runbook does not explain.
- O12 The publisher contract has no home a publisher can fetch: The feed envelope schema — the one artefact contract the eco-system is built on — lives inside platform/, so an independent publisher cannot validate its own envelope without cloning the platform; feeds' own verify script SKIPs when no sibling platform checkout is present.
- O13 The regulator's own README documents the retired mechanism: ico/README.md's 'What's here' section describes only the legacy ed25519-signed schema/ tree and never mentions penalty-schema/, the ADR-0019 envelope at v1/v2/v3 that every adopter actually pins.
- O14 The deck is never proven to render, and the one tool that would render it is unpinned: The marp render in verify-demo.sh is gated on DECK_RENDER=1, which nothing in the repository ever sets, so the gate proves the deck's content is honest and never proves it becomes slides; and when it does run it invokes marp-cli@latest, unpinned, in an estate that pins every other tool by version and SHA256.
- O15 The truth surface grades unreleased mainline, not what an adopter would pin: clone-estate.sh clones each unit's default branch, and its own comment still says no signed tag exists yet, though 24 signed tags now exist across the eight units — so the citable number grades HEAD, not the pinned, signed dependencies the thesis says consumption happens through.
- O16 Every clock in the estate fires ~4.5 hours later than every document says, and the hub badge is permanently red: The hub's truth workflow declares cron 05:47 UTC and actually fires around 09:54-10:27 UTC; and every scheduled run concludes 'failure' by design, so there is no signal distinguishing 'the estate has reds' from 'the instrument broke'.
- O17 driftwood's README is stale in the direction of over-claiming: The teaching-default adopter's README still promises an offline-safe bring-up that the hub's own runbook explicitly retracted for the whole estate, and still closes with a 'Phase 0 / what's here now vs later' section listing work that has since shipped.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- Who is the primary audience you are actually optimising for right now — the conference room, a named prospective adopter, ControlPlane's sales motion, or the argument itself as a written artefact? The estate currently spends effort as if all four are equally weighted. | WHY: The four audiences want incompatible things. A conference audience wants two beats that run live and a legible story; an adopter wants a template repo and a time-to-first-cage number; a publisher wants a fetchable envelope contract and an example; ControlPlane wants a licence and a clean seam between reusable and bespoke. Today none of the four is fully served, and several findings above (O5, O10, O12) are trivially cheap for one audience and irrelevant to the others. | CHANGES: It reorders the frontier. If the answer is the conference room, O1 (the second declared version) and O2 (rebuild the deck) dominate and O5/O10/O12 can wait. If it is adopters or ControlPlane, the licence, an ADOPTING.md and consolidating the adopter gate come first and the coexistence subject can stay a narrated claim. If it is the argument itself, most of the operational findings drop to minor and the documentation drift findings (O9, O13, O17) rise.
- Is a fourth adopter — one you did not author — a goal, or is the estate deliberately a closed demonstration of three example consumers? | WHY: NORTH-STAR section 1 says 'The orgs are example consumers that demonstrate the whole eco-system operating', which reads either way. If a real outside adopter is a goal, the three divergent adopter gates (O7), the absent onboarding page (O10) and the platform-resident envelope schema (O12) are blocking architecture, not documentation debt. If the estate is closed by design, they are all minor and should be recorded as deliberate. | CHANGES: It decides whether the adopter gate becomes a platform-published, pinned artefact (a real build with a real version), whether a template/scaffold repo gets created, and whether O7/O10/O12 are findings at all or just notes. It also determines whether 'the config-base pattern' stays in platform/README.md or is struck.
- Do you want the truth surface's clock to stand up a real cluster, or is 'the offline half plus the adopters' sampled facts' the honest and intended scope of pass=57? | WHY: Today truth.yml installs no kind and creates no cluster, so 12 of run 21's 18 skips are structural and can never turn green, while the adopters' own drift-sample lanes do run real KinD clusters daily and produce the only live facts. Nobody reading the TRUTH line is told this. The estate's rule is 'a green that could not look is a red' — but a permanent grey that structurally cannot look is a different thing again, and the surface has no vocabulary for it. | CHANGES: Either the gate grows a cluster-capable job (which would convert ~12 permanent SKIPs into real PASS/FAIL and materially change every headline number), or RUNBOOK section 5 and the deck gain one sentence scoping what pass= counts. It also affects whether the 18 skips should be reported as a separate 'structurally unlookable' row rather than folded in with genuine could-not-looks.
- Is the estate meant to be reusable by others — forkable, quotable, liftable into a client engagement — or is it a private demonstration you show and narrate? | WHY: The hub is public and has no licence, so today it is legally 'all rights reserved' while all eight units are Apache-2.0. That mismatch is either an oversight worth ten minutes or a deliberate position worth recording. It also determines whether the gitsign-verifier (O6) needs a real image and release train or can stay a time-boxed ConfigMap script. | CHANGES: A LICENSE file at the hub root, and a short 'what is reusable, what is bespoke' section in README that draws the seam explicitly (the envelope, the truth-surface discipline, fair.py and the compose refusals on one side; every up.sh, the git-server, the three adopter gates and the verifier on the other).
- The RUNBOOK says driftwood's bring-up reconciles the real signed GitHub remote; the script reconciles a git server it builds on the laptop seconds earlier. Which of those is the demo you want to give? | WHY: These are two different talks. The in-cluster git server is reliable at a venue and demonstrates Flux pinning immutably; it does not demonstrate a signed, cross-organisation dependency, which is the thesis. The real-remote path demonstrates the thesis and needs network, a gitsign-verifier pod that apk-installs at start, and a tolerance for Rekor being slow. Right now the code does one and the runbook narrates the other, so whichever you intended, one of them is wrong. | CHANGES: Either RUNBOOK section 1 is corrected (cheap, and the demo stays a pinning demo), or up.sh grows a --remote mode and the live provenance beat becomes real (a build, and it makes O6's verifier packaging urgent because it becomes load-bearing on stage).
- Ticket 58 decision (1) — declare a second policy line — has been waiting since 2026-08-31. Is there a reason not to take it, or has it simply not been in front of you? | WHY: Three verify scripts SKIP today for the single reason that versions.yaml declares one version, and those three cover coexistence, retirement and the shift-left window — three of the seven '-ables' and the claim most identified with the original talk. Ticket 63's isolated-default flip forces a major that could be that line, so the cost may be one decision rather than one build. Everything downstream of it (ticket 63, and beat 2 of any talk) is blocked. | CHANGES: Answering it unblocks tickets 58 and 63, converts three permanent SKIPs into gradeable checks, and restores the talk's second beat. Declining it, with a dated reason, would mean the estate deliberately no longer demonstrates multi-version coexistence — which is a defensible position (the retirement reasons in versions.yaml:29-50 are strong) but one the README, CONTEXT.md and PRD would then all need to stop claiming.

# process-and-record

VERDICT: Not yet fit, and the unfitness is concentrated in one place: the estate's architecture rests on ~84 decision items the owner is recorded as never having reasoned about, the durable record (docs/adr/) does not say so, and nothing converts them. The record's honesty is genuinely excellent — every bare-agree ticket quotes the owner verbatim and states in its own text that a bare agree does not ratify; ticket 08 self-reports its own budget breach on the day; ticket 60 cites a real numbered TRUTH line and discloses the caveat that limits it; the truth number has never been gamed (18 lines, none with fail=0, every red owned by a named ticket). But honesty about a debt is not the same as servicing it. The one rule written specifically to prevent this — GAPS.md:93 rule 1, "No recommendation attached to an architectural question" — was silently dropped when the rules were copied into map.md:16, and within three hours ~85 items with recommendations attached went to the owner in one batch and came back on one line. Fifteen resolved build tickets cite as proof a TRUTH line (run=7, 2026-08-29) whose hub tree contains none of the checks they claim; only one has been corrected and no ticket owns the other fourteen. Two ADRs superseded by decided owner positions carry no banner; four of the six ADRs born of the bare-agree batch do not record their own provisionality; one architectural reversal (Audit→Deny, ADR-0022's 2026-08-28 22:15 addendum) was taken inside an implementation run with no round at all and CONTEXT.md still contradicts it. What would make it fit: mark provisionality in the ADRs themselves, make Status: resolved illegal while items are PROVISIONAL (or record the owner's explicit acceptance that the recommendations ARE the architecture and retire the vocabulary), restore rule 1 to map.md, correct the fourteen ticket Answers and widen ticket 67(d)'s check to cover them, and put the fifteen assistant-resolved cross-ticket conflicts to the owner in the one-page panel-verdict shape that is the only format that has ever produced a reasoned reply.

STRENGTHS:
- The record never claims a ratification it does not have: all seventeen bare-agree tickets quote the owner verbatim and state in their own text that a bare agree does not ratify (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/04-the-feed-contract.md:17, 09-the-cage-ladder-v2.md:141, 15-price-everything-that-was-counted.md:78, 58-...:29-30).
- Breaches are self-reported on the day they happen: 08-the-pound-seam.md:17 records its own overrun ('The daily budget of five decisions was already spent by tickets 04 and 07 before this round; the owner answered on the day anyway, and that is recorded here'), and map.md:18 records the five-per-day waiver as an owner instruction rather than silently ignoring the rule.
- Every one of the 24 ADRs carries reconstructible rationale: a section inventory across docs/adr/*.md shows each has an '## Alternatives' / '## Considered options' / '## Why' block plus '## Consequences', so a newcomer can reconstruct why any ADR exists from the ADR alone. Five supersessions (0011, 0014, 0015, 0016, 0018) carry dated banners.
- Ticket 61's promise was kept and is now citable: talk/captures/verify_renovate_verify-renovate-merged-feed-pr.out reads 'SKIP: no merged Renovate feed-pin PR exists yet...' at run 17 (commit 93c2d79) and 'PASS: driftwood #20: Renovate raised threat-register v1 -> v2, Chris Nesbitt-Smith merged it, and party.yaml and composed/ moved together' from run 18 through run 21 unchanged. This closes REVIEW-2026-08-31 M8 on a numbered line.
- Ticket 60 is the standard the rest should meet: a genuinely numbered TRUTH line (run 20, verifiable in talk/truth.log), plus a self-disclosed caveat at 60-...:186 ('today's graded samples came from workflow_dispatch firings... not from the cron event'), plus a successor ticket (74) for the part that did not happen.
- The truth number has never been gamed: talk/truth.log's eighteen lines show fail counts 16,11,11,11,11,11,14,16,16,6,7,3,1,1,3,3,7,7 -- fail=0 has never occurred -- and each of run 21's seven reds maps onto a named open ticket (72, 73, 62, 66, 74). Process rule 4 ('done is the truth surface, never the demo') is operationally intact.
- The provisional/decided distinction is applied with real discipline: the same 'five questions in one batch' shape produced DECIDED once (D1-D5, on a reasoned reply) and PROVISIONAL the second time (ticket 58, bare 'Agree'). The record graded two owner replies differently on their actual wording rather than on the batching pattern.
- The enact guard's fifteen disable/re-enable cycles (2026-08-31 14:51 to 2026-09-01 09:59) are each a separate dated commit on main with a stated reason, the mode is a checked-in file visible in git blame, and twin/ENACT_MODE currently reads 'operations'. Two of the fifteen record the guard doing its job (fdfc2cf 'back to operations, the merges are not mine to make').

## [major] P1 The eco-system's architecture is the assistant's, decided in the asking: the drift review's own corrective against that was dropped when its rules were copied into the map, with no decision recorded
(original title: The corrective rule was dropped when the process rules were copied into the map, and the pathology it existed to stop recurred at higher intensity)
CLAIM: GAPS.md:93 process rule 1 ("No recommendation attached to an architectural question. State the trade, or make the call and record it as the assistant's.") was dropped when the drift review's process rules were carried into the live map: map.md:16 lists five rules "from the drift review" -- GAPS rules 2, 3, 4, 5 plus a new substitute about bare agrees -- and rule 1 (along with rule 6, which unlike rule 1 has an owning ticket) is absent; no wording of rule 1 appears anywhere in .scratch/ecosystem/, and no ticket owns it. The rule was the review's own headline remedy for its headline finding (REPORT.md:26: 482 decisions each carrying a recommendation, 157 bare architectural answers, 147 of them taking the recommendation; REPORT.md:249 and appendices/G-drift-findings.md:1434, which adds "do not do both and then cite his one word as agreement" and "no single reply may close more than one decision"). It was a review recommendation, not something the owner ratified -- REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:3 records only the north star as ratified -- but map.md:16 self-binds to the set, so the omission is a real discrepancy in the live process record. The pathology then recurred at higher per-reply intensity: fourteen held grilling rounds, five recommendations each, were put to the owner between the map's charting (8a14528, 2026-08-28 03:31) and their resolution (20568bc, 2026-08-28 12:32), and all 70 were accepted on the single line "ive already read the recommendations and I can't find fault with a single one" (GRILL-WALK.md:5 states the 70; all 14 ticket files carry that owner line; typical form at 13-lift-or-retire-the-original-mechanisms.md:60). Five further items (D1-D5) closed on a second owner line with a reason, and tickets 04, 07 and 08 had already closed 17 more on three earlier separate bare-agree lines. The pattern continued after the batch: ticket 58's round of 2026-08-31 records "Five questions were put with recommendations." The map's substitute rule records the resulting debt as provisional but does not slow its accrual.
EVIDENCE: .scratch/drift-review-2026-08-27/GAPS.md:93 carries rule 1 verbatim. .scratch/ecosystem/map.md:16 lists five process rules 'from the drift review' and rule 1 is not among them; `grep -rn "recommendation attached" .scratch/ecosystem/` returns no hits. The rule exists because .scratch/drift-review-2026-08-27/REPORT.md:26 measured the pathology: '482 decisions were put to you. Every one carried my recommendation. 306 (63%) got a bare "agree"... Of the 254 architectural decisions, 157 were bare, and 147 of those took my recommendation.' REPORT.md:249 names the remedy explicitly. The recommendation pattern continued: .scratch/ecosystem/issues/13-lift-or-retire-the-original-mechanisms.md:60 '➡️ (a). It adds no mechanism beyond what ticket 10 must build...'. Timeline from `git log`: map charted 8a14528 2026-08-28 03:31; fourteen grilling tickets resolved in one commit 20568bc 2026-08-28 12:32.
AMBITION: GAPS.md process rule 1; REPORT.md:26 and :249 (the drift review's own headline finding, ratified 2026-08-27)
OWNER: new none — Verified accurate and unowned. GAPS.md:93 rule 1 ("No recommendation attached to an architectural question...") is confirmed absent from map.md:16's process-rules list (which carries only rules 2 and 3's substance); `grep -rn "recommendation attached" .scratch/ecosystem/` returns no hits, confirming the claim. The only adjacent item is REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's "Refuted claims" entry on "17 of 22 decisions rest on a bare agree with no route to ratification," refuted via the provisional-convention rule — but that addresses ratification-route (rule 3's territory), not rule 1's recommendation-attachment prohibition, and never mentions recommendations. No open ticket, ADR, REGRILL-ANSWERS entry, NORTH-STAR §6 clause, or map Out-of-scope item claims ownership of this specific gap.
FIT IMPACT: The prior estate accumulated 157 bare architectural decisions over months; the current estate produced ~85 in a single reply, so per-reply density rose rather than fell. The provisional convention the map substituted records the debt but does not slow its accrual, and nothing converts it (see P2). The architecture the estate has already shipped signed tags on top of therefore rests on the exact failure mode the review was commissioned to end.
REMEDY: Restore rule 1 to map.md:16, or delete it from GAPS.md with a dated reason. A rule the owner never rescinded and the operating document silently omits is worse than either. For future rounds: state the trade with no recommendation, or make the call and record it as the assistant's.

## [major] P4 The honesty claim fails inside the tracker: fifteen NORTH-STAR §4 build tickets are marked resolved on a TRUTH line that arithmetically proves their checks were not yet in the gate, and fourteen remain uncorrected and unowned
(original title: Fifteen resolved tickets cite as proof a TRUTH line that exists and contradicts them; fourteen are still uncorrected and unowned)
CLAIM: Fifteen resolved tickets (21, 25, 26, 28, 29, 32, 36, 40, 41, 42, 43, 47, 49, 50, 52) each close with "Definition of done: its check is in `talk/verify-all.sh`. The run that recorded it is the TRUTH line of 2026-08-29." That line exists and is unique — talk/truth.log:5, `TRUTH 2026-08-29T12:03Z run=7 hub=918022b units=[driftwood=eacae33 ico=8902b66 ludlow=a800a58 nist=33a05df platform=58ef9c5 tuppence=751522b] pass=43 fail=11 skip=0 excluded=2 total=56` — and it grades the pre-build estate, so it is proof of the opposite of what the tickets cite it for. All six unit SHAs it names are commits dated 2026-08-22 to 2026-08-25, four to seven days before the "Built 2026-08-29 by the /implement run of 2026-08-28 to 29" the Answers claim, and unchanged across runs 4 through 9. The hub tree 918022b holds only `verify/{_estate.py,party,proportionality,provenance}` and a `talk/` with no build_deck.py, no verify-demo.sh and no captures/, so the named checks of tickets 21 (verify/feed-contract), 25 (verify/pound-seam), 28 (verify/schedules), 29 (verify/twin-evals), 47 (talk/verify-demo.sh) and 52 (verify/e2e) did not exist; platform at 58ef9c5 carries neither verify-source-verification.sh (ticket 41) nor verify-first-gate-determined-release.sh (ticket 43) nor identity/verify-federation.sh (ticket 32); and the feeds and insurer repos of tickets 21, 36, 49 and 50 are not in the run at all. Where a script of the right name did exist it held pre-build content — tuppence's and ludlow's verify-reconcile.sh at the run-7 SHAs contain zero five-fact or falsifier markers against fourteen at HEAD, though ticket 42's Answer describes that sampler as built. Run 7 discovered 56 scripts; the build's scripts were not discovered until run 12 (total=83). Exactly one of the fifteen has been corrected: ticket 40, by ticket 60 on 2026-09-01, whose dated note itself records that "The TRUTH line of 2026-08-29" was a local rehearsal. Fourteen carry no correction, and no ticket owns the false citation: ticket 60 scoped itself to ticket 40's Answer, and ticket 67's check (d) covers only pass/fail figures quoted in map.md — which would not catch a citation that names a date rather than a number. Two open tickets do charter dated corrections to two of the fourteen Answers (64 for ticket 29, 70 for ticket 28) for unrelated substantive reasons, leaving twelve Answers untouched by any chartered correction and the citation itself unowned in all fourteen. The map's existing correction (map.md:64-67) is weaker than the defect: it says only that the 65/0/16 figure was a rehearsal that "No TRUTH line records", while a reader chasing the tickets' citation finds a real, numbered line that actively contradicts them.
EVIDENCE: `grep -l "TRUTH line of 2026-08-29" .scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md` returns exactly 15 files. talk/truth.log's only 2026-08-29 line is `TRUTH 2026-08-29T12:03Z run=7 hub=918022b units=[driftwood=eacae33 ico=8902b66 ludlow=a800a58 nist=33a05df platform=58ef9c5 tuppence=751522b] pass=43 fail=11 skip=0 excluded=2 total=56`. `git ls-tree --name-only 918022b:verify/` returns only `_estate.py party proportionality provenance`; `git ls-tree --name-only origin/main:verify/` returns `_estate.py demo e2e feed-contract party pound-seam proportionality provenance renovate schedules twin-evals`. The run-7 unit SHAs are identical to runs 4, 5 and 6, i.e. the pre-build estate; total=56 versus 83 once the build's scripts were discovered at run 12. Ticket 40 alone carries a dated retraction (added by ticket 60, 2026-09-01). Ticket 67 scopes itself: 'The map's false 65/0/16 citation and the stale fog list were corrected at charting time on 2026-08-31; this ticket owns the rest' and then lists (a)-(d), none of which is the fourteen Answers; its check (d) covers map.md only.
AMBITION: map.md:16 process rule 5 (every ticket's DoD wires its check into the gate); map.md's own never-cite-a-rehearsal rule; NORTH-STAR §5 (one truth number with a date)
OWNER: new P4 — No existing ticket, review finding, or owner decision covers this. Ticket 67 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/67-*.md) explicitly scopes itself to "the map's false 65/0/16 citation and the stale fog list were corrected at charting time on 2026-08-31; this ticket owns the rest: (a)...(d)" — none of (a)-(d) touch the fourteen resolved tickets' Answer text, and its check (d) only wires "any pass/fail figure that map.md quotes" into the gate, not per-ticket Answer citations. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's M-list and its "Refuted claims" list contain no mention of "TRUTH line of 2026-08-29", run=7, or hub 918022b (grep confirms zero hits). REGRILL-ANSWERS.md likewise has no hits. Of the 15 tickets citing "TRUTH line of 2026-08-29" (grep -l confirms 15 files, matching the finding's count), only ticket 40 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/40*.md:26) carries a dated retraction note added 2026-09-01 by ticket 60 — the other fourteen (21,25,26,28,29,32,36,41,42,43,47,49,50,52) remain uncorrected and unowned by any open ticket. This is a genuinely new orphan, not a duplicate of ticket 67 or any REVIEW-2026-08-31 finding.
FIT IMPACT: This is sharper than the map's existing correction, which says only that 'No TRUTH line records' the 65/0/16 figure. A reader chasing the citation finds a real, numbered line that actively contradicts the claim -- proof of the opposite presented as proof. Fifteen of the estate's build tickets are therefore unverifiable from their own stated evidence, and the tracker reads 'resolved' on all of them.
REMEDY: Append the same dated note ticket 40 carries to the other fourteen Answers, and widen ticket 67(d)'s gate check from map.md to `.scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md` so any TRUTH figure quoted anywhere in the record must resolve to a real line whose tree contains the named check.

## [major] P5 The estate's headline differentiator — "price and cage, never refuse" — is false in shipped code, and the glossary, the ADR and the north star give three different answers with no owner ruling
(original title: An architectural reversal the owner never saw was written into an ADR inside an implementation run, and CONTEXT.md still contradicts it)
CLAIM: Commit 318052d (author date 2026-08-28 22:15, inside the 16:22->06:40 implementation run 3e83a16..6058943) added two dated addenda to ADR-0022, one of which promotes `governed-namespace-requires-claim` from `Audit` to `Deny` -- 'the one refusal the doctrine allows' -- with no owner round and no marking of its own. It reverses an explicitly owner-confirmed reversal: REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:80 records reversals 11-12 as 'Confirm: a MutatingPolicy defaults the strictest cage at CREATE; replaces the governed-namespace deny gate.' The shipped code followed one minute earlier (platform f5f3023, 2026-08-28 22:14; versions.yaml:152 and render-governed-namespace-guard.py:75 both `Deny`). Four records still say the opposite and none has been touched since: CONTEXT.md:239-241 ('There is no `CREATE` deny any more'), unchanged since 20568bc at 2026-08-28 12:32, nine hours before the addendum; ADR-0022's own Consequences ('Supersedes ADR-0014's CREATE deny'), in the same file as the addendum that reinstates it; ADR-0014's banner ('the CREATE deny on a governed namespace is superseded by ADR-0022'); and map.md:36 ('replaces Deny-to-issue').
