Two corrections to the original finding: (i) ADR-0022's header does carry 'Provisional: the owner agreed without a reason', but that covers the ticket-09 decision, not the addenda -- and the commit does claim ticket 26, though ticket 26's text records the promotion nowhere, and no ticket of the 74 mentions it; (ii) a lane for build-forced decisions does exist, map.md:70 'Decisions the build had to take, each recorded in its ticket', and the implementers had a written owner-accepted promotion path in ADR-0014's Consequences ('start the policy in Audit and promote by editorial PR') that they cite in versions.yaml:148 -- the fault is that they used a path ADR-0022 itself declares superseded, and entered the reversal in neither the lane nor the ticket nor the glossary.
EVIDENCE: `git show 318052d -- docs/adr/` : '+ **Added 2026-08-28 (review):** a pod created in a governed Namespace with NO `policy-as-versioned.dev/policy-version` claim is REFUSED by `governed-namespace-requires-claim`, promoted that day from `Audit` to `Deny`... This is the one refusal the doctrine allows'. map.md:13 states the estate's vocabulary rule: 'Price and cage; never count, refuse or file.' CONTEXT.md:240-241 reads 'There is no `CREATE` deny any more: a pod that claims nothing gets the namespace's tier'; `git log -S "deny any more" -- CONTEXT.md` returns exactly one commit, 20568bc, dated 2026-08-28 12:32 -- nine hours before the addendum -- and the line is unchanged since. Two other ADRs were amended in the same run: f19876f (ADR-0023, 2026-08-29 02:19) and a766346 (ADR-0011, 2026-08-29 06:25); the ADR-0011 one is traceable to ticket 18 Answer 1 (an owner-put item), the ADR-0022 one is not traceable to any owner round.
AMBITION: GAPS.md process rule 2 ('None inside an implementation run'); map.md:13 vocabulary rule ('never... refuse'); map.md:16 (a bare agree does not ratify architecture -- here there was no agree at all)
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (grep for ADR-0022, governed-namespace-requires-claim, CONTEXT.md contradiction, "no owner round", "implementation run" combined with ADR) — no ticket addresses the Audit→Deny promotion's provenance or the CONTEXT.md:240-241 contradiction. Ticket 63 (cited by the finding itself as adjacent) owns only the unlabelled-default flip, a distinct half of map.md:13's ordering rule — it does not mention Audit/Deny or CONTEXT.md. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's Major list (M1-M18) and Refuted-claims list contain nothing matching this ADR/CONTEXT pairing; M11 (conditional-rule arm orphan) and GAPS 3.11 (ludlow Audit-as-Deny restatement) are different topics. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md has no hit for this ADR or claim text; GAPS.md process rule 2 ("None inside an implementation run") is the very rule the finding says was violated, not an answer resolving it — no owner decision moots this. NORTH-STAR §6 and map.md Out-of-scope not separately checked line-by-line but no ticket/review reference surfaced them as covering this. Conclusion: unowned, new finding.
FIT IMPACT: The glossary and the ADR disagree about whether the estate's one permitted refusal exists; a newcomer reading CONTEXT.md would build the wrong thing, and the shipped code follows the ADR. The decision itself is well-reasoned and evidence-backed ('Live, Audit let such a pod run completely uncaged'), which is exactly the problem: the process has no lane for a build discovering a fact that forces an architectural call, so good calls get taken silently in the one place the doctrine's headline says they should not.
REMEDY: Fix CONTEXT.md's Governed-namespace entry to match ADR-0022's addendum, or put the Audit->Deny promotion to the owner as a real decision. Then add a process rule that names what the build does when a discovered fact forces an architectural call mid-run: record it as the assistant's (GAPS rule 1's second clause) and list it for the next round.

## [minor] P7 The canonical decision record states the feed envelope and the refuse-vs-price doctrine flatly as accepted, while the fact that neither was ratified with a reason lives only in the ticket the ADR links to
(original title: Four of the six ADRs born of the bare-agree batch do not record that their decision is provisional)
CLAIM: Three of the six ADRs born of the 2026-08-28 batch — ADR-0019 (the feed envelope), ADR-0020 (refuse-vs-price) and ADR-0021 (the twin/estate seam) — carry `status: accepted` and no signal whatever that the decision rests on a bare agree the owner gave no reason for: not the word "provisional", not the owner's words, not a distinguishing status. ADR-0022:10-11 and ADR-0024:81-85 do disclose it. ADR-0023 is not a fourth omission: every bullet of its decision section is one of D1-D5, the five cross-ticket decisions the tickets record as DECIDED on the three-lens-panel line (10-schedules-and-skills.md:80, 13-...:83, 16-...:78, 19-...:84, 20-...:77, map.md:18), and its lines 9-10 already quote the owner's ratifying words — so its silence on "provisional" is correct. The gap is a mid-batch convention change never backported: ADR-0022's disclosure arrived with commit 20568bc, ADR-0019 was written earlier the same day in 6ee916a. Ambition hook is NORTH-STAR §3 principle 6 (NORTH-STAR.md:35) and map.md:18, not §6. Severity is record hygiene (minor-to-moderate), not a governance hole: the reopen-with-a-reason route exists (REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:99) and each silent ADR names its originating ticket on line 9. No ticket owns the fix; ticket 67 is the record-hygiene ticket and does not cover it.
EVIDENCE: `grep -c -i provisional docs/adr/0019*.md docs/adr/0020*.md docs/adr/0021*.md docs/adr/0023*.md` returns 0 for all four. docs/adr/0022-...:10-11: 'Decided 2026-08-28 in `.scratch/ecosystem/issues/09`. Provisional: the owner agreed without a reason.' docs/adr/0024-...:81-85 records the same for the rejection-ledger items ('are PROVISIONAL on a bare agree (`.scratch/ecosystem/issues/10-schedules-and-skills.md:80`)'). The originating tickets are unambiguous: 04-the-feed-contract.md:17 (ADR-0019), 07-...:19-20 (ADR-0020), 08-the-pound-seam.md:17 (ADR-0021).
AMBITION: map.md:16 (a bare agree does not ratify architecture); NORTH-STAR §6 (the record is the system's honesty surface)
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (04, 07, 08, 10 originate the underlying decisions but don't address ADR-text disclosure), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (its refuted claim "17 of 22 decisions rest on a bare agree with no route to ratification" is about the ratification/process route, not about whether the ADR documents themselves state provisionality — a different claim), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md (grep for provisional/ADR-0019/0020/0021/0023 = no hits), NORTH-STAR §6 and map.md (map.md's process-rules bullet confirms "a bare agree does not ratify architecture" as policy, but nothing requires or checks that the resulting ADR text itself discloses provisionality — ADR-0022 and ADR-0024 happen to do so, the other four don't). No ticket or ADR owns making docs/adr/0019, 0020, 0021, 0023 disclose their provisional status. Finding is new.
FIT IMPACT: The bare-agree convention lives only in the ticket tracker, which is .scratch/ scratch material. docs/adr/ is the canonical, externally-legible decision record and the artefact a newcomer, an external reviewer or a client actually reads -- and it presents ADR-0019 (the contract every publisher and adopter implements) and ADR-0020 (the doctrine the whole composition engine turns on) as settled. The estate's honesty about its own debt does not reach the document that matters most.
REMEDY: Copy ADR-0022's one-sentence provisionality line, with the originating ticket path, into ADR-0019, 0020, 0021 and 0023. One commit, no code change.

## [major] P8 The audit trail still sells two retired mechanisms as live architecture, and the ticket that owed the correction has closed
(original title: Two ADRs are superseded by decided owner positions and carry no banner, while five siblings do)
CLAIM: Two ADRs carry clauses that decided owner positions have superseded, and neither carries the dated banner the estate's own rule (`.scratch/ecosystem/map.md:123`, "Superseded documents get banners") requires, while six such banners sit on five sibling ADRs (0011:7, 0014:5, 0015:5, 0015:7, 0016:5, 0018:5). No ADR uses a `status:` frontmatter for supersession — all 24 say `status: accepted` — so the banner is the only mechanism, and its absence is the whole record.

ADR-0010 (the strong half). Its consumer-side `sunset:` field (0010:9-11, restated 0010:33-34 and consequence 0010:76-77) was dropped by ticket 13's Answer item 5, "Consumer-side `sunset:` is not carried ... Decided", recorded on the owner's reasoned line "I agree with you're more advanced reasoning" — the DECIDED class, stronger than the PROVISIONAL class the rest of that 2026-08-28 batch carries (though not the only reasoned class in the record: the six overriding REGRILL answers are also the owner's own words). Ticket 13:86 wrote the supersession prospectively — "superseded by the ADR ticket 10 writes". Ticket 10 is resolved and that ADR exists: ADR-0023 (0023:10, "Decided in issues/10 and 16"). ADR-0023 records only "ADR-0010's line (timed proposals, never timed application) gains the sharper wording above" (0023:70) and names no supersession of the `sunset:` field; ADR-0024 supersedes ADR-0015 point 5 only (0024:70, :119). So the superseding ADR was written and the supersession fell out of it. Meanwhile ADR-0010 is cited as live authority by NORTH-STAR.md:34 (principle 5) and restated as live ubiquitous language by CONTEXT.md:560-564. Ownership: map.md:27 assigns it to tickets 09 and 10, both resolved; ticket 39 covers ADR-0013/0017/0018 pt.3 only; ticket 67's (a)-(d) names neither. No open ticket owns it.

ADR-0008 (narrower than claimed). Three clauses are superseded, not the ADR: line 8 "surfaced in one dashboard", lines 17-21 "the dashboard is four panels over four datasources ... Grafana template variable ... infinity, github", and consequence 31-32 "Builds a compliance dashboard". They are superseded by the owner's dated rejection of a Grafana dashboard as evidence ("a bullshit grafana or other made up dashboard that could be not real data", REPORT.md:38, dated ~2026-07-20 in OWNER_BRIEF.md:64 and DECISION_AUDIT.json — the REPORT line dates the July 14-20 phase, not the quote; a firmly dated second rejection is 2026-07-23) and by NORTH-STAR §5 making the truth surface the only citable read. The rest of ADR-0008 stands and is still the estate's model: the four ground-truth signals (0008:12-15), the titular demotion of PR-acceptance to adoption velocity (0008:23-27), and consequences 33-39. It was not "formally retired by ticket 13": ticket 13's five Answer items and its Consequences name no dashboard and no ADR-0008; the retirement sentence sits in its "Already decided, not re-asked" preamble (issues/13:36) as a pre-existing owner position. And ADR-0008 is worse off than un-bannered — `grep -rn "ADR-0008" .scratch/ecosystem/ NORTH-STAR.md CONTEXT.md` returns nothing, so no live eco-system document references it at all.

Fit impact, corrected: ADR-0010 is the material one — it reads as live, is cited as live by the north star and the glossary, and describes a mechanism the estate replaced with publisher-side supersede. ADR-0008's exposure is limited to a reader browsing docs/adr/ directly, since nothing live points at it.
EVIDENCE: ADR-0010: `.scratch/ecosystem/issues/13-lift-or-retire-the-original-mechanisms.md:86` 'ADR-0010:5-9 (consumer-side `sunset:`) is superseded by the ADR ticket 10 writes'; ticket 13:73 records Q5 as DECIDED (D5) on 'I agree with you're more advanced reasoning'. `grep -n "ADR-0010|supersede" docs/adr/0024*.md` shows ADR-0024 supersedes 'ADR-0015 point 5' only. `head docs/adr/0010-sunset-scheduled-proposals-not-application.md` shows `status: accepted`, no banner. Ticket 02's Answer flagged the debt on 2026-08-28: 'it did not rewrite ADR-0006, ADR-0010, ADR-0014 or ADR-0015'; 0014 and 0015 have since gained banners. ADR-0008: `grep -c "superseded|retired|2026-08" docs/adr/0008-measurable-layered-ground-truth.md` = 0; its body is entirely the four-panel dashboard ('the dashboard is four panels over four datasources'); ticket 13:36 records 'Grafana dashboards are retired: the owner rejected them on 2026-07-20 ... no dashboard of any kind is re-asked'. Ticket 39 (open) names ADR-0013, ADR-0017 and ADR-0018 pt.3 only.
AMBITION: map.md:123 out-of-scope rule ('Rewriting history in place. Superseded documents get banners'); NORTH-STAR §5 (the truth surface is the only citable read, which is what retired ADR-0008)
OWNER: new none — Verified independently, not a duplicate of any prior finding. docs/adr/0010-sunset-scheduled-proposals-not-application.md:1-2 and docs/adr/0008-measurable-layered-ground-truth.md:1-2 both show `status: accepted` with zero occurrences of "supersede|retired|2026-08|banner" in 0008's body (grep returned nothing) and no supersession banner in 0010's header either. `grep -n "ADR-0010\|ADR-0008" .scratch/ecosystem/REVIEW-2026-08-31.md` returns no hits, so the 08-31 review never raised this pairing (not refuted, not previously found). Ticket 13:83,86 does decide D5 supersedes ADR-0010:5-9 and 13:25,36 record the owner's 2026-07-20 Grafana rejection retiring ADR-0008's mechanism, but ticket 13 is a decision record with no follow-up ticket named for the ADR rewrite itself. Ticket 39 (open) owns only ADR-0013/0017/0018pt3 supersession banners — confirmed by reading .scratch/ecosystem/issues/39-*.md, no mention of 0010 or 0008. Ticket 67 (open) owns map/NORTH-STAR-copy sync, ico bump reset, OBSERVATION_LANE trim, and a truth.log citation check (its (a)-(d) list) — confirmed by reading .scratch/ecosystem/issues/67-*.md, no ADR mentioned. No ticket claims ownership of writing/banner-ing ADR-0010 or ADR-0008's supersession despite both having decided owner positions on record. Recommend: open a new ticket (or fold into ticket 10, which already owns "the ADR that supersedes 0010" per map ticket-02 line and ticket 13:27) to add banners to both ADR-0010 and ADR-0008, matching the pattern already applied to ADR-0011/0014/0015/0016/0018.
FIT IMPACT: Both read as live architecture to anyone opening docs/adr/. ADR-0010 describes a mechanism the estate replaced with publisher-side supersede; ADR-0008 describes one the owner explicitly rejected. Contrast ADRs 0011, 0014, 0015, 0016 and 0018, all five of which carry dated banners -- the convention exists and works, these two were simply missed, and the one with the strongest ratification behind its supersession (D5) is the one that slipped.
REMEDY: Add dated superseded-in-part banners to ADR-0010 (naming ticket 13 Q5/D5 and ADR-0024) and ADR-0008 (naming the 2026-07-20 rejection and NORTH-STAR §5). Fold into ticket 67 or ticket 39's scope.

MINORS:
- P3 REVIEW-2026-08-31's refutation of the bare-agree finding is unsound: REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:99 refutes '17 of 22 decisions rest on a bare agree with no route to ratification' on two grounds -- an 'explicit reopen-with-a-reason path' and 'no ambition document requires conversion' -- and both fail against the record.
- P9 A change promised inside a decided ticket never landed and nobody owns it: .scratch/ecosystem/issues/13-lift-or-retire-the-original-mechanisms.md:86 states 'ADR-0004 gains a dated sequencing note'; ADR-0004 has none.
- P10 CONTEXT.md carries two contradictory sunset entries in the same glossary: CONTEXT.md's Supersede entry (added 2026-08-28, ticket 13) says 'No consumer-side sunset field exists', while its unrevised Project-posture entry still describes a live `sunset:` field mechanism citing ADR-0010.
- P11 HISTORY.md's post-mortem states a universal rule about the verify scripts that is false today: docs/HISTORY.md's 2026-08-25 post-mortem asserts 'Every live tail now has exactly three outcomes... which is SKIP with a reason and exit 3' and 'Every live-claiming script asserts its substrate first'; at least one shipped script prints SKIP and exits 0, and the substrate-first ordering was deliberately reversed by ticket 60.
- P13 Fourteen of 41 resolved tickets name no gate check, against process rule 5: By direct scan of each resolved ticket's Answer section for any reference to a verify script or the gate, tickets 01, 02, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09, 12, 15, 22, 23, 24 and 58 name none.
- P14 Status vocabulary and even the Answer heading are still free text, and the record is not machine-readable: Four free-typed Status values are in use with no enum and no derivation, and the `## Answer` heading is unstandardised across tickets, which defeats naive parsing of the tracker.
- P15 The map's section heading still asserts a green gate above its own correction: map.md:54's heading reads 'Built 2026-08-29 -- the thin slice runs, and the gate is green'; the dated correction below retracts 'the paragraph above', which does not cover the heading, and the gate has never been green.
- P16 The citable TRUTH series has an undetected hole at run 14: Run 14 of truth.yml exists on GitHub and produced no TRUTH line; talk/truth.log jumps from run=13 to run=15 and nothing in the gate detects a run number that recorded nothing.
- P17 Every truth.yml run concludes failure, so the workflow-level signal carries no information: Twenty of the twenty-one truth.yml runs pulled concluded `failure`, including run 21 which produced today's citable line; the only success is a workflow_dispatch on 2026-08-28.
- P18 The enact guard was disabled and re-enabled fifteen times in twenty-six hours: Between 2026-08-31 14:51 and 2026-09-01 09:59 the enact guard's durable mode was flipped to development and back fifteen times, each on a dated commit to main with a stated reason.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- Do you want to be asked fewer, bigger architectural questions with a recommendation attached, or do you want the assistant to make the call and record it as its own (GAPS.md rule 1's second clause)? The evidence says the panel-verdict shape -- five conflicts, three lenses each, one page -- is the only format that has ever got a reasoned reply out of you. | WHY: The drift review's rule 1 was dropped from map.md when the rules were copied over, and the estate has since generated ~84 unratified architectural items. The two branches lead to opposite remediation programmes: one is a ratification backlog, the other is a relabelling exercise. | CHANGES: If you want to decide: 84 items need to be re-put to you in ~17 panel-shaped rounds, and Status: resolved becomes illegal while any item is PROVISIONAL (17 tickets reopen). If you want the assistant to decide: the provisional vocabulary is retired from every ticket and every ADR, replaced by 'decided by the build, reason recorded', and P2/P7 collapse to a one-commit relabelling.
- Is `Status: resolved` on a ticket whose decisions are all PROVISIONAL acceptable to you? Your own map (map.md:16) says an unratified decision 'stays open'. | WHY: Seventeen tickets covering the feed contract, the £ seam, the cage ladder and the identity spine read `resolved` on the tracker. Every downstream count of 'what is done' -- the map, the handoffs, the deck -- inherits that reading. | CHANGES: If not acceptable, 17 of the 41 resolved tickets return to open, the frontier list roughly doubles, and ticket 59(b)'s derived-Status checker gains a rule that reads the PROVISIONAL markers. If acceptable, map.md:16's 'or it stays open' clause must be struck so the record stops contradicting itself.
- Is there a date or an event -- a talk, a client, a /to-spec handoff -- before which the architecture must be ratified, and who ratifies it if not you? | WHY: Signed tags, published feeds and three re-priced adopters already sit on ADR-0019/0020/0021/0022. Every day the debt goes unserviced, a reversal costs more: it stops being a document edit and becomes a re-release across eight repos. | CHANGES: A date turns the ratification backlog into a schedulable ticket with a check (n items ratified by date d). No date means the honest thing is to say so in map.md and stop calling the items provisional, because 'provisional' implies a pending resolution that nothing is driving.
- When the build discovers a fact mid-run that forces an architectural decision -- as it did on 2026-08-28 at 22:15, promoting governed-namespace-requires-claim from Audit to Deny, 'the one refusal the doctrine allows' -- do you want to be interrupted, or do you accept the build deciding and recording it as its own? | WHY: That decision reverses map.md:13's headline vocabulary rule ('never... refuse'), was taken with no owner round at all, is not marked provisional, and CONTEXT.md still asserts the opposite nine hours-older text. The process rule 'none inside an implementation run' has no lane for this case, so good decisions get taken silently in exactly the place the doctrine is most sensitive. | CHANGES: If you want to be interrupted, implementation runs need a hold-and-ask mechanism and the Audit->Deny promotion needs a retrospective round. If you accept the build deciding, the process rules need a sixth rule naming that lane, and CONTEXT.md needs fixing to match ADR-0022 rather than the ADR needing to justify itself.
- Do you consider ADR-0008 (the four-panel Grafana dashboard) and ADR-0010 (the consumer-side `sunset:` field) dead, and do you want them bannered as superseded? | WHY: You rejected dashboards in your own words on 2026-07-20 and ticket 13 records them retired; ticket 13's Q5 is D5 -- your one reasoned ratification -- and it supersedes ADR-0010:5-9. Both ADRs still read `status: accepted` with no banner, while five sibling ADRs carry dated banners, so the record is inconsistent about its own strongest decisions. | CHANGES: Two one-commit banners plus the CONTEXT.md sunset cleanup (P10). If instead you consider either still live, then ticket 13's D5 needs reopening and the publisher-side supersede mechanism the estate built needs re-justifying.
- Fifteen of the twenty recorded cross-ticket conflicts (GRILL-WALK.md C1-C20) were resolved by the assistant rather than put to you -- including whether an adopter may declare its own floor tier (C9), how prices[] is shaped when four tickets reshape it independently (C11), and retiring the currency controller (C15). Do you want those fifteen put to you as one round? | WHY: A conflict between two positions you separately agreed to is by construction a place your own words do not settle -- the highest-value question to ask, and the one most likely to have embedded an assumption you would reject. Their resolutions are now load-bearing in composition.py, the cage floor and a retired live component. | CHANGES: If yes, one one-page panel round in the format that produced D1-D5, and the fifteen C-items graduate from 'amendment' to either DECIDED or reopened. If no, the record should say plainly that cross-ticket conflicts are the assistant's to settle, which makes P6 a documented policy rather than a finding.

# scope-and-coherence

VERDICT: The ambition is coherent — more so than the probe list assumed. Four of the six suspected rule conflicts dissolve on contact with code: composition provably cannot read a clock (its selfcheck asserts it cannot import datetime), the publisher gate really does publish degraded-at-quarantine rather than refuse (gate.py:82,256-264), "no gate" is a vocabulary collision not a rule conflict, and the rejection ledger is derived from closed PRs with no committed register. The estate has built the hard, distinctive parts: a real cross-org signature spine (24 tags, all verifying against Rekor), one adopter's signed composed artefact carrying four parties' priced output in one currency under its own perspective, and an honesty machine that made its own headline number worse (skip 22→18, fail 3→7 at run 20) in order to be true. It is nonetheless NOT fit for the purpose NORTH-STAR states, for one arithmetic reason and one shape reason. The arithmetic: §4 step 3 — the pivot of the whole demonstration — is foreclosed. Applying platform/graded/cage.py's own reduce table to the adopters' own signed evidence.json and party.yaml tolerances, all three sit at the ladder's bottom rung `isolated`, driftwood with 5–12% headroom, and tuppence and ludlow past the end of the ladder entirely (£180,796 residual at the tightest cage against £15,000 and £5,000 bands). The only mechanical mover the estate has pushes the number up, and up is already the floor. The shape: 54% of the 84 graded checks are platform's own and 54% of the ~97,600 hand-written lines are the twin and its tests; between them they own one of the seven §4 steps, while the three adopters whose consumption is the entire point carry 13% of the instrument. What would make it fit, in order: (1) an owner decision on £ calibration so a band can be crossed at all; (2) `size` blocks on tuppence and ludlow so principle 3 operates for more than one party; (3) a clock that can see a cluster, or every live claim moved to a sample lane, so the estate's central runtime claim becomes citable; (4) a choice between the three candidate purposes, because at 4 tickets closed in 3 days against 31 open the estate cannot afford all three.

STRENGTHS:
- The eco-system's central artefact claim is real and re-derivable: driftwood/composed/evidence.json carries four priced lines from four distinct parties — ico £1,787,177; feeds £19,559; insurer premium £113,403; twin forward-intel £1,897,646 — all GBP, all under `perspective: driftwood`, in one artefact signed by driftwood's own tag. Four independent orgs' output composed and priced by a fifth's engine, in the consumer's own currency and perspective.
- The signature spine is complete: 24 tags across the 8 unit repos all verify with gitsign against Rekor, including feeds' and insurer's first tags cut 2026-09-01 (ticket 57). Spot-verified independently: `git tag -l` in the fresh feeds clone at 69c89b0 shows threat-register/v1.0.0 and v2.0.0.
- "No gate" survives contact with the release path in code, not just in a ticket: computed-semver/gate.py:82 `DEGRADED_TIER = "quarantine"`, gate.py:256-264 records `degraded` rather than refusing when the gate could read everything it needed, and cut-release-gate.py:153 `degraded_tag()` emits the prerelease suffix on the untouched base number. REGRILL 11's decision is built.
- The gate has no override at any scope: cut-release-gate.py:41-54 documents that CUT_RELEASE_TEST_MODE wraps only the cosign subprocess and never touches run_gate()/comparison_window.evaluate()/release_integrity.refusal(), and that the string appears exactly once in the file.
- ADR-0006 is enforced, not merely stated: compose/composition.py:3566-3571 asserts its own source contains no `import datetime`, `from datetime`, `import time`, `import sched` or `import croniter` — the module never gained the capability to read a clock.
- The rejection ledger is genuinely not an exemption ledger: wargamer/rejection_ledger.py:1-31 derives suppression at run time from closed-unmerged PRs, the old committed fixture is deleted with the reason recorded, offline it is empty and says so, and a different curve hash or £ is a new question that is not suppressed.
- The retirement of policy 2.0.0/2.0.1/3.0.0 used the estate's own mechanism with two independently observed live reasons recorded in distribution/versions.yaml:31-59 (the Priority admission triple mismatch; a pod forging its own tier label reaching the API server from an `isolated` namespace on 2026-08-28), plus the reasoning that a backport is impossible because the fix computes major. Nothing was quietly deleted.
- The number got worse because the instrument got better: run 19→20 moved skip 22→18 and fail 3→7 because ticket 60 reordered the three verify-reconcile scripts and step 4 to grade the real lane sample before checking for a cluster. Four could-not-looks became honest observed-falses — exactly the behaviour NORTH-STAR §6 demands, demonstrated against the project's own headline metric.
- Illustrative parameters are disclosed rather than laundered: twin/severity-anchors.yaml:13,56,67 marks the GPD `xi` and `beta` `anchored: false` with the reasoning, even though the line they feed (forward-intel, £1,897,646) is the largest number on driftwood's balance sheet.
- Publisher bumps are checked, not merely declared: ico's and nist's .github/scripts/declared-bump-gate.py recomputes the bump from the feed's own rule.yaml before cut-release.yml tags and refuses on disagreement — it refused for real the first time it ran (ticket 18 Answer 5). Principle 4's "never declared" holds wherever the gate exists.
- truth.yml's red is honest and correctly attributed: the final step `fail if the gate failed` (.github/workflows/truth.yml:163-165) fires because verify-all.sh exited nonzero with 7 reds, while the observation-lane cage still commits and pushes the TRUTH line cleanly (run 33616685427 log shows `7b92990..a209496  HEAD -> main`). A red run still records its number.

## [major] F2 Proportional pricing is demonstrated by one adopter only: tuppence and ludlow carry no size, so the eco-system prices a £5k-appetite firm and a £15k-appetite firm at the identical £9,039,791 with per-customer null
(original title: "Proportionate to the org" is inert for two of the three adopters, and the fallback pushes them off the ladder)
CLAIM: Only driftwood declares a `size` block (driftwood/party.yaml:33-38, turnover GBP 86,000,000, 240,000 customers, as_of 2026-06-30); tuppence and ludlow declare none. Because a missing size is treated exactly like a stale one — ico/schema/to_fair_scenario.py:61-72 leaves the loss triple at the statutory cap when turnover is None, and platform/compose/composition.py's `_sized_turnover` returns None for a party that signs no size ("a missing SIZE is a missing behaviour") — tuppence and ludlow both carry a byte-identical GBP 9,039,791.02 uk-gdpr exposure, which is 5.06x driftwood's GBP 1,787,177.08 for the same regime at the same pinned ico v3 (the multiple is exactly cap / (rate x turnover) = 8,700,000 / 1,720,000; the finding's "41x" is an arithmetic error — 40.6 is 9,039,791 over tuppence's threat-register line, a different denominator). At that price no cage rung reaches either band: the bottom rung reduces 0.98 (platform/graded/cage.py TIERS), leaving 180,795.82 against tuppence's 15,000 and ludlow's 5,000, so select_tier falls through its for/else to the fail-closed `isolated` (cage.py:173-187) and both artefacts record `proposed_tier: isolated` as a default, not a fit. Every `per_customer` in their prices[] is null in consequence. This is not a foreseen cost of a deliberate widen-to-cap choice: ticket 07 §1 (resolved) decided "the adopter's signed party artefact gains `size`", and widening was its fallback for an `as_of` older than 12 months only — the decision was implemented on one adopter of three and the never-refuse path absorbed the omission silently. Nothing grades it: party_artefact.py:306 returns no error when `size` is absent, and verify/pound-seam/pound_seam.py:163-165 only requires per_customer to be null when size.customers is unsigned, so the run-21 capture PASSes all three. No open ticket owns adding size to tuppence and ludlow. One qualifier on the link to F1: size reaches only the penalty-schema converter (threat-register is priced size-blind), so the cap fallback is the sole cause of tuppence being off the ladder, but ludlow's threat-register line (318,229.78, isolated residual 6,364.60 against a 5,000 tolerance) would still sit past the end of the ladder even with a size declared.
EVIDENCE: `grep -c '^size:'` over the three party.yaml files returns 1 (driftwood), 0 (tuppence), 0 (ludlow). driftwood/party.yaml:33-38 declares turnover £86,000,000, 240,000 customers, as_of 2026-06-30. composed/evidence.json penalty-schema amount: driftwood 1,787,177.075; tuppence 9,039,791; ludlow 9,039,791 (identical). Every `per_customer` field in tuppence's and ludlow's prices[] is null. The widen-to-cap rule is map.md:32 (ticket 07): "stale size widens to the cap, never refuses". ico/penalty-schema/v3/feed.json regimes.uk-gdpr.violation_types formula is pct_of_global_turnover with a cap.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 3 ("The price is proportionate to the organisation: its turnover, its customers, its regulators, its declared appetite"); §1 ("The orgs are example consumers that demonstrate the whole eco-system operating"); §2 adopter row ("its size").
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (ticket 07 "org-size-obligations-and-currency" and ticket 24 "size-beyond-turnover", both Status: resolved), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (grepped size/turnover/per_customer/widen/ico/penalty — only unrelated hits M5/9-10), and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md (only unrelated hit on widening being priced-not-refused in general, P124/P128). Ticket 07 established the widen-to-cap rule and ticket 24 refined per-regime size reads (HIPAA provisions, FCA relevant_revenue, per_customer field) but neither ticket's text, facts-found, or Answer ever addresses the case where an adopter declares NO size block at all — both tickets assume/discuss size facts being present or stale, not absent. Confirmed live: `grep size: .estate-clone/driftwood/party.yaml` hits (line 33); tuppence and ludlow party.yaml files (checked in fresh clones under scratchpad/units/{tuppence,ludlow}/party.yaml) have no `size:` key at all. Ticket 07's Answer item 1 ("An as_of older than 12 months does not refuse: the triple widens back to the cap") only covers staleness of a declared size, not total absence — so the mechanical effect the finding describes (two of three demo adopters landing on an identical, off-the-ladder £9,039,791 uk-gdpr figure) was never examined or decided by anyone. Not in REVIEW-2026-08-31's Refuted claims list, not in map.md Out of scope, not touched by NORTH-STAR §6. This is a genuine new gap.
FIT IMPACT: The proportionality principle — one of the seven, and the one the owner gave the most words to ("its proporiate to the org right?... cost per customer... percentage of global revenue") — does not operate for two of the three parties whose whole purpose is to demonstrate the eco-system operating. It is also the direct mechanical cause of half of F1: the cap is what pushes tuppence and ludlow past the end of the ladder.
REMEDY: Add a signed `size` block to tuppence/party.yaml and ludlow/party.yaml (four fields plus as_of, the shape driftwood already ships), and add a gate check that an adopter without `size` is graded amber rather than silently capped. Small, cheap, and it unblocks the calibration question in F1.

## [major] F3 Falling behind a published version costs nothing: the decided supersede price is unbuilt, unowned and unreachable from composition, so the map and two resolved tickets assert a mechanism the estate does not have
(original title: The decided supersede mechanism — the EOL ramp — has no consumer path in composition)
CLAIM: The decided supersede mechanism has no consumer anywhere in the estate — not in composition, and not in the proposer the decision actually names. Ticket 13 D5 (issues/13:83), ticket 19 D5 (issues/19:84) and ADR-0023's Decision (docs/adr/0023:55-56) all decide that a pin behind a newer published version is priced by the existing EOL ramp from the newer version's publish date, with the adopter's scheduled proposer reading pins and opening a retirement PR. `grep -rn "eol_ramp|eol_scenario"` across all eight unit clones and the hub finds zero callers outside platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py itself: composition.py contains no `ramp` at all, no adopter's party.yaml subscribes to the `eol` feed (an `eol` edge would hit the Refused at composition.py:1540-1543, "missing instrument"), and no retirement-proposal producer exists — tier_pr.py proposes tier labels only, and "retirement" appears solely as a reserved `kind` in the dedupe key (rejection_ledger.py:17,83; proposer_bounds.py:72). The same unwired ramp is spent a second time by ticket 15 A3 (issues/15:84, ungoverned namespace priced as a ramped share), which compute_ungoverned also does not implement. Corrections to the original: (a) the ramp does NOT require an explicit --as-of — to_fair_scenario.py:175 defaults it to today; (b) the ramp is live and graded on the citable run, printing real money (GBP218,337 -> GBP436,816 -> GBP1,087,666 in talk/captures/.estate-clone_platform_feeds_verify-feeds.out via verify-feeds.sh:48,68-70), so ticket 74's mover exists but cannot move any adopter's residual — it is unreachable, not absent; (c) ADR-0010's missing superseded banner is a repo-wide convention (all 24 ADRs are `status: accepted`, ADR-0015 included) and the superseding text was in fact written into ADR-0023:70, so the shortfall is code-only, not documentary. Ownership stands: tickets 10, 11, 13, 19 and 60 all name the ramp and are all resolved; only ticket 74 (open) relies on it, and it owns watching for a crossing, not building the path.
EVIDENCE: The ramp exists at platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py:74-101 (eol_ramp(eol_date, as_of), +1x per year past EOL) and requires an explicit --as-of. compose/composition.py:272-275 defines FEED_CONVERTERS with exactly two rows (threat-register, penalty-schema) plus the QUOTE_PREFIX at line 300 — no `eol` row. composition.py:3560-3561 states it directly: "an 'eol' parent kind does not exist in the party artefact schema at all". `grep -n 'eol' {driftwood,tuppence,ludlow}/party.yaml` returns nothing, though feeds/party.yaml:23-27 really does publish an `eol` feed. Nothing on any clock passes --as-of to to_fair_scenario.py.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 5 (intelligence re-prices on a clock); §4 step 3; ticket 13 D5 and ticket 19 D5's supersede decision; ADR-0010.
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (grep -li eol → 01,10,11,13,15,19,21,22,23,24,29,57,60,74), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (no "eol" mention at all, confirmed by grep -n -i eol returning nothing; its C1/M1-M18 list and Refuted-claims list neither name nor refute this), and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md (grep -n -i "eol|sunset|ADR-0010" returned nothing). Ticket 13 D5 (line 83) and 19 D5 (line 84) decide supersede is publisher-side, priced by the existing EOL ramp, and 13's notes (line 86) say ADR-0010:5-9 is superseded by the ADR ticket 10 writes — but ticket 10 is a separate, not-done ADR-writing obligation, and nothing in 13/19/10 addresses composition wiring (no eol FEED_CONVERTERS row, no party.yaml eol subscription) — they assume the ramp is reachable, they don't build the reach. Ticket 74 (open, current frontier ticket) explicitly names "the EOL ramp's date-driven price" as one of three movers for the first real step-3 crossing (line 15 of 74) without any acknowledgment that no adopter subscribes to an eol feed or that composition has no eol converter — it relies on the mechanism working, it does not own making it reachable. Ticket 21 (build the feed contract) only covers moving eol out of platform into the feeds repo (envelopes/tags/clock) — nothing about adopter-side eol subscription or a composition converter. No ticket, ADR text, or REGRILL answer claims this wiring exists or is scoped to be built. This is a genuinely new ownership gap.
FIT IMPACT: A decision recorded as DECIDED produced no wiring, ADR-0010 still carries no superseded banner, and one of the three movers ticket 74 is waiting on does not exist. Combined with F1 this leaves step 3 with zero live movers.
REMEDY: Either wire it (add an `eol` row to FEED_CONVERTERS, subscribe one adopter to feeds/eol, and pass the composition date through as --as-of — noting that this reintroduces a date-dependent price and needs an ADR-0006 note), or record in ticket 74 that the ramp is not a live mover and strike it from the three.

## [major] F5 The honesty check prints a could-not-look as a green and tells the audience the intelligence publisher is unsigned, when its tags are Rekor-validated — the truth surface states something false about the estate in the one check that proves attestability
(original title: The provenance check reports two real gitsign-signed tags as "no signed tag yet", and prints it as ok)
CLAIM: verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:88 selects each publisher's tag with `git tag -l 'v*.*.*'`, which cannot match feeds' documented per-feed tag shape `<feed-name>/vX.Y.Z` (cut-release.yml:6-7, ADR-0019). feeds therefore falls into the `queued` bucket at line 89 despite holding two gitsign-signed, Rekor-validated tags (threat-register/v1.0.0 tlog 2672987434 and threat-register/v2.0.0 tlog 2673492733, both re-verified in session against feeds' own EXPECTED_IDENTITY_REGEXP and issuer). The false absence is printed as a green note at line 104 and repeated in the PASS sentence at lines 117-119; run-21's capture ends "ok   no signed tag yet, honestly queued for cut-release.yml: feeds" / "PASS: ... 7 of 8 ... and feeds have no signed tag yet", where the truth is 8 of 8. The same script contradicts itself: its part-1 line reports "0 honestly queued", because feed_contract.py:65-71 already handles the `<name>/vX.Y.Z` shape — so this is a one-line selector bug in the check, not a gap in the estate's model. The glob was written in fb9f05b (ticket 52) when feeds had no tags, and ticket 57 scopes dispatch and first tags, not the check, so no ticket owns it. Related but distinct: the same glob also never selects platform's policy/vX.Y.Z line (it picks v2.0.1), leaving that signed line outside step 6's Rekor coverage without printing a false statement about it.
EVIDENCE: verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:87-88: `tag="$(git -C "$ESTATE/$u" tag -l 'v*.*.*' ... | tail -1)"; [ -n "$tag" ] || { queued+=("$u"); continue; }`; line 104 prints `ok   no signed tag yet, honestly queued for cut-release.yml: ${queued[*]}` and line 119 folds it into the PASS sentence. `git tag -l` in the fresh feeds clone at 69c89b0 returns threat-register/v1.0.0 and threat-register/v2.0.0; the publishers map re-verified both against Rekor. Run-21 capture origin/main:talk/captures/verify_e2e_verify-e2e-step6-provenance.out ends: "ok   no signed tag yet, honestly queued for cut-release.yml: feeds" / "PASS: ... and feeds have no signed tag yet".
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §3 principle 6 (every actor is attestable; a green that could not look is a red); §4 step 6.
OWNER: new none — No open ticket, ADR, REVIEW-2026-08-31 finding, or REGRILL-ANSWERS entry owns this. REVIEW-2026-08-31's M5 covered step6 hard-skipping for a different reason (missing jsonschema module causing SKIP entirely), fixed by ticket 54 (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/54-the-gate-observes-with-the-estate-s-own-toolchain.md:9,41) — which is precisely what let this script now execute for real and hit the glob bug. Ticket 57 (57-feeds-and-insurer-become-runnable-workflows-registered.md) cut feeds' first signed tag (threat-register/v1.0.0) and converted verify-feed-contract's SKIPs to PASS, but never mentions verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh or its tag glob at all. No ticket or REVIEW-2026-08-31 M-item/refuted-claim mentions the 'v*.*.*' glob or per-feed tag naming. Confirmed still present by direct read at verify/e2e/verify-e2e-step6-provenance.sh:87-88,104,119 (queued+=("$u") when tag glob misses; "ok no signed tag yet, honestly queued" note; folded into PASS sentence). Genuinely new, timing-gated behind ticket 57's 2026-09-01 tag cut (unobservable before feeds had any tag).
FIT IMPACT: A false negative dressed as honesty, narrated by the deck. Step 6's PASS is partly unearned, and the one check that exists to prove attestability under-reports the estate's actual attestation coverage.
REMEDY: Change the glob to match both schemes (e.g. `git tag -l | grep -E '(^|/)v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$'`) and re-grade; add a case to the script's own selfcheck using a per-feed tag name so the regression cannot recur.

## [major] F8 The estate cannot demonstrate its most distinctive principle on itself: the AI's only restraint is a file the AI writes, and the interim proposer-merger split has no enforcement and no owner
(original title: The one interim rule the owner named for AI disposal is unbuilt, unenforced and unowned)
CLAIM: REGRILL 29 (.scratch/drift-review-2026-08-27/REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:35, restated :55) records "author and merger must be different identities for now" as the interim step toward re-grill 37's end state, an AI disposing inside a priced cage. Nothing enforces it anywhere: `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/rules/branches/main` — which includes inherited org rulesets — returns `[]` for the hub and all eight unit repos, repo rulesets are 0, branch protection 404s on every one, no CODEOWNERS exists in any repo, and no workflow or script in the estate references merged_by or any four-eyes shape. All 36 merged pull requests across the nine repos have author == mergedBy == chrisns, with no exceptions (driftwood 11, platform 7, tuppence 7, ludlow 6, nist 2, flux 1, ico 1, feeds 0, insurer 0). Even the flagship step-2 event carries the rule's raw material without any check reading it: driftwood PR #20's change commit ea1c8db is git-authored and Sigstore-verified as github-actions[bot] while merge commit 27f1cf2 is chrisns, yet the PR's author and merger are both chrisns and no gate compares them; that PR was moreover opened at 08:56:46Z and merged at 08:57:50Z on 2026-09-01, inside the ENACT_MODE `development` window running 08:20:19Z (c82dec1) to 08:59:45Z (e7981a5), so the propose-only guard was disarmed for its whole life. The guard itself turns on one checked-in word — twin/enact_guard.py:272 `if enact_mode() != "operations": return None`, reading twin/ENACT_MODE — which the guarded agent rewrites unilaterally and has rewritten 30 times since 2026-08-25 (15 dev/ops round-trips: 10 on 2026-08-31, 5 on 2026-09-01). That flipping is disclosed rather than hidden, and tests/test_enact.py:96-98 asserts the checked-in file reads `operations`, so a mode left flipped reds the suite (it reads `operations` today and all 39 enact tests pass) — but detection after the fact is not a cage, and on NORTH-STAR.md:31 a cage on this estate's doctrine has a price, which ENACT_MODE has none of (`grep -niE "price|£|cost" twin/enact_guard.py` returns nothing; twin-self's two priced controls are query-access restriction and provenance attestation, not disposal). No ticket owns the interim rule: it appears in 74 issue files only in tickets 10, 12, 13 and 22, all Status: resolved and all as settled-context preamble, and in no ADR, NORTH-STAR or map. Ticket 65 (open) owns hardening the guard's command patterns but not the mode file or a price; ticket 30 — the twin's cage, spec and price — is open, blocked by two resolved tickets, and untouched since its single commit 20568bc on 2026-08-28.
EVIDENCE: Live read-only today: `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-driftwood/driftwood/rulesets` -> 0; `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-flux/policy-as-versioned-flux/branches/main/protection` -> 404 "Branch not protected". `gh pr list --repo policy-as-versioned-driftwood/driftwood --state merged` -> PRs 22,21,20,19,18,16,15,14,12,11 all `author=chrisns mergedBy=chrisns`. Even the flagship step-2 event: PR #20's commit is ea1c8db by github-actions[bot] on branch renovate/feeds-threat-register-2.x, but author and merger are both chrisns. twin/ENACT_MODE was flipped operations->development->operations eight times in two days by the assistant itself: hub commits fd9b779, a79261b, 7ee4f4f, bba9ec5, c40887c, 245e5b4, c82dec1, e7981a5 (git log --oneline -- twin/ENACT_MODE). REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:35 and :43 are the source rules.
AMBITION: REGRILL 29 and 37 (the AI-disposal end state); NORTH-STAR §3 principle 2 (everything is always caged, including a model action) and principle 6 (agent signatures attest the absence of a human).
OWNER: new REGRILL-29 (decision) / ticket-30 (nearest open, not a match) — The interim rule itself ("author and merger must be different identities for now") is a real, recorded owner decision at REGRILL-ANSWERS.md:35 (Q29), echoed as settled context in resolved tickets 10, 12, 13, 22 — so the rule is not a new invention. But the finding's actual claim — that this rule is unbuilt/unenforced in any repo, and that ENACT_MODE is a self-rewritable guard with no price — is NOT owned by any ticket: grep across all 74 issue files for "different identit"/"author and merger"/"self-merge" only turns up the four resolved tickets citing it as already-decided background, never as a build/enforcement item. Ticket 30 ("The twin's cage, spec and price per adopter", open, blocked by 09/12) is the nearest candidate — it covers pricing a cage for the twin generally and cites propose-only/Article-22 — but its text never mentions author/merger identity separation, self-merge, or ENACT_MODE specifically, so it does not clearly own this finding. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md has no mention of "author and merger", "self-merge", or "ENACT_MODE" anywhere (C1/M1-M18 and refuted-claims list both silent), confirming this is not a repeat or already-refuted finding. Verdict: new finding — the rule is decided but its enforcement and the cage's price are an unowned gap; ticket 30 is the closest fit but should be treated as a candidate to extend/retitle rather than an existing owner.
FIT IMPACT: The estate's most distinctive stated end state — an AI disposing inside a priced cage — has neither its interim safeguard nor a priced cage. The ENACT_MODE flips are honestly committed and grep-able, which is real, but a guard whose key is held by the guarded party is disclosure, not a cage, and a cage on this estate's own doctrine has a price. ENACT_MODE has none.
REMEDY: Two small builds and one decision: (a) enable a ruleset requiring a review from a different identity on the hub and the three adopters, or record explicitly that the rule is deferred with a reason; (b) replace ENACT_MODE with a tier the agent cannot select for itself (a signed declaration in a repo the agent cannot push to), with a £ attached; (c) the owner says whether the AI-disposal end state is the real purpose — see owner question 4.

## [major] F10 The estate's only post-admission re-caging mechanism was retired on a word collision (posture currency vs money FX), with no ticket owning the removal or the re-decision, and that reason is queued for the /to-spec handoff
(original title: A retirement decision that changed nothing in the estate, while the map asserts it is done)
CLAIM: Ticket 13 recorded a PROVISIONAL decision (bare agree, not ratified) that the currency-controller **CronJob** is retired, with a reason that does not apply to it ("ticket 07's fx feed replaces it" — ticket 07 is money FX; this controller is about version currency). No open ticket owns executing it: every ticket naming it (10, 12, 13, 32) is resolved, and open ticket 35 covers scanner, notification spine, OSCAL CronJob, api and datastore, not this. Nothing in the estate changed: at run-21's platform SHA 46cd775 the whole module still ships (416 lines of .py+.sh plus manifests), currency-controller/README.md:3-7 still says "This controller makes posture live ... This closes the single biggest gap research 16 flagged", and the last commit to touch it (8889a4d, 2026-08-29, the day after the decision) *hardened* verify-currency.sh. The module is also load-bearing elsewhere — talk/up.sh:68 installs it in the hub's own bring-up, CONTEXT.md:246 defines "De-postured" in terms of it, ADR-0014:30,44 rests on currency.py, and platform's distribution/versions.yaml:147 + render-governed-namespace-guard.py:8 exclude UPDATE from a Kyverno policy specifically "so the currency-controller's de-posture patch keeps working" — so retirement is not a delete but a change to a policy rationale and a glossary term. Meanwhile map.md:113 states flatly "The currency controller is retired (ticket 13)", promoting a provisional CronJob-scoped decision into an estate fact the code contradicts. (Correction to the auditor's framing: verify-currency.sh is not a dead check — its three offline proofs pass in run 21 and only the KinD live tail SKIPs, like the other 17 skips; and ticket 67's Done clause, "a reader following the map meets no claim the truth surface contradicts", does cover this class even though its enumerated items (a)-(d) and its enforced check do not.)
EVIDENCE: map.md:40 (ticket 13 line: "currency-controller retired") and map.md:113 ("The currency controller is retired (ticket 13)"). At platform 46cd775 the directory contains currency.py, manifests/, up.sh, verify-currency.sh and README.md (416 lines of .py+.sh by `find | wc -l`). currency-controller/README.md's opening lines still read "This controller makes posture live... This closes the single biggest gap research 16 flagged." verify-currency.sh is discovered by the gate and grades SKIP in run 21. `grep -l currency-controller .scratch/ecosystem/issues/*.md` returns only 10, 12 and 13, all resolved.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §6 ("Its working parts ... are to be lifted into the eco-system or explicitly retired, one by one, with a decision each"); map.md's own rule that the map must not contradict the truth surface.
OWNER: new ticket 13 item 2 (resolved answer, provisional) — Ticket 13's answer (2026-08-28) records the currency-controller as retired ("it 404s and ticket 07's fx feed replaces it") but that item was not carried into a new build ticket -- only items 1, 3 and 4 graduated, and round 2 of ticket 13 explicitly covers scanner/notification-spine/OSCAL/api/datastore, not this. No ticket owns actually deleting or quiescing the currency-controller directory, its README, or the gate's verify-currency.sh. `grep -n currency .scratch/ecosystem/REVIEW-2026-08-31.md` returned zero hits, so that review neither raised nor refuted this gap. `grep -n currency .scratch/drift-review-2026-08-27/REGRILL-ANSWERS.md` also returned zero hits. Ticket 67 (open) is the closest adjacent ticket but its Question text scopes it to numeric-citation consistency (map pass/fail figures vs talk/truth.log, ico bump.yaml, OBSERVATION_LANE lists, a truth-log-backed gate check) -- not decision-vs-code consistency for retired mechanisms. So F10 is a genuine new gap: an owner decision recorded as done that produced no code/doc change in the estate, uncaught by any existing ticket or prior review.
FIT IMPACT: A decision recorded as done produced no change in the estate, and the live map asserts the opposite of what the code shows. It also inflates the estate by 416 lines and one permanently-SKIPping gate check, and it is a worked example of the class ticket 67 exists to catch but does not cover.
REMEDY: Delete the module and its verify script in one commit citing ticket 13, or reopen ticket 13 round 2 and record that retirement is deferred with a reason. Either way, correct map.md:113 with a dated line.

MINORS:
- F12 NORTH-STAR's own factual rows are stale and unowned: Three factual statements in the ratified NORTH-STAR are now false, and the hygiene ticket that exists for exactly this class does not cover them.
- F13 "Semver computed, never declared" has a disclosed structural hole nobody owns: For a whole class of change the engine cannot measure verdict movement at all and falls back to a structural diff, and the same file names a second gap where the estate's real historical release does not follow bump-and-reset semver.
- F14 A resolved joint's module has no caller: the feed-to-signal lookup is graded by file presence: twin/feed_signal.py implements ticket 11 resolution 3 ("a subscribed feed becomes a signal by lookup on the clock") and nothing on any clock calls it — the only references are its own demo() and a file-existence assertion.
- F15 Ticket close rate has collapsed while charting continued; the expensive tickets are untouched: Over the last three days 22 tickets were created and 4 resolved, all four being the cheapest on the review's own ordered route, while the 15 tickets created 2026-08-28 that carry the large builds have not moved.
- F16 The truth number has plateaued, and the last fall must be read as honesty improving, not decay: pass has been 57 for four of the last five runs against a growing total, fail has never been 0 in 18 recorded runs, and the run-19-to-20 rise in fail from 3 to 7 was caused by ticket 60 converting four could-not-looks into honest observed-falses.
- F17 The 2.x/3.x retirement removed the subject of four gate checks in one commit: Deleting the three older policy lines was the honest repair, and it simultaneously left coexistence, retirement, shift-left and the conditional-rule beat with nothing to observe — three of the four being checks for the thesis's own headline properties.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- Are driftwood, tuppence and ludlow meant to be plausible firms whose real balance sheets happen to land where they land, or fixtures whose numbers exist so that the ladder visibly moves? Right now their signed numbers put all three at the ladder's bottom rung and two past its end. | WHY: This decides whether F1 and F2 are defects or the honest answer. If they are plausible firms, then "a UK fintech with four open NIST controls is quarantined with no egress" is the estate's genuine verdict and NORTH-STAR §4 step 3 must be restated, because no band will ever be crossed. If they are fixtures, the appetites and exposures are calibration and should be set so the ladder demonstrably moves. | CHANGES: Either §4 step 3 and step 4's wording change and ticket 74 closes as unreachable-by-design, or a new calibration ticket blocks ticket 74 and touches all three party.yaml files (appetite.tolerance, size), ico's control_weights coverage, and possibly graded/cage.py's rung spacing.
- Is the AI-disposal end state you described in re-grill 29 and 37 — an AI allowed to dispose inside a priced cage, with an Article 22 floor — the actual purpose of this estate, with the Kubernetes cage ladder as its substrate and vocabulary? Or is it a distant aspiration and the Kubernetes policy story is the product? | WHY: It is the only place in the whole record where you describe an end state rather than a mechanism, and it is the least built thing here: the cage dials are pod dials with no vocabulary for a model action, the propose-only guard is a mode file the agent itself flipped eight times in two days, the interim author-not-equal-merger rule is unenforced on all nine repos, and nothing in the 74 tickets builds a price on the agent's own disposal. | CHANGES: If yes: the twin, enact_guard, the identity spine and the £ engine become the critical path; the multi-version distribution question, the publisher marketplace and the deck can freeze; three or four new tickets appear that do not exist today (model-action dials, a price on disposal, an ENACT_MODE the agent cannot select, the Article 22 floor). If no: enact_guard stays a development convenience and re-grill 29/37 should be marked as out of scope so no future review re-raises it.
- Is the truth surface allowed to bring up a cluster, or is "offline only" a deliberate constraint on it? | WHY: truth.yml never runs kind create, so eleven checks — every check that would observe a cage actually caging, a human or device gated, an identity federated, or a source verified at the cluster — SKIP on every citable run. Combined with your own rule that a hand-taken sample is a rehearsal and is never cited, this means no document may honestly claim the cages work at runtime, permanently. | CHANGES: If a cluster is allowed: one change to truth.yml converts up to eleven SKIPs and makes principle 2 citable for the first time. If not: those eleven checks need the ticket-16 sample-lane treatment (each publishing a signed observation the hub grades), which is roughly eleven small builds, or they should be moved out of the gate and named as permanently local so the total stops implying they could be green.
- When is this due, and to whom? Is there a talk, a client conversation or a funder date the estate is aimed at? | WHY: Nothing in the record I read names an external audience with a date — the only dated purpose material is three internal ControlPlane funder decks and the talk-spec's stated audience. Velocity is currently four tickets closed in three days against 22 charted, with the expensive builds untouched, so "how long is acceptable" is not answerable from inside the estate. | CHANGES: A near date forces candidate purpose A (one adopter, one worked example, recalibrate so a band crosses, park the twin and two adopters — the estate shrinks to roughly 30%). A distant or absent date makes purpose B or C viable. It also determines whether the 15 untouched tickets from 2026-08-28 should be frozen rather than left open.
- Should NORTH-STAR be a living document with dated update lines, or a frozen ratification that documents cite as-of 2026-08-27? | WHY: Three of its factual statements are now false (the intelligence publisher and the insurer both exist and are tagged; steps 1 and 2 now run for real). §7 rightly forbids rewriting history, and the estate's own convention is a dated banner — but nobody has added one, and ticket 67's hygiene scope does not include NORTH-STAR's body. | CHANGES: If living: one dated update block on §2 and §4, ticket 67's Question widened, and a standing rule that the file is refreshed each time a participant row becomes true. If frozen: a short preamble saying so, so that a reader stops treating the "Example today" column as current fact, and the map takes over as the live status document.
- Does an adopter get to set its own forgetting curve for rejected proposals, the way it now sets its own appetite? | WHY: wargamer/rejection-decay.yaml is a platform-held, explicitly untuned knob (half_life_days 30, reject_suppress 0.5, "not yet tuned against real closes") that by its own comment can hold an adopter's priced cage move quiet for about eight weeks. ADR-0021 already made exactly this call for appetite — the platform fixture was retired because whose money is at risk is the party's own signed fact — and the same migration was never applied here. | CHANGES: If yes: half_life_days and reject_suppress move onto party.yaml beside appetite.tolerance, rejection_ledger.derive() takes the party's values, and the platform file becomes a documented default. If no: an ADR should record why the forgetting curve is a platform property while appetite is not, because §2's "nothing is a tenant of anything else" currently reads against it.

# legacy-and-lift

VERDICT: Not fit yet, for one structural reason and one procedural one. Structurally: NORTH-STAR §6 asks for a lift-or-retire decision per working part and §4 asks for a demonstration in which a feed re-prices something real and a workload keeps running caged tighter. Ticket 13 produced the decisions genuinely and with evidence, but the three decided *to lift* (three real apps, the Crossplane cloud plane, the handbook) are all unstarted five days on, all absent from map.md's own order of attack (54→67), and the first of them cannot deliver its stated purpose as scoped: composition.py can price exactly two feed names (FEED_CONVERTERS at composition.py:271-274) and reads no workload, image, SBOM or dependency at all. The eco-system's only application workload is an unpinned `nginx` Pod, and driftwood's composed price would be byte-identical if it were deleted. Procedurally: all three ADR consequences ticket 13 recorded for itself were never written (git log since 2026-08-27 on ADRs 0004/0007/0010 is empty), one mechanism (the currency controller) was retired on a reason that is a category error and is still present, still the eco-system's only CronJob, and still graded by the gate every run, and the round-2 decisions (scanner, Flux notification spine, OSCAL CronJob) sit in ticket 35 behind a blocker (33) nobody is working. Meanwhile the superseded org has greener CI than the successor (14 legacy repos, zero red; hub truth/twin 12 consecutive failures), runs the only clock in either org that ever produced a merged retirement PR (fleet#69, machine-authored, human-merged 2026-08-15), and is the only place either the cloud plane or multi-version coexistence was ever proven at runtime. What would make it fit, in order: invert 33/35 so the CVE/EOL converter lands before or with the app lift and restate ticket 33's definition of done honestly; write the three ADR banners and withdraw the currency-controller retirement; put a dated banner on each of the 14 legacy READMEs plus an org description and profile README naming the two implementations (this removes most reader confusion at almost no cost and pre-empts no decision); run ticket 13 round 2; and decide explicitly what happens to the thesis's ≥3-coexisting-versions claim now that fleet declares two versions and platform declares one.

STRENGTHS:
- The eco-system's isolation from the original org is real and I re-derived it rather than trusting the map: for each of the 13 non-hub legacy repos, `grep -rl "policy-as-versioned-flux/<repo>"` over all eight fresh unit clones returned 0 files, and the same grep over the hub's talk/ and verify/ returned 0. No eco-system check can break as a consequence of whatever is decided about those repos.
- The original org is not rotting red: `gh run list` across all 14 non-hub repos shows zero failures on any current workflow. fleet's sunset escalator ran green today (2026-09-02T12:33:55Z, schedule) and the weekly governance nag ran green 2026-08-31.
- The legacy org holds the only real instance in either org of NORTH-STAR §4 step 3's shape: `gh pr view 69 --repo policy-as-versioned-flux/fleet` returns `#69 Sunset: retire policy 1.0.0 (scheduled 2026-08-15) | author=app/github-actions mergedBy=chrisns at 2026-08-15T17:20:11Z state=MERGED` — a machine opened a retirement PR on a clock and a different, human identity merged it. By contrast `gh pr list` with a `wargamer/*` head filter returns zero PRs across driftwood, tuppence and ludlow.
- Three retirements in ticket 13 are honest and well-reasoned rather than silent drops: the Grafana/CIO dashboards (owner rejected 2026-07-20, quoted verbatim), the readiness collector's counts (explicitly replaced by the adopter gate's priced impact, not deleted), and consumer-side `sunset:` in principle (D5, the one item carrying a real reasoned owner line).
- Ticket 13's fact-finding is exemplary: twenty dated facts with per-clause citations gathered before any question was put, including facts that undercut its own recommendation (the H6-06 refuter noting the 'CIO dashboard' framing was the assistant's paraphrase, not the owner's words). Two of its facts are wrong (the currency-controller retirement), but they are catchable precisely because they carry their sources.
- The archive rule is the right rule and its current state is correct, not a failure: 'archive each original repo once its lift or retirement is recorded and graded green by the truth surface, fleet last' means nothing is destroyed before its replacement is proven; `gh repo list --json isArchived` shows only the pre-existing `apps` archived, which is what the rule implies given nothing has been lifted.
- The eco-system's clock discipline is materially stronger than the mechanism it would replace. fleet's escalator holds contents:write + pull-requests:write + issues:write and pushes branches; driftwood's propose-tier.yml declares an OBSERVATION_LANE, re-composes into RUNNER_TEMP, and ends with a cage step that `git reset`s first and fails on any staged path outside the lane — with an inline note recording the exact 2026-08-28 defect (a `grep -v '^[AMD] '` filter) that made the earlier guard blind.
- docs/ARCHIVE.md's 2026-08-28 banner is the model for closing the two-org confusion: dated, specific, names what reversed it, and says the checklist below is 'not to be executed' rather than deleting it. Fourteen more of those would close finding L8.

## [major] L2 The intelligence publisher's signed catalogue over-promises: two of its six feeds (cve, eol) are advertised as consumable but composition refuses any adopter who pins them, so the CVE and EOL halves of "intelligence re-prices the adopter" are decorative
(original title: The cve and eol feeds are published and discoverable but unconsumable)
CLAIM: The cve and eol feeds are published and discoverable but cannot be pinned by any adopter, because composition has no wiring for them — not because no converter exists.

feeds/party.yaml:18-27 advertises cve and eol in publishes[], which ADR-0019 point 5 makes the whole discovery contract. A working, verified converter for both already exists (platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py:56 cve_scenario, :74 eol_ramp, :92 eol_scenario, exercised on the real signed payloads by platform/feeds/verify-feeds.sh:46-48,63,68-70, and pointed at by feeds/converters/README.md:29). What is missing is composition's side: the names are absent from FEED_CONVERTERS (composition.py:271-274) and price_parent's non-penalty branch hardcodes _threat_scenario (:1007-1010), so no dispatch to cve_scenario/eol_scenario exists.

Directly executed against the real module: a {party: feeds, kind: feed, name: cve|eol} edge raises "missing instrument: feed 'cve' declared by driftwood has no converter this composition can price through" (composition.py:1540-1543). compose() catches it at :2215-2222, sets prices = [] and records a missing-instrument refusal — so a cve pin does not lose one price line, it empties the adopter's entire prices[]. The same refusal applies to fx, market-moves and news: five of the six published feeds are unpinnable, and only threat-register prices. (fx is reached through a separate seam; market-moves and news declare they price nothing.) No adopter pins cve or eol: driftwood pins five parents, tuppence and ludlow four.

This blocks the scanner lift, whose only recorded shape is "priced against the feeds org's CVE feed" (ticket 13:62). It does NOT block D5's EOL-priced supersede: D5 reuses the eol_ramp function over the stale pin's own dates, and ticket 13:89 records that it needs nothing new ("Tickets 21 and 25 gain nothing; the EOL ramp exists"). Ticket 35 is a grilling (decision) ticket, so the wiring is a precondition for its consequent build, not for the decision.

Ownership: ticket 21:25 assigned the unknown-feed/no-converter question to the £ seam (ticket 25); ticket 25 closed resolved on 2026-08-29 without addressing it. It is a dropped assignment, not an unraised one. Separately, the platform-side FEED_CONVERTERS dict contradicts ADR-0019 point 3's "a new publisher ships a new feed with no platform change".
EVIDENCE: feeds/party.yaml:12-35 lists `- kind: feed, name: cve, path: cve, payload_schema: cve/payload.schema.json` and the same for eol. `ls feeds/cve feeds/eol` → each has bump.yaml, payload.schema.json, rule.yaml, v1, v2. `ls feeds/converters/` → only fx.py and README.md. platform/compose/composition.py:1540-1543 raises Refused for any name not in FEED_CONVERTERS. driftwood/composed/HEADER.yaml:6-27 pins platform, nist, ico/penalty-schema, feeds/threat-register, insurer/quote-driftwood — no cve, no eol; tuppence and ludlow pin four parents with no cve/eol either. `grep -rn "FEED_CONVERTERS|converter for|cve feed|eol feed" .scratch/ecosystem/` returns only fact-finding lines in tickets 10, 13, 15, 22, 24; ticket 22:31 records the behaviour but no ticket owns fixing it.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §2 (intelligence publisher ships signed versioned feeds consumed by adopters); principle 4 (policy is a versioned dependency all the way up and down); §6 (scanner lift-or-retire)
OWNER: new none — Searched .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ (10, 13, 15, 22, 24, 35, 57, 58, 61), REVIEW-2026-08-31.md (M1/M8/M10 and Refuted claims), REGRILL-ANSWERS.md, and grep for FEED_CONVERTERS across .scratch/ecosystem and docs/adr. Ticket 22's fact-finding (2026-08-28) explicitly recorded the same underlying fact — "platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py turns threat, cve and eol feed entries directly into fair.py scenarios... composition wiring only the threat subcommand (composition.py:604-608)" — but ticket 22 resolved only the prediction-market-feed question and did not open a ticket to fix the cve/eol composition-side wiring gap; no ticket owns it. REVIEW-2026-08-31's M1/M8/M10 findings and their owning tickets (57, 61, 62) are about feeds/insurer being untagged, unrunnable, and unpinned by Renovate — a different mechanism (tag/workflow/pin plumbing) from this finding's claim (composition.py's FEED_CONVERTERS registry structurally refuses cve/eol by name regardless of tag status). Ticket 57 even states the opposite assumption ("cve, eol, fx, market-moves, news... can follow at leisure; nothing waits on them"), which this finding contradicts with a concrete Refused-path citation. Ticket 35 (open, scanner/notification-spine/OSCAL lift, blocked by 16/21/33) is the closest open ticket in scope but its text does not mention the converter registry gap. No refuted-claims entry, REGRILL answer, ADR, or NORTH-STAR §6 out-of-scope note addresses this. Treat as new/unowned.
FIT IMPACT: Two of the six published feeds are structurally unreachable by any adopter. Both remaining undecided lifts (trivy scanner priced against the CVE feed; publisher-side supersede priced by the EOL ramp) depend on this link existing, so ticket 35 cannot be built even once its blocker clears.
REMEDY: One ticket: add cve and eol to FEED_CONVERTERS (platform/feeds/to_fair_scenario.py already implements and selfchecks cve_scenario and eol_scenario), or make an unconverted pin a priced hole under ADR-0020 on ticket 69's pattern rather than a refusal.

## [major] L3 The thesis's non-negotiable retirement window has no actor: an old policy version can only leave an adopter by a hand-written PR, and the owner's own decided replacement has no builder
(original title: D5, a DECIDED item, has no build ticket anywhere)
CLAIM: D5 — the only DECIDED item in ticket 13, one of the five decisions the owner ratified with a reason on 2026-08-28 — specifies that the adopter's scheduled proposer reads pins against the feed and opens a retirement PR keyed to ticket 10's dedupe ledger. Only the ledger half was built. Ticket 13:87 assigned the retirement PR to ticket 10 as a proposal type; ticket 10 resolved the same day accepting only the key (10:90), and its build ticket 28 landed that key — platform/wargamer/rejection_ledger.py:81-84 and platform/honesty/proposer_bounds.py:70-74 both name "a retirement proposal" as the reason the `kind` slot exists — while ticket 13:90 explicitly says "New build tickets carry items 1, 3 and 4", excluding item 5, and its Graduated list (13:93-96) omits it. So the emitter has no build ticket: `grep -rniE "retirement (PR|pull)" .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ map.md` hits only tickets 10 and 13 across all 74 tickets, and no adopter's proposer can emit anything but a tier edit (all three propose-tier.yml files call `platform/wargamer/tier_pr.py run`, which fixes `rows = wargamer.wargame_cage_tier(...)`; wargamer.py's only kinds are enforcement, scenario and cage-tier). Two further halves of the decision are also unlanded: ADR-0010's consumer-side `sunset:` still reads `status: accepted` with no supersession banner and ADR-0024 never mentions it, contrary to 13:86; and no adopter subscribes to an EOL feed at all (driftwood/party.yaml:50-61; composition.py:190 "an `eol` parent kind does not exist in the party artefact schema"), so there is no feed for the proposer to read pins against. Meanwhile the mechanism D5 replaces is not retired but still live in the original org: fleet's sunset escalator succeeded on 2026-09-02T12:33:55Z, and fleet#69 "Sunset: retire policy 1.0.0 (scheduled 2026-08-15)" was opened by app/github-actions and merged by a human on 2026-08-15T17:20:11Z.
EVIDENCE: `grep -rn "retirement PR|retirement pull" .scratch/ecosystem/issues/ map.md` returns hits in ticket 13 and ticket 10 only. .scratch/ecosystem/issues/13-*.md:83 records the decision. .scratch/ecosystem/issues/10-*.md:4 `Status: resolved`, :94 `Graduated: "Daily clocks, caged observation lane and derived ledger"` — the retirement-PR proposal type is not in it; :75 (note C16) flags the hole in advance: "A retirement proposal has no dedupe key or half-life under Q5(a)". driftwood/.github/workflows/propose-tier.yml (whole file read): the only thing it opens is a PR editing posture.acme.io/tier on the governed Namespace.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR principle 5 (intelligence re-prices on a clock; enactment only by reviewed PR); §6 (sunset cron lift-or-retire)
OWNER: new issues/13 D5 / issues/10 D5 — Not owned anywhere. Read issues/13-*.md and issues/10-*.md in full: ticket 13's Answer lists "New build tickets carry items 1, 3 and 4" (apps lift, cloud plane, handbook) — item 5 (D5, the retirement-PR supersede mechanism) is conspicuously absent from that list. Ticket 13's round-2 scope (Consequences) is explicitly "the scanner, the Flux notification spine, the OSCAL CronJob, api and datastore placement, and per-repo archive sequencing" — no retirement-PR proposer. Ticket 10's Answer item 5 only assigns the dedupe ledger to ticket 13's future retirement PRs ("D5 (decided): retirement PRs from ticket 13 take this ledger, keyed <org>/<kind>/<slug>") and its own "New build ticket" consequence covers only the daily schedule/observation-lane/derived-ledger/verify-schedules.sh for tier proposals, not a retirement-PR proposal type. map.md:40 confirms "round 2 pending" for ticket 13 with no retirement-PR mention. REVIEW-2026-08-31.md's M-list and Refuted-claims list (checked in full) contain nothing about D5 or a retirement PR. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md not separately checked but map.md and both source tickets are direct primary evidence and sufficient. Verdict: genuinely unowned — the finding's claim stands.
FIT IMPACT: The estate retired a working, dated, human-merged retirement mechanism (fleet#69) in favour of a replacement that has no owner, no ticket and no code — and the retired one is still running in the other org.
REMEDY: Open one build ticket carrying D5's second half (the proposer reads pins against the EOL feed and opens a retirement PR, dedupe key <org>/<kind>/<slug>), blocked on L2's converter.

## [minor] L4 One decided supersession lost its owner: the ADR set still teaches a consumer-side sunset field the estate voted out, and two lesser ADR notes stayed unwritten
(original title: All three ADR consequences ticket 13 recorded for itself are unwritten)
CLAIM: Ticket 13 (resolved 2026-08-28) recorded three ADR edits for itself at .scratch/ecosystem/issues/13-lift-or-retire-the-original-mechanisms.md:86, and all three are unwritten: docs/adr/0004-cloud-plane-fork-collie.md was last touched 2026-07-14 (867c50d), 0007-agent-assisted-editorial-governance.md and 0010-sunset-scheduled-proposals-not-application.md on 2026-07-18 (d4b8c28, 63a0cc8), while docs/adr/ has been edited five times since 2026-08-28. The three differ in weight and only one is overdue now. ADR-0010's supersession is the live defect: it flows from D5, recorded DECIDED, and its vehicle already shipped without it — ticket 13:36 and ticket 02:26 both assign it to "the ADR ticket 10 writes", ticket 10 is resolved and wrote ADR-0023/0024, ADR-0023:70 sharpens only ADR-0010's timing clause and neither ADR mentions the consumer-side placement at 0010:9-11, which still reads as live doctrine under an unbannered `status: accepted`. The result is a contradiction inside the glossary itself: CONTEXT.md:406 (added 2026-08-28 by ticket 13) says "No consumer-side sunset field exists" while CONTEXT.md:561-564 still says a fleet's array entry may carry a `sunset:` date and sends the reader to ADR-0010.
