ADR-0004's dated sequencing note and ADR-0007's last-mile confirmation flow from items 3 and 4, both recorded PROVISIONAL, whose build tickets 35 (blocked by 16, 21, 33) and 34 (blocked by 09) are open and which ticket 13 says will not close before round 2; neither ADR asserts something the estate decided against — item 3 says "ADR-0004 stays accepted" and item 4 confirms ADR-0007's proposed section rather than reversing it, so what is owed is a dated note and the deletion of "(proposed — confirm)" at 0007:31. No ticket names any of the three edits: 33, 34 and 35 do not, and ticket 67's items (a)-(d) do not. Two corrections to the record of the finding itself: the auditor's quoted git log used the non-existent path docs/adr/0010-sunset-scheduled-proposals.md (a pathspec matching nothing exits 0 with no output), so that evidence was vacuous as run though the conclusion survives re-derivation; and NORTH-STAR §7's dated-banner rule governs five named documents, none of them an ADR — the ADR banner convention is repo practice (0011:7, 0014:5, 0015:5,7, 0016:5, 0018:5).
EVIDENCE: `git log --oneline --since=2026-08-27 -- docs/adr/0004-cloud-plane-fork-collie.md docs/adr/0007-agent-assisted-editorial-governance.md docs/adr/0010-sunset-scheduled-proposals.md` returns nothing. docs/adr/0010-*.md heads `status: accepted` with no banner (six other ADRs — 0013, 0014, 0015, 0016, 0018, 0024 — carry supersession text); the only later reference, docs/adr/0023-*.md:70, sharpens ADR-0010's timing clause and says nothing about the consumer-side placement D5 superseded. docs/adr/0004-*.md still reads `status: accepted` and "The cloud plane is an **integral second plane of the floor** (not deferred) ... proven at the admission level on KiND" with no dated note. docs/adr/0007-*.md:31 still reads "## Last-mile to non-technical consumers (proposed — confirm)". Ticket 13's own line: .scratch/ecosystem/issues/13-*.md:86.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §7 (add a dated banner to each superseded document; do not rewrite history); principle 6 (the record is falsifiable)
OWNER: new ticket 13 (resolved 2026-08-28) — Ticket 13's own Answer/Consequences text (issues/13-*.md, "Answer" section) promises exactly the three ADR edits the finding cites verbatim: "ADR-0010:5-9 ... is superseded by the ADR ticket 10 writes. ADR-0004 gains a dated sequencing note. ADR-0007's last-mile section is confirmed by item 4." Ticket 13 is Status: resolved, but resolving it did not itself execute these edits — they were forward commitments, and no other open ticket picks them up: ticket 67 (open, "the record matches the surface") scopes only (a) drift-review/NORTH-STAR link parity, (b) ico bump.yaml reset, (c) OBSERVATION_LANE trims, (d) a truth.log gate check — it never names ADR-0004/0007/0010 (grep for those strings in issues/67-*.md returns nothing). REVIEW-2026-08-31.md has zero mentions of ADR-0004, ADR-0007, or ADR-0010 (grep returns nothing), so it neither lists nor refutes this as C1/M1-M18. REGRILL-ANSWERS.md not checked directly but ticket 13 postdates the 2026-08-27 drift review and is the source of the commitment itself, so no earlier owner decision could have mooted a promise made later. This is a new, unowned finding: a resolved ticket left an explicit self-recorded consequence unexecuted, and nothing tracks the follow-through.
FIT IMPACT: A reader following ADR-0004 today is told the Crossplane cloud plane is integral and KiND-proven; the eco-system has zero Crossplane. A reader following ADR-0010 is told the consumer-side sunset field is live doctrine; it was decided away. The ADR set is the estate's decision record and three of its entries currently assert what the estate has decided against.
REMEDY: Three dated banners, roughly ten lines total — the cheapest correction available in the estate.

## [major] L5 The map retires the only mechanism that keeps a running pod's posture true after admission, on a confusion between two meanings of "currency" — and the gate grades it every run as still alive
(original title: The currency controller is recorded as retired, on a wrong reason, and is not retired)
CLAIM: Ticket 13 item 2 (issues/13-...:77) records the currency controller as retired on two clauses that both fail on the primary sources, and map.md:113 restates that retirement flatly, under "Not yet specified", as a closed question — while the module is present, installed and graded. Clause 1, "ticket 07's fx feed replaces it", is a homonym confusion: the controller's currency is version currency ("posture re-evaluated after admission ... when a running workload's admitted version is retired from the platform array", README.md:1-7), ticket 07's is money and FX (07:22, "the FX rate is a signed feed, kind: feed, name: fx"); the owner's own GAPS register keeps them as separate rows 2.8 and 3.18. Clause 2, "it 404s", traces to ticket 12:24, an HTTP 404 from GET .../resourcesets/policy-versions on a cluster with no ResourceSet, which ticket 12 itself classifies "Substrate repair, not a decision (GAPS 2.8)", and which GAPS 1.4 and 2.8 both prescribe fixing. The item's headline "Reason: two live implementations is the finding (H9-11)" cannot apply either: no legacy repo contains a currency controller (gh api trees for fleet, policy, governance-agent, cloud, c2p-collector, readiness-collector return no matching path), so there is exactly one implementation. Meanwhile the module is present at run-21's platform SHA 46cd775, is the eco-system's only CronJob (manifests/cronjob.yaml:12,19; the same grep over the other seven units and over the hub returns nothing), is installed by talk/up.sh:68 and listed in talk/RUNBOOK.md:79, and is graded by the gate every run — as SKIP, never PASS, since the live tail has never found a cluster (capture .estate-clone_platform_currency-controller_verify-currency.out on origin/main; not in verify-exclusions.txt). Two live normative documents still depend on it: CONTEXT.md:245-246 defines the glossary term "De-postured" through it, in an entry rewritten 2026-08-28, the same day the retirement was recorded; ADR-0014 (status accepted, superseded only in part) at :29-35 calls de-posturing "the estate's own cage" and names currency.py as what performs it. Separately, resolved ticket 32 scoped "make the currency controller fail loudly on a missing ResourceSet" (32:9) and never did it: its Answer never mentions the module, grep -rn 404 over currency-controller/ returns nothing, currency.py:141,148-157 leaves the ResourceSet GET unguarded, and currency.py's last commit is e34ae7f of 2026-08-23, predating both tickets. Two resolved tickets therefore hold contradictory dispositions with no cross-reference, and the eco-system's only CronJob is unowned. Two qualifications to the original wording: ticket 13 marks Q1-Q4 (including this item) PROVISIONAL on a bare agree, and its staged rule would not have deleted anything yet because the module has never graded green — which makes map.md:113's unqualified "The currency controller is retired" the sharper defect rather than an un-executed deletion.
EVIDENCE: platform/currency-controller/README.md:1-8 — 'posture re-evaluated after admission ... when a running workload's admitted version is retired from the platform array, a bounded reconcile pass re-patches its posture (default) or evicts it, and the SVID follows'. map.md ticket 07 line — 'FX is a signed `fx` feed'. `ls platform/currency-controller/` at platform=46cd775 → currency.py, manifests/, up.sh, README.md, verify-currency.sh; manifests/cronjob.yaml:19 `schedule: "* * * * *"`. `grep -rln "kind: CronJob"` over all eight units returns only this file. `git show origin/main:talk/captures/.estate-clone_platform_currency-controller_verify-currency.out | tail -1` → `SKIP: offline proof holds; live tail could not look: kind cluster 'driftwood' is not listed by kind get clusters — stale posture is re-evaluated post-admission`. .scratch/ecosystem/issues/32-*.md:3-5 `Status: resolved`, scope includes 'make the currency controller fail loudly on a missing ResourceSet'. `grep -rn 404 platform/currency-controller/` → nothing; the 404 premise traces to ticket 12:24, an HTTP 404 from GET .../resourcesets/policy-versions on a cluster with no ResourceSet — a substrate absence.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §6 (one decision each, lift or retire); §5 (the truth surface is the only citable read)
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 13 (Answer item 2, provisional; round 2 not closed) with contradiction against ticket 32 (resolved 2026-08-29) — Ticket 13's resolved-but-provisional Answer item 2 states the currency controller "is retired ... because it 404s and ticket 07's fx feed replaces it" — verbatim the wrong-reason claim in the finding (the 404 traces to ticket 12:24's substrate absence, not to ticket 07's FX feed). map.md:113 repeats this retirement as settled fact. Ticket 13 itself says "Ticket 13 does not close before round 2," so the retirement is not finalized. Ticket 32 (resolved 2026-08-29) separately scoped a repair to the same module with no reference to ticket 13's disposition — the contradiction the finding names is real and already latent in ticket 13's own C15 cross-ticket note (which flagged assignment ambiguity but not the reason error or liveness). REVIEW-2026-08-31.md has no C/M or refuted-claims entry for the currency controller (grep confirmed empty), and REGRILL-ANSWERS.md's only currency hit (line 39, re-grill 33) is about FX/perspective pricing, unrelated. No owner decision moots this: the finding should be routed to ticket 13 round 2 (or a new ticket) to correct the stated reason and confirm/deny actual retirement against the live module found at platform=46cd775.
FIT IMPACT: The estate's map records a retirement that did not happen, on a reason that does not survive reading the module's README. The eco-system's only CronJob is therefore unowned: nominally retired, actually graded, live tail never observed.
REMEDY: Withdraw the retirement or re-retire it with a reason grounded in what the module does; correct map.md:113; if it stays, give it an owner and a plan for its live tail.

## [minor] L6 The superseded org's daily clock still runs and can quietly eat the audit-trail exhibit — real, but the truth surface never claimed that namespace, and ticket 13 keeps it live on purpose
(original title: Two live clocks run in the project's own GitHub org, outside the truth surface)
CLAIM: Three scheduled workflows still run green in the project's original GitHub org and none of them is visible to the truth surface: fleet's sunset escalator (`cron: "0 8 * * *"`, `contents: write`, last success 2026-09-02T12:33:55Z), fleet's weekly governance nag and policy's weekly governance nag (both `cron: "0 9 * * 1"`, last successes 2026-08-31T16:44:53Z and 16:28:37Z). The gate globs `verify*.sh` from `.estate-clone` and `verify/` only (talk/verify-all.sh:46), `clone-estate.sh:23` clones only the eight units, and schedules.py builds its targets from the ESTATE directory listing plus the hub (schedules.py:137-145, 85-86), so no gate script anywhere in the hub or the eight units references a legacy repo and no TRUTH line would move if any of the three clocks stopped. fleet additionally carries nine `verify-*.sh` scripts the glob never reaches and the exclusions file never names. Two corrections to the finding as filed: (a) the disposition IS owned and decided — ticket 13 (resolved) records these exact clocks in its Facts and answers Q2 with "original repos archived one by one after their replacement grades green, fleet last, after ticket 16's fan-out" (map.md:40, provisional on a bare agree, round 2 pending), so ticket 56 is the wrong owner to cite; (b) this is not a violation of NORTH-STAR §5, whose completeness rule is over globbed verify scripts, but an unobserved live mechanism sitting on the §6 boundary, where §6:68 names "sunset cron" among the parts owed a lift-or-retire decision. The residual gap is narrower than "the truth surface is incomplete": between now and ticket 16 grading green, three clocks with write permissions run inside the project's own org with no instrument that would report their stopping, and nothing dates or bounds that window.
EVIDENCE: clone-estate.sh:23 `UNITS=(platform driftwood tuppence ludlow nist ico feeds insurer)` — no legacy repo. verify/schedules/schedules.py:85-86 `REMOTE = "policy-as-versioned-{unit}/{unit}"` and `HUB_REMOTE = "policy-as-versioned-flux/policy-as-versioned-flux"`; :138 '(unit, checkout root, remote) for every unit plus the hub'. `gh run list --repo policy-as-versioned-flux/fleet --limit 5` → sunset escalator schedule success 2026-09-02T12:33:55Z; weekly governance nag schedule success 2026-08-31T16:44:53Z. NORTH-STAR.md:54 'One command, on a schedule, in CI, is the only source any document may cite for "what works"'.
AMBITION: NORTH-STAR §5 (the truth surface) and §6 (the original org's working parts are lifted or explicitly retired)
OWNER: new issues/56 (adjacent, not owning); issues/13 (adjacent, not owning) — Ticket 56 ("the citable run can see whether the clocks ran") is the closest existing ticket and matches REVIEW-2026-08-31's M6, but its own text scopes the fix to "the eight orgs' unpinned scripts" (hub + the 8 unit repos in clone-estate.sh's UNITS list) — it never mentions fleet or the legacy org. Ticket 13 ("lift or retire the original mechanisms") owns the eventual decision to lift/retire fleet's sunset-escalator and other mechanisms (item 2 of its Answer: "fleet goes last, after ticket 16's fan-out reconciles"), but that decision is recorded PROVISIONAL and unexecuted — fleet is still live today — and ticket 13 says nothing about observing fleet's clocks in the interim via the truth surface. REVIEW-2026-08-31's Refuted-claims list has "No scheduled clock has ever fired in any org" refuted, but that refutation is about the eco-system's own clocks firing, not about legacy-org clock observability. No ticket, ADR, or REGRILL answer addresses the specific gap: fleet's two live scheduled workflows run inside the project's own GitHub org but outside both clone-estate.sh's UNITS list and verify/schedules/schedules.py's REMOTE template, so no TRUTH line moves if they break. This is a genuine new finding, adjacent to but not covered by ticket 56 or ticket 13.
FIT IMPACT: While a mechanism is neither lifted nor archived it runs unobserved inside the boundary NORTH-STAR §5 claims to cover, so the truth surface's implicit claim to completeness is not quite true.
REMEDY: Either disable fleet's workflows (deferred under 13 item 2, fleet-last, so not yet), or add one honest sentence to the TRUTH-line documentation recording that two clocks in the reference org sit deliberately outside the surface.

## [major] L7 Principle 4's "older lines are patchable" is not true today: the platform runs a single version because the older lines could not be patched, so the thesis's headline multi-version mechanism is undemonstrated and unowned
(original title: Neither implementation demonstrates the thesis's >=3-coexisting-versions requirement today)
CLAIM: The 2022 Medium post's requirement — "the runtime must support multiple policy versions simultaneously — at least three semver versions" (research/03-blogs-thesis.md:40, :172, :207; glossed as a count and called "the crux of the original implementation" at CONTEXT.md:153-155) — is met by neither org's declared version set today. Fleet's array declares two (clusters/cluster1/policy-versions.yaml, elements 2.0.0/tag 2.0.3 and 2.2.0) since fleet#69 removed the 1.0.0 element on 2026-08-15 — proposed by the sunset cron, merged by the owner, exactly as ADR-0010 requires. Platform's declares one (distribution/versions.yaml:77, 4.0.0) since the dated RETIRED 2026-08-29 note dropped 2.0.0/2.0.1/3.0.0 and the 2.0.2/3.0.1 backports for two independently observed defects. The estate says so with its own instruments: on run 21 both verify-coexistence.sh and verify-retirement.sh SKIP because "versions.yaml declares one version (4.0.0)". Two corrections to scope: (a) the ≥3 requirement is the Medium post's, not the talk's — the talk carries the seven "-able" properties, and research/03-blogs-thesis.md:13-17 says so explicitly; (b) what falls short is the declared set, not the mechanism — both ResourceSets range an arbitrary-length array, and no cluster was probed for either org, so this is a claim about declarations, not about observed runtime coexistence. On ownership: the platform half is already found (REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:69, M11(1)), decided provisionally (ticket 58 Q1a) and owned by open ticket 63, whose isolated-default flip is designated the second declared line (5.0.0) and is "ready to run". What genuinely has no owner is the step from two declared versions to three, and any decision about fleet's own two-version set. Ticket 71 is not the gate on re-authoring the older lines: versions.yaml:42-53 gives the reason as ADR-0022's namespaceObject read being a computed major, which cannot ship as a patch on the 2.x/3.x lines; ticket 71 is the separate Kyverno 1.19 engine-support question.
EVIDENCE: `gh api repos/policy-as-versioned-flux/fleet/contents/clusters/cluster1/policy-versions.yaml` decoded: inputs[0].versions holds exactly two elements, `version: "2.0.0"` (tag 2.0.3) and `version: "2.2.0"` — the third, 1.0.0, was retired by the sunset cron via fleet#69 on 2026-08-15. platform/distribution/versions.yaml declares one element, 4.0.0, under a dated 2026-08-29 comment: 'RETIRED 2026-08-29 -- 2.0.0, 2.0.1, 3.0.0 and the two patch backports 2.0.2 and 3.0.1 were all here' for two observed live defects. research/03-blogs-thesis.md:12-18 attributes the >=3-version runtime requirement to the talk. CONTEXT.md:153-155 still calls multi-version coexistence of >=3 versions 'the crux of the original implementation'.
AMBITION: 2022 thesis / the talk's >=3-coexisting-versions requirement; NORTH-STAR principle 4 (older lines are patchable)
OWNER: owned-by-open-ticket ticket 63 (open, unblocked by resolved grilling ticket 58) — This is REVIEW-2026-08-31's M11 (.scratch/ecosystem/REVIEW-2026-08-31.md:69: "One declared version leaves coexistence, retirement and shift-left without a subject"), which was charted into ticket 58 (grilling, Status: resolved) and graduated to ticket 63 (Status: open, Blocked by: 58 — now unblocked per its 2026-08-31 comment). Ticket 58's held round decided Q1: "the ticket-63 isolated flip is the next computed major and becomes the second line" (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/58-grilling-the-four-architectural-gaps-and-the-untagged-p.md). Ticket 63's own text says the flip "can be the coexistence subject" and its comment confirms "the flip cut IS the second declared line (the engine computes a major, so 5.0.0)" and that "Blocker 58 is resolved; this ticket is ready to run" (.scratch/ecosystem/issues/63-the-unlabelled-default-flips-to-isolated.md).

Caveat, not fully covered by ticket 63: the owner decision and ticket 63 only restore platform to a SECOND declared version (one more, not >=3). Finding L7's specific ">=3-coexisting-versions" headline number is therefore not fully re-established even once 63 ships — there is a decided, open, in-flight fix for "coexistence has no subject" at all, but not an explicit commitment back to three-or-more. Fleet's two-version state (retired 1.0.0 via fleet#69) is not mentioned in ticket 58/63/REVIEW-2026-08-31 at all — I found no ticket or ADR discussing fleet's version count specifically. So: the general "no subject for coexistence" gap is owned (ticket 63, in flight); the specific ">=3" threshold claim is not explicitly owned by any ticket I found — recommend flagging that residual as a narrower, still-open sub-point rather than fully refuted/closed.
FIT IMPACT: The honest disposition of the original org can no longer rest on 'the eco-system already shows this better'. On the thesis's own headline mechanism, the successor shows less than the predecessor, and both show less than the claim.
REMEDY: Decide explicitly whether the >=3-version claim is retained, narrowed (the mechanism supports N, demonstrated at N=1 because the older bodies were unsafe) or retired, and correct CONTEXT.md:153-155 where a reader meets it.

MINORS:
- L9 Ticket 13's status header contradicts its own text: Ticket 13 reads `Status: resolved` at line 4 while its own body says twice that it does not close before round 2, which has not run.
- L10 The Renovate dashboard fix was parked on 'whichever ticket first touches renovate.json' and that ticket did not do it: Ticket 13 folded GAPS 3.25 (turn the dependency dashboard on) into the next ticket to touch renovate.json; ticket 61 touched driftwood's renovate.json twice since and left dependencyDashboard false and enabledManagers restricted to custom.regex in all three adopters.
- L11 fleet's sunset escalator fetches its own script from another repo unpinned: The legacy org's daily clock checks out policy-as-versioned-flux/governance-agent with no ref, so it runs whatever is on that repo's default branch — inside the org whose entire argument is that dependencies are pinned and bumped by reviewed PR.
- L12 clone-estate.sh's header is stale in two ways that matter to a reader of the truth surface: The script that defines the boundary of the truth surface says it clones six units when it clones eight, and states as a live limitation that no signed tag exists yet, when 24 unit tags now verify.
- L13 The hub README's 'Project actions queued' list carries a completed item: README.md queues 'Harvest controlplaneio/collie' as an outstanding action, but the harvest is done and lives in the legacy cloud repo, and ADR-0004 describes it in the past tense.

OWNER QUESTIONS:
- Is the point of lifting ledger, storefront and reports to make the £ move when a feed moves — or is it enough that a real, recognisable application runs caged, with the price still coming only from feeds and declared size? | WHY: Ticket 13 Q1's recorded reason is the first ('§4 step 3 is only honest with a real dependency a feed can move'), but ticket 33's scope delivers only the second, and composition.py cannot price a workload at all. The two readings imply different builds and different definitions of done. | CHANGES: If the answer is the first, ticket 35's CVE/EOL converter must come before or with ticket 33, ticket 33's DoD becomes a price check, and finding L1 is a real blocker. If the second, ticket 33 stands as written, its DoD becomes a 'runs caged' check, and the £-from-the-app question moves to an explicitly later round — but the estate must then stop citing §4 step 3 as a thing the lift will make honest.
- The thesis's headline mechanism is three or more policy versions coexisting at runtime. fleet declares two today and platform declares one, for good reasons in both cases. Do you still want that claim demonstrated, and if so by which org? | WHY: It is the single requirement on which the superseded implementation currently shows more than the successor, and it is the reason a reader might argue fleet should stay alive. CONTEXT.md:153-155 still calls it 'the crux'. | CHANGES: If it is retained, something has to re-establish a 3-version array — most likely re-authoring 2.x/3.x bodies under ADR-0022 in platform, which is real work behind ticket 71's Kyverno question, and fleet cannot be archived until it lands. If it is narrowed to 'the mechanism supports N, demonstrated at N=1 because the older bodies were unsafe', CONTEXT.md, README.md and the thesis-mapping documents need that sentence, and fleet's archiving is unblocked.
- Do you want the original org's 14 repos to keep reading as a live system on GitHub until each one is lifted, or should they carry a dated 'superseded reference implementation' banner now, ahead of any archiving decision? | WHY: Banners are reversible and pre-empt nothing; archiving is not. Today there is no org description, no profile README, and no repo says it is superseded, while several link into the hub's current documents as authority for claims those documents no longer make. | CHANGES: If banners now: about 40 lines of README edits plus one org profile page closes most of finding L8 this week, independent of tickets 33/34/35. If not: L8 stays open until the last lift lands, and every reader who arrives before then has to work out which of two implementations is the system.
- The currency controller was recorded retired on the grounds that ticket 07's fx feed replaces it. Reading its README (post-admission posture reconciliation, not money), was that the mechanism you meant to retire — and if it stays, who owns it? | WHY: It is the eco-system's only CronJob, it is graded by the gate every run, a separately resolved ticket 32 scoped a repair to it, and map.md currently tells a reader it is gone. | CHANGES: If it was a mistake, the retirement is withdrawn, map.md:113 is corrected, and the module needs an owner and a plan for its live tail. If you did mean to retire a posture-currency mechanism, the reason needs restating on grounds that survive reading the README, ticket 32's repair should be reverted, and the module and its gate script should be deleted rather than left running.
- fleet's sunset escalator is the only clock in either org that has ever opened a retirement PR a human then merged (fleet#69). D5 replaced it with a publisher-side supersede that no ticket builds. Is running that lifted mechanism once, for real, part of what 'the eco-system has run end to end' means to you? | WHY: It determines whether finding L3 is a gap to close or an accepted deferral, and it is one of only five items in the estate you decided with a stated reason rather than a bare agree. | CHANGES: If yes, a new build ticket carries D5's second half (blocked on the CVE/EOL converter of L2), fleet's escalator stays alive as the fallback until it grades green, and NORTH-STAR §4's step list arguably gains an eighth beat. If no, ADR-0010's supersession is still owed a banner but the retirement-PR proposer can be recorded as explicitly out of the demonstration.
- ADR-0004 still asserts the Crossplane cloud plane is an integral, non-deferred, KiND-proven second plane, and that proof exists only in the legacy fleet repo. Is the eco-system's claim today 'two planes' or 'one plane, with the second sequenced after the Pod slice'? | WHY: The hub README's opening sentence, CONTEXT.md's 'Two planes' section and ADR-0004 all currently say the first; the eight unit repos contain zero Crossplane. Ticket 13 item 3 decided the second but the promised dated note was never written. | CHANGES: If 'one plane for now', three documents get a dated sentence and the front-door overclaim in L8 and L4 closes cheaply. If 'two planes', ticket 13's sequencing is wrong and the cloud-plane lift into tuppence moves onto the route rather than sitting behind the Pod slice.
